## I / 第一章

### I / 第一节

===Original===
The roof of the Sheridan Apartment House, near Washington Square, New York. Let us examine it. There will be stirring happenings on this roof in due season, and it is well to know the ground.

The Sheridan stands in the heart of New York’s Bohemian and artistic quarter. If you threw a brick from any of its windows, you would be certain to brain some rising interior decorator, some Vorticist sculptor or a writer of revolutionary *vers libre*. And a very good thing, too. Its roof, cosy, compact and ten storeys above the street, is flat, paved with tiles and surrounded by a low wall, jutting up at one end of which is an iron structure—the fire-escape. Climbing down this, should the emergency occur, you would find yourself in the open-air premises of the Purple Chicken restaurant—one of those numerous oases in this great city where, in spite of the law of Prohibition, you can still, so the cognoscenti whisper, “always get it if they know you.” A useful thing to remember.

On the other side of the roof, opposite the fire-escape, stands what is technically known as a “small bachelor apartment, penthouse style.” It is a white-walled, red-tiled bungalow, and the small bachelor who owns it is a very estimable young man named George Finch, originally from East Gilead, Idaho, but now, owing to a substantial legacy from an uncle, a unit of New York’s Latin Quarter. For George, no longer being obliged to earn a living, has given his suppressed desires play by coming to the metropolis and trying his hand at painting. From boyhood up he had always wanted to be an artist; and now he is an artist; and, what is more, probably the worst artist who ever put brush to canvas.

===Chinese===
纽约，华盛顿广场附近，谢里丹公寓楼（Sheridan Apartment House）的屋顶。让我们先来端详端详它。用不了多久，这片屋顶上就要掀起几桩热闹风波，把地形摸熟是大有好处的。

谢里丹公寓楼矗立在纽约波西米亚与艺术区的腹心地带。从楼里随便哪扇窗户朝下扔一块砖头，包管会砸破某位冉冉上升的室内装饰师的脑袋，不然就是某位漩涡派（Vorticist）雕塑家，再不然就是某位写革命性自由诗（vers libre）的诗人。而且这实在是桩大好事。它的屋顶舒适、紧凑，高出街面十层楼，平展展地铺着瓷砖，四周围一圈矮墙，矮墙的一端突兀地戳出一座铁家伙——防火梯。倘若遇上紧急情况，顺着它爬下去，你就会发现自己落在了紫鸡餐厅（the Purple Chicken）的露天场子上——那是这座大都会里星罗棋布的绿洲之一；尽管有禁酒令（Prohibition）之法，可正如懂行的人们交头接耳所说，在这种地方“只要人家认得你，就总能弄到酒”。记住这一条，大有好处。

在屋顶另一侧、与防火梯遥遥相对的地方，立着一片按专门术语讲叫做“小单身汉公寓，屋顶平台式”（small bachelor apartment, penthouse style）的所在。那是一座白墙红瓦的平房，而拥有它的小单身汉，是一位名叫乔治·芬奇（George Finch）的十分可敬的年轻人——老家本是爱达荷州东吉利亚德（East Gilead, Idaho），如今托一位叔父留下一笔可观遗产的福，已经成了纽约拉丁区的一分子。乔治既然不必再为糊口奔波，便把压抑多年的心愿尽情放了出来，一头扎进大都会，试起了画笔。打从孩提时代起，他就一心想当艺术家；如今他当上了艺术家；而且，很可能是自有画笔戳上画布以来最糟糕的一位艺术家。

===Original===
For the rest, that large round thing that looks like a captive balloon is the water-tank. That small oblong thing that looks like a summerhouse is George Finch’s outdoor sleeping-porch. Those things that look like potted shrubs are potted shrubs. That stoutish man sweeping with a broom is George’s valet, cook, and man-of-all-work, Mullett.

And this imposing figure with the square chin and the horn-rimmed spectacles which, as he comes out from the door leading to the stairs, flash like jewels in the sun, is no less a person than J. Hamilton Beamish, author of the famous Beamish Booklets (“Read Them and Make the World Your Oyster”) which have done so much to teach the populace of the United States observation, perception, judgment, initiative, willpower, decision, business acumen, resourcefulness, organisation, directive ability, self-confidence, driving-power, originality—and, in fact, practically everything else from Poultry-Farming to Poetry.

The first emotion which any student of the Booklets would have felt on seeing his mentor in the flesh—apart from that natural awe which falls upon us when we behold the great—would probably have been surprise at finding him so young. Hamilton Beamish was still in the early thirties. But the brain of Genius ripens quickly: and those who had the privilege of acquaintance with Mr. Beamish at the beginning of his career say that he knew everything there was to be known—or behaved as if he did—at the age of ten.

===Chinese===
至于屋顶上其余各物：那个又大又圆、瞧着像系留气球的家伙，是水塔；那个又小又长、瞧着像凉亭的家伙，是乔治·芬奇的户外睡廊；那些瞧着像盆栽灌木的，就是盆栽灌木；那个提着扫帚扫来扫去的微胖男人，是乔治的贴身男仆兼厨子兼大小杂务总管，马利特（Mullett）。

而这尊气宇轩昂的人物——方方的下巴，一副角质架眼镜，随着他从通往楼梯的那扇门里走出来，镜片在阳光下熠熠生辉，宛如宝石——不是别人，正是大名鼎鼎的 J·汉密尔顿·比米什（J. Hamilton Beamish），著名的《比米什小册子》（Beamish Booklets）（“读它，让世界成为你的牡蛎”）的作者。这套小册子居功至伟，教会了合众国大众观察力、感知力、判断力、主动性、意志力、决断力、商业头脑、随机应变、组织才干、指挥能力、自信心、推进力、独创性——实际上，还有从养鸡场经营到写诗，几乎其余的一切本事。

任何一个研读过小册子的人，倘若亲眼见到自己的导师，头一种涌起的情绪——撇开我们瞻仰伟人时难免的那种敬畏不谈——多半是吃惊：此人竟如此年轻。汉密尔顿·比米什才三十出头。然而天才的大脑成熟得快：凡在比米什先生事业发轫之初有幸结识他的人都可以作证，早在十岁那年，凡是这世上该知道的事他就已经无所不知——至少举手投足俨然如此。

---
===Original===
Hamilton Beamish’s first act on reaching the roof of the Sheridan was to draw several deep breaths—through the nose, of course. Then, adjusting his glasses, he cast a flashing glance at Mullett: and, having inspected him for a moment, pursed his lips and shook his head.

“All wrong!” he said.

The words, delivered at a distance of two feet in the man’s immediate rear, were spoken in the sharp, resonant voice of one who Gets Things Done—which, in its essentials, is rather like the note of a seal barking for fish. The result was that Mullett, who was highly strung, sprang some eighteen inches into the air and swallowed his chewing-gum. Owing to that great thinker’s practice of wearing No-Jar Rubber Soles (“They Save the Spine”), he had had no warning of Mr. Beamish’s approach.

===Chinese===
汉密尔顿·比米什登上谢里丹公寓屋顶后做的头一件事，是深深吸了几口气——当然，是用鼻子吸的。随后他扶正眼镜，朝马利特倏地射去一瞥；端详了片刻，他抿起嘴唇，摇了摇头。

“全不对！”他说。

这句话是在那人正后方两英尺的地方发射出来的，用的又是那种使命必达的人物特有的尖利而洪亮的嗓门——究其本质，颇像海豹讨鱼时的吠声。结果是神经绷得极紧的马利特朝空中蹿起了十八英寸，把嘴里的口香糖囫囵咽了下去。只因那位大思想家素有穿“无震”橡胶鞋底（No-Jar Rubber Soles，广告词：“保护脊椎”）的习惯，马利特对比米什先生的临近竟无半点预感。

===Original===
“All wrong!” repeated Mr. Beamish.

And when Hamilton Beamish said “All wrong!” it meant “All wrong!” He was a man who thought clearly and judged boldly, without hedging or vacillation. He called a Ford a Ford.

“Wrong, sir?” faltered Mullett, when, realising that there had been no bomb-outrage after all, he was able to speak.

“Wrong. Inefficient. Too much waste motion. From the muscular exertion which you are using on that broom you are obtaining a bare sixty-three or sixty-four percent of result-value. Correct this. Adjust your methods. Have you seen a policeman about here?”

===Chinese===
“全不对！”比米什先生又说了一遍。

汉密尔顿·比米什说“全不对”，意思就是“全不对”。此人思路清晰，判断果敢，绝不含糊其辞、首鼠两端。福特就是福特，他有一说一。

“错了，先生？”马利特讷讷道。直到明白过来压根不曾发生什么炸弹暴行，他才恢复了说话的能力。

“错了。低效。无用动作太多。你在那把扫帚上使出的肌肉力气，换来的成果价值至多百分之六十三四。纠正。调整方法。这一带你见到过警察没有？”

===Original===
“A policeman, sir?”

Hamilton Beamish clicked his tongue in annoyance. It was waste motion, but even efficiency experts have their feelings.

“A policeman. I said a policeman and I meant a policeman.”

Mullett cleared his throat.

===Chinese===
“警察，先生？”

汉密尔顿·比米什烦恼地咂了咂舌。这又是一个无用动作，可效率专家毕竟也是血肉之躯，自有七情六欲。

“警察。我说的是警察，指的就是警察。”

马利特清了清嗓子。

===Original===
“Would he be wanting anything, sir?” he asked a little nervously.

“He wants to become a poet. And I am going to make him one.”

“A poet, sir?”

“Why not? I could make a poet out of far less promising material. I could make a poet out of two sticks and a piece of orange-peel, if they studied my booklet carefully. This fellow wrote to me, explaining his circumstances and expressing a wish to develop his higher self, and I became interested in his case and am giving him special tuition. He is coming up here today to look at the view and write a description of it in his own words. This I shall correct and criticise. A simple exercise in elementary composition.”

“I see, sir.”

===Chinese===
“他会有事相求吗，先生？”他有点儿紧张地问。

“他想当个诗人。而我，就要把他造就成一位诗人。”

“诗人，先生？”

“有什么不可以？比这更没指望的材料，我都能造出诗人来。两根枯树枝加一块橘子皮，只要肯用心研读我的小册子，我也能把它们造就成诗人。这位仁兄写信给我，陈明了自己的境况，表示愿意开发更高尚的自我。我对他的情况产生了兴趣，眼下正对他单独施教。他今天要上来这里观景，再用自己的话写一篇景色描写。写完由我来批改指谬。不过是基础作文的一项简单练习罢了。”

“明白了，先生。”

===Original===
“He is ten minutes late. I trust he has some satisfactory explanation. Meanwhile, where is Mr. Finch? I would like to speak to him.”

“Mr. Finch is out, sir.”

“He always seems to be out nowadays. When do you expect him back?”

“I don’t know, sir. It all depends on the young lady.”

===Chinese===
“他迟到了十分钟。但愿他有个说得过去的解释。在此期间——芬奇先生呢？我有话要同他讲。”

“芬奇先生出门了，先生。”

“他如今好像老是不在家。估摸他什么时候回来？”

“说不准，先生。全得看那位年轻小姐。”

===Original===
“Mr. Finch has gone out with a young lady?”

“No, sir. Just gone to look at one.”

“To look at one?” The author of the Booklets clicked his tongue once more. “You are drivelling, Mullett. Never drivel—it is dissipation of energy.”

“It’s quite true, Mr. Beamish. He has never spoken to this young lady—only looked at her.”

===Chinese===
“芬奇先生是同一位年轻小姐一道出去了？”

“不是，先生。只是去看人家的。”

“去看人家的？”小册子的作者又咂了一下舌。“你这是在胡话连篇，马利特。切莫胡话连篇——那是精力的挥霍。”

“千真万确，比米什先生。那位小姐，他连一句话都没跟人家讲过——只是看。”
===Original===
“Explain yourself.”

“Well, sir, it’s like this. I’d noticed for some time past that Mr. Finch had been getting what you might call choosey about his clothes. …”

“What do you mean, choosey?”

“Particular, sir.”

“Then say particular, Mullett. Avoid jargon. Strive for the Word Beautiful. Read my booklet on Pure English. Well?”

===Chinese===
“把话说清楚。”

“是这么回事，先生。我早就留意到，一段日子以来，芬奇先生对穿衣裳变得越来越——可以这么说——‘事儿’了。……”

“‘事儿’是什么意思？”

“就是讲究，先生。”

“那就说‘讲究’，马利特。避免行话俚语。力求言辞之美。读读我那本《纯正英语》（Pure English）的小册子。往下说。”

===Original===
“Particular about his clothes, sir, I noticed Mr. Finch had been getting. Twice he had started out in blue with the invisible pink twill and then suddenly stopped at the door of the elevator and gone back and changed into the dove-grey. And his ties, Mr. Beamish. There was no satisfying him. So I said to myself ‘Hot dog!’ ”

“You said what?”

“Hot dog, Mr. Beamish.”

“And why did you use this revolting expression?”

“What I meant was, sir, that I reckoned I knew what was at the bottom of all this.”

===Chinese===
“我是说，先生，我留意到芬奇先生对衣裳越来越讲究了。有两回，他都穿着那身蓝中隐粉斜纹的西装出了门，走到电梯门口又突然刹住，折回去换上了鸽灰色。还有他的领带，比米什先生。简直没法叫他称心。于是我就自个儿在心里念叨了一句：‘哇噻！’”

“你说你念叨了句什么？”

“哇噻，比米什先生。”

“你干吗要用这种叫人恶心的说法？”

“我的意思是，先生，我寻思我摸清了这一切的根由。”

===Original===
“And were you right in this reckoning?”

A coy look came into Mullett’s face.

“Yes, sir. You see, Mr. Finch’s behaviour having aroused my curiosity, I took the liberty of following him one afternoon. I followed him to Seventy-Ninth Street, East, Mr. Beamish.”

“And then?”

===Chinese===
“那你这番寻思，寻思准了没有？”

马利特的脸上浮起一层忸怩作态的神色。

“寻思准了，先生。是这样的：芬奇先生的举动勾起了我的好奇，一天下午我便斗胆跟了他一趟。我一直跟到东七十九街，比米什先生。”

“然后呢？”

===Original===
“He walked up and down outside one of those big houses there, and presently a young lady came out. Mr. Finch looked at her, and she passed by. Then Mr. Finch looked after her and sighed and came away. The next afternoon I again took the liberty of following him, and the same thing happened. Only this time the young lady was coming in from a ride in the Park. Mr. Finch looked at her, and she passed into the house. Mr. Finch then remained staring at the house for so long that I was obliged to go and leave him at it, having the dinner to prepare. And what I meant, sir, when I said that the duration of Mr. Finch’s absence depended on the young lady, was that he stops longer when she comes in than when she goes out. He might be back at any minute, or he might not be back till dinnertime.”

Hamilton Beamish frowned thoughtfully.

“I don’t like this, Mullett.”

“No, sir?”

“It sounds like love at first sight.”

“Yes, sir.”

===Chinese===
“他在那儿一幢大宅子外头踱过来踱过去，不多时，一位年轻小姐出来了。芬奇先生望着她，她走了过去。芬奇先生又目送着她，叹了口气，转身回去了。第二天下午，我再次斗胆跟了他一趟，同样的戏码又上演了一遍。只是这一回，那位小姐是在公园里骑完马回来。芬奇先生望着她，她进了宅子。随后芬奇先生对着那座房子呆望，呆望得没完没了，我因为还得做晚饭，只好撇下他先走一步。所以，先生，我说芬奇先生出门多久‘全得看那位年轻小姐’，意思是：她出来的时候，他呆得短；她进去的时候，他呆得长。他说不定这一分钟就回来，也说不定要到开晚饭才回来。”

汉密尔顿·比米什若有所思地皱起了眉头。

“我不喜欢这个，马利特。”

“不喜欢吗，先生？”

“这听上去像是一见钟情。”

“是的，先生。”

===Original===
“Have you read my booklet on ‘The Marriage Sane’?”

“Well, sir, what with one thing and another and being very busy about the house. …”

“In that booklet I argue very strongly against love at first sight.”

“Do you, indeed, sir?”

===Chinese===
“我那本《理智婚姻》（The Marriage Sane）的小册子，你读过没有？”

“这个嘛，先生，一来这也要张罗那也要张罗，二来家里的活计实在忙不过来……”

“在那本小册子里，我旗帜鲜明地反对一见钟情。”

“是嘛，先生？”

===Original===
“I expose it for the mere delirious folly it is. The mating of the sexes should be a reasoned process, ruled by the intellect. What sort of a young lady is this young lady?”

“Very attractive, sir.”

“Tall? Short? Large? Small?”

“Small, sir. Small and roly-poly.”

Hamilton Beamish shuddered violently.

“Don’t use that nauseating adjective! Are you trying to convey the idea that she is short and stout?”

===Chinese===
“我把它那点癫狂愚蠢的原形揭露得体无完肤。两性的结合，应当是一个受理智统辖的理性过程。这位年轻小姐，是怎样一位年轻小姐？”

“非常动人，先生。”

“高？矮？大？小？”

“小，先生。小小的个子，圆滚滚的身材。”

汉密尔顿·比米什剧烈地打了个寒噤。

“不许用这个叫人作呕的形容词！你想表达的意思，莫非是她又矮又胖？”

===Original===
“Oh no, sir, not stout. Just nice and plump. What I should describe as cuddly.”

“Mullett,” said Hamilton Beamish, “you will not, in my presence and while I have my strength, describe any of God’s creatures as cuddly. Where you picked it up, I cannot say, but you have the most abhorrent vocabulary I have ever encountered. … What’s the matter?”

The valet was looking past him with an expression of deep concern.

“Why are you making faces, Mullett?” Hamilton Beamish turned. “Ah, Garroway,” he said, “there you are at last. You should have been here ten minutes ago.”

===Chinese===
“哦不，先生，不胖。就是丰润得恰到好处。照我的说法，叫肉嘟嘟。”

“马利特，”汉密尔顿·比米什说，“只要我人在场、气还在，就不许你用‘肉嘟嘟’来形容上帝造的任何生灵。这词儿你是打哪儿拾来的，我说不好，可你这一套词汇，算是我平生所遇最可憎的。……怎么了？”

那男仆正越过他的肩头望过去，满脸深忧。

“你干吗挤眉弄眼的，马利特？”汉密尔顿·比米什转过身来。“啊，加罗威（Garroway），”他说，“你可算到了。你十分钟前就该来。”

===Original===
A policeman had come out onto the roof.

===Chinese===
一名警察已经走上了屋顶。

### II / 第二节

===Original===
The policeman touched his cap. He was a long, stringy policeman, who flowed out of his uniform at odd spots, as if Nature, setting out to make a constable, had had a good deal of material left over which it had not liked to throw away but hardly seemed able to fit neatly into the general scheme. He had large, knobby wrists of a geranium hue and just that extra four or five inches of neck which disqualify a man for high honours in a beauty competition. His eyes were mild and blue, and from certain angles he seemed all Adam’s apple.

“I must apologise for being late, Mr. Beamish,” he said. “I was detained at the station-house.” He looked at Mullett uncertainly. “I think I have met this gentleman before?”

===Chinese===
警察抬手碰了碰帽檐。这是一位精瘦细长的警察，身上好几处地方把制服撑得鼓鼓囊囊，仿佛造化动工造一名警察的时候，剩下了好大一把材料，扔了舍不得，塞进整体设计又实在没有合适的地方。他生着一双天竺葵红色的大疙瘩手腕，外加那多出来的四五英寸脖子——凭这四五英寸，一个人在选美大赛里就休想问鼎高名次。他的眼睛温和，蓝蓝的，从某些角度看过去，满眼只看得见喉结。

“迟到的事，务请比米什先生恕罪，”他说，“我在分局叫事情绊住了。”他迟疑地打量着马利特。“这位先生，我从前好像见过？”

===Original===
“No, you haven’t,” said Mullett quickly.

“Your face seems very familiar.”

“Never seen me in your life.”

“Come this way, Garroway,” said Hamilton Beamish, interrupting curtly. “We cannot waste time in idle chatter.” He led the officer to the edge of the roof and swept his hand round in a broad gesture. “Now, tell me. What do you see?”

===Chinese===
“没有，你没有见过。”马利特抢着说。

“可你的面孔瞧着熟极了。”

“你这辈子压根儿没见过我。”

“到这边来，加罗威，”汉密尔顿·比米什生硬地打断道。“闲磕牙是浪费光阴。”他把这位警官领到屋顶边沿，手臂划出一个大开大合的手势。“来，告诉我。你看见了什么？”

===Original===
The policeman’s eye sought the depths.

“That’s the Purple Chicken down there,” he said. “One of these days that joint will get pinched.”

“Garroway!”

“Sir?”

===Chinese===
警察的眼睛朝楼下深处望去。

“底下那家，就是紫鸡餐厅，”他说，“早晚有一天，那铺子要叫人端了。”

“加罗威！”

“有，先生？”

===Original===
“For some little time I have been endeavouring to instruct you in the principles of pure English. My efforts seem to have been wasted.”

The policeman blushed.

“I beg your pardon, Mr. Beamish. One keeps slipping into it. It’s the effect of mixing with the boys—with my colleagues—at the station-house. They are very lax in their speech. What I meant was that in the near future there was likely to be a raid conducted on the premises of the Purple Chicken, sir. It has been drawn to our attention that the Purple Chicken, defying the Eighteenth Amendment, still purveys alcoholic liquors.”

===Chinese===
“有些日子了，我一直竭力把纯正英语的法则传授给你。看来我的心血全然白费。”

警察红了脸。

“比米什先生，请您原谅。人总不免顺嘴溜出俚语来。这是在分局同那帮弟兄——同我的同事们——厮混惯了的缘故。他们说话都很不检点。我的意思是，先生，近期之内，很可能对紫鸡餐厅的场所展开一次搜查行动。上级提请我们注意：紫鸡餐厅公然蔑视宪法第十八修正案（Eighteenth Amendment），至今仍在供应酒精饮品。”

===Original===
“Never mind the Purple Chicken. I brought you up here to see what you could do in the way of a word-picture of the view. The first thing a poet needs is to develop his powers of observation. How does it strike you?”

The policeman gazed mildly at the horizon. His eye flitted from the rooftops of the city, spreading away in the distance, to the waters of the Hudson, glittering in the sun. He shifted his Adam’s apple up and down two or three times, as one in deep thought.

“It looks very pretty, sir,” he said at length.

“Pretty?” Hamilton Beamish’s eyes flashed. You would never have thought, to look at him, that the J. in his name stood for James and that there had once been people who had called him Jimmy. “It isn’t pretty at all.”

===Chinese===
“紫鸡餐厅的事不必再提。我带你上来，是要看看你把眼前这片景致作成一幅文字写生的功夫如何。诗人首先要培养的，是观察力。你看着这景色怎么样？”

警察温吞地凝望天际。目光从远处铺展开去的城市屋脊，滑到哈得孙河在阳光下闪闪烁烁的水面上。他上下挪动了两三回喉结，一副苦思冥想的架势。

“瞧着真好看，先生，”他末了说。

“好看？”汉密尔顿·比米什目光一闪。单看外表，你绝不会想到他名字里那个 J 代表的是“詹姆斯”（James），更想不到当年还曾有人管他叫过“吉米”。“半点也不好看。”
===Original===
“No, sir?”

“It’s stark.”

“Stark, sir?”

“Stark and grim. It makes your heart ache. You think of all the sorrow and sordid gloom which those roofs conceal, and your heart bleeds. I may as well tell you, here and now, that if you are going about the place thinking things pretty, you will never make a modern poet. Be poignant, man, be poignant!”

“Yes, sir. I will, indeed, sir.”

===Chinese===
“是吗，先生？”

“是凄怆。”

“凄怆，先生？”

“凄怆，而且阴郁。它叫你心里发疼。你想到那些屋顶底下藏着的种种悲苦与污浊的暗淡，你的心便在流血。我不妨趁此时此地跟你说破：你要是走到哪儿都把东西想得‘好看’，就永远成不了一个现代诗人。要沉痛，伙计，要沉痛！”

“是，先生。我一定沉痛，先生。”

===Original===
“Well, take your notebook and jot down a description of what you see. I must go down to my apartment and attend to one or two things. Look me up tomorrow.”

“Yes, sir. Excuse me, sir, but who is that gentleman over there, sweeping with the broom? His face seemed so very familiar.”

“His name is Mullett. He works for my friend, George Finch. But never mind about Mullett. Stick to your work. Concentrate! Concentrate!”

“Yes, sir. Most certainly, Mr. Beamish.”

===Chinese===
“好，拿出你的笔记本，把眼见的景象草草记下一篇来。我得下楼回自己寓所，去办一两件事。明天来找我。”

“是，先生。恕我多问一句，先生，那边那位提着扫帚扫地的先生是谁？他的面孔实在眼熟得很。”

“他叫马利特，替我的朋友乔治·芬奇做事。不过马利特的事你就别管了。专心做你的功课。聚精会神！聚精会神！”

“是，先生。一定照办，比米什先生。”

===Original===
He looked with doglike devotion at the thinker: then, licking the point of his pencil, bent himself to his task.

Hamilton Beamish turned on his No-Jar rubber heel and passed through the door to the stairs.

===Chinese===
他一脸犬马般的赤诚仰望这位思想家；随后舔了舔铅笔尖，埋头做他的功课去了。

汉密尔顿·比米什穿着无震橡胶鞋跟转过身，穿过那扇门，往楼梯去了。

### III / 第三节

===Original===
Following his departure, silence reigned for some minutes on the roof of the Sheridan. Mullett resumed his sweeping, and Officer Garroway scribbled industriously in his notebook. But after about a quarter of an hour, feeling apparently that he had observed all there was to observe, he put book and pencil away in the recesses of his uniform and, approaching Mullett, subjected him to a mild but penetrating scrutiny.

“I feel convinced, Mr. Mullett,” he said, “that I have seen your face before.”

“And I say you haven’t,” said the valet testily.

“Perhaps you have a brother, Mr. Mullett, who resembles you?”

“Dozens. And even mother couldn’t tell us apart.”

===Chinese===
他走后，谢里丹公寓的屋顶安静了好几分钟。马利特重新挥起扫帚，加罗威警官则在笔记本里埋头奋笔。约莫一刻钟之后，他显然自觉该观察的都已观察殆尽，便把本子和铅笔收进制服的层层深处，踱到马利特跟前，用一种温和却洞穿一切的目光把他细细打量了一遍。

“马利特先生，”他说，“我确信从前见过您的面孔。”

“我说你没见过。”男仆没好气地说。

“马利特先生，也许您有一位兄弟，长得跟您一个模样？”

“几十个呐。连我妈都认不出谁是谁。”

===Original===
The policeman sighed:

“I am an orphan,” he said, “without brothers or sisters.”

“Too bad.”

“Stark,” agreed the policeman. “Very stark and poignant. You don’t think I could have seen a photograph of you anywhere, Mr. Mullett?”

“Haven’t been taken for years.”

“Strange!” said Officer Garroway meditatively. “Somehow—I cannot tell why—I seem to associate your face with a photograph.”

===Chinese===
警察叹了口气：

“我是个孤儿，”他说，“无兄无妹。”

“惨呐。”

“凄怆，”警察深表同感，“非常凄怆，非常沉痛。马利特先生，您说，我有没有可能在什么地方见过您的一张照片？”

“多少年没照过相了。”

“怪了！”加罗威警官沉吟道，“不知怎么——说不出为什么——我总觉得您的面孔跟哪张照片有过牵扯。”

===Original===
“Not your busy day, this, is it?” said Mullett.

“I am off duty at the moment. I seem to see a photograph—several photographs—in some sort of collection. …”

There could be no doubt by now that Mullett had begun to find the consideration difficult. He looked like a man who has a favourite aunt in Poughkeepsie, and is worried about her asthma. He was turning to go, when there came out on to the roof from the door leading to the stairs a young man in a suit of dove-grey.

===Chinese===
“今儿个您不忙，是吧？”马利特说。

“我这会儿歇班。我好像看见一张照片——好几张照片——收在什么册子里。……”

到这时候，毫无疑问，马利特已经开始觉得这道思考题越来越难对付了。他那副模样，活像一个在波基普西（Poughkeepsie）住着一位心爱的姑妈、正为她的哮喘病发愁的人。他正要转身走开，通往楼梯的那扇门里却走出来一个穿鸽灰色西装的年轻人，上了屋顶。

===Original===
“Mullett!” he called.

The other hurried gratefully towards him, leaving the officer staring pensively at his spacious feet.

“Yes, Mr. Finch?”

===Chinese===
“马利特！”他喊道。

那位如蒙大赦，慌忙迎上前去，留下警官对着自己那双开阔的大脚出神凝思。

“来了，芬奇先生？”

===Original===
It is impossible for a historian with a nice sense of values not to recognise the entry of George Finch, following immediately after that of J. Hamilton Beamish, as an anticlimax. Mr. Beamish filled the eye. An aura of authority went before him as the cloud of fire went before the Israelites in the desert. When you met J. Hamilton Beamish, something like a steam-hammer seemed to hit your consciousness and stun it long before he came within speaking-distance. In the case of George Finch nothing of this kind happened.

George looked what he was, a nice young small bachelor, of the type you see bobbing about the place on every side. One glance at him was enough to tell you that he had never written a Booklet and never would write a Booklet. In figure he was slim and slight; as to the face, pleasant and undistinguished. He had brown eyes which in certain circumstances could look like those of a stricken sheep; and his hair was of a light chestnut colour. It was possible to see his hair clearly, for he was not wearing his hat but carrying it in his hand.

He was carrying it reverently, as if he attached a high value to it. And this was strange, for it was not much of a hat. Once it may have been, but now it looked as if it had been both trodden on and kicked about.

===Chinese===
一个价值观精微的史家，绝不可能认不出：乔治·芬奇紧随 J·汉密尔顿·比米什之后登场，实属反高潮。比米什先生能占满人的眼眶。一股威仪之气为他先行开路，一如旷野之中火云行在以色列人前头。你同 J·汉密尔顿·比米什打照面，早在他还离着说话能听见的距离老远，就仿佛有一柄蒸汽锤砸上你的意识，把它砸得晕头转向。到了乔治·芬奇这里，诸如此类的情形一概没有发生。

乔治看上去是什么样，就是什么样：一个体面的年轻小单身汉，满街蹦跶随处可见的那一号人。只消看他一眼，你就足以断定：这人从没写过小册子，将来也绝不会写小册子。他身形修长瘦削；面孔嘛，悦目而不出众。生一双棕色的眼睛，在某些情形下活像挨了打的绵羊的眼睛；头发是浅栗色的。他的头发能叫人看得清清楚楚，因为他帽子没戴在头上，而是托在手里。

他托得毕恭毕敬，仿佛那顶帽子价值连城。这就怪了，因为那实在算不上一顶什么帽子。兴许从前是，可如今它看上去，又像叫人踩过，又像叫人踢过。

===Original===
“Mullett,” he said, regarding this relic with a dreamy eye, “take this hat and put it away.”

“Throw it away, sir?”

“Good heavens, no! Put it away—very carefully. Have you any tissue-paper?”

“Yes, sir.”

“Then wrap it up very carefully in tissue-paper and leave it on the table in my sitting-room.”

“Very good, sir.”

===Chinese===
“马利特，”他眼神缥缈地端详着这件残骸，说，“把这顶帽子收起来，好好收藏。”

“扔了它，先生？”

“天老爷，不行！收藏起来——要收得非常仔细。有棉纸吗？”

“有，先生。”

“那就用棉纸仔仔细细把它包起来，搁在我起居室的桌上。”

“遵命，先生。”
===Original===
“Pardon me for interrupting,” said a deprecating voice behind him, “but might I request a moment of your valuable time, Mr. Finch?”

Officer Garroway had left his fixed point, and was standing in an attitude that seemed to suggest embarrassment. His mild eyes wore a somewhat timid expression.

“Forgive me if I intrude,” said officer Garroway.

“Not at all,” said George.

“I am a policeman, sir.”

“So I see.”

===Chinese===
“恕我打扰，”他身后响起一个惶恐告罪的声音，“芬奇先生，可否恳请您赏我一分钟宝贵的时间？”

加罗威警官已经离开了他站定的位置，立在那里，浑身的姿势仿佛都在诉说窘迫。他温和的眼睛里带着几分怯意。

“倘若冒犯，务请宽恕。”加罗威警官说。

“没有的事，”乔治说。

“我是个警察，先生。”

“我看得出来。”

===Original===
“And,” said Officer Garroway sadly, “I have a rather disagreeable duty to perform, I fear. I would avoid it, if I could reconcile the act with my conscience, but duty is duty. One of the drawbacks to the policeman’s life, Mr. Finch, is that it is not easy for him always to do the gentlemanly thing.”

“No doubt,” said George.

Mullett swallowed apprehensively. The hunted look had come back to his face. Officer Garroway eyed him with a gentle solicitude.

===Chinese===
“而且，”加罗威警官愁苦地说，“只怕我有一桩相当不讨人喜欢的差事要办。这差事我本想避开的，只要良心那道关过得去；可职责就是职责。当警察这一行当的诸多苦处之一，芬奇先生，就是没法子时时处处都把事情办得绅士派。”

“想必如此，”乔治说。

马利特惶恐地咽了口唾沫。那副被追猎的神色又回到了他脸上。加罗威警官满怀温柔的关切望着他。

===Original===
“I would like to preface my remarks,” he proceeded, “by saying that I have no personal animus against Mr. Mullett. I have seen nothing in my brief acquaintance with Mr. Mullett that leads me to suppose that he is not a pleasant companion and zealous in the performance of his work. Nevertheless, I think it right that you should know that he is an ex-convict.”

“An ex-convict!”

“Reformed,” said Mullett hastily.

“As to that, I cannot say,” said Officer Garroway. “I can but speak of what I know. Very possibly, as he asserts, Mr. Mullett is a reformed character. But this does not alter the fact that he has done his bit of time: and in pursuance of my duty I can scarcely refrain from mentioning this to the gentleman who is his present employer. The moment I was introduced to him, I detected something oddly familiar about Mr. Mullett’s face, and I have just recollected that I recently saw a photograph of him in the Rogues Gallery at Headquarters. You are possibly aware, sir, that convicted criminals are ‘mugged’—that is to say, photographed in various positions—at the commencement of their term of incarceration. This was done to Mr. Mullett some eighteen months ago when he was sentenced to a year’s imprisonment for an inside burglary job. May I ask how Mr. Mullett came to be in your employment?”

===Chinese===
“在开口之前，”他接着说，“我想先声明：我对马利特先生绝无半点私怨。同马利特先生短暂相识以来，我没看出任何迹象，能使我疑心他不是个令人愉快的伙伴，不是个做事尽心卖力的人。尽管如此，我认为应当让您知道：他是一名刑满释放犯。”

“刑满释放犯！”

“已经改好了。”马利特忙不迭地说。

“这一点，我无从置喙，”加罗威警官说，“我只能就我所知而言。正如他自己所讲，马利特先生极可能已经痛改前非。可这改变不了一个事实：他吃过一段牢饭；而我本着职责，几乎不能不向雇用他的先生提及此事。方才一经引见，我就察觉马利特先生的脸有种说不出的眼熟，刚刚想起来：前不久，我在总局的罪犯照片馆（Rogues Gallery）里见过他的一张照片。先生，您或许晓得：定罪的罪犯在开始服刑的时候，都要‘留档存照’——也就是说，摆出各种姿势照相。马利特先生拍这份档案照，是在大约十八个月以前，当时他因一桩内盗案被判了一年监禁。冒昧问一句，马利特先生是怎么到您手下做事的？”

===Original===
“He was sent to me by Mr. Beamish. Mr. Hamilton Beamish.”

“In that case, sir, I have nothing further to say,” said the policeman, bowing at the honoured name. “No doubt Mr. Beamish had excellent reasons for recommending Mr. Mullett. And, of course, as Mr. Mullett has long since expiated his offence, I need scarcely say that we of the Force have nothing against him. I merely considered it my duty to inform you of his previous activities in case you should have any prejudice against employing a man of his antecedents. I must now leave you, as my duties compel me to return to the station-house. Good afternoon, Mr. Finch.”

“Good afternoon.”

“Good day, Mr. Mullett. Pleased to have met you. You did not by any chance run into a young fellow named Joe the Gorilla while you were in residence at Sing-Sing? No? I’m sorry. He came from my home town. I should have liked news of Joe.”

Officer Garroway’s departure was followed by a lengthy silence. George Finch shuffled his feet awkwardly. He was an amiable young man, and disliked unpleasant scenes. He looked at Mullett. Mullett looked at the sky.

===Chinese===
“是比米什先生推荐给我的。汉密尔顿·比米什先生。”

“既然如此，先生，我没有更多的话要讲了，”警察说着，朝那个备受尊崇的名字躬了躬身。“比米什先生推荐马利特先生，想必自有十分高明的道理。何况马利特先生早已为他的罪过付清了代价，那么自然——这一点几乎不必说——我们警务同人对他并无芥蒂。我不过是觉得，把他的过往禀告于您是我的职责，免得您对雇用有这般前科的人若心存芥蒂，也好心里有数。眼下我必须告辞了，公务在身，我得回分局去。再见，芬奇先生。”

“再见。”

“扰了，马利特先生。幸会幸会。您在辛辛监狱（Sing Sing）服刑那阵子，没碰巧遇上一个外号‘大猩猩’乔（Joe the Gorilla）的小伙子吗？没有？可惜。他是跟我同乡。我还想打听打听乔的近况哩。”

加罗威警官去后，跟着是好长一阵沉默。乔治·芬奇拖着脚局促地蹭来蹭去。他是个和气的年轻人，不爱瞧难堪的场面。他看马利特。马利特看天。

===Original===
“Er—Mullett,” said George.

“Sir?”

“This is rather unfortunate.”

“Most unpleasant for all concerned, sir.”

===Chinese===
“呃——马利特，”乔治开了口。

“哎，先生。”

“这事可真不巧。”

“于有关各方都极其不愉快，先生。”

===Original===
“I think Mr. Beamish might have told me.”

“No doubt he considered it unnecessary, sir. Being aware that I had reformed.”

“Yes, but even so. … Er—Mullett.”

“Sir?”

“The officer spoke of an inside burglary job. What was your exact—er—line?”

“I used to get a place as a valet, sir, and wait till I saw my chance, and then skin out with everything I could lay my hands on.”

===Chinese===
“我看比米什先生本该跟我打声招呼。”

“他无疑是觉得没有必要，先生。他知道我已经改好了。”

“话虽如此，可是……呃——马利特。”

“哎，先生。”

“警官说起一桩内盗案。你当年干的确切是——呃——哪一路？”

“我早先的路数，先生，是谋一份贴身男仆的差事，静静守着，等机会一到，就把凡是够得着的统统卷上，一走了之。”

===Original===
“You did, did you?”

“Yes, sir.”

“Well, I do think Mr. Beamish might have dropped me a quiet hint. Good heavens! I may have been putting temptation in your way for weeks.”

“You have, sir—very serious temptation. But I welcome temptation, Mr. Finch. Every time I’m left alone with your pearl studs, I have a bout with the Tempter. ‘Why don’t you take them, Mullett?’ he says to me, ‘Why don’t you take them?’ It’s splendid moral exercise, sir.”

===Chinese===
“原来如此，是吗？”

“是，先生。”

“唉，我真觉得比米什先生该私下里给我递个话儿。我的老天爷！没准这几个礼拜，我一直在往你跟前送诱惑呢。”

“您送了，先生——还是了不得的大诱惑。可我欢迎诱惑，芬奇先生。每回单单留下我跟您的珍珠礼服扣作伴，我都要同那诱惑魔较量一场。‘怎么不拿走呢，马利特？’他冲我念叨，‘怎么不拿走呢？’这真是绝好的道德操练，先生。”
===Original===
“I suppose so.”

“Yes, sir, it’s awful what that Tempter will suggest to me. Sometimes, when you’re lying asleep, he says ‘Slip a sponge of Chloroform under his nose, Mullett, and clear out with the swag!’ Just imagine it, sir.”

“I am imagining it.”

“But I win every time, sir. I’ve not lost one fight with that old Tempter since I’ve been in your employment, Mr. Finch.”

===Chinese===
“但愿如此吧。”

“可不是，先生，那诱惑魔给我出的主意，说出来吓死人。有时候，您正睡着，他就说：‘把一团浸了氯仿的海绵凑到他鼻子底下，马利特，带上赃物开溜！’您想想看吧，先生。”

“我在想。”

“可我回回都得胜，先生。打从到您府上做事起，我跟那老诱惑魔交手就没输过一回，芬奇先生。”

===Original===
“All the same, I don’t believe you’re going to remain in my employment, Mullett.”

Mullett inclined his head resignedly.

“I was afraid of this, sir. The moment that flat-footed cop came on to this roof, I had a presentiment that there was going to be trouble. But I should appreciate it very much if you could see your way to reconsider, sir. I can assure you that I have completely reformed.”

“Religion?”

“No, sir. Love.”

The word seemed to touch some hidden chord in George Finch. The stern, set look vanished from his face. He gazed at his companion almost meltingly.

===Chinese===
“话虽这么说，我看你在我这儿是做不长久了，马利特。”

马利特听天由命地垂下了头。

“我就怕这个，先生。那个大脚板警察一踏上这片屋顶，我就有了预感，要出岔子。可您要是能设法回心转意，我会感激不尽的，先生。我可以向您担保，我已经彻头彻尾改好了。”

“靠宗教？”

“不是，先生。靠爱情。”

这个词仿佛拨动了乔治·芬奇心里某根隐秘的弦。他脸上那副铁板的神气登时不见了。他望着自己的仆人，眼神几乎要化了。

===Original===
“Mullett! Do you love?”

“I do, indeed, sir. Fanny’s her name, sir. Fanny Welch. She’s a pickpocket.”

“A pickpocket!”

“Yes, sir. And one of the smartest girls in the business. She could take your watch out of your waistcoat, and you’d be prepared to swear she hadn’t been within a yard of you. It’s almost an art, sir. But she’s promised to go straight, if I will, and now I’m saving up to buy the furniture. So I do hope, sir, that you will reconsider. It would set me back if I fell out of a place just now.”

===Chinese===
“马利特！你在恋爱？”

“千真万确，先生。她叫芬妮，先生。芬妮·韦尔奇（Fanny Welch）。她是个扒手。”

“扒手！”

“是，先生。还是这一行里顶伶俐的姑娘之一。她能从您马甲里头把表摸走，事后您还准备赌咒发誓，说她压根没进过您一码之内。这差不多是一门艺术了，先生。可她答应了要改邪归正，只要我也肯改；如今我正攒钱置办家具呢。所以我千盼万盼，先生，盼您收回成命。眼下这个节骨眼上要是丢了差事，我这一番盘算可就全泡汤了。”

===Original===
George wrinkled his forehead.

“I oughtn’t to.”

“But you will, sir?”

“It’s weak of me.”

“Not it, sir. Christian, I call it.”

George pondered.

===Chinese===
乔治皱起额头。

“我本不该留你。”

“可您会留下的，先生？”

“这是我心软。”

“这哪是心软，先生。照我看，这叫基督徒的心肠。”

乔治沉吟起来。

===Original===
“How long have you been with me, Mullett?”

“Just on a month, sir.”

“And my pearl studs are still there?”

“Still in the drawer, sir.”

“All right, Mullett. You can stay.”

“Thank you very much, indeed, sir.”

===Chinese===
“你跟了我多久了，马利特？”

“快满一个月了，先生。”

“我的珍珠礼服扣还原封没动？”

“还在抽屉里，先生。”

“好吧，马利特。你可以留下。”

“实在万分感谢，先生。”

===Original===
There was a silence. The setting sun flung a carpet of gold across the roof. It was the hour at which men become confidential.

“Love is very wonderful, Mullett!” said George Finch.

“Makes the world go round, I often say, sir.”

===Chinese===
一阵沉默。落日在屋顶上铺开一方金毯。这正是男人们爱说体己话的时辰。

“爱情真是奇妙啊，马利特！”乔治·芬奇说。

“爱情叫世界转动，我常这么说，先生。”

===Original===
“Mullett.”

“Sir?”

“Shall I tell you something?”

“If you please, sir.”

“Mullett,” said George Finch, “I, too, love.”

“You surprise me, sir.”

===Chinese===
“马利特。”

“哎，先生。”

“有件事说给你听听，好不好？”

“您请讲，先生。”

“马利特，”乔治·芬奇说，“我，也在恋爱。”

“您这话叫我吃了一惊，先生。”
===Original===
“You may have noticed that I have been fussy about my clothing of late, Mullett?”

“Oh, no, sir.”

“Well, I have been, and that was the reason. She lives on East Seventy-Ninth Street, Mullett. I saw her first lunching at the Plaza with a woman who looked like Catherine of Russia. Her mother, no doubt.”

“Very possibly, sir.”

===Chinese===
“或许您留意到了，近来我对衣着格外讲究，马利特？”

“哦，没有，先生。”

“可我就是讲究，根子就在这儿。她住东七十九街，马利特。我头一回见她，是在广场饭店（the Plaza）吃午饭，同桌有位妇人，相貌活像俄国女皇叶卡捷琳娜。多半是她母亲。”

“多半错不了，先生。”

===Original===
“I followed her home. I don’t know why I’m telling you this, Mullett.”

“No, sir.”

“Since then I have haunted the sidewalk outside her house. Do you know East Seventy-Ninth Street?”

“Never been there, sir.”

===Chinese===
“我跟在她后头，一直跟到她家门口。连我自己也不明白，干吗要把这些讲给你听，马利特。”

“是，先生。”

“打那以后，我就像鬼魂似的缠上了她家门口的人行道。你认得东七十九街吗？”

“从来没去过，先生。”

===Original===
“Well, fortunately it is not a very frequented thoroughfare, or I should have been arrested for loitering. Until today I have never spoken to her, Mullett.”

“But you did today, sir?”

“Yes. Or, rather, she spoke to me. She has a voice like the fluting of young birds in the springtime, Mullett.”

“Very agreeable, no doubt, sir.”

===Chinese===
“嗯，幸好那条街人迹不稠，不然我早让当局按游荡罪抓起来了。到今天为止，我连一句话都没同她讲过，马利特。”

“可您今天讲了，先生？”

“讲了。或者不如说，是她跟我讲的。她的嗓音，恰似春日雏鸟的啁啾婉转，马利特。”

“想必非常悦耳，先生。”

===Original===
“Heavenly would express it better. It happened like this, Mullett. I was outside the house, when she came along leading a Scotch terrier on a leash. At that moment a gust of wind blew my hat off and it was bowling past her, when she stopped it. She trod on it, Mullett.”

“Indeed, sir?”

“Yes, this hat which you see in my hand, has been trodden on by Her. This very hat.”

“And then, sir?”

===Chinese===
“说‘天上仙乐’才更贴切。事情是这样的，马利特。我正在宅子外头站着，她牵着一根皮带，带一条苏格兰梗犬（Scotch terrier）走过来了。就在这时，一阵狂风刮跑了我的帽子，帽子骨碌碌打她身旁滚过去，叫她给拦住了。她一脚踩住了它，马利特。”

“哦，是嘛，先生？”

“是的，你瞧见的、我手里这顶帽子，叫‘她’亲手踩过。就是这顶帽子。”

“后来呢，先生？”

===Original===
“In the excitement of the moment she dropped the leash, and the Scotch terrier ran off round the corner in the direction of Brooklyn. I went in pursuit, and succeeded in capturing it in Lexington Avenue. My hat dropped off again and was run over by a taxicab. But I retained my hold of the leash, and eventually restored the dog to its mistress. She said—and I want you to notice this very carefully, Mullett—she said ‘Oh, thank you so much!’”

“Did she, indeed, sir?”

“She did. Not merely ‘Thank you!’ or ‘Oh, thank you!’ but ‘Oh, thank you so much!’ ” George Finch fixed a penetrating stare on his employee. “I think that is significant, Mullett.”

“Extremely, sir.”

===Chinese===
“那一刻她心慌意乱，皮带脱了手，那条苏格兰梗犬绕过街角，朝布鲁克林的方向撒腿跑了。我拔腿就追，终于在列克星敦大道（Lexington Avenue）上把它擒获。我的帽子又掉了，叫一辆出租汽车碾了过去。可我死死攥住那根皮带，末了把狗完璧归赵，交还到女主人手上。她说——马利特，这一句我要你分外仔细地记住——她说：‘哦，太谢谢您啦！’”

“她真这么说了，先生？”

“真说了。不是干巴巴一句‘谢谢！’，也不是‘哦，谢谢！’，而是‘哦，太谢谢您啦！’”乔治·芬奇目光如锥，盯住自己的雇员。“依我看，这就大有文章了，马利特。”

“大有文章，先生。”

===Original===
“If she had wished to end the acquaintance then and there, would she have spoken so warmly?”

“Impossible, sir.”

“And I’ve not told you all. Having said ‘Oh, thank you so much!’ she added: ‘He *is* a naughty dog, isn’t he?’ You get the extraordinary subtlety of that, Mullett? The words ‘He is a naughty dog’ would have been a mere statement. By adding ‘Isn’t he?’ she invited my opinion. She gave me to understand that she would welcome discussion on the subject. Do you know what I am going to do, directly I have dressed, Mullett?”

===Chinese===
“她要是存心当时当地就断了我这份相识，话能说得这么热络吗？”

“断不会，先生。”

“还有呢，我没跟你讲完。说完‘哦，太谢谢您啦！’，她跟着又添了一句：‘它真是个淘气包，是不是呀？’这一句里头的千般微妙，你咂摸出来没有，马利特？光说‘它真是个淘气包’，不过是一句陈述。添上‘是不是呀’，她就是在邀请我发表高见。她这是让我明白：这个话题，她乐意奉陪，细细讨论。你可知道，我一换好衣裳，打算去做什么，马利特？”

===Original===
“Dine, sir?”

“Dine!” George shuddered. “No! There are moments when the thought of food is an outrage to everything that raises Man above the level of the beasts. As soon as I have dressed—and I shall dress very carefully—I am going to return to East Seventy-Ninth Street and I am going to ring the doorbell and I am going to go straight in and inquire after the dog. Hope it is none the worse for its adventure and so on. After all, it is only the civil thing. I mean these Scotch terriers … delicate, highly-strung animals. … Never can tell what effect unusual excitement may have on them. Yes, Mullett, that is what I propose to do. Brush my dress clothes as you have never brushed them before.”

“Very good, sir.”

===Chinese===
“用晚饭，先生？”

“用晚饭！”乔治浑身一抖。“不！人生总有那么些时刻，一想到吃食，就是对使人类高于禽兽的一切事物的亵渎。我一换好衣裳——而且我要换得格外精心——就重回东七十九街，按响门铃，径直进门，问候那条狗。问一问它经了这场惊险可还安好，诸如此类。说到底，这也不过是尽一点人情礼数。我是说，这些苏格兰梗……娇贵、神经质的动物。……反常的惊吓会对它们起什么作用，谁说得准呢。对，马利特，我就打算这么办。把我那身礼服刷一刷，拿出你此生未见的功夫刷。”

“遵命，先生。”

===Original===
“Put me out a selection of ties. Say, a dozen.”

“Yes, sir.”

“And—did the bootlegger call this morning?”

“Yes, sir.”

“Then mix me a very strong whisky-and-soda, Mullett,” said George Finch. “Whatever happens, I must be at my best tonight.”

===Chinese===
“给我挑几条领带摆出来。来它一打。”

“是，先生。”

“还有——今儿早上私酒贩子来过了吗？”

“来过了，先生。”

“那就给我调一杯烈极了的威士忌苏打，马利特，”乔治·芬奇说。“不管今晚出什么事，我都必须拿出最佳状态。”

### IV / 第四节

===Original===
To George, sunk in a golden reverie, there entered some few minutes later, jarring him back to life, a pair of three-pound dumbbells, which shot abruptly out of the unknown and came trundling across the roof at him with a repulsive, clumping sound that would have disconcerted Romeo. They were followed by J. Hamilton Beamish on all fours. Hamilton Beamish, who believed in the healthy body as well as the sound mind, always did half an hour’s open-air work with the bells of an evening: and, not for the first time, he had tripped over the top stair.

He recovered his balance, his dumbbells and his spectacles in three laboursaving movements: and with the aid of the last-named was enabled to perceive George.

“Oh, there you are!” said Hamilton Beamish.

“Yes,” said George, “and. …”

===Chinese===
乔治正沉浸在金色的冥想里；几分钟之后，闯进来搅扰他、把他震回尘寰的，是一对三磅哑铃——它们冷不防从未知世界蹿出，咕噜噜滚过屋顶，朝他直冲过来，发出一种叫罗密欧也要心惊肉跳的、讨厌的哐啷声。哑铃后头，跟着四肢着地的 J·汉密尔顿·比米什。汉密尔顿·比米什既信健全的心智，也信健壮的体魄，每天傍晚总要到户外跟哑铃厮混半个钟头；而这已不是头一遭——他又在最高一级台阶上绊了跟头。

他以三个省力的动作恢复了平衡、寻回了哑铃、捡回了眼镜；借了末一项的助力，他这才看见了乔治。

“啊，你在这儿！”汉密尔顿·比米什说。

“是啊，”乔治说，“可是……”

===Original===
“What’s all this I hear from Mullett?” asked Hamilton Beamish.

“What,” inquired George simultaneously, “is all this I hear from Mullett?”

“Mullett says you’re fooling about after some girl uptown.”

“Mullett says you knew he was an ex-convict when you recommended him to me.”

Hamilton Beamish decided to dispose of this triviality before going on to more serious business.

===Chinese===
“马利特跟我讲的事，都是怎么回事？”汉密尔顿·比米什问。

“马利特跟我讲的事，”乔治不约而同地问，“都是怎么回事？”

“马利特说，你在上城追什么小姐，四处瞎转悠。”

“马利特说，你把他推荐给我的时候，就知道他是个刑满释放犯。”

汉密尔顿·比米什决定先把这个小节打发掉，再谈更要紧的正事。

===Original===
“Certainly,” he said. “Didn’t you read my series in the *Yale Review* on the ‘Problem of the Reformed Criminal’? I point out very clearly that there is nobody with such a strong bias towards honesty as the man who has just come out of prison. It stands to reason. If you had been laid up for a year in hospital as the result of jumping off this roof, what would be the one outdoor sport in which, on emerging, you would be most reluctant to indulge? Jumping off roofs, undoubtedly.”

George continued to frown in a dissatisfied way.

“That’s all very well, but a fellow doesn’t want ex-convicts hanging about the home.”

===Chinese===
“当然，”他说。“我在《耶鲁评论》（Yale Review）上连载的那组《改过自新的罪犯问题》，你没读过吗？我阐述得再清楚不过：世上再没有谁比刚出监狱的人更偏向诚实了。道理明摆着。假如你从这屋顶上跳下去，在医院足足躺上一年，那么痊愈出门之后，你最不肯沾手的户外运动是什么？当然是跳屋顶，无疑。”

乔治依旧不满意地皱着眉头。

“道理是不错，可谁家也不乐意家里晃着个刑满释放犯。”

===Original===
“Nonsense! You must rid yourself of this old-fashioned prejudice against men who have been in Sing-Sing. Try to look on the place as a sort of University which fits its graduates for the problems of the world without. Morally speaking, such men are the student body. You have no fault to find with Mullett, have you?”

“No, I can’t say I have.”

“Does his work well?”

“Yes.”

“Not stole anything from you?”

“No.”

===Chinese===
“谬论！你非得撇掉这种老掉牙的偏见，别再歧视进过辛辛监狱的人。你不妨把那地方看成一所能干的大学，专为它的毕业生历练本领，好去应付外部世界的种种问题。从道德上讲，这些人便是那大学的学生团体。马利特有什么可挑剔的，你说？”

“没有，说不上来。”

“活儿干得好吗？”

“好。”

“没偷过你的东西？”

“没有。”

===Original===
“Then why worry? Dismiss the man from your mind. And now let me hear all about this girl of yours.”

“How do you know anything about it?”

“Mullett told me.”

“How did he know?”

“He followed you a couple of afternoons and saw all.”

===Chinese===
“那你还愁什么？把这个人从脑子里开除掉。现在，说说你那位小姐，来龙去脉从头讲给我听。”

“这事儿你怎么会知道？”

“马利特告诉我的。”

“他又怎么知道的？”

“他有几个下午一路跟着你，看了个满眼。”

===Original===
George turned pink.

“I’ll go straight in and fire that man. The snake!”

“You will do nothing of the kind. He acted as he did from pure zeal and faithfulness. He saw you go out, muttering to yourself. …”

“Did I mutter?” said George, startled.

“Certainly you muttered. You muttered, and you were exceedingly strange in your manner. So naturally Mullett, good zealous fellow, followed you to see that you came to no harm. He reports that you spend a large part of your leisure goggling at some girl in Seventy-Ninth Street, East.”

===Chinese===
乔治的脸腾地红了。

“我这就进去，把那家伙辞了。这条毒蛇！”

“你可别这么办。他那样做，纯粹出于一片热忱忠心。他看见你出门，嘴里念念有词。……”

“我念念有词了？”乔治吃了一惊。

“当然念念有词。你一边念叨，一边举止异样得吓人。马利特自然——他是个又好又热心的家伙——一路跟上，好保你平安无事。据他汇报，你闲暇时光一大部分，都用来在东七十九街直眉瞪眼地瞅某位小姐。”

===Original===
George’s pink face turned a shade pinker. A sullen look came into it.

“Well, what about it?”

“That’s what I want to know—what about it?”

“Why shouldn’t I goggle?”

“Why should you?”

“Because,” said George Finch, looking like a stuffed frog, “I love her.”

===Chinese===
乔治红透的脸又深了一层红。一层愠色浮了上来。

“哼，瞅又怎么样？”

“这正是我要问的——瞅究竟怎么样？”

“我干吗瞅不得？”

“你凭什么要瞅？”

“因为，”乔治·芬奇说道，一副填制青蛙标本的神情，“我爱她。”
===Original===
“Nonsense!”

“It isn’t nonsense.”

“Have you ever read my booklet on ‘The Marriage Sane’?”

“No, I haven’t.”

“I show there that love is a reasoned emotion that springs from mutual knowledge, increasing over an extended period of time, and a community of tastes. How can you love a girl when you have never spoken to her and don’t even know her name?”

===Chinese===
“胡闹！”

“不是胡闹。”

“我那本《理智婚姻》的小册子，你读过没有？”

“没有，没读过。”

“我在那里面论证：爱情是一种有理有据的情感，萌发于双方的了解——了解须经长期的积累——与性情的投合。你同一位小姐连话都没讲过，连她的名字都不知道，你怎么爱得起来？”

===Original===
“I do know her name.”

“How?”

“I looked through the telephone directory till I found out who lived at Number 16, East Seventy-Ninth Street. It took me about a week, because. …”

“Sixteen East Seventy-Ninth Street? You don’t mean that this girl you’ve been staring at is little Molly Waddington?”

George started.

===Chinese===
“我知道她的名字。”

“怎么知道的？”

“我把电话号码簿从头翻到底，才查明了东七十九街 16 号住的是谁。花了差不多一个礼拜，因为……”

“东七十九街 16 号？你直眉瞪眼瞅的那位小姐，该不会就是小小的莫莉·沃丁顿（Molly Waddington）吧？”

乔治吓了一跳。

===Original===
“Waddington is the name, certainly. That’s why I was such an infernal time getting to it in the book. Waddington, Sigsbee H.” George choked emotionally, and gazed at his friend with awed eyes. “Hamilton! Hammy, old man! You—you don’t mean to say you actually know her? Not positively know her?”

“Of course I know her. Know her intimately. Many’s the time I’ve seen her in her bathtub.”

George quivered from head to foot.

“It’s a lie! A foul and contemptible. …”

“When she was a child.”

“Oh, when she was a child?” George became calmer. “Do you mean to say you’ve known her since she was a child? Why, then you must be in love with her yourself.”

===Chinese===
“姓沃丁顿，一点不错。我就是这么着，在簿子里翻了老半天才翻到的。沃丁顿，西格斯比·H。”乔治激动得喉咙发哽，满怀敬畏地望着他的朋友。“汉密尔顿！汉米，老伙计！你——你该不会是说，你真的认识她？不是实打实地认识她？”

“我当然认识她。熟得不能再熟。她泡在浴缸里的样子，我见过不知多少回。”

乔治从头到脚抖了个遍。

“你撒谎！卑鄙无耻的……”

“那时候她还是个小孩子。”

“哦，她小时候啊？”乔治平静下来。“你是说，打她小时候起你就认识她？那么你自己准是爱上她了。”

===Original===
“Nothing of the kind.”

“You stand there and tell me,” said George incredulously, “that you have known this wonderful girl for many years and are not in love with her?”

“I do.”

George regarded his friend with a gentle pity. He could only explain this extraordinary statement by supposing that there was some sort of a kink in Hamilton Beamish. Sad, for in so many ways he was such a fine fellow.

===Chinese===
“绝无此事。”

“你就站在那儿告诉我，”乔治满脸不信地说，“你认识了这位了不得的姑娘这么多年，居然没有爱上她？”

“就是没有。”

乔治怀着温柔的怜悯望着他的朋友。这句奇谈怪论，他只有用一种办法解释：汉密尔顿·比米什身上，某处拧了劲儿。可悲，因为无论从哪一方面看，他都算得一个难得的好人。

===Original===
“The sight of her has never made you feel that, to win one smile, you could scale the skies and pluck out the stars and lay them at her feet?”

“Certainly not. Indeed, when you consider that the nearest star is several million. …”

“All right,” said George. “All right. Let it go. And now,” he went on simply, “tell me all about her and her people and her house and her dog and what she was like as a child and when she first bobbed her hair and who is her favourite poet and where she went to school and what she likes for breakfast. …”

===Chinese===
“你就从来没有一见到她就觉得：为了博她嫣然一笑，你可以攀上九霄，摘下星辰，铺在她的脚下？”

“从来没有。何况你要想到，离我们最近的那颗星也有好几百万……”

“行了，”乔治说。“行了。这笔账不算了。现在，”他朴实无华地接着说，“把她的事统统讲给我听——她一家的人，她的宅子，她的狗，她小时候什么样，她头一回剪短发是什么时候，她最喜爱的诗人是谁，她在哪儿上的学，早饭她爱吃些什么。……”
===Original===
Hamilton Beamish reflected.

“Well, I first knew Molly when her mother was alive.”

“Her mother is alive. I’ve seen her. A woman who looks like Catherine of Russia.”

“That’s her stepmother. Sigsbee H. married again a couple of years ago.”

“Tell me about Sigsbee H.”

Hamilton Beamish twirled a dumbbell thoughtfully.

===Chinese===
汉密尔顿·比米什想了想。

“是这样，我认识莫莉的时候，她母亲还在世。”

“她母亲好端端活着呢。我见过。一位相貌活像俄国女皇叶卡捷琳娜的妇人。”

“那是她的继母。西格斯比·H 是两三年前续的弦。”

“给我讲讲西格斯比·H。”

汉密尔顿·比米什捻着一只哑铃，若有所思。

===Original===
“Sigsbee H. Waddington,” he said, “is one of those men who must, I think, during the formative years of their boyhood have been kicked on the head by a mule. It has been well said of Sigsbee H. Waddington that, if men were dominoes, he would be the double-blank. One of the numerous things about him that rule him out of serious consideration by intelligent persons is the fact that he is a synthetic Westerner.”

“A synthetic Westerner?”

“It is a little known, but growing, subspecies akin to the synthetic Southerner—with which curious type you are doubtless familiar.”

“I don’t think I am.”

“Nonsense. Have you never been in a restaurant where the orchestra played Dixie?”

“Of course.”

===Chinese===
“西格斯比·H·沃丁顿（Sigsbee H. Waddington），”他说，“属于这样一类人：依我看，他们在性格成形的学生时代，准是叫骡子踢过脑袋。有人说得好：倘若人皆骨牌，西格斯比·H·沃丁顿便是那张双白板。他有许许多多令人无法认真看待的毛病，其中之一，便是他是个合成西部人。”

“合成西部人？”

“这是个鲜为人知、却日渐繁盛的亚种，与合成南方人（synthetic Southerner）是近亲——那类古怪人种，你一定不会陌生。”

“我可不觉得我熟。”

“岂有此理。乐队奏《迪克西》（Dixie，美国南方名曲）的馆子，你从来没进过？”

“当然进过。”

===Original===
“Well, then, on such occasions you will have noted that the man who gives a rebel yell and springs on his chair and waves a napkin with flashing eyes is always a suit-and-cloak salesman named Rosenthal or Bechstein who was born in Passaic, New Jersey, and has never been farther South than Far Rockaway. That is the synthetic Southerner.”

“I see.”

“Sigsbee H. Waddington is a synthetic Westerner. His whole life, with the exception of one summer vacation when he went to Maine, has been spent in New York State: and yet, to listen to him, you would think he was an exiled cowboy. I fancy it must be the effect of seeing too many Westerns in the movies. Sigsbee Waddington has been a keen supporter of the motion-pictures from their inception: and was, I believe, one of the first men in this city to hiss the villain. Whether it was Tom Mix who caused the trouble, or whether his weak intellect was gradually sapped by seeing William H. Hart kiss his horse I cannot say: but the fact remains that he now yearns for the great open spaces and, if you want to ingratiate yourself with him, all you have to do is to mention that you were born in Idaho—a fact which I hope that, as a rule, you carefully conceal.”

===Chinese===
“那么，那种场合你准留意到过：吼出南军呐喊（美国南北战争时南军的冲锋吼叫）、跳上椅子、挥舞餐巾、两眼放光的那个男人，永远是个叫罗森塔尔（Rosenthal）或者贝希斯坦（Bechstein）的西服大衣推销员，生在新泽西州帕塞伊克（Passaic, New Jersey），平生到过的最南的地方是法洛克威（Far Rockaway）。这就是合成南方人。”

“懂了。”

“西格斯比·H·沃丁顿是个合成西部人。他这辈子，除去去缅因州过的那一个暑假，全在纽约州度过；可你要是听他讲话，准以为他是个被放逐的牛仔。我想这准是电影里的西部片看多了的缘故。自电影问世起，西格斯比·沃丁顿就是它热心的拥趸；而且我相信，本市头一拨朝银幕上的坏蛋喝倒彩的人里，就有他一个。是汤姆·米克斯（Tom Mix）招的祸，还是威廉·H·哈特（William H. Hart）亲吻自己的马把他那点孱弱的智力慢慢泡软了，我不敢断言；但事实是，他如今满心向往辽阔的原野，你要是想讨他的好，只消提一句你生在爱达荷州就行——不过这一节，我希望你平日里向来是小心藏着掖着的。”

===Original===
“I will,” said George enthusiastically. “I can’t tell you how grateful I am to you, Hamilton, for giving me this information.”

“You needn’t be. It will do you no good whatever. When Sigsbee Waddington married for the second time, he to all intents and purposes sold himself down the river. To call him a cipher in the home would be to give a too glowing picture of his importance. He does what his wife tells him—that and nothing more. She is the one with whom you want to ingratiate yourself.”

“How can this be done?”

“It can’t be done. Mrs. Sigsbee Waddington is not an easy woman to conciliate.”

===Chinese===
“我会藏的，”乔治兴冲冲地说，“汉密尔顿，你给我这条情报，我感激得没法说。”

“不必感激。这条情报对你毫无用处。西格斯比·沃丁顿第二次结婚的时候，就千真万确地把自己整个儿卖掉了。说他在家中是个无足轻重的零，都算是给他描金绣粉、高抬了身价。太太叫他做什么，他就做什么——仅此而已，别无其他。你真正要讨好的，是他那位太太。”

“这该怎么个讨好法？”

“讨不了。西格斯比·沃丁顿夫人，可不是个容易说合的女人。”

===Original===
“A tough baby?” inquired George anxiously.

Hamilton Beamish frowned.

“I dislike the expression. It is the sort of expression Mullett would use: and I know few things more calculated to make a thinking man shudder than Mullett’s vocabulary. Nevertheless, in a certain crude, horrible way it does describe Mrs. Waddington. There is an ancient belief in Tibet that mankind is descended from a demoness named Drasrinmo and a monkey. Both Sigsbee H. and Mrs. Waddington do much to bear out this theory. I am loath to speak ill of a woman, but it is no use trying to conceal the fact that Mrs. Waddington is a bounder and a snob and has a soul like the underside of a flat stone. She worships wealth and importance. She likes only the rich and the titled. As a matter of fact, I happen to know that there is an English lord hanging about the place whom she wants Molly to marry.”

“Over my dead body,” said George.

===Chinese===
“是个硬茬儿？”乔治惴惴地问。

汉密尔顿·比米什皱起眉头。

“我不喜欢这个说法。这是马利特那一路人用的字眼：一个肯动脑筋的人听了马利特的词汇量要打寒噤，世上再没有几件事比这更灵验了。不过话说回来，这个字眼虽然粗鄙、可怕，倒也确实道着了沃丁顿夫人的几分真相。西藏自古有一种信念：人类是一个名叫德拉辛莫（Drasrinmo）的魔女同一只猴子的后裔。西格斯比·H 同沃丁顿夫人这两位，恰是这一学说的有力佐证。背后说女人的坏话，我本不愿意；可沃丁顿夫人是个俗物、一个势利鬼，灵魂活像扁平石头贴地的那一面——这桩事实，捂是捂不住的。她崇拜财富与地位，只看得上有钱的、有头衔的。说实话，我恰好知道：有位英国勋爵常在那宅子里走动，她一心想让莫莉嫁给他。”

“除非踩着我的尸体过去，”乔治说。
===Original===
“That could no doubt be arranged. My poor George,” said Hamilton Beamish, laying a dumbbell affectionately on his friend’s head, “you are taking on too big a contract. You are going out of your class. It is not as if you were one of these dashing, young Lochinvar fellows. You are mild and shy. You are diffident and timid. I class you among Nature’s white mice. It would take a woman like Mrs. Sigsbee Waddington about two and a quarter minutes to knock you for a row of Portuguese ashcans—er, as Mullett would say,” added Hamilton Beamish with a touch of confusion.

“She couldn’t eat me,” said George valiantly.

“I don’t know so much. She is not a vegetarian.”

===Chinese===
“那想必不难安排。我可怜的乔治，”汉密尔顿·比米什说着，满怀慈爱地把一只哑铃搁在朋友的头顶，“你接下的是一桩过于庞大的合同。你要跨出自己的阶层了。你又不是那种天不怕地不怕的洛钦瓦尔（Lochinvar，司各特诗中横刀夺爱的豪侠青年）式的年轻人。你温和，腼腆。你畏缩，胆怯。我把你归在造化的小白鼠那一类里。像西格斯比·沃丁顿夫人那样的女人，只要两分十五秒，就能把你打翻在地，打得你像一排葡萄牙垃圾桶——呃，借马利特的说法，”汉密尔顿·比米什添了一句，语气微觉慌乱。

“她又不能把我吃了，”乔治豪气干云地说。

“那可说不定。她不是吃素的。”

===Original===
“I was thinking,” said George, “that you might take me round and introduce me. …”

“And have your blood on my head? No, no.”

“What do you mean, my blood? You talk as if this woman were a syndicate of gunmen. I’m not afraid of her. To get to know Molly”—George gulped—“I would fight a mad bull.”

Hamilton Beamish was touched. This great man was human.

===Chinese===
“我在想，”乔治说，“你或许可以领我上门，替我引见引见。……”

“回头你有了三长两短，血债记在我账上？不行，不行。”

“什么叫我的血债？听你这话音，这位夫人倒像一伙职业枪手结成的辛迪加了。我才不怕她。为了认识莫莉，”乔治喉咙一哽，“疯公牛我也敢跟它斗。”

汉密尔顿·比米什动容了。这位伟人，原来也有血肉人情。

===Original===
“These are brave words, George. You extort my admiration. I disapprove of the reckless, unconsidered way you are approaching this matter, and I still think you would be well advised to read *The Marriage Sane* and get a proper estimate of Love: but I cannot but like your spirit. If you really wish it, therefore, I will take you round and introduce you to Mrs. Sigsbee H. Waddington. And may the Lord have mercy on your soul.”

“Hamilton! Tonight?”

“Not tonight. I am lecturing to the West Orange Daughters of Minerva tonight on The Modern Drama. Some other time.”

“Then tonight,” said George, blushing faintly. “I think I may as well just stroll round Seventy-Ninth Street way and—er—well, just stroll round.”

===Chinese===
“话很豪壮，乔治。你逼出了我的钦佩。你处理这件事的莽撞轻率，我不敢恭维；我至今认为你还是先读一读《理智婚姻》，对爱情获得一个正确的估价才是正办：可你这股子精神，我又不能不喜欢。既然你当真愿意，我就领你上门，把你引见给西格斯比·H·沃丁顿夫人。愿上帝怜悯你的灵魂。”

“汉密尔顿！今晚就去？”

“今晚不行。今晚我要去西奥兰治，给‘密涅瓦之女会’（Daughters of Minerva）讲学，题目是《现代戏剧》。改天吧。”

“那就今晚，”乔治说，微微红了脸。“我想我干脆到七十九街那一带遛一遛，然后——呃——嗯，就是遛一遛。”

===Original===
“What is the good of that?”

“Well, I can look at the house, can’t I?”

“Young blood!” said Hamilton Beamish indulgently. “Young blood!”

He poised himself firmly on his No-Jars, and swung the dumbbell in a forceful arc.

===Chinese===
“那有什么用处？”

“嗯，我总可以瞧瞧那座房子吧，是不是？”

“血气方刚啊！”汉密尔顿·比米什宽厚地说。“血气方刚啊！”

他在无震橡胶鞋底上把身形稳稳站定，将哑铃抡出一道刚劲的弧线。

### V / 第五节

===Original===
“Mullett,” said George.

“Sir?”

“Have you pressed my dress clothes?”

“Yes, sir.”

“And brushed them?”

“Yes, sir.”

===Chinese===
“马利特，”乔治说。

“哎，先生。”

“我的礼服熨好了吗？”

“熨好了，先生。”

“刷过了吗？”

“刷过了，先生。”

===Original===
“My ties—are they laid out?”

“In a neat row, sir.”

George coughed.

“Mullett!”

“Sir?”

===Chinese===
“我的领带——摆出来了吗？”

“整整齐齐一排，先生。”

乔治咳嗽了一声。

“马利特！”

“哎，先生。”

===Original===
“You recollect the little chat we were having just now?”

“Sir?”

“About the young lady I—er. …”

“Oh, yes, sir.”

===Chinese===
“咱们刚才那番小小的谈话，你还记得吧？”

“您说什么，先生？”

“就是关于那位我——呃……”

“哦，记得，先生。”

===Original===
“I understand you have seen her.”

“Just a glimpse, sir.”

George coughed again.

“Ah—rather attractive, Mullett, didn’t you think?”

“Extremely, sir. Very cuddly.”

===Chinese===
“听说你见过她。”

“只瞥了一眼，先生。”

乔治又咳嗽了一声。

“啊——相当地动人，马利特，你不觉得吗？”

“动人极了，先生。而且肉嘟嘟。”

===Original===
“The exact adjective I would have used myself, Mullett!”

“Indeed, sir?”

“Cuddly! A beautiful word.”

“I think so, sir.”

===Chinese===
“这正是我自己要用的那个形容词，马利特！”

“哦，是嘛，先生？”

“肉嘟嘟！多美的一个词儿。”

“我也这么想，先生。”

===Original===
George coughed for the third time.

“A lozenge, sir?” said Mullett solicitously.

“No, thank you.”

“Very good, sir.”

===Chinese===
乔治第三次咳嗽起来。

“要不要含片润喉糖，先生？”马利特关切地问。

“不用，谢谢。”

“遵命，先生。”

===Original===
“Mullett!”

“Sir?”

“I find that Mr. Beamish is an intimate friend of this young lady.”

“Fancy that, sir!”

“He is going to introduce me.”

“Very gratifying, I am sure, sir.”

===Chinese===
“马利特！”

“哎，先生。”

“我发觉，比米什先生是这位年轻小姐的挚交。”

“真没想到，先生！”

“他要替我引见。”

“想必可喜可贺，先生。”

===Original===
George sighed dreamily.

“Life is very sweet, Mullett.”

“For those that like it, sir—yes, sir.”

“Lead me to the ties,” said George.

===Chinese===
乔治如梦似幻地叹了口气。

“人生真甜美啊，马利特。”

“对喜欢它的人来说是甜美，先生——是的，先生。”

“领我去看领带吧。”乔治说。

## II / 第二章

### I / 一

===Original===
At the hour of seven-thirty, just when George Finch was trying out his fifth tie, a woman stood pacing the floor in the Byzantine boudoir at Number 16, Seventy-Ninth Street, East.

At first sight this statement may seem contradictory. Is it possible, the captious critic may ask, for a person simultaneously to stand and pace the floor? The answer is Yes, if he or she is sufficiently agitated as to the soul. You do it by placing yourself on a given spot and scrabbling the feet alternately like a cat kneading a hearthrug. It is sometimes the only method by which strong women can keep from having hysterics.

Mrs. Sigsbee H. Waddington was a strong woman. In fact, so commanding was her physique that a stranger might have supposed her to be one in the technical, or circus, sense. She was not tall, but had bulged so generously in every possible direction that, when seen for the first time, she gave the impression of enormous size. No theatre, however little its programme had managed to attract the public, could be said to be “sparsely filled” if Mrs. Waddington had dropped in to look at the show. Public speakers, when Mrs. Waddington was present, had the illusion that they were addressing most of the population of the United States. And when she went to Carlsbad or Aix-les-Bains to take the waters, the authorities huddled together nervously and wondered if there would be enough to go round.

===Chinese===
七点半这个时辰，正当乔治·芬奇（George Finch）试到第五条领带的当口，东七十九街 16 号那间拜占庭风格的闺房里，一个女人正站着在地板上踱步。

乍一看，这句话似乎自相矛盾。好挑剔的批评家也许要问：一个人可能同时站着又踱步吗？答案是：可能，只要他或她灵魂里搅动得足够厉害。做法是把自己钉在某一点上，两只脚轮番抓挠，活像猫儿揉捏炉前地毯。这有时是女中豪杰们免得歇斯底里发作的唯一法门。

西格斯比·H·沃丁顿夫人（Mrs. Sigsbee H. Waddington）便是个女中豪杰。事实上，她体格之威武雄壮，教初见的人没准会以为她是技术意义上——也就是马戏班意义上——的那种“女大力士”。她个子不高，却朝着每一个可能的方向丰腴发展得如此慷慨，以致头一回见到她，人只会留下一个庞大的印象。哪家剧院的戏码再怎么招不来看客，只要沃丁顿夫人赏光进场看戏，就绝说不上“观众稀稀拉拉”。演说家一见沃丁顿夫人在座，便恍然觉得台下的听众是美利坚合众国的大半人口。而她去卡尔斯巴德（Carlsbad）或艾克斯莱班（Aix-les-Bains）饮泉疗养时，当地当局总要惴惴不安地挤作一团，犯愁泉水可够不够大家分。

===Original===
Her growing bulk was a perpetual sorrow—one of many—to her husband. When he had married her, she had been slim and svelte. But she had also been the relict of the late P. Homer Horlick, the Cheese King, and he had left her several million dollars. Most of the interest accruing from this fortune she had, so it sometimes seemed to Sigsbee H. Waddington, spent on starchy foods.

Mrs. Waddington stood and paced the floor, and presently the door opened.

“Lord Hunstanton,” announced Ferris, the butler.

The standard of male looks presented up to the present in this story has not been high: but the man who now entered did much to raise the average. He was tall and slight and elegant, with frank blue eyes—one of them preceded by an eyeglass—and one of those clipped moustaches. His clothes had been cut by an inspired tailor and pressed by a genius. His tie was simply an ethereal white butterfly, straight from heaven, that hovered over the collar-stud as if it were sipping pollen from some exotic flower. (George Finch, now working away at number eight and having just got it creased in four places, would have screamed hoarsely with envy at the sight of that tie.)

===Chinese===
她那日渐壮硕的身形，是丈夫心头一桩绵延不绝的哀愁——而他的哀愁远不止这一桩。当年娶她的时候，她苗条娉婷；可她同时也是已故“奶酪大王”P·霍默·霍利克（P. Homer Horlick）的遗孀，而霍利克给她留下了几百万美元的家产。在西格斯比·H·沃丁顿看来，这笔财产生出的利息，多半都叫她花在了淀粉质食物上。

沃丁顿夫人站着踱步不已，不多时，房门开了。

“亨斯坦顿勋爵（Lord Hunstanton）到。”男管家费里斯（Ferris）通报说。

本书登场至今，男性的相貌水准实在不高；但此刻进来的这位，大大拉高了大伙儿的平均分。他修长清瘦，风度翩翩，生一双坦率的蓝眼睛——其中一只前面架着单片眼镜——蓄着那种修剪得齐齐整整的小胡子。他的衣裳出自一位灵感附体的裁缝之手，又经一位天才熨烫过。他的领带干脆就是一只空灵的白蝴蝶，直接从天堂下凡，盘旋在领扣上方，仿佛正从某朵异域奇花里啜饮花粉。（乔治·芬奇此刻正鏖战到第八条，刚刚还把它弄出四道褶痕，见了那条领带非哑着嗓子嫉妒得尖叫不可。）

===Original===
“Well, here I am,” said Lord Hunstanton. He paused for a moment, then added, “What, what!” as if he felt that it was expected of him.

“It was so kind of you to come,” said Mrs. Waddington, pivoting on her axis and panting like a hart after the water-brooks.

“Not at all.”

“I knew I could rely on you.”

===Chinese===
“瞧，我来了。”亨斯坦顿勋爵说。他顿了片刻，又添一句：“可不是，可不是！”仿佛觉得不说这一句有失职守。

“您能赏光前来，真是太好了。”沃丁顿夫人原地转过身来，气喘吁吁，宛如公鹿切慕溪水。

“哪里哪里。”

“我就知道能指望您。”

===Original===
“You have only to command.”

“You’re such a true friend, though I have known you only such a short time.”

“Is anything wrong?” asked Lord Hunstanton.

===Chinese===
“您只管吩咐。”

“您真是位忠实的朋友，虽然我认识您才这么短的时日。”

“出了什么岔子吗？”亨斯坦顿勋爵问。

===Original===
He was more than a little surprised to find himself at seven-forty in a house where he had been invited to dine at half-past eight. His dressing had been interrupted by a telephone-call from Mrs. Waddington’s butler, begging him to come round at once: and, noting his hostess’s agitation, he hoped that nothing had gone wonky with the dinner.

“Everything is wrong!”

Lord Hunstanton sighed inaudibly. Did this mean cold meat and a pickle?

“Sigsbee is having one of his spells!”

“You mean he has been taken ill?”

===Chinese===
他心里远不止微微诧异：人家请的是八点半来赴宴，他怎么七点四十就置身于这宅子里了。他更衣打扮换到一半，被沃丁顿夫人的男管家一通电话打断，电话里求他即刻过来；而眼见女主人这般心绪激荡，他但愿晚宴没出什么乱子。

“样样都出了岔子！”

亨斯坦顿勋爵无声地叹了口气。莫非这意味着今晚要吃冷肉配酸黄瓜？

“西格斯比那老毛病又犯了！”

“您是说，他病倒了？”

===Original===
“Not ill. Fractious.” Mrs. Waddington gulped. “It’s so awful that this should have occurred on the night of an important dinner-party, after you have taken such trouble with his education. I have said a hundred times that, since you came, Sigsbee has been a different man. He knows all the forks now, and can even talk intelligently about soufflés.”

“I am only too glad if any little pointers I have been able to. …”

“And when I take him out for a run he always walks on the outside of the pavement. And here he must go, on the night of my biggest dinner-party, and have one of his spells.”

“What is the trouble? Is he violent?”

“No. Sullen.”

===Chinese===
“不是病，是闹别扭。”沃丁顿夫人哽了一下。“偏偏赶在我这重要晚宴的晚上发作，您在他教养上又费了那么大的心血，真是太糟糕了。我说过一百遍，自打您来了，西格斯比换了个人。如今各式刀叉他全认得了，谈起舒芙蕾（soufflé）来也能头头是道。”

“只要我那几条小指点能派上些用场，我就……”

“如今我带他出门遛弯，他总走人行道的外侧。可他偏赶上我这一年最要紧的晚宴，犯起老毛病来了。”

“究竟怎么啦？他动手打人吗？”

“不。是闷着气。”

===Original===
“What about?”

Mrs. Waddington’s mouth set in a hard line.

“Sigsbee is pining for the West again!”

“You don’t say so?”

“Yes, sir, he’s pining for the great wide open spaces of the West. He says the East is effete and he wants to be out there among the silent canyons where men are men. If you want to know what I think, somebody’s been feeding him Zane Grey.”

“Can nothing be done?”

===Chinese===
“为什么事？”

沃丁顿夫人的嘴抿成了一条僵硬的直线。

“西格斯比又犯起思念西部的心病来了！”

“不会吧？”

“千真万确，先生，他害的是心病，一心想着西部那辽阔的旷野。他说东部蔫头耷脑、萎靡不振，他要出去，去那些幽寂的峡谷之间，到男人算得男人的地方去。您要是问我的看法，准是有人一直拿赞恩·格雷（Zane Grey）的小说喂他。”

“就一点办法也没有？”

===Original===
“Yes—in time, I can get him right if I’m given time, by stopping his pocket-money. But I need time, and here he is, an hour before my important dinner, with some of the most wealthy and exclusive people in New York expected at any moment, refusing to put on his dress clothes and saying that all a man that is a man needs is to shoot his bison and cut off a steak and cook it by the light of the western stars. And what I want to know is, what am I to do?”

Lord Hunstanton twisted his moustache thoughtfully.

“Very perplexing.”

“I thought if you went and had a word with him. …”

“I doubt if it would do any good. I suppose you couldn’t dine without him?”

“It would make us thirteen.”

===Chinese===
“办法是有的——只要给我时间。克扣他的零用钱，假以时日，我能把他调理回来。可我需要时间，而他呢，离我这要紧的晚宴只剩一个钟头，纽约最豪富、最顶尖的几位人物随时驾到，他却死活不肯换上礼服，还说什么凡称得上男人的，要的不过是射一头野牛、割下一块牛排、就着西天星光烤来吃。我想知道的是：我该怎么办？”

亨斯坦顿勋爵若有所思地捻捻胡子。

“着实令人为难。”

“我想，要不您过去跟他说句话……”

“我怕不大管用。没有他，你们难道就不能开饭？”

“那可就凑成十三个人了。”

===Original===
“I see.” His Lordship’s face brightened. “I’ve got it! Send Miss Waddington to reason with him.”

“Molly? You think he would listen to her?”

“He is very fond of her.”

Mrs. Waddington reflected.

“It’s worth trying. I’ll go up and see if she is dressed. She is a dear girl, isn’t she, Lord Hunstanton?”

“Charming, charming.”

===Chinese===
“原来如此。”勋爵大人面露喜色。“有了！派沃丁顿小姐去劝劝他。”

“莫莉（Molly）？您觉得他会听她的？”

“他顶疼她了。”

沃丁顿夫人思忖片刻。

“值得一试。我上楼瞧瞧她换好衣裳没有。她是个招人疼的好姑娘，是不是，亨斯坦顿勋爵？”

“可爱，实在可爱。”

===Original===
“I’m sure I’m as fond of her as if she were my own daughter.”

“No doubt.”

“Though, of course, dearly as I love her, I am never foolishly indulgent. So many girls today are spoiled by foolish indulgence.”

“True.”

“My great wish, Lord Hunstanton, is one day to see her happily married to some good man.”

His lordship closed the door behind Mrs. Waddington and stood for some moments in profound thought. He may have been wondering what was the earliest he could expect a cocktail, or he may have been musing on some deeper subject—if there is a deeper subject.

===Chinese===
“我敢说，我爱她简直跟亲生女儿一个样。”

“想必如此。”

“当然，虽说爱她爱得深，我可不昏聩地娇惯。如今多少姑娘都毁在糊涂的溺爱上。”

“这话不假。”

“我最大的心愿，亨斯坦顿勋爵，就是有朝一日看她欢欢喜喜地嫁给一位好人家男子。”

沃丁顿夫人出去后，勋爵大人带上门，深深地沉思了好一会儿。他也许在盘算鸡尾酒最快能等到什么时候，也许在思索某个更深奥的题目——倘若世上真有比这更深奥的题目的话。

### II / 二

===Original===
Mrs. Waddington navigated upstairs, and paused before a door near the second landing.

“Molly!”

“Yes, mother?”

Mrs. Waddington was frowning as she entered the room. How often she had told this girl to call her “mater”!

But this was a small point, and not worth mentioning at a time like the present. She sank into a chair with a creaking groan. Strong woman though she was, Mrs. Sigsbee Waddington, like the chair, was near to breaking down.

===Chinese===
沃丁顿夫人一路领航，迤逦上楼，在第二个楼梯口近旁的一扇门前停住。

“莫莉！”

“哎，妈妈？”

沃丁顿夫人进屋时眉头拧着。她多少回叮嘱过这丫头，要叫她“妈咪”（mater）！

不过这是桩小事，眼下这种时候不值得提。她吱嘎一声呻吟着，陷进一把椅子里。再怎么是位女中豪杰，西格斯比·沃丁顿夫人也和这把椅子一样，眼看就要散架了。

===Original===
“Good heavens, mother! What’s the matter?”

“Send her away,” muttered Mrs. Waddington, nodding at her stepdaughter’s maid.

“All right, mother. I shan’t want you any more, Julie. I can manage now. Shall I get you a glass of water, mother?”

Molly looked at her suffering stepparent with gentle concern, wishing that she had something stronger than water to offer. But her late mother had brought her up in that silly, stuffy way in which old-fashioned mothers used to bring up their daughters: and, incredible as it may seem in these enlightened days, Molly Waddington had reached the age of twenty without forming even a nodding acquaintance with alcohol. Now, no doubt, as she watched her stepmother gulping before her like a moose that has had trouble in the home, she regretted that she was not one of those sensible modern girls who always carry a couple of shots around with them in a jewelled flask.

===Chinese===
“我的天哪，妈妈！您怎么啦？”

“叫她出去。”沃丁顿夫人朝继女的女仆扬了扬下巴，咕哝道。

“好的，妈妈。朱莉（Julie），不用你伺候了，我自己来就行。要我给您倒杯水吗，妈妈？”

莫莉温温柔柔、关切备至地望着这位受难的继母，只恨拿不出比水更有劲儿的东西来孝敬。可她已故的母亲当年用的正是那种又傻又古板的教养法，老派母亲向来是这么把女儿带大的：因此，纵然在这开化的年代听来难以置信，莫莉·沃丁顿长到二十岁，连跟酒打个点头之交都不曾有过。此刻，眼见继母在她面前大气直喘，活像一头家里遭了烦心事的驼鹿，她无疑在懊悔自己不是那种明白事理的摩登姑娘——人家随身总在镶宝小酒壶里揣着几口烈酒。

===Original===
But, though a defective upbringing kept her from being useful in this crisis, nobody could deny that, as she stood there half-dressed for dinner, Molly Waddington was extremely ornamental. If George Finch could have seen her at that moment. … But then if George Finch had seen her at that moment, he would immediately have shut his eyes like a gentleman: for there was that about her costume, in its present stage of development, which was not for the male gaze.

Still, however quickly he had shut his eyes, he could not have shut them rapidly enough to keep from seeing that Mullett, in his recent remarks on an absorbing subject, had shown an even nicer instinct for the mot juste than he had supposed. Beyond all chance for evasion or doubt, Molly Waddington was cuddly. She was wearing primrose knickers, and her silk-stockinged legs tapered away to little gold shoes. Her pink fingers were clutching at a blue dressing-jacket with swan’s-down trimming. Her bobbed hair hung about a round little face with a tip-tilted little nose. Her eyes were large, her teeth small and white and even. She had a little brown mole on the back of her neck and—in short, to sum the whole thing up, if George Finch could have caught even the briefest glimpse of her at this juncture, he must inevitably have fallen over sideways, yapping like a dog.

===Chinese===
话说回来，虽然这缺了课的教养叫她在此危急关头帮不上忙，却谁也不能否认：一身晚妆才上到一半、就这么站在那儿的莫莉·沃丁顿，极具观赏价值。倘若乔治·芬奇此刻能看见她……不过话说回来，乔治·芬奇若当真此刻看见了她，必定立刻像个绅士那样阖上眼睛：因为她这身行头，就眼下的完成度而言，自有其不宜男性观看的成分。

话虽如此，无论他阖眼阖得多快，也快不过让他错过这样一桩事实：马利特（Mullett）前些日子就一个引人入胜的话题发表高见时，对“恰到好处的字眼儿”的直觉，比他自以为的还要精到。无可遁逃，也无可置疑——莫莉·沃丁顿是个叫人只想搂一把的姑娘。她穿一条报春花黄的宽短裤，着丝袜的双腿往下渐收，末端是一双小小的金鞋。她粉嫩的手指拢着一件镶天鹅绒毛边的蓝色晨衣。齐耳的短发垂在一张圆润的小脸旁，配一只微微上翘的小鼻子。她眼睛大大的，牙齿小小的、白白的、齐齐的。脖子后头有一颗小小的棕色痣，而且——总而言之，一言以蔽之，乔治·芬奇倘能在这个节骨眼上哪怕瞥上最短的一眼，也必定侧身栽倒，汪汪叫唤，像条小狗。

===Original===
Mrs. Waddington’s breathing had become easier, and she was sitting up in her chair with something like the old imperiousness.

“Molly,” said Mrs. Waddington, “have you been giving your father Zane Grey?”

“Of course not.”

===Chinese===
沃丁顿夫人的呼吸已见平顺，她从椅子里直起身来，颇带几分往日那股专横劲儿。

“莫莉，”沃丁顿夫人说，“你是不是一直在拿赞恩·格雷喂你爸爸？”

“当然没有。”

===Original===
“You’re sure?”

“Quite. I don’t think there’s any Zane Grey in the house.”

“Then he’s been sneaking out and seeing Tom Mix again,” said Mrs. Waddington.

“You don’t mean … ?”

“Yes! He’s got one of his spells.”

===Chinese===
“你敢肯定？”

“肯定。家里应该一本赞恩·格雷也没有。”

“那他就是又偷偷溜出去看汤姆·米克斯（Tom Mix）的电影了，”沃丁顿夫人说。

“您该不是说……？”

“正是！他老毛病又犯了。”

===Original===
“A bad one?”

“So bad that he refuses to dress for dinner. He says that if the boys”—Mrs. Waddington shuddered—“if the boys don’t like him in a flannel shirt, he won’t come in to dinner at all. And Lord Hunstanton suggested that I should send you to reason with him.”

“Lord Hunstanton? Has he arrived already?”

“I telephoned for him. I am coming to rely on Lord Hunstanton more and more every day. What a dear fellow he is!”

“Yes,” said Molly, a little dubiously. She was not fond of his lordship.

===Chinese===
“犯得凶吗？”

“凶得连晚饭的礼服都不肯换了。他说，要是‘弟兄们’”——沃丁顿夫人打了个哆嗦——“要是弟兄们嫌他穿法兰绒衬衫不像样，他就干脆不来吃饭了。后来亨斯坦顿勋爵出主意，叫我来派你去劝劝他。”

“亨斯坦顿勋爵？他人已经到了？”

“我打电话请他来的。如今我一天比一天更倚重亨斯坦顿勋爵了。这人多可爱呀！”

“是啊。”莫莉应道，语气不无迟疑。她并不喜欢勋爵大人。

===Original===
“So handsome.”

“Yes.”

“Such breeding.”

“I suppose so.”

“I should be very happy,” said Mrs. Waddington, “if a man like Lord Hunstanton asked you to be his wife.”

===Chinese===
“多俊朗。”

“是。”

“多有出身教养。”

“大概吧。”

“倘若亨斯坦顿勋爵那样的人开口，求你嫁给他，”沃丁顿夫人说，“我可就称心如意啦。”

===Original===
Molly fiddled with the trimming of her dressing-jacket. This was not the first time the subject had come up between her stepmother and herself. A remark like the one just recorded was Mrs. Waddington’s idea of letting fall a quiet hint.

“Well. …” said Molly.

“What do you mean, well?”

“Well, don’t you think he’s rather stiff?”

===Chinese===
莫莉捻弄着晨衣上的毛边。继母和她之间，这个话题已不是头一回冒出来。方才记下的那句话，在沃丁顿夫人的算盘里，就算悄悄递了个话头。

“这个嘛……”莫莉说。

“什么叫‘这个嘛’？”

“这个嘛，你不觉得他相当死板吗？”

===Original===
“Stiff!”

“Don’t you find him a little starchy?”

“If you mean that Lord Hunstanton’s manners are perfect, I agree with you.”

===Chinese===
“死板！”

“你不觉得他有点儿拘板乏味吗？”

“要是说亨斯坦顿勋爵的礼数完美无缺，这话我同意。”

===Original===
“I’m not sure that I like a man’s manners to be too perfect,” said Molly meditatively. “Don’t you think a shy man can be rather attractive?” She scraped the toe of one gold shoe against the heel of the other. “The sort of man I think I should rather like,” she said, a dreamy look in her eyes, “would be a sort of slimmish, smallish man with nice brown eyes and rather gold-y, chestnutty hair, who kind of looks at you from a distance because he’s too shy to speak to you and, when he does get a chance to speak to you, sort of chokes and turns pink and twists his fingers and makes funny noises and trips over his feet and looks rather a lamb and. …”

Mrs. Waddington had risen from her chair like a storm-cloud brooding over a countryside.

“Molly!” she cried. “Who is this young man?”

“Why, nobody of course! Just someone I sort of imagined.”

“Oh!” said Mrs. Waddington, relieved. “You spoke as if you knew him.”

===Chinese===
“拿不准男人礼数太完美到底合不合我的心意，”莫莉沉吟道，“你不觉得羞涩的男人有时倒相当有魅力？”她拿一只金鞋的鞋尖去蹭另一只的鞋跟。“我想我该会喜欢的那种人，”她说，眼神里一片悠然神往，“是个瘦瘦小小的人儿，长一双好看的棕眼睛，头发带点儿金乎乎、栗茸茸的；他隔着老远望着你，因为害羞不敢跟你说话；等真得了机会开口，又哽住、脸红、绞手指、发出怪腔怪调、绊自己的脚，瞧着活像只小绵羊，而且……”

沃丁顿夫人已从椅子里立起身来，宛如一朵阴云沉沉压向田野。

“莫莉！”她嚷道，“这个年轻人是谁？”

“哎呀，当然谁也不是！就是我自己胡乱想出来的那么一个人。”

“哦！”沃丁顿夫人松了口气，“听你那口气，倒像真认识他似的。”

===Original===
“What a strange idea!”

“If any young man ever does look at you and make funny noises, you will ignore him.”

“Of course.”

Mrs. Waddington started.

“All this nonsense you have been talking has made me forget about your father. Put on your dress and go down to him at once. Reason with him! If he refuses to come in to dinner, we shall be thirteen, and my party will be ruined.”

“I’ll be ready in a couple of minutes. Where is he?”

===Chinese===
“多怪的念头！”

“将来若真有哪个小伙子这样望着你、发出怪腔怪调，你只当没看见。”

“好的。”

沃丁顿夫人猛地一个激灵。

“你净说这些不着调的话，害我把你爸爸都忘了。快穿上裙子，这就下去找他。好好劝劝他！他若不肯来吃晚饭，咱们可就十三个人了，我的宴会也就全毁了。”

“我几分钟就好。他在哪儿？”

===Original===
“In the library.”

“I’ll be right down.”

“And, when you have seen him, go into the drawing-room and talk to Lord Hunstanton. He is all alone.”

“Very well, mother.”

===Chinese===
“书房。”

“我这就下去。”

“见完他，再进客厅去陪陪亨斯坦顿勋爵。他一个人孤零零的呢。”

“好的，妈妈。”

===Original===
“Mater.”

“Mater,” said Molly.

She was one of those nice, dutiful girls.

===Chinese===
“妈咪。”

“妈咪。”莫莉改了口。

她正是那种又乖又听话的姑娘。

### III / 三

===Original===
In addition to being a nice, dutiful girl, Molly Waddington was also a persuasive, wheedling girl. Better proof of this statement can hardly be afforded than by the fact that, as the clocks were pointing to ten minutes past eight, a red-faced little man with stiff grey hair and a sulky face shambled down the stairs of Number 16, East Seventy-Ninth Street, and, pausing in the hall, subjected Ferris, the butler, to an offensive glare. It was Sigsbee H. Waddington, fully, if sloppily, dressed in the accepted mode of gentlemen of social standing about to dine.

The details of any record performance are always interesting, so it may be mentioned that Molly had reached the library at seven minutes to eight. She had started wheedling at exactly six minutes and forty-five seconds to the hour. At seven fifty-four Sigsbee Waddington had begun to weaken. At seven fifty-seven he was fighting in the last ditch: and at seven fifty-nine, vowing he would ne’er consent, he consented.

Into the arguments used by Molly we need not enter fully. It is enough to say that, if a man loves his daughter dearly, and if she comes to him and says that she has been looking forward to a certain party and is wearing a new dress for that party, and if, finally, she adds that, should he absent himself from that party, the party and her pleasure will be ruined—then, unless the man has a heart of stone, he will give in. Sigsbee Waddington had not a heart of stone. Many good judges considered that he had a head of concrete, but nobody had ever disparaged his heart. At eight precisely he was in his bedroom, shovelling on his dress clothes: and now, at ten minutes past, he stood in the hall and looked disparagingly at Ferris.

===Chinese===
莫莉·沃丁顿不光乖巧听话，还是个会哄人会磨人的姑娘。这句话最好的佐证，莫过于下面这个事实：时钟刚指过八点十分，一个红脸膛的小个子男人，硬邦邦的灰头发，满脸的怏怏不乐，蹭蹭磨磨地下得东七十九街 16 号的楼梯来，在门厅里站定，瞪了男管家费里斯一眼，眼神颇为无礼。正是西格斯比·H·沃丁顿，穿戴倒是齐全——虽说不免邋遢——一副上流体面人家绅士开晚饭前的标准行头。

但凡创下纪录的成绩，细节总是耐人寻味的，因此不妨交代几句：莫莉是八点差七分走进书房的。差六分四十五秒，她准时开劝。七点五十四分，西格斯比·沃丁顿开始松动。七点五十七分，他已作困兽之斗；到了七点五十九分，他口口声声决计不肯——却已经肯了。

莫莉究竟用了哪些说辞，此处无须细表。只需说明这一点就够：倘若一位父亲疼女儿疼得要命，而女儿走到跟前说，她盼这场宴会盼了好久，还为它备下了一条新裙子；倘若她再补上一句，他若缺席，宴席与她的兴致都要泡汤——那么，除非这位父亲的心是石头凿的，否则必定让步。西格斯比·沃丁顿的心不是石头凿的。许多高明的行家认定他长的是一颗混凝土脑袋，可从没有人贬低过他的心肠。八点整，他在卧室里把礼服往身上铲；如今八点过十分，他站在门厅里，拿鄙夷的眼光打量费里斯。

===Original===
Sigsbee Waddington thought Ferris was an overfed wart.

Ferris thought Sigsbee Waddington ought to be ashamed to appear in public in a tie like that.

But thoughts are not words. What Ferris actually said was:

“A cocktail, sir?”

And what Sigsbee Waddington actually said was:

“Yup! Gimme!”

===Chinese===
西格斯比·沃丁顿认定，费里斯是颗喂得太肥的肉疣。

费里斯则认定，西格斯比·沃丁顿系着这么条领带出门见人，简直该害臊。

然而心想归心想，出口的话是另一回事。费里斯实际说的是：

“要来杯鸡尾酒吗，先生？”

而西格斯比·沃丁顿实际说的是：

“要！拿来！”

===Original===
There was a pause. Mr. Waddington still unsoothed, continued to glower. Ferris, resuming his marmoreal calm, had begun to muse once more, as was his habit when in thought, on Brangmarley Hall, Little-Seeping-in-the-Wold, Salop, Eng., where he had spent the early, happy days of his butlerhood.

“Ferris!” said Mr. Waddington at length.

“Sir?”

“You ever been out West, Ferris?”

“No, sir.”

“Ever want to go?”

===Chinese===
一阵冷场。沃丁顿先生余怒未消，兀自瞪眼。费里斯恢复了他那大理石一般的沉静，再度——这是他陷入思索时的老习惯——神游起布兰马利庄园（Brangmarley Hall）来：英格兰什罗普郡，利特尔-西平-因-沃尔德（Little-Seeping-in-the-Wold）；他管家生涯里早年的幸福时光就是在那儿度过的。

“费里斯！”沃丁顿先生终于开腔。

“先生？”

“你上西部去过没有，费里斯？”

“没有，先生。”

“想过要去吗？”

===Original===
“No, sir.”

“Why not?” demanded Mr. Waddington belligerently.

“I understand that in the Western States of America, sir, there is a certain lack of comfort, and the social amenities are not rigorously observed.”

“Gangway!” said Mr. Waddington, making for the front door.

He felt stifled. He wanted air. He yearned, if only for a few brief instants, to be alone with the silent stars.

===Chinese===
“没想过，先生。”

“怎么就没想过？”沃丁顿先生挑着火气质问。

“据我所知，美国的西部各州，先生，舒适方面有所欠缺，社交上的种种讲究也不大有人严格遵行。”

“闪开道！”沃丁顿先生说着，径直奔前门而去。

他憋闷得慌。他想要空气。他渴望——哪怕只有短短片刻——独自与静谧的星星相处。

===Original===
It would be idle to deny that, at this particular moment, Sigsbee H. Waddington was in a dangerous mood. The history of nations shows that the wildest upheavals come from those peoples that have been most rigorously oppressed: and it is so with individuals. There is no man so terrible in his spasmodic fury as the henpecked husband during his short spasms of revolt. Even Mrs. Waddington recognised that, no matter how complete her control normally, Sigsbee H., when having one of his spells, practically amounted to a rogue elephant. Her policy was to keep out of his way till the fever passed, and then to discipline him severely.

As Sigsbee Waddington stood on the pavement outside his house, drinking in the dust-and-gasolene mixture which passes for air in New York and scanning the weak imitation stars which are the best the East provides, he was grim and squiggle-eyed and ripe for murders, stratagems and spoils. Molly’s statement that there was no Zane Grey in the house had been very far from the truth. Sigsbee Waddington had his private store, locked away in a secret cupboard, and since early morning *Riders of the Purple Sage* had hardly ever been out of his hand. During the afternoon, moreover, he had managed to steal away to a motion-picture house on Sixth Avenue where they were presenting Henderson Hoover and Sara Svelte in *That L’il Gal from the Bar B Ranch*. Sigsbee Waddington, as he stood on the pavement, was clad in dress clothes and looked like a stage waiter, but at heart he was wearing chaps and a Stetson hat and people spoke of him as Two-Gun Thomas.

A Rolls-Royce drew up at the kerb, and Mr. Waddington moved a step or two away. A fat man alighted and helped his fatter wife out. Mr. Waddington recognised them. B. and Mrs. Brewster Bodthorne. B. Brewster was the first vice-president of Amalgamated Toothbrushes, and rolled in money.

===Chinese===
无从否认，此时此刻的西格斯比·H·沃丁顿正处在一种危险的心境里。各民族的历史表明，最狂暴的动荡，恰恰出自受压迫最酷烈的人民；个人也是一样。普天之下，痉挛式的怒火再没有比这更可怕的了——惧内丈夫短暂造反时的发作便是。这一点连沃丁顿夫人也心中有数：不论平日里她管得多么周全，西格斯比·H 一旦犯起老毛病，实际上无异于一头离群发狂的独象。她的方略是避其锋芒，等热病退了，再严加管教。

西格斯比·沃丁顿站在自家宅子外的便道上，吞饮着纽约那种冒充空气的尘土掺汽油混合物，端详着东部所能拿得出手的上等货色——那几颗有气无力的仿制星星。他面色阴沉，两眼拧成两团乱麻，满脑子的凶谋、诡计与劫掠，一触即发。莫莉说家里没有赞恩·格雷，这话离事实甚远。西格斯比·沃丁顿私藏着一批存货，锁在暗橱里；打一早起，《紫艾草骑士》（Riders of the Purple Sage）就几乎没离开过他的手。此外，下午他还设法偷空溜到第六大道一家电影院，那里正上映亨德森·胡佛（Henderson Hoover）与萨拉·斯维尔特（Sara Svelte）联袂主演的《巴B牧场来的小姑娘》（That L’il Gal from the Bar B Ranch）。此刻站在便道上的西格斯比·沃丁顿，一身上流礼服，瞧着像个舞台上的侍应生；可在他心里，他绑着皮套裤、扣着斯泰森毡帽，人们提起他来，都叫“双枪”托马斯（Two-Gun Thomas）。

一辆劳斯莱斯停靠到路边，沃丁顿先生挪开了一两步。一个胖子下了车，又搀他那位更胖的太太下来。沃丁顿先生认得他们：B·布鲁斯特·博德索恩（B. Brewster Bodthorne）夫妇。B·布鲁斯特是联合牙刷公司（Amalgamated Toothbrushes）的第一副总经理，家里铜钱打着滚。

===Original===
“Pah!” muttered Mr. Waddington, sickened to the core.

The pair vanished into the house, and presently another Rolls-Royce arrived, followed by a Hispano-Suiza. Consolidated Popcorn and wife emerged, and then United Beef and daughter. A consignment worth on the hoof between eighty and a hundred million.

“How long?” moaned Mr. Waddington. “How long?”

And then, as the door closed, he was aware of a young man behaving strangely on the pavement some few feet away from him.

===Chinese===
“呸！”沃丁顿先生低声咕哝，恶心到了肺腑深处。

这一对消失进门内；转眼又是一辆劳斯莱斯驶到，后头还跟着一辆西班牙-苏萨（Hispano-Suiza）。“联合爆米花”（Consolidated Popcorn）偕夫人现身，接着“联合牛肉”（United Beef）携女公子驾到。这一批货色，按活口作价，也在八千万至一亿之间。

“还要多久？”沃丁顿先生哀鸣道，“还要多久？”

正在大门阖上之际，他发觉几英尺开外的便道上，有个年轻人举止怪异。

### IV / 四

===Original===
