It’s horrible!” He wrenched the bundle from his pocket and threw it to the policeman. “Here, take it before I weaken again. Give me the three hundred quick and let me get away.”

“I don’t know how to thank you, sir.”

“Don’t thank me, don’t thank me. One﻿—two﻿—three,” said Mr. Waddington, counting the bills. “Don’t thank me at all. It’s a pleasure.”

===Chinese===

西格斯比·H·沃丁顿仿佛从一场恍惚中醒转过来。他抖了抖身子，怔怔地瞪着警察，那神情像是在问：“我在哪儿？”他发出一声深长的、悔恨交加的叹息。

“钱这东西真是魔鬼！”他说。“把一个人的原则和良好决心泡得酥烂，这有多可怕！十足的贪心——方才我说不肯把股票让给你，就是贪心在作祟。彻头彻尾、贪得无厌的贪心。瞧瞧我这个人吧，银行里躺着千百万的家当，一转眼的工夫，竟起意要抵抗一股慷慨的冲动——不肯对一位我喜欢他脸皮的同类行一桩善举。太可怕了！”他从口袋里扯出那捆股票，朝警察掷了过去。“拿着，趁我没再度心软。快把那三百块数给我，让我走。”

“我真不知道该怎么谢您才好，先生。”

“别谢，别谢。一——二——三。”沃丁顿先生点着钞票说。“千万甭谢。我乐意。”

### III / 三

===Original===

Upstairs, while the conversation just recorded was in progress, Frederick Mullett was entertaining his fiancée, Fanny Welch, to a light collation in the kitchen of George Finch’s apartment. It is difficult for a man to look devotional while his mouth is full of cold beef and chutney﻿—but not impossible, for Mullett was doing it now. He gazed at Fanny very much as George Finch had gazed at Molly Waddington, Hamilton Beamish at Madame Eulalie, and as a million other young men in New York and its outskirts were or would shortly be gazing at a million other young women. Love had come rather late to Frederick Mullett, for his had been a busy life, but it had come to stay.

Externally, Fanny Welch appeared not unworthy of his devotion. She was a pretty little thing with snapping black eyes and a small face. The thing you noticed about her first was the slim shapeliness of her hands with their long, sensitive fingers. One of the great advantages of being a pickpocket is that you do have nice hands.

“I like this place,” said Fanny, looking about her.

“Do you, honey?” said Mullett tenderly. “I was hoping you would. Because I’ve got a secret for you.”

“What’s that?”

“This is where you and me are going to spend our honeymoon!”

===Chinese===

楼上，正当方才记录在案的这场谈话进行之际，弗雷德里克·马利特（Frederick Mullett）正在乔治·芬奇公寓的厨房里，以一顿小食便点款待他的未婚妻芬妮·韦尔奇（Fanny Welch）。一个男人嘴里塞满冷牛肉和酸辣酱的时候，要摆出一副仰望圣像般的虔诚神情，实在是难——但也并非绝无可能，因为马利特此刻正在这么做。他凝望芬妮的眼神，同乔治·芬奇凝望莫莉·沃丁顿的、汉密尔顿·比米什凝望尤拉莉夫人的如出一辙，也同纽约城内城外百万别的小伙子正在、或即将凝望百万别的姑娘的目光一模一样。爱情降临到弗雷德里克·马利特身上，算是相当晚了，因为他这一辈子忙忙碌碌；可它一来，就住下不走了。

从外表看，芬妮·韦尔奇倒也不辜负他这份痴心。她是个标致的小东西，一双黑眼睛亮闪闪的，生着一张小脸。你头一眼注意到的，是她那双手的纤巧匀停，手指又细又长，灵敏极了。当扒手的一大好处就是：你的手必定生得漂亮。

“我喜欢这地方。”芬妮环顾四周说。

“是吗，宝贝儿？”马利特柔声细气地说，“我就盼着你喜欢呢。因为我有个秘密要告诉你。”

“什么秘密？”

“你和我往后就在这儿度蜜月！”

===Original===

“What, in this kitchen?”

“Of course not. We’ll have the run of the whole apartment, with the roof thrown in.”

“What’ll Mr. Finch have to say to that?”

“He won’t know, pettie. You see, Mr. Finch has just gone and got engaged to be married himself, and he’ll be off on his honeymoon-trip, so the whole place’ll be ours for ever so long. What do you think of that?”

“Sounds good to me.”

“I’ll take and show you the place in a minute or two. It’s the best studio-apartment for miles around. There’s a nice large sitting-room that looks on to the roof, with French windows so that you can stroll out and take the air when you like. And there’s a sleeping-porch on the roof, in case the weather’s warm. And a bath H. and C., with shower. It’s the snuggest place you’ll ever want to find, and you and I can stay perched up here like two little birds in a nest. And, when we’ve finished honeymooning, we’ll go down to Long Island and buy a little duck-farm and live happy ever after.”

===Chinese===

“什么，就在这间厨房里？”

“当然不是。整套公寓都归你我随便用，连屋顶都搭上。”

“那芬奇先生会怎么说呢？”

“他不会知道的，小心肝。你瞧，芬奇先生自己刚刚订了婚，要去蜜月旅行，这一整套房好久好久都归咱们。你看这主意妙不妙？”

“听着不赖。”

“过上一两分钟，我就领你把这房子看个遍。方圆好几英里，再找不出比这更好的工作室公寓了。有一间宽敞漂亮的客厅，正对屋顶，装着落地窗，你想什么时候出去透气，就什么时候踱出去。屋顶上还有一座睡廊，天热了正用得着。浴室冷热水俱全，带淋浴。你往后再也找不着比这更温馨的去处，你我就栖在这儿，像窝里的一双小鸟儿。等蜜月度完，咱们就下长岛（Long Island）去，买一座小小的养鸭场，从此过着幸福的日子。”

===Original===

Fanny looked doubtful.

“Can you see me on a duck-farm, Freddy?”

“Can I?” Mullett’s eyes beamed adoration. “You bet I can see you there﻿—standing in a gingham apron on the old brick path between the hollyhocks, watching little Frederick romping under the apple-tree.”

“Little *who*?”

“Little Frederick.”

“Oh? And did you notice little Fanny clinging to my skirts?”

===Chinese===

芬妮面露疑色。

“你想象得出我在养鸭场里的模样吗，弗雷迪？”

“想象得出？”马利特的眼里闪着爱慕的光。“我当然想得出你在那儿的模样——系一条花格布围裙，站在蜀葵夹道的老砖径上，望着小弗雷德里克在苹果树底下撒欢儿。”

“小‘谁’？”

“小弗雷德里克。”

“哦？那你瞧见没有，小芬妮还扒着我的裙子呢。”

===Original===

“So she is. And William John in his cradle on the porch.”

“I think we’d better stop looking for awhile,” said Fanny. “Our family’s growing too fast.”

Mullett sighed ecstatically.

“Doesn’t it sound quiet and peaceful after the stormy lives we’ve led. The quacking of the ducks.﻿ ﻿… The droning of the bees.﻿ ﻿… Put back that spoon, dearie. You know it doesn’t belong to you.”

Fanny removed the spoon from the secret places of her dress and eyed it with a certain surprise.

“Now, how did that get there?” she said.

===Chinese===

“还真有呢。门廊的摇篮里还躺着个威廉·约翰。”

“依我说，咱们这会儿先别往下看了。”芬妮说，“咱们这一家子添丁太快啦。”

马利特如痴如醉地叹了口气。

“比起咱们俩过去那些惊涛骇浪的日子，这听着多清静、多安宁啊。鸭子的嘎嘎声……蜜蜂的嗡嗡声……乖，把那把勺子放回去。你知道那不是你的东西。”

芬妮从衣裳的隐秘处摸出那把勺子，还带着几分诧异端详着它。

“咦，它怎么跑到那儿去了？”她说。

===Original===

“You snitched it up, sweetness,” said Mullett gently. “Your little fingers just hovered for a moment like little bees over a flower, and the next minute the thing was gone. It was beautiful to watch, dearie, but put it back. You’ve done with all that sort of thing now, you know.”

“I guess I have,” said Fanny wistfully.

“You don’t guess you have, precious,” corrected her husband-to-be. “You know you have. Same as I’ve done.”

“Are you really on the level now, Freddy?”

“I’m as honest as the day is long.”

“Work at nights, eh? Mullett, the human moth. Goes through his master’s clothes like a jealous wife.”

===Chinese===

“是你自个儿顺来的，小甜甜。”马利特温声细气地说，“你那小手指头先在花儿上头盘旋了那么一小会儿，活像一只采蜜的小蜜蜂，转眼那东西就没了。看着真叫美，乖乖，可你得放回去。那一套营生，你如今已经洗手不干了，你知道的。”

“我想我是不干了。”芬妮怅然若失地说。

“不是‘我想我不干了’，心肝儿，”她未来的丈夫纠正道，“是‘我知道我不干了’。跟我一样。”

“你如今真的走正道了，弗雷迪？”

“我如今清白得很，跟光天化日似的。”

“改在夜里干活，是吧？马利特，人间的扑灯蛾。翻检起主人的衣裳来，跟醋劲大发的老婆一个样儿。”

===Original===

Mullett laughed indulgently.

“The same little Fanny! How you do love to tease. Yes, precious, I’m through with the game for good. I wouldn’t steal a bone collar-stud now, not if my mother came and begged me on her bended knee. All I want is my little wife and my little home in the country.”

Fanny frowned pensively.

“You don’t think it’ll be kind of quiet down on that duck-farm? Kind of slow?”

“Slow?” said Mullett, shocked.

“Well, maybe not. But we’re retiring from business awful young, Freddy.”

===Chinese===

马利特宽容大度地笑了。

“还是那个小芬妮！你就是爱打趣。是啊，心肝儿，这一行我是彻底金盆洗手了。如今就是一枚骨头领扣我也决不下手，哪怕我妈跪着双膝来苦苦哀求。我心心念念的，就是我那小媳妇，和乡下的一处小家。”

芬妮沉吟着蹙起了眉头。

“你不觉得那养鸭场底下会有些冷清吗？有些闷？”

“闷？”马利特大惊失色。

“呃，也许不闷。可咱们收山收得也太早了呀，弗雷迪。”

===Original===

A look of concern came into Mullett’s face.

“You don’t mean you still have a hankering for the old game?”

“Well, what if I do?” said Fanny defiantly. “You do, too, if you’d only come clean and admit it.”

The look of concern changed to one of dignity.

“Nothing of the kind,” said Mullett. “I give you my word, Fanny, that there isn’t the job on earth that could tempt me now. And I do wish you would bring yourself to feel the same, honey.”

“Oh, I’m not saying I would bother with anything that wasn’t really big. But, honest to goodness, Freddy, it would be a crime to sidestep anything worthwhile, if it came along. It isn’t as if we had all the money in the world. I’ve picked up some nice little things at the stores and I suppose you’ve kept some of the stuff you got away with, but outside of that we’ve nothing but the bit of cash we’ve managed to save. We’ve got to be practical.”

===Chinese===

马利特脸上泛起一层忧虑。

“你该不会还对老本行恋恋不舍吧？”

“唔，就算舍不下又怎么样？”芬妮不服气地说，“你也一样，只要你肯坦白承认。”

那层忧虑化作了一脸正色。

“压根儿没有的事。”马利特说。“芬妮，我对天起誓，如今这世上再没有什么买卖引诱得动我了。我也真心盼着你能跟我一个心思，宝贝儿。”

“哦，我不是说够不上大份的活儿我也乐意费神。可是，说句良心话，弗雷迪，真有一桩上算的买卖送到眼前，却绕着道儿走，那才是造孽呢。咱们又不是金山银山，家底儿有的是。铺子里我陆陆续续捡过几件可心的小玩意儿，你手上想必也还留着些得手的货色；可除了这些，咱们就只剩下省吃俭用攒下的那点现钱了。咱们得务实一点儿。”

===Original===

“But, sweetie, think of the awful chances you’d be taking of getting pinched.”

“I’m not afraid. If they ever do nab me, I’ve got a spiel about my poor old mother.﻿ ﻿…”

“You haven’t got a mother.”

“Who said I had?﻿ ﻿… a spiel about my poor old mother that would draw tears from the Woolworth Building. Listen! ‘Don’t turn me over to the police, mister, I only did it for ma’s sake. If you was out of work for weeks and starvin’ and you had to sit and watch your old ma bendin’ over the washtubs.﻿ ﻿…’ ”

“Don’t, Fanny, please! I can’t bear it even though I know it’s just a game. I.﻿ ﻿… Hello! Somebody at the front door. Probably only a model wanting to know if Mr. Finch has a job for her. You wait here, honey. I’ll get rid of her and be back in half a minute.”

===Chinese===

“可是，甜心，你想过没有，叫人逮住的风险有多大？”

“我不怕。真让他们逮着了，我有一套可怜老母亲的现成说辞……”

“你压根儿就没有妈。”

“谁说我有了？……一套可怜老母亲的说辞，能把伍尔沃思大楼（Woolworth Building）都说得掉下眼泪。你听：‘先生，别把我扭送警察局，我全是为了俺妈呀。你要是一连几个星期找不着活儿、饿得两眼发花，还得干坐着，眼巴巴瞧着你老娘弯着腰泡在洗衣盆跟前……’”

“别说了，芬妮，求你了！明知道只是闹着玩儿，我也受不了。我……嘿，前门有人。多半是哪个模特儿，来问芬奇先生有没有活儿派给她。你在这儿等着，宝贝儿。我这就把她打发走，半分钟就回来。”

### IV / 四

===Original===

More than twenty times that period had elapsed, however, before Frederick Mullett returned to the kitchen. He found his bride-to-be in a considerably less amiable mood than that in which he had left her. She was standing with folded arms, and the temperature of the room had gone down a number of degrees.

“Pretty girl?” she inquired frostily, as Mullett crossed the threshold.

“Eh?”

“You said you were going to send that model away in half a minute, and I’ve been waiting here nearer a quarter of an hour,” said Fanny, verifying this statement by a glance at the wristwatch, the absence of which from their stock was still an unsolved mystery to a prosperous firm of jewellers on Fifth Avenue.

Mullett clasped her in his arms. It was a matter of some difficulty, for she was not responsive, but he did it.

“It was not a model, darling. It was a man. A guy with grey hair and a red face.”

===Chinese===

然而，等到弗雷德里克·马利特回到厨房，光阴已经流走了那“半分钟”的二十多倍。他发现，比起他离开的时候，他那准新娘的心境已然恶劣了一大截。她抱着双臂站在那儿，屋里的温度降了好几度。

“姑娘挺漂亮吧？”马利特跨进门槛的时候，她冷若冰霜地问。

“嗯？”

“你说半分钟就能把那模特儿打发走，可我在这里等了差不多有一刻钟。”芬妮说，说着瞥了一眼腕上的表，为这番话作了见证——至于这块表怎么就会从第五大道上一家生意兴隆的珠宝行的存货里不翼而飞，那家殷实字号至今仍旧一头雾水。

马利特张开双臂搂她。这件事颇费了些周章，因为她毫无回应，但他到底还是搂上了。

“来的不是模特儿，亲爱的。是个男人。一个白头发、红脸膛的家伙。”

===Original===

“Oh? What did he want?”

Mullett’s already somewhat portly frame seemed to expand, as if with some deep emotion.

“He came to tempt me, Fanny.”

“To tempt you?”

“That’s what he did. Wanted to know if my name was Mullett, and two seconds after I had said it was he offered me three hundred dollars to perpetrate a crime.”

“He did? What crime?”

===Chinese===

“哦？他想干什么？”

马利特本就有些富态的身躯仿佛又胀大了一圈，像是被某种深沉的感情撑了起来。

“他是来试探我的，芬妮。”

“试探你？”

“千真万确。他问我是不是姓马利特，我说是；话音落下才两秒钟，他就出三百美元，要我干一桩罪案。”

“他敢？什么罪案？”

===Original===

“I didn’t wait for him to tell me. I spurned his offer and came away. That’ll show you if I’ve reformed or not. A nice, easy, simple job he said it was, that I could do in a couple of minutes.”

“And you spurned him, eh?”

“I certainly spurned him. I spurned him good and plenty.”

“And then you came away?”

“Came right away.”

“Then listen here,” said Fanny in a steely voice, “it don’t seem to me that your times add up right. You say he made you this offer two seconds after he heard your name. Well, why did it take you a quarter of an hour to get back to this kitchen? If you want to know what I think, it wasn’t a red-faced man with grey hair at all﻿—it was one of these Washington Square vamps and you were flirting with her.”

===Chinese===

“我没等他说出口。我把他的提议一口啐了回去，抬腿就走。这总够证明我改没改邪归正了吧。他说那是桩又体面又省事的简单活儿，我两三分钟就能办妥。”

“你就这么把他啐回去了，嗯？”

“啐了个结结实实。啐得那叫一个透彻底。”

“然后你就走开了？”

“撒腿就走。”

“那好，你听着，”芬妮的声音冷得像钢，“依我看，你这笔时间账可对不上呀。你说他听见你的名字才两秒钟，就开了那个价。那好，你怎么花了一刻钟才回到这间厨房？要问我想什么，我看来的根本就不是什么红脸膛的白毛男人——是华盛顿广场那一路的骚狐狸，你在跟她调情。”

===Original===

“Fanny!”

“Well, I’ve read *Gingery Stories*, and I know what it’s like down here in Bohemia with all these artists and models and everything.”

Mullett drew himself up.

“Your suspicions pain me, Fanny. If you care to step out on to the roof, you can peek in at the sitting-room window and see him for yourself. He’s waiting there for me to bring him a drink. The reason I was so long coming back was that it took him ten minutes before he asked my name. Up till then he just sat and spluttered.”

“All right. Take me out on the roof.”

“There!” said Mullett, a moment later. “Now perhaps you’ll believe me.”

===Chinese===

“芬妮！”

“哼，我可是读过《泼辣故事》（Gingery Stories）的。这波西米亚地界儿上是什么光景，我清楚得很——画家啦，模特儿啦，五花八门。”

马利特把腰板挺得笔直。

“你的猜疑叫我痛心，芬妮。你只要到屋顶上去，从客厅的窗户往里一瞟，就能亲眼见到他。他在那儿等我送酒呢。我回来得晚，是因为他足足过了十分钟才问起我的名字。在那之前，他光坐在那儿扑扑地喷粗气。”

“行。带我上屋顶。”

“瞧见了吧！”过了一会儿，马利特说，“这会儿总该信我了吧。”

===Original===

Through the French windows of the sitting-room there was undeniably visible a man of precisely the appearance described. Fanny was remorseful.

“Did I wrong my poor little Freddy, then?” she said.

“Yes, you did.”

“I’m sorry. There!”

She kissed him. Mullett melted immediately.

“I must go back and get that drink,” he said.

===Chinese===

透过客厅的落地窗，千真万确能望见一个男子，相貌同描述的分毫不差。芬妮满心懊悔。

“这么说，我冤枉了我可怜的小弗雷迪？”她说。

“没错，冤枉了。”

“对不起。喏！”

她亲了他一下。马利特登时化成了一摊。

“我得回去把酒送去了。”他说。

===Original===

“And I must be getting along.”

“Oh, not yet,” begged Mullett.

“Yes, I must. I’ve got to look in at one or two of the stores.”

“Fanny!”

“Well, a girl’s got to have her trousseau, hasn’t she?”

Mullett sighed.

===Chinese===

“我也该动身了。”

“哎，先别走，”马利特央求道。

“不行，非走不可。我还得到一两家铺子里转一转。”

“芬妮！”

“嗐，姑娘家总得置办嫁妆呀，是不是？”

马利特叹了口气。

===Original===

“You’ll be very careful, precious?” he said anxiously.

“I’m always careful. Don’t you worry about me.”

Mullett retired, and Fanny, blowing a parting kiss from her pretty fingers, passed through the door leading to the stairs.

===Chinese===

“你会万般小心的，心肝儿？”他忧心忡忡地问。

“我一向小心。你甭替我操心。”

马利特退了回去。芬妮用漂亮的指尖飞了一个临别的吻，穿过那扇通楼梯的门走了。

---
===Original===

It was perhaps five minutes later, while Mullett sat dreaming golden dreams in the kitchen and Sigsbee H. Waddington sat sipping his whisky-and-soda in the sitting-room, that a sudden tap on the French window caused the latter to give a convulsive leap and spill most of the liquid down the front of his waistcoat.

He looked up. A girl was standing outside the window, and from her gestures he gathered that she was requesting him to open it.

===Chinese===

大约五分钟之后，马利特正坐在厨房里做着金光灿灿的美梦，西格斯比·H·沃丁顿正坐在客厅里小口呷着他的威士忌苏打，落地窗上忽然响起轻轻一叩，吓得后者浑身痉挛地一蹦，把杯中大半的酒都泼在了马甲前襟上。

他抬头一看。窗外立着一个姑娘，从她打的手势看，他明白她是要他把窗子打开。

### V / 五

===Original===

It was some time before Sigsbee H. Waddington could bring himself to do so. There exist, no doubt, married men of the baser sort who would have enjoyed the prospect of a tête-à-tête chat with a girl with snapping black eyes who gesticulated at them through windows: but Sigsbee Waddington was not one of them. By nature and training he was circumspect to a degree. So for awhile he merely stood and stared at Fanny. It was not until her eyes became so imperative as to be practically hypnotic that he brought himself to undo the latch.

“And about time, too,” said Fanny, with annoyance, stepping softly into the room.

“What do you want?”

“I want a little talk with you. What’s all this I hear about you asking people to perpetrate crimes for you?”

Sigsbee Waddington’s conscience was in such a feverish condition by now that this speech affected him as deeply as the explosion of a pound of dynamite would have done. His vivid imagination leaped immediately to the supposition that this girl who seemed so intimate with his private affairs was one of those Secret Service investigation agents who do so much to mar the comfort of the amateur in crime.

“I don’t know what you’re talking about,” he croaked.

===Chinese===

西格斯比·H·沃丁顿过了好一阵，才硬着头皮走过去开窗。诚然，世上不乏品流低下的已婚男子，一想到能跟一个隔着窗户朝自己比比划划的黑眼睛姑娘促膝密谈，便心花怒放；可西格斯比·沃丁顿不在此列。无论天性还是教养，他都谨慎周密到了极处。于是有那么一会儿，他只是站着，直勾勾地瞪着芬妮。直到那双眼睛里的意思急切得近乎催眠，他才终于肯去拨开门闩。

“也该开了。”芬妮带着愠恼说，脚步轻巧地进了屋。

“你想干什么？”

“我想跟你聊聊。外头传你到处请人替你干犯罪的勾当，这是怎么回事？”

西格斯比·沃丁顿的良心到这会儿正发着高烧，这话砸在他身上，不亚于一磅炸药轰然开花。他那活跃的想象力，立刻蹦到了一个假设上：这个对他的私事了如指掌的姑娘，是那些秘密侦缉探员中的一个——正是这班人马，专一搅扰业余罪犯的安宁。

“我不知道你在说什么。”他哑着嗓子咕哝道。

===Original===

“Oh, shucks!” said Fanny impatiently. She was a business girl and disliked this beating about the bush. “Freddy Mullett told me all about it. You want someone to do a job for you and he turned you down. Well, take a look at the understudy. I’m here, and, if the job’s in my line, lead me to it.”

Mr. Waddington continued to eye her warily. He had now decided that she was trying to trap him into a damaging admission. He said nothing, but breathed stertorously.

Fanny, a sensitive girl, misunderstood his silence. She interpreted the look in his eye to indicate distrust of the ability of a woman worker to deputise for the male.

“If it’s anything Freddy Mullett could do, I can do it,” she said. She seemed to Mr. Waddington to flicker for a moment. “See here!” she said.

Before Mr. Waddington’s fascinated gaze she held up between her delicate fingers a watch and chain.

“What’s that?” he gasped.

===Chinese===

“嗐，得了！”芬妮不耐烦地说。她是吃这行饭的姑娘，最烦人兜圈子。“弗雷迪·马利特全跟我说了。你要找人替你办件事，他没接下来。那好，请瞧瞧替补的吧。本人现在就到。只要活儿对路，你就把我领去。”

沃丁顿先生继续满腹戒心地打量她。他已经断定，她是想设一个套，诱他说出招灾惹祸的话来。他一声不吭，只是呼哧呼哧地喘着粗气。

芬妮是个敏感的姑娘，把这份沉默理解岔了。她将那眼神解读为：此人信不过女工能顶得了男工的班。

“凡是弗雷迪·马利特干得了的，我都干得了。”她说。在沃丁顿先生眼里，她的身影仿佛晃了一晃。“瞧好了！”她说。

在沃丁顿先生看呆了的眼皮底下，她用纤纤十指举起了一块带链子的怀表。

“这是什么？”他倒抽了一口冷气。

===Original===

“What does it look like?”

Mr. Waddington knew exactly what it looked like. He felt his waistcoat dazedly.

“I didn’t see you take it.”

“Nobody don’t ever see me take it,” said Fanny proudly, stating a profound truth. “Well, then, now you’ve witnessed the demonstration, perhaps you’ll believe me when I say that I’m not so worse. If Freddy can do it, I can do it.”

A cool, healing wave of relief poured over Sigsbee H. Waddington’s harassed soul. He perceived that he had wronged his visitor. She was not a detective, after all, but a sweet, womanly woman who went about lifting things out of people’s pockets so deftly that they never saw them go. Just the sort of girl he had been wanting to meet.

“I am sure you can,” he said fervently.

===Chinese===

“你看它像什么？”

沃丁顿先生一清二楚它像什么。他晕晕乎乎地摸了摸自己的马甲。

“我可没看见你下手。”

“从来就没有人看见过我下手。”芬妮自豪地说，道出了一条深刻的真理。“好啦，如今这手艺你也亲眼见识过了，我说我还不是那么糟劲儿，这回总该信了吧。弗雷迪干得了的，我也干得了。”

一股清凉的、带有疗愈之功的宽慰，漫过了西格斯比·H·沃丁顿备受煎熬的灵魂。他明白自己错怪了这位访客。她根本不是什么侦探，而是一位甜美可亲、一身女人味的女士，专在人家口袋里往外捧东西，手法轻巧得叫人浑然不觉。正是他一心想结识的那种姑娘。

“你当然干得了。”他热切地说。

===Original===

“Well, what’s the job?”

“I want someone to steal a pearl necklace.”

“Where is it?”

“In the strongroom at the bank.”

Fanny’s mobile features expressed disappointment and annoyance.

“Then what’s the use of talking about it? I’m not a safe-smasher. I’m a delicately nurtured girl that never used an oxyacetylene blowpipe in her life.”

===Chinese===

“那么，是什么活儿？”

“我要找个人去偷一条珍珠项链。”

“项链在哪儿？”

“在银行的保险库里。”

芬妮那张灵动多变的脸，摆出了失望加恼火。

“那还谈个什么劲儿？我又不是砸保险柜的。我可是娇生惯养的姑娘，这辈子连氧乙炔喷枪都没有摸过。”

===Original===

“Ah, but you don’t understand,” said Mr. Waddington hastily. “When I say that the necklace is in the strongroom, I mean that it is there just now. Eventually it will be taken out and placed among the other wedding-presents.”

“This begins to look more like it.”

“I can mention no names, of course.﻿ ﻿…”

“I don’t expect you to.”

“Then I will simply say that A, to whom the necklace belongs, is shortly about to be married to B.”

“I might have known it. Doing all those bridge problems together, they kind of got fond of one another.”

===Chinese===

“啊，可你没弄明白，”沃丁顿先生急忙说，“我说项链在保险库里，意思是它眼下在那儿。到那一天，它终归要取出来，同别的结婚礼物摆在一处。”

“这听着才像点儿话了。”

“当然，我不能指名道姓……”

“我也没指望你指。”

“那么我就这么直说吧：甲，项链的所有人，不久就要同乙成婚了。”

“我早该料到。天天凑在一块儿解那些桥牌题，这俩就这么好上了。”

===Original===

“I have my reasons for thinking that the wedding will take place down at Hempstead on Long Island, where C, A’s stepmother, has her summer home.”

“Why? Why not in New York?”

“Because,” said Mr. Waddington simply, “I expressed a wish that it should take place in New York.”

“What have you got to do with it?”

“I am D, C’s husband.”

“Oh, the fellow who could fill a tank with water in six hours fifteen minutes while C was filling another in five hours, forty-five? Pleased to meet you.”

===Chinese===

“我有我的理由相信，婚礼将在长岛的亨普斯特德（Hempstead）举行，甲的继母丙在那儿有一座避暑宅子。”

“为什么？干嘛不在纽约办？”

“因为，”沃丁顿先生朴实地说，“我表示过我的意愿，希望婚礼在纽约举行。”

“这跟你有什么相干？”

“我就是丁，丙的丈夫。”

“哦，就是那个能花六个钟头一刻钟灌满一池水、而丙灌另一池得花五个钟头三刻钟的家伙？幸会幸会。”

===Original===

“I am now strongly in favour of the Hempstead idea,” said Mr. Waddington. “In New York it might be difficult to introduce you into the house, whereas down at Hempstead you can remain concealed in the garden till the suitable moment arrives. Down at Hempstead the presents will be on view in the dining-room, which has French windows opening on to a lawn flanked with shrubberies.”

“Easy!”

“Just what I thought. I will, therefore, make a point tonight of insisting that the wedding take place in New York, and the thing will be definitely settled.”

Fanny eyed him reflectively.

“It all seems kind of funny to me. If you’re D and you’re married to C and C is A’s stepmother, you must be A’s father. What do you want to go stealing your daughter’s necklace for?”

“Say, listen,” said Mr. Waddington urgently, “the first thing you’ve got to get into your head is that you’re not to ask questions.”

===Chinese===

“我现在是死心塌地赞成亨普斯特德方案了。”沃丁顿先生说。“在纽约，要把你弄进宅子里去，只怕不容易；可到了亨普斯特德，你可以一直藏身在花园里，等到合宜的时辰来临。在亨普斯特德，礼物将陈列在餐厅里，餐厅开着落地窗，窗外正对一片草坪，两侧绿篱灌木，葱茏成行。”

“小菜一碟！”

“我也是这么盘算的。所以今晚我打定主意，一定要坚持婚礼在纽约办——这么一来，地方就板上钉钉，定在亨普斯特德了。”

芬妮若有所思地打量他。

“这里头怎么瞧怎么蹊跷。你是丁，娶的是丙，丙又是甲的继母，那你准是甲的父亲。你干嘛要偷你亲闺女的项链呀？”

“喂，听着，”沃丁顿先生急切地说，“头一件你得刻进脑子的事：不许提问题。”

===Original===

“Only my girlish curiosity.”

“Tie a can to it,” begged Mr. Waddington. “This is a delicate business, and the last thing I want is anybody snooping into motives and first causes. Just you go ahead, like a nice girl, and get that necklace and pass it over to me when nobody’s looking, and then put the whole matter out of your pretty little head and forget about it.”

“Just as you say. And now, coming down to it, what is there in it for me?”

“Three hundred dollars.”

“Not nearly enough.”

“It’s all I’ve got.”

===Chinese===

“不过是人家小女子的好奇心罢了。”

“趁早把这份好奇心撂下，”沃丁顿先生央告道。“这可是一桩精细的买卖，我最怕的就是有人刨根问底，追索动机来由。你就乖乖地照直办：把那条项链弄到手，趁没人瞧见递到我手上，然后把这桩事从你漂亮的小脑瓜里统统请出去，忘个一干二净。”

“你说了算。那么，现在谈正经的：这里头有我什么好处？”

“三百美元。”

“差得远呢。”

“我身上统共就这么多了。”

===Original===

Fanny meditated. Three hundred dollars, though a meagre sum, was three hundred dollars. You could always use three hundred dollars when you were furnishing, and the job, as outlined, seemed simple.

“All right,” she said.

“You’ll do it?”

“I’m on.”

“Good girl,” said Mr. Waddington. “Where can I find you when I want you?”

“Here’s my address.”

===Chinese===

芬妮盘算起来。三百美元固然寒碜，可三百美元终究是三百美元。置办家具的时候，三百美元到什么时候都顶用；而这活儿，照方才那番勾勒，瞧着也简单。

“成。”她说。

“你接了？”

“我入伙。”

“好姑娘。”沃丁顿先生说。“我要找你的时候，上哪儿找你？”

“这是我的地址。”

===Original===

“I’ll send you a line. You’ve got the thing clear?”

“Sure. I hang about in the bushes till there’s nobody around, and then I slip into the room and snitch the necklace.﻿ ﻿…”

“… and hand it over to me.”

“Sure.”

“I’ll be waiting in the garden just outside, and I’ll meet you the moment you come out. The very moment. Thus,” said Mr. Waddington with a quiet, meaning look at his young friend, “avoiding any rannygazoo.”

===Chinese===

“我会给你捎封短信。这事儿你心里有谱了？”

“有谱。我先在树丛里猫着，等四下没人，再溜进屋去把项链顺出来……”

“……然后交到我手上。”

“成。”

“我就在紧挨着的花园里候着，你一出来就碰头。一出就碰。这样，”沃丁顿先生不动声色地、意味深长地瞟了他的年轻朋友一眼，“就免掉了任何猫腻。”

===Original===

“What do you mean by rannygazoo?” said Fanny warmly.

“Nothing, nothing,” said Mr. Waddington with a deprecating wave of the hand. “Just rannygazoo.”

===Chinese===

“你管什么叫猫腻？”芬妮火辣辣地问。

“没什么，没什么。”沃丁顿先生不以为然地摆了摆手。“就是猫腻，随口一说。”

## VII / 第七章

===Original===
There are, as everybody knows, many ways of measuring time: and right through the ages learned men have argued heatedly in favour of their different systems. Hipparchus of Rhodes sneered every time anybody mentioned Marinus of Tyre to him: and the views of Ahmed Ibn Abdallah of Baghdad gave Purbach and Regiomontanus the laugh of their lives. Purbach in his bluff way said the man must be a perfect ass: and when Regiomontanus, whose motto was Live and let live, urged that Ahmed Ibn was just a young fellow trying to get along and ought not to be treated too harshly, Purbach said Was that so? and Regiomontanus said Yes, that was so, and Purbach said that Regiomontanus made him sick.
Tycho Brahe measured time by means of altitudes, quadrants, azimuths, cross-staves, armillary spheres and parallactic rules: and, as he often said to his wife when winding up the azimuth and putting the cat out for the night, nothing could be fairer than that. And then in 1863 along came Dollen with his *Die Zeitbestimmung vermittelst des tragbaren Durchgangsinstruments im Verticale des Polarsterns* (a bestseller in its day, subsequently filmed under the title *Purple Sins*), and proved that Tycho, by mistaking an armillary sphere for a quadrant one night after a bump-supper at Copenhagen University, had got his calculations all wrong.
The truth is that time cannot be measured. To George Finch, basking in the society of Molly Waddington, the next three weeks seemed but a flash. Whereas to Hamilton Beamish, with the girl he loved miles away in East Gilead, Idaho, it appeared incredible that any sensible person could suppose that a day contained only twenty-four hours. There were moments when Hamilton Beamish thought that something must have happened to the sidereal moon and that time was standing still.

===Chinese===
众所周知，计量时间的法子多得很：千百年来，饱学之士一直为各自的体系争得面红耳赤。罗得岛的喜帕恰斯（Hipparchus of Rhodes）每回听人提起推罗的马里努斯（Marinus of Tyre），必定嗤之以鼻；而巴格达的艾哈迈德·伊本·阿卜杜拉（Ahmed Ibn Abdallah of Baghdad）那一套见解，则让普尔巴赫（Purbach）和雷吉奥蒙塔努斯（Regiomontanus）笑掉了此生的大牙。普尔巴赫粗声粗气地说，这家伙准是个十足的大蠢驴；雷吉奥蒙塔努斯的座右铭是“自己活，也让别人活”，便劝解说，艾哈迈德·伊本不过是个想混出个人样的年轻人，不该对他太过苛责。普尔巴赫说“是吗？”雷吉奥蒙塔努斯说“就是这么回事”，普尔巴赫说雷吉奥蒙塔努斯真叫他恶心。

第谷·布拉赫（Tycho Brahe）计量时间，靠的是高度角、象限仪、方位角、直角器、浑天仪和视差尺；正如他夜里一边给方位角上发条、一边放猫出门时常对太太说的：没有比这更公道的了。然而到了 1863 年，多伦（Dollen）挟着他那部《借助便携式中星仪于北极星垂直圈内测定时间》（Die Zeitbestimmung vermittelst des tragbaren Durchgangsinstruments im Verticale des Polarsterns）——当年的一部畅销书，后来还搬上银幕，改名《紫色罪孽》（Purple Sins）——横空出世，证明第谷有一晚在哥本哈根大学赴过一场闹酒晚宴之后，错把浑天仪当成了象限仪，计算全都错了个底朝天。

其实，时间是没法计量的。对乔治·芬奇来说，有莫莉·沃丁顿相伴厮守，如沐春风，接下来的三个星期不过是电光石火的一闪。而对汉密尔顿·比米什来说，心上人远在爱达荷州东吉利亚德，相隔千山万水，随便哪个明白人竟会以为一天只装得下二十四个钟点，实在匪夷所思。有好几回，汉密尔顿·比米什甚至疑心是恒星月出了什么毛病，时间整个儿停摆了。

===Original===
But now the three weeks were up, and at any minute he might hear that she was back in the metropolis. All day long he had been going about with a happy smile on his face, and it was with a heart that leaped and sang from pure exuberance that he now turned to greet Officer Garroway, who had just presented himself at his apartment.
“Ah, Garroway!” said Hamilton Beamish. “How goes it? What brings you here?”
“I understood you to say, sir,” replied the policeman, “that I was to bring you my poem when I had completed it.”
“Of course, of course. I had forgotten all about it. Something seems to have happened to my memory these days. So you have written your first poem, eh? All about love and youth and springtime, I suppose?﻿ ﻿… Excuse me.”

===Chinese===
好在三个星期终于到了头，说不定哪一分钟他就会得到消息，说她已经回到了大都会。这一整天，他脸上都挂着欢喜的笑容四处走动；此刻他转过身去，迎接刚到寓所来访的加罗威警官，一颗心纯粹乐得欢蹦乱跳、放声歌唱。

“啊，加罗威！”汉密尔顿·比米什说。“近来怎么样？什么风把你吹来了？”

“先生，您上次是说，”警察答道，“等我把诗写完了，就带来给您看。”

“当然，当然。我忘得一干二净了。这些天我的记性像是出了点毛病。这么说，你的头一首诗写成了，嗯？写的是爱情、青春和春光吧，我猜？……失陪一下。”

===Original===
The telephone-bell had rung: and Hamilton Beamish, though the instrument had disappointed him over and over again in the past few days, leaped excitedly to snatch up the receiver.
“Hello?”
This time there was no disappointment. The voice that spoke was the voice he had heard so often in his dreams.
“Mr. Beamish. I mean, Jimmy?”
Hamilton Beamish drew a deep breath. And so overcome was he with sudden joy that for the first time since he had reached years of discretion he drew it through the mouth.

===Chinese===
电话铃响了：尽管过去这几天这部电话机一次次叫他失望，汉密尔顿·比米什还是欣喜若狂地一跃而起，一把抓起听筒。

“喂？”

这一回没有失望。开口的那个嗓音，正是他在梦里听过千百遍的声音。

“比米什先生。不对，我是说，吉米（Jimmy）？”

汉密尔顿·比米什深深吸了一口气。突如其来的喜悦叫他忘乎所以，打从懂事以来，这口气破天荒头一遭是用嘴吸的。

===Original===
“At last!” he cried.
“What did you say?”
“I said ‘At last!’ Since you went away every minute has seemed an hour.”
“So it has to me.”
“Do you mean that?” breathed Hamilton Beamish fervently.

===Chinese===
“终于盼到了！”他喊道。

“你说什么？”

“我说‘终于盼到了！’打你走后，每一分钟都像一个钟头那么长。”

“我那儿也一样。”

“真的吗？”汉密尔顿·比米什满腔热忱地低声问。

===Original===
“Yes. That’s the way minutes do seem in East Gilead.”
“Oh, ah, yes,” said Mr. Beamish, a little damped. “When did you get back?”
“A quarter of an hour ago.”
Hamilton Beamish’s spirits soared once more.

===Chinese===
“是啊。在东吉利亚德，一分钟看上去就是这么长。”

“哦，呃，是啊，”比米什先生说，兴致蔫了几分。“你什么时候回来的？”

“一刻钟以前。”

汉密尔顿·比米什的情绪重新扶摇直上。

===Original===
“And you called me up at once!” he said emotionally.
“Yes. I wanted to know Mrs. Waddington’s telephone number at Hempstead.”
“Was that the only reason?”
“Of course not. I wanted to hear how you were.﻿ ﻿…”
“Did you? Did you?”

===Chinese===
“你一到就马上给我打了电话！”他动情地说。

“是啊。我想打听沃丁顿夫人在亨普斯特德的电话号码。”

“就为这个？”

“当然不是。我还想问问你近来好不好。……”

“真的？真的想问？”

===Original===
“… and if you had missed me.”
“Missed you!”
“Did you?”
“Did I!”
“How sweet of you. I should have thought you would have forgotten my very existence.”
“Guk!” said Hamilton Beamish, completely overcome.

===Chinese===
“……还想知道你想没想我。”

“想没想你！”

“你想吗？”

“你说想不想！”

“你嘴真甜。我还当你会把人家忘得一干二净呢。”

“咕克！”汉密尔顿·比米什说，整个人彻底神魂颠倒。

===Original===
“Well, shall I tell you something? I missed you, too.”
Hamilton Beamish drew another completely unscientific deep breath, and was about to pour his whole soul into the instrument in a manner that would probably have fused the wire, when a breezy masculine voice suddenly smote his eardrum.
“Is that Ed?” inquired the voice.
“No,” thundered Hamilton Beamish.
“This is Charley, Ed. Is it all right for Friday?”

===Chinese===
“那么，要不要告诉你一件事？我也想你。”

汉密尔顿·比米什又深吸了一口全无科学依据的气，正要把整个灵魂一股脑儿倾进话筒——那股架势多半会把电话线烧熔——忽然一个朗爽的男声猛敲上他的耳膜。

“是埃德（Ed）吗？”那声音问。

“不是！”汉密尔顿·比米什轰然道。

“我是查利（Charley）呀，埃德。星期五那天行不行？”

===Original===
“It is not!” boomed Hamilton Beamish. “Get off the wire, you blot! Go away, curse you!”
“Certainly, if you want me to,” said a sweet, feminine voice. “But.﻿ ﻿…”
“I beg your pardon! I am sorry, sorry, sorry. A fiend in human shape got on the wire,” explained Mr. Beamish hastily.
“Oh! Well, what were we saying?”
“I was just going to.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“不行！”汉密尔顿·比米什瓮声瓮气地吼道。“把线路让出来，你这污点！走开，该死的！”

“好的，既然您叫我走，”一个甜润的女声说。“可是……”

“抱歉抱歉！对不起，对不起，对不起。是个披着人皮的恶魔抢占了线路，”比米什先生慌忙解释。

“哦！那么，咱们刚才说到哪儿了？”

“我刚要说……”

===Original===
“I remember. Mrs. Waddington’s telephone number. I was looking through my mail just now, and I found an invitation from Miss Waddington to her wedding. I see it’s tomorrow. Fancy that!”
Hamilton Beamish would have preferred to speak of other things than trivialities like George Finch’s wedding, but he found it difficult to change the subject.
“Yes. It is to take place at Hempstead tomorrow. George is staying down there at the inn.”
“It’s going to be a quiet country wedding, then?”
“Yes. I think Mrs. Waddington wants to hush George up as much as possible.”
“Poor George!”

===Chinese===
“我想起来了。沃丁顿夫人的电话号码。我方才正翻着邮件，翻出一封沃丁顿小姐发来的婚礼请柬。原来就在明天。你说巧不巧！”

汉密尔顿·比米什宁可谈点别的，也不愿谈乔治·芬奇的婚礼这类琐事，可他发觉这话题实在难以岔开。

“是啊。婚礼定在明天，在亨普斯特德举行。乔治这几天就住在那边的小旅馆里。”

“这么说，会是一场安安静静的乡村婚礼喽？”

“是啊。我看沃丁顿夫人恨不得把乔治捂得严严实实，越不声张越好。”

“可怜的乔治！”

===Original===
“I am going down by the one-thirty train. Couldn’t we travel together?”
“I am not sure that I shall be able to go. I have an awful lot of things to see to here, after being away so long. Shall we leave it open?”
“Very well,” said Hamilton Beamish resignedly. “But, in any case, can you dine with me tomorrow night?”
“I should love it.”
Hamilton Beamish’s eyes closed, and he snuffled for awhile.

===Chinese===
“我打算坐一点半的火车下去。咱们能不能结伴同行？”

“我说不准去不去得成。离家这么久，这儿有一大堆事等着我料理。这事先撂着不定，好不好？”

“好吧，”汉密尔顿·比米什无可奈何地说。“不过，无论如何，明儿晚上能来陪我吃饭吗？”

“太愿意了。”

汉密尔顿·比米什阖上了眼睛，抽了好一阵子鼻子。

===Original===
“And what is Mrs. Waddington’s number?”
“Hempstead 4076.”
“Thanks.”
“We’ll dine at the Purple Chicken, shall we?”
“Splendid.”

===Chinese===
“那么，沃丁顿夫人的电话号码是多少？”

“亨普斯特德 4076。”

“谢谢。”

“咱们去紫鸡餐厅吃饭，好不好？”

“好极了。”

===Original===
“You can always get it there, if they know you.”
“Do they know you?”
“Intimately.”
“Fine! Well, goodbye.”
Hamilton Beamish stood for a few moments in deep thought: then, turning away from the instrument, was astonished to perceive Officer Garroway.

===Chinese===
“在那儿总能弄到酒，只要人家认得你。”

“人家认得你吗？”

“熟得不能再熟。”

“太好了！那么，再见。”

汉密尔顿·比米什出神地站了片刻：随后从电话机旁转过身来，猛然吃惊地发现加罗威警官还在屋里。

===Original===
“I’d forgotten all about you,” he said. “Let me see, what did you say you had come for?”
“To read you my poem, sir.”
“Ah, yes, of course.”
The policeman coughed modestly.
“It is just a little thing, Mr. Beamish﻿—a sort of study, you might say, of the streets of New York as they appear to a policeman on his beat. I would like to read it to you, if you will permit me.”

===Chinese===
“我把你忘得干干净净了，”他说。“让我想想，你刚才说来着，你是来干什么的？”

“给您念我写的诗，先生。”

“啊，对，当然。”

警察谦虚地咳了一声。

“不过是件小东西，比米什先生——您不妨说，是一篇习作，写的是纽约街头在一个巡逻警察眼里的模样。您要是允许，我想念给您听听。”

===Original===
Officer Garroway shifted his Adam’s apple up and down once or twice: and, closing his eyes, began to recite in the special voice which he as a rule reserved for giving evidence before magistrates.
“Streets!”
“That is the title, eh?”
“Yes, sir. And also the first line.”
Hamilton Beamish started.

===Chinese===
加罗威警官把喉结上下颠动了一两回：然后闭上眼睛，开始朗诵，用的是他平日在地方法官面前作证时才动用的那种特别腔调。

“街道！”

“这就是题目，嗯？”

“是，先生。也是第一行。”

汉密尔顿·比米什吓了一跳。

===Original===
“Is it *vers libre*?”
“Sir?”
“Doesn’t it rhyme?”
“No, sir. I understood you to say that rhymes were an outworn convention.”
“Did I really say that?”

===Chinese===
“是自由诗吗？”

“您说什么，先生？”

“它不押韵？”

“不押，先生。您上次说，押韵是过时的老一套。”

“我真说过这话？”

===Original===
“You did, indeed, sir. And a great convenience I found it. It seems to make poetry quite easy.”
Hamilton Beamish looked at him perplexedly. He supposed he must have spoken the words which the other had quoted, and yet that he should deliberately have wished to exclude a fellow-creature from the pure joy of rhyming “heart” with “Cupid’s dart” seemed to him in his present uplifted state inconceivable.
“Odd!” he said. “Very odd. However, go on.”
Officer Garroway went once more through the motions of swallowing something large and sharp, and shut his eyes again.

===Chinese===
“您确实说过，先生。我发现这可帮了大忙。写诗好像一下子变得轻而易举。”

汉密尔顿·比米什大惑不解地望着他。想必自己确实说过对方引用的那番话；然而，说自己存心要剥夺同胞把“心”同“丘比特之箭”押上韵的那份纯然乐趣，在他眼下这般心花怒放的当口，实在无法想象。

“怪了！”他说。“怪得很。不过，念下去。”

加罗威警官又把吞咽什么又大又尖的东西的动作演了一遍，重新闭上了眼睛。

===Original===
> “Streets!
 

 Grim, relentless, sordid streets!
 

 Miles of poignant streets,
 

 East, West, North,
 

 And stretching starkly South;
 

 Sad, hopeless, dismal, cheerless, chilling
 

 Streets!”

===Chinese===
> “街道！
 

 阴郁、无情、污浊的街道！
 

 绵延数英里沉痛的街道，
 

 向东，向西，向北，
 

 又凄怆地伸向南方；
 

 悲哀、无望、凄凉、冷清、寒气袭人的
 

 街道！”

===Original===
Hamilton Beamish raised his eyebrows.

> “I pace the mournful streets
 

 With aching heart.”

===Chinese===
汉密尔顿·比米什扬起了眉毛。

> “我踏着哀伤的街道
 

 心里发疼。”

===Original===
“Why?” asked Hamilton Beamish.
“It is part of my duties, sir. Each patrolman is assigned a certain portion of the city as a beat.”
“I mean, why do you pace with aching heart?”
“Because it is bleeding, sir.”

===Chinese===
“为什么？”汉密尔顿·比米什问。

“这是我的职分所在，先生。每个巡警都由上面划给城里某一段地界，当作巡逻区。”

“我是说，你干吗心里发疼地踏来踏去？”

“因为它在流血，先生。”

===Original===
“Bleeding? You mean your heart?”
“Yes, sir. My heart is bleeding. I look at all the sordid gloom and sorrow and my heart bleeds.”
“Well, go on. It all seems very peculiar to me, but go on.”

===Chinese===
“流血？你是说你的心？”

“是，先生。我的心在流血。我看着满街污浊的暗淡与悲苦，我的心便在流血。”

“好吧，念下去。这一切在我看来古怪透顶，不过你念下去。”

===Original===
> “I watch grey men slink past
 

 With shifty, sidelong eyes
 

 That gleam with murderous hate;
 

 Lepers that prowl the streets.”

Hamilton Beamish seemed about to speak, but checked himself.

===Chinese===
> “我看灰色的行人闪身溜过
 

 一双闪烁游移、斜眼睨视的眼睛
 

 闪着杀机与仇恨的凶光；
 

 全是潜行街头的麻风病人。”

汉密尔顿·比米什像是正要开口，又把话咽了回去。

===Original===
> “Men who once were men,
 

 Women that once were women,
 

 Children like wizened apes,
 

 And dogs that snarl and snap and growl and hate.
 

 Streets!
 

 Loathsome, festering streets!
 

 I pace the scabrous streets
 

 And long for death.”

===Chinese===
> “昔日曾是男子汉的男人，
 

 昔日曾是女人的女人，
 

 干瘪如小老猴的孩童，
 

 还有龇牙、厮咬、咆哮、怀恨的恶犬。
 

 街道！
 

 可憎、溃烂的街道！
 

 我踏着满目疮痍的街道
 

 但求一死。”

===Original===
Officer Garroway stopped, and opened his eyes: and Hamilton Beamish, crossing the room to where he stood, slapped him briskly on the shoulder.
“I see it all,” he said. “What’s wrong with you is liver. Tell me, have you any local pain and tenderness?”
“No, sir.”
“High temperature accompanied by shiverings and occasional rigours?”
“No, sir.”

===Chinese===
加罗威警官停下来，睁开了眼睛：汉密尔顿·比米什穿过房间走到他跟前，利利索索地拍了一下他的肩膀。

“我全明白了，”他说。“你的毛病出在肝上。告诉我，你有没有什么局部疼痛和压痛？”

“没有，先生。”

“高热，伴有寒战和偶发的僵直阵发？”

“没有，先生。”

===Original===
“Then you have not a hepatic abscess. All that is the matter, I imagine, is a slight sluggishness in the oesophageal groove, which can be set right with calomel. My dear Garroway, it surely must be obvious to you that this poem of yours is all wrong. It is absurd for you to pretend that you do not see a number of pleasant and attractive people on your beat. The streets of New York are full of the most delightful persons. I have noticed them on all sides. The trouble is that you have been looking on them with a bilious eye.”
“But I thought you told me to be stark and poignant, Mr. Beamish.”
“Nothing of the kind. You must have misunderstood me. Starkness is quite out of place in poetry. A poem should be a thing of beauty and charm and sentiment, and have as its theme the sweetest and divinest of all human emotions﻿—Love. Only Love can inspire the genuine bard. Love, Garroway, is a fire that glows and enlarges, until it warms and beams upon multitudes, upon the universal heart of all, and so lights up the whole world and all Nature with its generous flames. Shakespeare speaks of the ecstasy of love, and Shakespeare knew what he was talking about. Ah, better to live in the lowliest cot, Garroway, than pine in a palace alone. In peace, Love tunes the shepherd’s reed: in war he mounts the warrior’s steed. In halls, in gay attire is seen; in hamlets, dances on the green. Love rules the court, the camp, the grove, and men below and saints above; for love is heaven and heaven is love. Get these simple facts into your silly fat head, Garroway, and you may turn out a poem worth reading. If, however, you are going to take this absurd attitude about festering streets and scabrous dogs and the rest of it, you are simply wasting your time and would be better employed writing subtitles for the motion-pictures.”

===Chinese===
“那么你就没有肝脓肿。依我看，毛病无非是食管沟略有迟滞，服点甘汞（calomel）便可疏通。我亲爱的加罗威，你这首诗通盘皆错，这一点明摆着，想必连你自己也看得出来。你佯装在自己的巡逻区上看不见一个个讨人喜欢、招人喜爱的人影，未免荒唐。纽约街头满是顶顶可亲可爱的人物，我四面八方都注意到了他们。毛病出在，你一直用一只苦胆发炎的眼睛在看他们。”

“可我记得您是叫我写得凄怆而沉痛来着，比米什先生。”

“从来没有的话。你一定是误会了我。凄怆这一套在诗里全然不合时宜。诗应当是美、魅力与深情的东西，主题该用人类一切情感当中最甜美、最神圣的那一种——爱情。唯有爱情能激发真正的诗人。爱，加罗威，是一团愈燃愈旺的火焰，直到它温暖、辉耀万千大众，辉耀众生共有的宇宙之心，用它慷慨的烈焰把整个世界、整个自然都照得通亮。莎士比亚写过爱的狂喜，莎士比亚可是清楚自己在说什么的。啊，加罗威，宁可住在最寒微的茅舍，也强似独守宫阙憔悴。太平年月，爱情调弄牧人的芦笛；烽火连天，爱情跨上战士的鞍马。华堂之上，它衣冠楚楚地现身；村野绿茵，它翩翩起舞。爱情主宰着宫廷、军营与林薮，下治世间凡人，上驭天上圣徒；因为爱即是天堂，天堂即是爱。把这些浅显的道理塞进你那颗又蠢又笨的脑袋，加罗威，你或许还能捣鼓出一首值得一读的诗。可你若是抱着这副荒唐架子不放，什么溃烂的街道、疮痍的恶犬，诸如此类，那你纯粹是在浪费时间，还不如去给电影写插卡字幕。”

===Original===
Officer Garroway was not a man of forceful character. He bowed his head meekly before the storm.
“I see what you mean, Mr. Beamish.”
“I should hope you did. I have put it plainly enough. I dislike intensely this modern tendency on the part of young writers to concentrate on corpses and sewers and despair. They should be writing about Love. I tell thee Love is nature’s second sun, Garroway, causing a spring of virtues where he shines. All love is sweet, given or returned. Common as light is love, and its familiar voice wearies not ever. True love’s the gift which God has given to man alone beneath the heaven. It is not﻿—mark this, Garroway﻿—it is not fantasy’s hot fire, whose wishes soon as granted die. It liveth not in fierce desire, with fierce desire it does not die. It is the secret sympathy, the silver link, the silken tie, which heart to heart and mind to mind in body and in soul can bind.”
“Yes, sir. Exactly, Mr. Beamish. I quite see that.”

===Chinese===
加罗威警官不是个性子刚硬的人。他在这场暴风雨面前乖乖地低下了头。

“我明白您的意思，比米什先生。”

“你明白就好。我已经说得够浅白不过了。如今年轻的作家动不动一头扎进死尸、阴沟和绝望里去，这种现代风气叫我深恶痛绝。他们该写的是爱情。我告诉你，加罗威，爱是自然的第二个太阳，它照临何处，何处便涌起美德的春泉。爱皆是甜的，无论施与还是回报。爱同光一样寻常，它那熟悉的声音永不叫人厌倦。真爱是上帝在天底下独独赐给人的礼物。它不是——记住这一点，加罗威——它不是幻想的烈火，愿望刚一得偿便告熄灭；它不活在炽情里，也不随炽情一同死去。它是暗中的契合，是银色的链环、丝质的纽带，能把心与心、灵与灵，在肉体与灵魂之中紧紧缚住。”

“是，先生。一点不错，比米什先生。我完全明白了。”

===Original===
“Then go away and rewrite your poem on the lines I have indicated.”
“Yes, Mr. Beamish.” The policeman paused. “Before I go, there is just one other thing.﻿ ﻿…”
“There is no other thing in the world that matters except love.”
“Well, sir, there are the motion-pictures, to which you made a brief allusion just now, and.﻿ ﻿…”
“Garroway,” said Hamilton Beamish, “I trust that you are not going to tell me that, after all I have done to try to make you a poet, you wish to sink to writing motion-picture scenarios?”

===Chinese===
“那就回去，照我指的路子，把你的诗重写一遍。”

“是，比米什先生。”警察顿了顿。“走之前，还有一件小事。……”

“这世上除了爱情，没有任何别的事情要紧。”

“这个嘛，先生，还有电影来着，您方才顺口提了一句，还有……”

“加罗威，”汉密尔顿·比米什说，“但愿你接下来不是要告诉我，说我费了这么大力气想把你造就成一位诗人，你倒想堕落到去写电影脚本？”

===Original===
“No, sir. No, indeed. But some little time ago I happened to purchase a block of stock in a picture company, and so far all my efforts to dispose of it have proved fruitless. I have begun to entertain misgivings as to the value of these shares, and I thought that, while I was here, I would ask you if you knew anything about them.”
“What is the company?”
“The Finer and Better Motion Picture Company of Hollywood, California, Mr. Beamish.”
“How many shares did you buy?”
“Fifty thousand dollars worth.”

===Chinese===
“不，先生。当然不。只是不久之前，我碰巧买下了一家电影公司的一批股票，到目前为止，我想把它脱手的种种努力全都付诸东流。这些股票到底值不值钱，我心里已经开始打鼓，想着趁我在这儿，不如问一问您，看您可知道它们什么底细。”

“哪家公司？”

“加利福尼亚州好莱坞的更精更美电影公司，比米什先生。”

“你买了多少股？”

“五万美元的。”

===Original===
“How much did you pay?”
“Three hundred dollars.”
“You were stung,” said Hamilton Beamish. “The stock is so much waste paper. Who sold it to you?”
“I have unfortunately forgotten his name. He was a man with a red face and grey hair. And if I’d got him here now,” said Officer Garroway with honest warmth, “I’d soak him so hard it would jolt his grandchildren. The smooth, salve-slinging crocodile!”

===Chinese===
“你付了多少钱？”

“三百美元。”

“你叫人敲了竹杠，”汉密尔顿·比米什说。“那些股票纯粹是一叠废纸。卖给你的是谁？”

“可惜他的名字我给忘了。是个红脸膛、灰头发的家伙。要是这会儿他能站在这儿，”加罗威警官满心赤诚地说，“我非把他饱揍一顿不可，揍得他孙儿辈都跟着打晃。这个油头滑脑、兜售膏药的鳄鱼！”

===Original===
“It is a curious thing,” said Hamilton Beamish musingly, “there seems to be floating at the back of my consciousness a sort of nebulous memory having to do with this very stock you mention. I seem to recall somebody at some time and place consulting me about it. No, it’s no good, it won’t come back. I have been much preoccupied of late, and things slip my mind. Well, run along, Garroway, and set about rewriting that poem of yours.”
The policeman’s brow was dark. There was a rebellious look in his usually mild eyes.
“Rewrite it nothing! It’s the goods.”
“Garroway!”
“I said New York was full of lepers, and so it is. Nasty, oily, lop-eared lepers that creep up to a fellow and sell him scabrous stock that’s not worth the paper it’s printed on. That poem is right, and I don’t alter a word of it. No, sir!”

===Chinese===
“说来也怪，”汉密尔顿·比米什沉吟道，“我意识的深处好像漂着一团雾蒙蒙的记忆，恰恰同你说的这只股票有牵扯。我仿佛记得，某年某月某地，有人为这事找过我。不成，不成了，想不起来了。近来我操心的事太多，好多事情都从我脑子里溜了号。好了，走吧走吧，加罗威，回去动手重写你那首诗。”

警察眉头一沉。一贯温和的眼睛里透出一股犯上的倔强。

“重写什么重写！这诗就是好货。”

“加罗威！”

“我说过纽约满街都是麻风病人，事实正是如此。下流、油腻、耷拉着耳朵的麻风病人，悄悄凑到人跟前，把一文不值、连印它的纸都不如的烂疮股票卖给你。那首诗没有说错，一个字我也不改。没错，先生！”

===Original===
Hamilton Beamish shook his head.
“One of these days, Garroway, love will awaken in your heart and you will change your views.”
“One of these days,” replied the policeman frigidly, “I shall meet that red-faced guy again, and I’ll change his face. It won’t be only my heart that’ll be aching by the time I’ve finished with him.”

===Chinese===
汉密尔顿·比米什摇了摇头。

“总有一天，加罗威，爱情会在你心里苏醒，到那时你就改观了。”

“总有一天，”警察冷冰冰地答道，“我会再撞上那个红脸膛的家伙，到那时我就来改一改他的脸。等我把那人收拾利索了，疼的可就不光是我的心口了。”

## VIII / 第八章

===Original===

George Finch’s wedding-day dawned fair and bright. The sun beamed down as if George by getting married were doing it a personal favour. The breezes, playing about him, brought with them a faint but well-defined scent of orange-blossom. And from the moment when they had finished the practical business of getting outside their early worm, all the birds for miles around had done nothing but stand in the trees singing Mendelssohn’s Wedding March. It was the sort of day to make a man throw out his chest and say “Tra-la!”: and George did so.

Delightful, he reflected, as he walked up from the inn after lunch, to think that in a few short hours he and Molly would be bowling away together in a magic train, each revolution of its wheels taking them nearer to the Islands of the Blest and﻿—what was almost more agreeable﻿—farther away from Mrs. Waddington.

It would be idle to deny that in the past three weeks George Finch had found his future mother-in-law something of a trial. Her consistent failure to hide the pain which the mere sight of him so obviously caused her was damping to an impressionable young man. George was not vain, and if Molly’s stepmother had been content to look at him simply as if she thought he was something the cat had dragged out of the ashcan, he could have borne up. But Mrs. Waddington went further. Her whole attitude betrayed her belief that the cat, on inspecting George, had been disappointed. Seeing what it had got, her manner suggested, it had given him the look of chagrin which cats give when conscious of effort wasted and had gone elsewhere to try again. A lover, counting the days until the only girl in the world shall be his, will see sweetness and light in practically everything: but George Finch, despite his most earnest endeavours, had been compelled to draw the line at Mrs. Waddington.

However, these little annoyances were, after all, the merest trifles: and the thought, as he approached the house, that inside it there sat a suffering woman who, thinking of him, mourned and would not be comforted, did nothing to diminish his mood of overflowing happiness. He entered the grounds, humming lightly: and, starting to pass up the drive, came upon Hamilton Beamish, smoking a thoughtful cigarette.

===Chinese===

乔治·芬奇（George Finch）的婚礼之日破晓时分，天朗气清，阳光明媚。太阳慷慨地泼洒着光辉，仿佛乔治成婚倒是对它个人的天大恩惠。微风绕着他嬉戏追逐，送来一缕若有若无却分明可辨的橙花香气。而方圆数英里之内的群鸟，自打把早起的虫子囫囵吞下、办完这桩正经事务之后，就再没干过别的，只是立在枝头，高唱门德尔松（Mendelssohn）的《婚礼进行曲》。这是那种叫男人挺起胸膛、朗声喊一声“特拉——啦！”的日子；乔治也果真喊了。

午饭后，他从旅馆一路步行走来，心中暗想：美妙啊，再过短短几个钟头，他和莫莉·沃丁顿（Molly Waddington）就要同乘一列神奇的列车飞驰而去了；车轮每转一圈，便把他们朝极乐福岛（the Islands of the Blest）载近一程，而——这一层几乎更为可心——离沃丁顿夫人（Mrs. Waddington）又远了一程。

无可否认，过去这三个星期里，乔治·芬奇发觉他的未来岳母实在有点叫人吃不消。单是瞧他一眼，她显然便痛楚不堪，而这份痛楚她一贯掩饰不住，这对一个感受敏锐的年轻人无异于兜头冷水。乔治并不虚荣，倘若莫莉的继母满足于只用一种眼神看他——仿佛看他是个从垃圾桶里被猫叼出来的玩意儿——他本还撑得住。可沃丁顿夫人走得更远。她浑身上下的做派都在泄露她的信念：那只猫把乔治叼来之后，验看之下，大失所望。她的神气分明在说，猫儿看清自己叼到的是什么货色，便露出了那种懊丧的表情——猫儿们自觉力气白白糟蹋时便是这副嘴脸——随即撇下他，到别处重试运气去了。一个数着日子、只等普天下独一无二的姑娘归属于自己的恋人，几乎能在一切事物里看见甜蜜与光明；可乔治·芬奇尽管掏心掏肺地努力，到头来也只得把界线划在沃丁顿夫人身上。

不过，这些小小的不痛快终究只是鸡毛蒜皮：当他走近宅邸时，一想到里面正坐着一位受苦的妇人，一想到他便哀恸不止、谁劝也不肯受安慰，这念头非但没有叫他的满心欢喜打上半点折扣。他哼着轻快的小曲走进宅园；刚顺着车道往上走，就撞见了汉密尔顿·比米什（Hamilton Beamish），正抽着一支若有所思的香烟。

===Original===

“Hullo,” said George. “So you’ve got here?”

“Correct,” said Hamilton Beamish.

“How do you think Molly is looking?”

“Charming. But I only caught a glimpse of her as she was hurrying off.”

===Chinese===

“哈罗，”乔治说，“原来你已经到这儿了？”

“不错，”汉密尔顿·比米什说。

“你看莫莉的模样怎么样？”

“俏丽动人。不过她匆匆往外赶的时候，我只瞥见了她一眼。”

===Original===

“Hurrying off?”

“Yes. There has been a slight hitch in the proceedings. Didn’t you know?”

“My God! Tell me!” said George, clutching his friend’s arm.

“Ouch!” said Hamilton Beamish, releasing the arm and rubbing it. “It is nothing to get excited about. All that has happened is that the clergyman who was to have married you has met with an accident. His wife telephoned just now to say that, while standing on a chair and trying to reach down a volume of devotional thought from an upper shelf, he fell and sprained his ankle!”

===Chinese===

“匆匆往外赶？”

“是啊。事情的进程出了点小岔子。你不知道吗？”

“我的天！快告诉我！”乔治说，一把攥住朋友的胳膊。

“哎哟！”汉密尔顿·比米什说，一边松开胳膊揉搓着。“这没什么好大惊小怪的。出的事不过是：原本要为你们主婚的那位牧师出了意外。他太太方才来电话说，他站在椅子上，想从高架取下一部灵修箴言之类的书，结果摔了下来，扭了脚脖子！”

===Original===

“The poor fish!” said George warmly. “What does he want to go doing that sort of thing for at a time like this? A man ought to decide once and for all at the outset of his career whether he is a clergyman or an acrobat and never deviate from his chosen path. This is awful news, Hamilton. I must rush about and try to find a substitute. Good heavens! An hour or so before the wedding, and no clergyman!”

“Calm yourself, George. The necessary steps are being taken. I think Mrs. Waddington would have been just as pleased to let the whole thing drop, but Molly became very active. She telephoned in all directions, and eventually succeeded in locating a disengaged minister in the neighbourhood of Flushing. She and Mrs. Waddington have gone off together in the car to fetch him. They will be back in about an hour and a half.”

“You mean to tell me,” demanded George, paling, “that I shall not see Molly for an hour and a half?”

===Chinese===

“可怜虫！”乔治满腔热忱地说。“都这种时候了，他干吗还要去耍那种把戏？一个人在事业发轫之初，就该一锤定音，拿定主意自己究竟要当牧师还是要当杂技演员，选定之后就绝不偏离自己选定的道路。这消息太可怕了，汉密尔顿。我得赶紧四处张罗，设法找一位替补。我的老天爷！离开婚礼只剩个把钟头，牧师却没了着落！”

“冷静点，乔治。必要的步骤已经在办了。我看，沃丁顿夫人巴不得整桩事情就此告吹，可莫莉却大显身手，行动起来雷厉风行。她四下里打电话，终于在法拉盛（Flushing）左近找到了一位得闲的牧师。她已经同沃丁顿夫人一道坐汽车接人去了。一个半钟头上下就能回来。”

“你的意思是说，”乔治脸色发白地质问道，“整整一个半钟头，我都见不着莫莉？”

===Original===

“Absence makes the heart grow fonder. I quote Thomas Haynes Bayly. And Frederick William Thomas, a poet of the early nineteenth century, amplifies this thought in the lines:

> ‘ ’Tis said that absence conquers love:
 

 But oh, believe it not:
 

 I’ve tried, alas, its power to prove,
 

 But thou art not forgot.’

Be a man, George. Clench your hands and try to endure.”

===Chinese===

“离别能使心更加眷恋。我引用的是托马斯·海恩斯·贝利（Thomas Haynes Bayly）。而十九世纪初期的诗人弗雷德里克·威廉·托马斯（Frederick William Thomas）又把这一层意思演绎成这样的诗行：

> 都说离别能征服爱情：
 

 哦，千万别信这一套：
 

 我尝过它的手段，唉，
 

 你却没有被忘掉。

拿出点男子汉气概来，乔治。攥紧拳头，竭力忍一忍。”

===Original===

“It’s sickening.”

“Be brave,” said Hamilton Beamish. “I know just how you feel. I, also, am going through the torment of being parted from the one woman.”

“Absolutely sickening! A clergyman, and not able to stand on a chair without falling off!” A sudden, gruesome thought struck him. “Hamilton! What’s it a sign of when the clergyman falls off a chair and sprains his ankle on the morning of the wedding?”

“How do you mean, what is it a sign of?”

===Chinese===

“真叫人窝火。”

“勇敢些，”汉密尔顿·比米什说，“你这份心情我全懂。我自己也在受着与天下唯一女子分离的煎熬。”

“简直窝火透顶！堂堂一位牧师，站上一把椅子都能摔下来！”突然，一个阴森森的念头攫住了他。“汉密尔顿！婚礼当天早上，牧师从椅子上摔下来扭了脚脖子，这算个什么兆头？”

“你这话什么意思，什么兆头？”

===Original===

“I mean, is it bad luck?”

“For the clergyman, undoubtedly.”

“You don’t think it means that anything is going to go wrong with the wedding?”

“I have never heard of any such superstition. You must endeavour to control these fancies, George. You are allowing yourself to get into a thoroughly overwrought condition.”

===Chinese===

“我是说，这是不是不吉利？”

“对牧师来说，毋庸置疑。”

“你不觉得这说明婚礼上要出什么岔子吗？”

“这样的迷信我闻所未闻。你务必设法约束这些胡思乱想，乔治。你这是任由自己陷入一种神经过度亢奋的状态。”

===Original===

“Well, what sort of a condition do you expect a fellow to be in on his wedding-morning, with clergymen falling off chairs wherever he looks?”

Hamilton smiled tolerantly.

“I suppose nerves are inevitable on such an occasion. I notice that even Sigsbee H., who can scarcely consider himself a principal in this affair, is thoroughly jumpy. He was walking on the lawn some little time ago, and when I came up behind him and laid a hand on his shoulder, he leaped like a startled roe. If Sigsbee H. Waddington possessed a mind, I would say that there was something on it. No doubt he is brooding on the West again.”

===Chinese===

“哼，成婚那天一大早，眼睛往哪儿看，都是牧师从椅子上往下栽，你倒指望一个小伙子该处于什么样的状态？”

汉密尔顿宽宏大量地微微一笑。

“我想这种场合神经紧张在所难免。我注意到，连西格斯比·H（Sigsbee H.）都惶惶不可终日——虽说这场喜事里他怎么也算不上主角。方才不久，他正在草坪上踱步，我从他背后走近，往他肩头一搭手，他竟像受惊的狍子似的蹦了起来。倘若西格斯比·H·沃丁顿（Sigsbee H. Waddington）有颗头脑的话，我要说那上面必定压着心事。不用说，他八成又在暗自惦念西部了。”

===Original===

The sun was still shining brightly, but somehow the day seemed to George to have grown overcast and chill. A grey foreboding had come upon him.

“I wish this hadn’t happened.”

“Exactly what the clergyman said.”

“It isn’t fair that a delicate, highly-strung girl like Molly should be upset like this at such a time.”

===Chinese===

太阳依旧明晃晃地照着，可不知怎么，这一天在乔治眼里竟变得阴晦而寒凉。一团灰色的不祥之感悄悄爬上了他的心头。

“但愿这事没发生过。”

“牧师说的正是这句话。”

“像莫莉这样娇嫩、神经脆弱的姑娘，偏在这种节骨眼上受这般惊扰，太不公道了。”

===Original===

“I think you exaggerate the effects of the occurrence on Molly. She seemed to me to be bearing it with equanimity.”

“She wasn’t pale?”

“Not in the least.”

“Or agitated?”

===Chinese===

“我看你把这事对莫莉的影响夸大了。在我眼里，她处之泰然。”

“她脸色没发白？”

“丝毫没有。”

“也没心神不宁？”

===Original===

“She seemed quite her normal self.”

“Thank God!” said George.

“In fact, the last thing she said to Ferris, as the car drove away, was.﻿ ﻿…”

“What?”

===Chinese===

“她看上去同平日一模一样。”

“谢天谢地！”乔治说。

“事实上，汽车开走的时候，她对费里斯（Ferris）说的最后一句话是……”

“说什么？”

===Original===

Hamilton Beamish had broken off. He was frowning.

“My memory is terrible. It is the effect, of course, of love. I have just remembered.﻿ ﻿…”

“What did Molly say?”

“I have forgotten. But I have just remembered what it was that I was told to tell you as soon as you arrived. It is curious how often the mention of a name will, as it were, strike a chord. I spoke of Ferris, and it has just come back to me that Ferris gave me a message for you.”

===Chinese===

汉密尔顿·比米什话说到一半，戛然而止。他皱起了眉头。

“我的记性糟透了。这自然是恋爱的缘故。我方才想起来了……”

“莫莉说什么来着？”

“忘了。不过我方才想起来，人家叮嘱你一到就转告你的那句话是什么了。说来也怪，提起一个名字，往往就像拨响一根琴弦。我方才提到费里斯，这就想起来了：费里斯托我给你捎个口信。”

===Original===

“Oh, darn Ferris!”

“He asked me, when I saw you, to say that a female of some kind was calling you up on the telephone earlier in the morning. He told her that you were at the inn, and advised her to get you there, but she said it didn’t matter, as she was coming down here immediately. She said she had known you in East Gilead.”

“Oh?” said George, indifferently.

“And her name, if I remember rightly, was Dubbs or Tubbs or Jubbs or﻿—no, I have it. My memory is better than I supposed. It was May Stubbs. Does it convey anything to you?”

===Chinese===

“哦，去他的费里斯！”

“他嘱咐我，见到你就说，一大早有个不知是哪路的女人打电话找过你。他告诉她你在旅馆，劝她上那儿去找你，可她说不要紧，她马上就到这儿来。她说她在东吉利亚德就认识你。”

“哦？”乔治淡淡地应了一声。

“至于她的名字，要是我没记错，叫什么达布斯（Dubbs）、塔布斯（Tubbs）、贾布斯（Jubbs）——不对，我想起来了。我的记性比我自己估量的要强。是梅·斯塔布斯（May Stubbs）。这个名字，你可有印象？”

## IX / 第九章

===Original===
It chanced that as he spoke these light and casual words Hamilton Beamish, glancing down, noted that his shoelace had come untied. Stooping to attend to this he missed seeing George’s face. Nor﻿—for he was a man who concentrated even on the lightest task the full attention of a great mind﻿—did he hear the other’s sudden, whistling gasp of astonishment and horror. A moment later, however, he observed out of the corner of his eye something moving: and, looking, perceived that George’s legs were wobbling strangely.

Hamilton Beamish straightened himself. He was now in a position to see George steadily and see him whole: and the spectacle convinced him at once that something in the message he had just delivered must have got right in among his friend’s ganglions. George Finch’s agreeable features seemed to be picked out in a delicate Nile-green. His eyes were staring. His lower jaw had fallen. Nobody who had ever seen a motion-picture could have had the least doubt as to what he was registering. It was dismay.

“My dear George!” said Hamilton Beamish, concerned.

===Chinese===
说来也巧，正当他说着这些轻描淡写的闲话时，汉密尔顿·比米什低头一瞧，发现自己的鞋带开了。他弯腰去系鞋带，错过了看见乔治脸色的机会。也正因为如此——他是一个哪怕对最不足道的小事也要投入伟大头脑全部注意力的人——他没有听见对方猝然发出的那一声惊骇的、吹哨似的抽气。不过片刻之后，他从眼角瞥见有什么东西在动；定睛一看，原来乔治的两条腿正在莫名其妙地打晃。

汉密尔顿·比米什直起身来。这下他能把乔治端详个仔细、看个周全了：眼前的景象立刻叫他确信，他方才捎来的那个消息里，必有某种东西一杆子捅进了朋友的神经节丛里。乔治·芬奇那张讨人喜欢的面孔，仿佛被人用一层淡淡的尼罗河绿细细勾勒过。他两眼直勾勾的。下巴也耷拉下来了。但凡看过一场电影的人，对他此刻表演的是什么，断不会有一丝一毫的怀疑。那是惊惶失措。

“我亲爱的乔治！”汉密尔顿·比米什关切地说。

===Original===
“Wok.﻿ ﻿… Wuk.﻿ ﻿… Wok.﻿ ﻿…” George swallowed desperately. “Wok name did you say?”

“May Stubbs.” Hamilton Beamish’s expression grew graver, and he looked at his friend with sudden suspicion. “Tell me all, George. It is idle to pretend that the name is strange to you. Obviously it has awakened deep and unpleasant memories. I trust, George, that this is not some poor girl with whose happiness you have toyed in the past, some broken blossom that you have culled and left to perish by the wayside?”

George Finch was staring before him in a sort of stupor.

===Chinese===
“袜特。……乌克。……袜特……”乔治拼命咽了口唾沫。“袜特名字？你说叫什么名字？”

“梅·斯塔布斯。”汉密尔顿·比米什的神色愈发凝重，他带着突然涌起的疑心望向朋友。“把一切都告诉我，乔治。硬说这名字对你是全然陌生的，那是枉然。显然，它唤醒了你心底深处不愉快的记忆。乔治，但愿她不是你从前玩弄过其幸福的哪位可怜姑娘，不是你随手摘下、又撇在路边任其凋零的哪朵残花吧？”

乔治·芬奇直勾勾地瞪着前方，恍恍惚惚。

===Original===
“All is over!” he said dully.

Hamilton Beamish softened.

“Confide in me. We are friends. I will not judge you harshly, George.”

===Chinese===
“一切都完了！”他木然道。

汉密尔顿·比米什的语气缓和下来。

“向我袒露吧。我们是朋友。我不会苛刻地评判你，乔治。”

===Original===
A sudden fury melted the ice of George’s torpor.

“It’s all that parson’s fault!” he cried vehemently. “I knew all along it meant bad luck. Gosh, what a Paradise this world would be if only clergymen could stand on chairs without spraining their ankles! I’m done for.”

“Who is this May Stubbs?”

===Chinese===
一股陡然而起的怒火，融化了乔治麻木的坚冰。

“这全得怪那个牧师！”他激愤地嚷道。“我打头就知道这事儿要出霉头。天哪，要是牧师们站上椅子都闪不着脚脖子，这世界该是一座多么美好的乐园啊！我这下全完了。”

“这个梅·斯塔布斯究竟是什么人？”

===Original===
“I knew her in East Gilead,” said George hopelessly. “We were sort of engaged.”

Hamilton Beamish pursed his lips.

“Apart from the slovenly English of the phrase, which is perhaps excusable in the circumstances, I cannot see how you can have been ‘sort of’ engaged. A man is either engaged or he is not.”

===Chinese===
“我是在东吉利亚德认识她的，”乔治绝望地说。“我们差不多算是订过婚。”

汉密尔顿·比米什抿了抿嘴唇。

“撇开这句话里马虎草率的英语不谈——就眼下这情形，或许情有可原——我不明白你怎么可能‘差不多’订过婚。一个男人，要么订了婚，要么没订。”

===Original===
“Not where I come from. In East Gilead they have what they call understandings.”

“And there was an understanding between you and this Miss Stubbs?”

“Yes. Just one of those boy and girl affairs. You know. You see a girl home once or twice from church, and you take her to one or two picnics, and people kid you about her, and﻿ ﻿… well, there you are. I suppose she thought we were engaged. And now she’s read in the papers about my wedding, and has come to make herself unpleasant.”

“Did you and this girl quarrel before you separated?”

===Chinese===
“在我老家那儿可不是这样。在东吉利亚德，人们有一种叫做‘默契’的东西。”

“那么，你跟这位斯塔布斯小姐之间，就有过这么一份默契？”

“是啊。就是少男少女之间那种事儿。你懂的。你从教堂送姑娘回家一两回，再带她去一两次野餐，人们就拿她来打趣你，然后……瞧，就这么着了。我想她认定我们订了婚。如今她在报上读到了我结婚的消息，就跑来找不痛快来了。”

“分手以前，你跟这姑娘吵过架吗？”

===Original===
“No. We sort of drifted apart. I took it for granted that the thing was over and done with. And when I saw Molly.﻿ ﻿…”

Hamilton Beamish laid a hand upon his arm.

“George,” he said, “I want you to give me your full attention: for we have arrived now at the very core of the matter. Were there any letters?”

“Dozens. And of course she has kept them. She used to sleep with them under her pillow.”

===Chinese===
“没有。我们不知不觉就疏远了。我以为这事自然而然就算了结了。后来我遇见了莫莉……”

汉密尔顿·比米什把手搭上他的手臂。

“乔治，”他说，“我要你全神贯注地听着：因为我们现在已经触及整个事情的核心了。你们通过信吗？”

“几十封。她当然全都留着。她从前睡觉都把它们枕在枕头底下。”

===Original===
“Bad!” said Hamilton Beamish, shaking his head. “Very bad!”

“And I remember her saying once that she believed in breach of promise suits.”

Hamilton Beamish frowned. He seemed to be deploring the get-rich-quick spirit of the modern girl, who is not content to sit down and wait for her alimony.

“You think it certain that she is coming here with the intention of making trouble?”

===Chinese===
“糟了！”汉密尔顿·比米什摇头道。“糟透了！”

“我还记得她有一回说过，她信奉毁约之诉那一套。”

汉密尔顿·比米什皱起了眉头。他似乎是在痛惜现代姑娘们那种急于发财的作风——她们可不满足于安安稳稳坐着等赡养费。

“你认定她上这儿来就是存心捣乱？”

===Original===
“What other reason could she have?”

“Yes, I fancy you are right. I must think. I must think. Let me think.”

And, so saying, Hamilton Beamish turned sharply to the left and began to walk slowly round in a circle, his hands behind his back and his face bent and thoughtful. His eyes searched the ground as if to wrest inspiration from it.

Few sights in this world are more inspiring than that of a great thinker actually engaged in thought: and yet George Finch, watching his friend, chafed. He had a perhaps forgivable craving for quick results: and Hamilton Beamish, though impressive, did not seem to be getting anywhere.

===Chinese===
“她还能有什么别的原因？”

“不错，我想你是对的。我得思考。我得思考。让我思考。”

说着，汉密尔顿·比米什猛地向左一转，开始缓缓地兜着圈子踱步，双手背在身后，脑袋低垂，一脸沉思。他的目光在地上逡巡，仿佛要从地里硬生生拧出灵感来。

世上很少有什么景象，比一位大思想家实实在在地进行思考更令人鼓舞了；然而乔治·芬奇眼巴巴望着朋友，心里焦躁难耐。他巴望立竿见影，这份急切或许情有可原：可汉密尔顿·比米什虽然气势慑人，看上去却毫无进展。

===Original===
“Have you thought of anything?” he asked, as the other came round for the third time.

Hamilton Beamish held up a hand in silent reproof, and resumed his pacing. Presently he stopped.

“Yes?” said George.

“With regard to this engagement.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“你琢磨出什么没有？”他问道。此时对方已经第三次兜回来了。

汉密尔顿·比米什举起一只手，无声地表示申斥，接着又踱开了步子。过了一会儿，他停住了。

“怎么？”乔治说。

“关于这桩婚约……”

===Original===
“It wasn’t an engagement. It was an understanding.”

“With regard to this understanding or engagement, the weak spot in your line of defence is undoubtedly the fact that it was you who broke it off.”

“But I didn’t break it off.”

“I used the wrong expression. I should have said that it was you who took the initiative. You left East Gilead and came to New York. Therefore, technically, you deserted this girl.”

===Chinese===
“那不是婚约。那是默契。”

“关于这份默契也好婚约也好，你防线上最薄弱的一点，毫无疑问，就是拆伙的人是你。”

“可我并没有拆伙呀。”

“我用词失当。我本该说，采取主动的人是你。你离开东吉利亚德，来到了纽约。因此，从法律角度讲，是你抛弃了这位姑娘。”

===Original===
“I wish you wouldn’t say things like that. Can’t you understand that it was just one of those boy and girl affairs which come to an end of themselves?”

“I was looking at the thing from a lawyer’s viewpoint. And may I point out that the affair appears not to have come to an end. What I am trying to make clear is this: that, if you had wished it to come to an end, you should, before you left East Gilead, have arranged somehow that this Miss Stubbs broke off the engagement.”

“Understanding.”

“The engagement or understanding. That would have cleaned the slate. You should have done something that would have made her disgusted with you.”

===Chinese===
“求你别这么说行不行。你难道就不明白，那不过是少男少女之间自生自灭的一段情吗？”

“我这是从律师的角度看问题。恕我指出，这段情看起来并没有寿终正寝。我想讲明白的就是这一点：倘若你当时指望它了结，你就该在离开东吉利亚德之前设法安排，叫这位斯塔布斯小姐来退掉这桩婚约。”

“默契。”

“婚约也好，默契也好。那样账就一笔勾销了。你本该做点什么，叫她对你厌恶透顶。”

===Original===
“How could I? I’m not the sort of fellow who can do things like that.”

“Even now, it seems to me, if you could do something that would revolt this Miss Stubbs﻿ ﻿… make her recoil from you with loathing.﻿ ﻿…”

“Well, what?”

“I must think,” said Hamilton Beamish.

===Chinese===
“我哪做得出来？我不是那种干得出这种事的人。”

“依我看，就算到了眼下这步田地，只要你还能做点什么叫这位斯塔布斯小姐恶心……叫她一见到你就憎恶得直往后缩……”

“说呀，做什么？”

“我得思考。”汉密尔顿·比米什说。

===Original===
He did four more laps.

“Suppose you had committed some sort of crime?” he said, returning to the fixed point. “Suppose she were to find out you were a thief? She wouldn’t want to marry you if you were on your way to Sing-Sing.”

“No. And neither would Molly.”

“True. I must think again.”

===Chinese===
他又兜了四圈。

“假如你犯过这样那样的罪行呢？”他说，又绕回了那个固定的思路上。“假如她发觉你是个贼呢？要是你眼看就要进辛辛监狱，她可不会想嫁给你。”

“是不会。可莫莉也不会了。”

“有理。我得再思考。”

===Original===
It was some moments later that George, eyeing his friend with the growing dislike which those of superior brainpower engender in us when they fail to deliver the goods in our times of crisis, observed him give a sudden start.

“I think I have it,” said Hamilton Beamish.

“Well?”

“This Miss Stubbs. Tell me, is she straitlaced? Prudish? Most of those village girls are.”

===Chinese===
过了片刻，乔治眼巴巴盯着朋友，心里的反感愈来愈浓——大难当头之际，那些智力超群之辈偏又交不出货色来，这种反感便会在我们心里油然而生——蓦地，他瞅见朋友浑身一震。

“我想我有主意了。”汉密尔顿·比米什说。

“怎么讲？”

“这位斯塔布斯小姐。告诉我，她规矩吗？假正经吗？那些乡下姑娘多半都这样。”

===Original===
George reflected.

“I don’t remember ever having noticed. I never did anything to make her prudish about.”

“I think we may assume that, having lived all her life in a spot like East Gilead, she is. The solution of this difficulty, then, is obviously to lead her to suppose that you have become a reprobate.”

“A what?”

===Chinese===
乔治回想了一下。

“我不记得曾经留意过。我从没干过什么能叫她假正经的事。”

“我想我们可以认定：一个在东吉利亚德那种地方过了一辈子的人，必定如此。那么，解决这个难题的办法，显然就是叫她相信，你已经堕落成了一个浪荡子。”

“一个什么？”

===Original===
“A Don Juan. A Lothario. A libertine. It should be perfectly easy. She has seen motion-pictures of life in New York, and will not be hard to convince that you have deteriorated since you came to live there. Our plan of action now becomes straightforward and simple. All we have to do is to get some girl to come along and say that you have no right to marry anybody but her.”

“What!”

“I can see the scene now. This Miss Stubbs is sitting beside you, a dowdy figure in her homemade village gown. You are talking of the old days. You are stroking her hand. Suddenly you look up and start. The door has opened and a girl, all in black with a white face, is entering. Her eyes are haggard, her hair disordered. In her arms she clasps a little bundle.”

“No, no! Not that!”

===Chinese===
“一个唐·璜（Don Juan）。一个洛泰里奥（Lothario）。一个登徒子。这再容易不过了。她看过描绘纽约生活的电影，要叫她相信你打从搬去那儿住就日渐堕落下去，绝非难事。我们的行动方针如今变得直截了当、简单明了。我们要做的，只是找一位姑娘来，说你除了娶她之外，无权娶任何人。”

“什么！”

“这幕戏如今在我眼前活灵活现。这位斯塔布斯小姐坐在你身旁，一副土里土气的模样，穿着自家缝的乡下裙衫。你们叙着旧。你抚弄着她的手。忽然你抬头一望，怔住了。门开了，一个黑衣白脸的姑娘正走进来。她双目憔悴，鬓发散乱。怀里还紧紧搂着一个小包袱。”

“不，不！千万别来这一手！”

===Original===
“Very well, we will dispense with the bundle. She stretches out her arms to you. She totters. You rush to support her. The scene is similar to one in Haddon Chambers’ ‘Passersby.’ ”

“What happened in that?”

“What could happen? The fiancée saw the ruined girl had the greater claim, so she joined their hands together and crept silently from the room.”

George laughed mirthlessly.

===Chinese===
“很好，包袱就免了。她朝你伸出双臂。她踉跄了一下。你抢步上前扶住她。这场戏同哈登·钱伯斯（Haddon Chambers）那出《过路人》（Passersby）里的一幕如出一辙。”

“那出戏里后来怎么样了？”

“还能怎么样？那位未婚妻见这位被毁掉的姑娘更有权利，便把他两个的手合在一起，悄悄从屋里退了出去。”

乔治惨然一笑，毫无欢意。

===Original===
“There’s just one thing you’re overlooking. Where are we going to get the white-faced girl?”

Hamilton Beamish stroked his chin.

“There is that difficulty. I must think.”

“And while you’re thinking,” said George coldly, “I’ll do the only practical thing there is to be done, and go down to the station and meet her, and have a talk with her and try to get her to be sensible.”

===Chinese===
“只有一样你漏掉了。咱们上哪儿找那个白脸姑娘去？”

汉密尔顿·比米什摩挲着下巴。

“这确是一重难题。我得思考。”

“趁你思考的工夫，”乔治冷冷地说，“我要去干唯一一件切实可行的事：下去到车站接她，同她谈一谈，设法劝她明白事理。”

===Original===
“Perhaps that would be as well. But I still feel that my scheme would be the ideal one, if only we could find the girl. It is too bad that you have not a dark past.”

“My dark past,” said George bitterly, “is all ahead of me.”

He turned and hurried down the drive. Hamilton Beamish, still meditating, made his way towards the house.

He had reached the lawn, when, as he stopped to light a cigarette to assist thought, he saw a sight that made him drop the match and draw back into the shelter of a tree.

Hamilton Beamish stopped, looked and listened. A girl had emerged from a clump of rhododendrons, and was stealing softly round the lawn towards the dining-room window.

===Chinese===
“那样也好。不过我仍旧觉得，只要能找到那位姑娘，我的方案才是理想方案。可惜你还没有一段见不得人的过去。”

“我那段见不得人的过去，”乔治辛酸地说，“还全在我前头呢。”

他转过身，匆匆顺着车道去了。汉密尔顿·比米什仍在沉思，朝宅子踱去。

他刚走到草坪边上，正停下来点一支烟帮着思考，猛可里瞧见一幅景象，唬得他火柴脱手，急忙缩到一棵树的掩蔽后面。

汉密尔顿·比米什止步、张望、侧耳倾听。一个姑娘已经从一丛杜鹃花后头钻了出来，正蹑手蹑脚绕过草坪，朝餐厅的窗户潜行过去。

---
===Original===
Girlhood is the season of dreams. To Fanny Welch, musing over the position of affairs after Sigsbee H. Waddington had given her her final instructions, there had come a quaint, fantastic thought, creeping into her mind like a bee into a flower﻿—the thought that if she got to the house an hour earlier than the time he had mentioned, it might be possible for her to steal the necklace and keep it for herself.

The flaw in the scheme, as originally outlined, had seemed to her all along to lie in the fact that Mr. Waddington was to preside over the enterprise and take the loot from her the moment she had got it. The revised plan appeared immeasurably more attractive, and she proceeded to put it into action.

Luck seemed to be with her. Nobody was about, the window was ajar, and there on the table lay that which she had now come to look on in the light of a present for a good girl. She crept out of her hiding-place, stole round the edge of the lawn, entered the room, and had just grasped the case in her hand, when it was borne sharply in upon her that luck was not with her so much as she had supposed. A heavy hand was placed upon her shoulder: and, twisting round, she perceived a majestic-looking man with a square chin and horn-rimmed spectacles.

===Chinese===
少女时代是做梦的季节。芬妮·韦尔奇在西格斯比·H·沃丁顿向她交代完最后的指示之后，一边揣度着眼下的局面，一边有个古怪离奇的念头悄悄爬进心里，恰似一只蜜蜂钻进花心——那念头便是：倘若她比他交代的钟点提前一个钟头摸到宅子，说不定就能把项链偷到手，留给自己。

照原先勾画的那套方案，毛病在她看来始终在于这一点：沃丁顿先生要亲自督办这项买卖，赃物一到她手，就得交到他的手里。修订过的计划显得吸引力大得多，她便动手付诸实行了。

运气似乎站在她这一边。四下无人，窗户虚掩，桌上端端正正摆着那件她如今已经看作是发给乖姑娘的礼物的物件。她从藏身之处爬出来，沿着草坪边缘悄悄溜过去，进了屋，手刚把那只盒子抓到手里，便猛然省悟：运气并不像她自以为的那样向着她。一只沉甸甸的手按上了她的肩膀；她扭头一看，只见一位仪表威严的男人，方方的下巴，一副角质架眼镜。

===Original===
“Well, young lady!” said this person.

Fanny breathed hard. These little contretemps are the risk of the profession, but that makes them none the easier to bear philosophically.

“Put down that jewel-case.”

===Chinese===
“好啊，小姑娘！”这位人物开口道。

芬妮喘着粗气。这类小小的不测风云，本是干这一行躲不掉的风险；可这并不能叫人把心放宽，泰然受之。

“把那只珠宝盒放下。”

===Original===
Fanny did so. There was a pause. Hamilton Beamish moved to the window, blocking it up.

“Well?” said Fanny.

Hamilton Beamish adjusted his spectacles.

===Chinese===
芬妮照办了。一阵沉默。汉密尔顿·比米什挪到窗前，把窗口堵了个严实。

“怎么样？”芬妮说。

汉密尔顿·比米什扶了扶眼镜。

===Original===
“Well, you’ve got me. What are you going to do?”

“What do you expect me to do?”

“Turn me over to the police?”

===Chinese===
“行啊，你把我逮住了。你打算拿我怎么办？”

“你指望我怎么办？”

“把我扭送去警察局？”

===Original===
The figure in the window nodded curtly. Fanny clasped her hands together. Her eyes filled with tears.

“Don’t turn me over to the bulls, mister! I only did it for ma’s sake.﻿ ﻿…”

“All wrong!”

===Chinese===
窗前那条身影生硬地点了点头。芬妮两手紧紧攥在一起，眼里噙满了泪水。

“别把我交到条子手里，大爷！我干这个全是为了我家老娘。……”

“全不对！”

===Original===
“If you was out of work and starvin’ and you had to sit and watch your poor old ma bendin’ over the washtub.﻿ ﻿…”

“All wrong!” repeated Hamilton Beamish forcefully.

“What do you mean, all wrong?”

===Chinese===
“要是你失了业、饿着肚皮，只能干坐着，眼看你可怜的老娘弯腰曲背地浆洗衣裳。……”

“全不对！”汉密尔顿·比米什斩钉截铁地又说了一遍。

“你说‘全不对’，是什么意思？”

===Original===
“Mere crude Broadway melodrama. That stuff might deceive some people, but not me.”

Fanny shrugged her shoulders.

“Well, I thought it was worth trying,” she said.

Hamilton Beamish was regarding her keenly. That busy brain was never still, and now it had begun to work with even more than its normal intensity.

===Chinese===
“不过是百老汇情节剧里的粗劣老套。这种玩意儿兴许骗得了别人，骗不了我。”

芬妮耸了耸肩膀。

“哼，我寻思总值得一试嘛。”她说。

汉密尔顿·比米什目光锐利地打量着她。那颗一刻不闲的脑袋从来不肯安分，此刻更以超出平常的强度运转起来。

===Original===
“Are you an actress?”

“Me? I should say not. My folks are awful particular.”

“Well, you have considerable dramatic ability. There was a ring of sincerity in that drivel you just recited which would have convinced most men. I think I could use you in a little drama which I have been planning. I’ll make a bargain with you. I have no wish to send you to prison.”

“Spoken like a man.”

===Chinese===
“你是干演员的吗？”

“我？哪儿能呢。我们家的人规矩大着呢。”

“嗯，你颇有表演才能。你方才背诵的那一番胡话里，自有一种真诚的调门，足能叫大多数人信以为真。我正在筹划一出小戏，我想我用得上你。我跟你做一笔交易。我不想送你进监狱。”

“这才像句男子汉说的话。”

===Original===
“I ought to, of course.”

“Yes, but it’s a lot better fun doing things that you oughtn’t, isn’t it?”

“Well, the point is, I have a friend who is in a difficulty, and it occurs to me that you can get him out.”

“Always glad to oblige.”

===Chinese===
“我本来是应该送你进去的。”

“话是这么说，可专干那些不该干的事，乐子才多呢，是不是？”

“嗯，是这样的：我有一位朋友正陷在困境里，我忽然想到，你能把他搭救出来。”

“随时乐意效劳。”

===Original===
“My friend is going to be married today, and he has just heard that a previous fiancée of his, whom in the excitement inseparable from falling in love with the girl who is to be his bride he had unfortunately overlooked, is on her way here.”

“To make trouble?”

“Precisely.”

===Chinese===
“我的朋友今天就要成婚，方才却听说，他从前有过一位未婚妻——在坠入爱河难免的心醉神迷之中，爱上的又是将做他新娘的那位姑娘，他便不幸把她忘了个干净——如今正动身上这儿来。”

“来捣乱？”

“正是。”

===Original===
“Well, what can I do about it?”

“Just this. For five minutes I want you to play the role of my friend’s discarded victim.”

“I don’t get it.”

“I will put it more plainly. In a short while this girl will arrive, probably in company with my friend, who has gone to meet her at the station. You will be waiting outside here. At an appropriate moment you will rush into the room, hold out your arms to my friend and cry ‘George! George! Why did you desert me? You don’t belong to that girl there. You belong to me﻿—the woman you have wronged!’ ”

===Chinese===
“那么，我能帮上什么忙呢？”

“很简单。我只要你花上五分钟，扮演一下我朋友抛弃掉的那位受害人。”

“我没听明白。”

“我说得更明白些。再过片刻这位姑娘就要到了，多半由我那位朋友陪着——他去年站接她去了。你就在外头候着。到时候你冲进屋来，朝我朋友伸出双臂，喊道：‘乔治！乔治！你为什么抛弃我？你不属于那边那个姑娘。你属于我——属于你糟蹋过的女人！’”

===Original===
“Not on your life!”

“What!”

Fanny drew herself up haughtily.

“Not on your life!” she said. “Suppose my husband got to hear of it!”

===Chinese===
“打死我也不干！”

“什么！”

芬妮傲然挺直了身子。

“打死我也不干！”她说。“要是我丈夫听说了呢！”

===Original===
“Are you married?”

“Married this morning at the Little Church Round the Corner.”

“And you come here and try to steal things on your wedding-day!”

“Why not? You know as well as I do what it costs nowadays to set up house.”

===Chinese===
“你结婚了？”

“今天早上刚在转角小教堂结的婚。”

“你倒好，大喜的日子跑上这儿来偷东西！”

“怎么不行？如今安一个家要花多少钱，你跟我一样门儿清。”

===Original===
“Surely it would be a severe shock to your husband to find that you had been sent to prison! I think you had better be reasonable.”

Fanny scraped the floor with her shoe.

“Would this thing you want me to do get into the papers?”

“Good heavens, no!”

===Chinese===
“要是发觉你被送进了监狱，你丈夫准得大受震动！我看你最好是放明白些。”

芬妮拿鞋尖蹭着地板。

“你要我干的这件事，会不会登到报上？”

“老天爷，绝不会！”

===Original===
“And there’s another thing. Suppose I did come in and pull that spiel, who would believe it?”

“The girl would. She is very simple.”

“She must be.”

===Chinese===
“还有一条。就算我真冲进去念了那一套说辞，谁会信呢？”

“那姑娘会信。她头脑很简单。”

“那她准是简单到家了。”

===Original===
“Just an ignorant village girl. The sort who would naturally recoil from a man in the circumstances I have outlined.”

“Suppose they ask me questions?”

“They won’t.”

“But suppose they do? Suppose the girl says Where did you meet him and when did all this happen and what the hell and all like that, what do I say?”

===Chinese===
“不过是个没见过世面的乡下姑娘。正是那种照我方才描绘的情形，见了那男人就自然要退避三舍的姑娘。”

“万一他们问我话呢？”

“他们不会的。”

“可万一他们偏要问呢？万一那姑娘问你：你在哪儿认识他的？这一切都是什么时候的事？这究竟算怎么回事？还有诸如此类的问话，我该怎么答？”

===Original===
Hamilton Beamish considered the point.

“I think the best plan would be for you to pretend, immediately after you have spoken the words I have indicated, that emotion has made you feel faint. Yes, that is best. Having said those words, exclaim ‘Air! Air! I want air!’ and rush out.”

“Now you’re talking. I like that bit about rushing out. I’ll go so quick, they won’t see me.”

===Chinese===
汉密尔顿·比米什琢磨了一下这个问题。

“我想最稳妥的办法是：你一说完我指定的话，马上装作激动得发晕。对，这样最好。话音一落，就嚷：‘空气！空气！我要空气！’随即冲出门去。”

“这还像句话。冲出门去这一手我喜欢。我溜得飞快，保管他们连影儿都瞧不见。”

===Original===
“Then you are prepared to do this thing?”

“Looks as if I’d got to.”

“Good. Kindly run through your opening speech. I must see that you are letter-perfect.”

“George! George!﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“这么说，这件事你愿意干了？”

“看来是不干不成了。”

“好。请把你那段开场白先过一遍。我必须确认你一字不差。”

“乔治！乔治！……”

===Original===
“Pause before the second George and take in breath. Remember that the intensity or loudness of the voice depends on the amplitude of the movement of the vocal chords, while pitch depends on the number of vibrations per second. Tone is strengthened by the resonance of the air on the air-passages and in the pharyngeal and oral cavities. Once more, please.”

“George! George! Why did you desert me?﻿ ﻿…”

“Arms extended.”

“You don’t belong to that girl there.”

===Chinese===
“第二个‘乔治’前头停一拍，吸口气。记住：声音的强弱，取决于声带动作的振幅；音的高低，取决于每秒钟振动的次数。而气流在气道与咽腔、口腔中的共鸣，则能使音色饱满。请再来一遍。”

“乔治！乔治！你为什么抛弃我？……”

“双臂张开。”

“你不属于那边那个姑娘。”

===Original===
“Pause. Breath.”

“You belong to me﻿—the woman you have wronged!”

Hamilton Beamish nodded with restrained approval.

“Not bad. Not at all bad. I should have liked, if it had been possible, to have an expert examine your thyro-arytenoid ligaments: and I wish there had been time for you to study my booklet on ‘Voice Production.’﻿ ﻿… However, I think it will do. Now go back and hide in the rhododendrons. This girl may be arriving at any moment.”

===Chinese===
“停顿。吸气。”

“你属于我——属于你糟蹋过的女人！”

汉密尔顿·比米什矜持地颔首称许。

“不错。相当不错。若有可能，我本想请一位专家检查一下你的甲杓韧带（thyro-arytenoid ligaments）；也恨没有时间让你研读我那本《发声术》（Voice Production）小册子……不过，我想也还过得去。现在回到杜鹃花丛里藏好。那位姑娘随时可能到达。”

## X / 第十章

===Original===

Hamilton Beamish strolled out into the hall. Something attempted, something done, had earned a cigarette. He was just lighting one, when there was a grinding of wheels on the gravel, and through the open door he saw Madame Eulalie alighting from a red two-seater car. He skipped joyously to meet her.

“So you managed to come after all!”

Madame Eulalie shook his hand with that brisk amiability which was one of her main charms.

“Yes. But I’ve got to turn right round and go back again. I’ve three appointments this afternoon. I suppose you’re staying on for the wedding?”

“I had intended to. I promised George I would be his best man.”

===Chinese===

汉密尔顿·比米什信步踱进门厅。有所尝试，有所作为，便挣得一支香烟。他刚要点上一支，忽听砾石车道上车轮碾出一片辚辚声响；透过敞开的大门，他望见尤拉莉夫人正从一辆红色双座小汽车里下来。他欢天喜地地一路小跑迎了上去。

“你到底还是设法来啦！”

尤拉莉夫人同他握了握手，那份爽利可亲，正是她顶要紧的魅力之一。

“是啊。可我得立刻掉头再赶回去。今天下午我另有三个预约。我想，你是要留下来参加婚礼的吧？”

“我本来是这样打算的。我答应过乔治，要给他当男傧相。”

===Original===

“That’s a pity. I could have driven you back.”

“Oh, I can easily cancel the thing,” said Hamilton Beamish quickly. “In fact, I will, directly George returns. He can get dozens of best men﻿—dozens.”

“Returns? Where has he gone?”

“To the station.”

===Chinese===

“那可真不巧。我本来还能开车捎你回去的。”

“哦，这种事取消起来最容易不过了，”汉密尔顿·比米什赶忙说，“实话实说，乔治一回来我就去取消。男傧相他能招来几十个——几十个。”

“回来？他上哪儿去了？”

“上车站去了。”

===Original===

“What a nuisance. I came especially to see him. Still, it doesn’t matter. I had better see Miss Waddington for a moment, I suppose.”

“She is out.”

Madame Eulalie raised her eyebrows.

“Doesn’t anybody stay in the house in these parts when there’s going to be a wedding?”

===Chinese===

“真不凑巧。我还是特地来见他的呢。不过，也不要紧。我想，我还是先见一见沃丁顿小姐吧。”

“她出门了。”

尤拉莉夫人扬起了眉毛。

“这地方的人家要办婚礼，难道就没有一个人肯待在屋里的吗？”

===Original===

“There has been a slight accident,” explained Hamilton Beamish. “The clergyman sprained his ankle, and Mrs. Waddington and Molly had gone to Flushing to pick up an understudy. And George has gone to the station.﻿ ﻿…”

“Yes, why has George gone to the station?”

Hamilton Beamish hesitated. Then, revolted by the thought that he should be hiding anything from this girl, he spoke.

“Can you keep a secret?”

===Chinese===

“出了一桩小小的意外，”汉密尔顿·比米什解释道，“牧师把脚踝扭伤了，沃丁顿夫人和莫莉上法拉盛接一位替补去了。还有乔治，他上车站去了……”

“是啊，乔治上车站去做什么？”

汉密尔顿·比米什迟疑了一下。随后，一想到自己竟要有什么瞒着这位姑娘，他便深觉可鄙，开口说了。

“你能保守秘密吗？”

===Original===

“I don’t know. I’ve never tried.”

“Well, this is something quite between ourselves. Poor George is in trouble.”

“Any worse trouble than most bridegrooms?”

“I wish you would not speak like that,” said Hamilton Beamish, pained. “You seem to mock at Love.”

===Chinese===

“这我说不准。我还从来没试过。”

“嗯，这事只在你我之间说说。可怜的乔治遇上麻烦了。”

“比大多数新郎官的麻烦还要糟的麻烦？”

“求你别这样说话，”汉密尔顿·比米什痛心地说，“你这话简直是在讥诮爱情。”

===Original===

“Oh, I’ve nothing against Love.”

“Thank you, thank you!”

“Don’t mention it.”
