“Love is the only thing worth while in the world. In peace, Love tunes the shepherd’s reed, in war he mounts the warrior’s steed.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===

“哦，我跟爱情可没有过节。”

“多谢，多谢！”

“不必客气。”

“爱情是这世上唯一值得要的东西。太平时节，爱为牧人的芦笛调音；烽火岁月，爱又跨上武士的战马。……”

===Original===

“Yes, doesn’t he. You were going to tell me what George is in trouble about.”

Hamilton Beamish lowered his voice.

“Well, the fact is, on the eve of his wedding an old acquaintance of his has suddenly appeared.”

“Female?”

===Chinese===

“是啊，可不嘛。你还是接着说吧，乔治到底为什么事遇上麻烦了。”

汉密尔顿·比米什压低了嗓音。

“事情是这样的，就在他婚礼的前夕，他的一位旧相识突然冒了出来。”

“女的？”

===Original===

“Female.”

“I begin to see.”

“George wrote her letters. She still has them.”

“Worse and worse.”

===Chinese===

“女的。”

“我有点儿明白了。”

“乔治给她写过信。那些信如今还在她手里。”

“越来越糟了。”

===Original===

“And if she makes trouble it will stop the wedding. Mrs. Waddington is only waiting for an excuse to forbid it. Already, she has stated in so many words that she is suspicious of George’s morals.”

“How absurd! George is like the driven snow.”

“Exactly. A thoroughly fine-minded man. Why, I remember him once leaving the table at a bachelor dinner because someone told an improper story.”

“How splendid of him! What was the story?”

===Chinese===

“而她若是闹将起来，婚礼就得告吹。沃丁顿夫人正愁没个由头来叫停呢。她已经明明白白放过话，说乔治的品行靠不住。”

“荒唐透顶！乔治冰清玉洁，如同新雪。”

“正是这话。一位心地高尚透顶的人。可不是嘛，我记得有一回单身汉晚宴上，只因为有人讲了个不登大雅的故事，他就离席走人了。”

“他真是了不起！那是个什么故事？”

===Original===

“I don’t remember. Still, Mrs. Waddington has this opinion of him, so there it is.”

“All this sounds very interesting. What are you going to do about it?”

“Well, George has gone to the station to try to intercept this Miss Stubbs and reason with her.”

“Miss Stubbs?”

===Chinese===

“我不记得了。不管怎样，沃丁顿夫人对他就是这个看法，事情就这么明摆着。”

“这一番听着可真有意思。那你打算怎么办呢？”

“是这样的，乔治已经上车站去了，想截住这位斯塔布斯小姐，同她讲讲道理。”

“斯塔布斯小姐？”

===Original===

“That is her name. By the way, she comes from your home town, East Gilead. Perhaps you know her?”

“I seem to recollect the name. So George has gone to reason with her?”

“Yes. But, of course, she will insist on coming here.”

“That’s bad.”

===Chinese===

“她就叫这个名字。对了，她跟你是同乡，也是东吉利亚德人。说不定你认识她？”

“这名字我好像有点儿耳熟。这么说，乔治是去同她讲道理了？”

“是啊。不过，她当然会执意要来这里的。”

“那可不妙。”

===Original===

Hamilton Beamish smiled.

“Not quite so bad as you think,” he said. “You see, I have been giving the matter some little thought, and I may say I have the situation well in hand. I have arranged everything.”

“You have?”

“Everything.”

===Chinese===

汉密尔顿·比米什微微一笑。

“没有你想的那么不妙，”他说，“你瞧，这事我也略费过些心思，可以说，全局已稳稳握在我手心里。一切都安排停当了。”

“都安排好了？”

“一切。”

===Original===

“You must be terribly clever.”

“Oh, well!” said Hamilton Beamish modestly.

“But, of course, I knew you were, the moment I read your Booklets. Have you a cigarette?”

“I beg your pardon.”

===Chinese===

“你一定聪明得不得了。”

“哦，哪里哪里！”汉密尔顿·比米什谦虚地说。

“不过，这自然不必说，打从读了你那些小册子的头一眼起，我就知道你聪明。能给我一支香烟吗？”

“失敬失敬。”

===Original===

Madame Eulalie selected a cigarette from his case and lit it. Hamilton Beamish, taking the match from her fingers, blew it out and placed it reverently in his left top waistcoat-pocket.

“Go on,” said Madame Eulalie.

“Ah, yes,” said Hamilton Beamish, coming out of his thoughts. “We were speaking about George. It appears that George, before he left East Gilead, had what he calls an understanding, but which seems to me to have differed in no respect from a definite engagement, with a girl named May Stubbs. Unpleasant name!”

“Horrible. Just the sort of name I would want to change.”

===Chinese===

尤拉莉夫人从他的烟盒里拣出一支香烟，点着了。汉密尔顿·比米什从她指间接过火柴，一口吹熄，恭恭敬敬地收进马甲左上的口袋。

“接着说，”尤拉莉夫人说。

“啊，对了，”汉密尔顿·比米什从沉思里回过神来，“我们方才正说到乔治。看来，乔治离开东吉利亚德之前，同一位名叫梅·斯塔布斯的姑娘有过一段交情——他管那叫‘默契’，可依我看，那跟板上钉钉的婚约并没有半点分别。难听的名字！”

“难听极了。正是那种我巴不得赶紧改掉的名字。”

===Original===

“He then came into money, left for New York, and forgot all about her.”

“But she didn’t forget all about him?”

“Apparently not. I picture her as a poor, dowdy little thing﻿—you know what these village girls are﻿—without any likelihood of getting another husband. So she has clung to her one chance. I suppose she thinks that by coming here at this time she will force George to marry her.”

“But you are going to be too clever to let anything like that happen?”

===Chinese===

“随后他得了遗产，动身来了纽约，把她忘了个一干二净。”

“可她没把他忘得一干二净？”

“显然没有。我估摸她是个寒碜、土气的小可怜儿——乡下姑娘是什么路数，你是知道的——再想嫁个丈夫，八成是没有指望的了。所以她就死死抱住这唯一的机会不放。依我看，她打的主意是，挑这么个节骨眼找上门来，逼得乔治娶她。”

“可你足智多谋，断不会容许那种事发生，对吧？”

===Original===

“Precisely.”

“Aren’t you wonderful!”

“It is extremely kind of you to say so,” said Hamilton Beamish, pulling down his waistcoat.

“What have you arranged?”

===Chinese===

“正是此意。”

“你真是了不得！”

“承蒙你这么说，真是客气了，”汉密尔顿·比米什说着，抻了抻马甲。

“你安排了什么呢？”

===Original===

“Well, the whole difficulty is that at present George is in the position of having broken the engagement. So, when this May Stubbs arrives, I am going to get her to throw him over of her own free-will.”

“And how do you propose to do that?”

“Quite simply. You see, we may take it for granted that she is a prude. I have, therefore, constructed a little drama, by means of which George will appear an abandoned libertine.”

“George!”

===Chinese===

“是这样，全部难处就在于：眼下乔治的处境，是个撕毁婚约的负心人。所以，等这位梅·斯塔布斯一到，我要设法叫她凭自己的自由意志把他甩了。”

“那你打算怎么个设法呢？”

“再简单不过。你瞧，我们满可以认定，她必定是个拘谨守礼的道学姑娘。因此，我编排了一出小小的戏，一演之下，乔治在她眼里就是个荒唐放浪的浪荡子了。”

“乔治？！”

===Original===

“She will be shocked and revolted and will at once break off all relations with him.”

“I see. Did you think all this out by yourself?”

“Entirely by myself.”

“You’re too clever for one man. You ought to incorporate.”

===Chinese===

“她会大为震惊，深恶痛绝，立时同他一刀两断。”

“明白了。这一切都是你自己想出来的？”

“完完全全是我自己想出来的。”

“一个人的聪明到了你这地步，就嫌太多了。你该注册成立一家公司才对。”

===Original===

It seemed to Hamilton Beamish that the moment had arrived to speak out frankly and without subterfuge, to reveal in the neatest phrases at his disposal the love which had been swelling in his heart like some yeasty ferment ever since he had first taken a speck of dust out of this girl’s eye on the doorstep of Number Sixteen, East Seventy-Ninth Street. And he was about to begin doing so, when she looked past him and uttered a pleased laugh.

“Why, Georgie Finch!”

Hamilton Beamish turned, justly exasperated. Every time he endeavoured to speak his love, it seemed that something had to happen to prevent him. Yesterday it had been the loathsome Charley on the telephone, and now it was George Finch. George was standing in the doorway, flushed as if he had been walking quickly. He was staring at the girl in a manner which Hamilton Beamish resented. To express his resentment he coughed sharply.

George paid no attention. He continued to stare.

===Chinese===

在汉密尔顿·比米什看来，时机已然来临：该直截了当、毫不遮掩地剖白心迹了——自从在东七十九街 16 号的门阶上头一回替这位姑娘取掉眼里的一粒尘沙，这份爱便像酵母一般在他心里发着酵，一个劲儿地往上涌，此刻正要用他腹中现成的最漂亮的辞句，把它和盘托出。他正要开口，她却越过他的肩头望去，发出一声欢快的笑。

“哎呀，这不是乔吉·芬奇吗！”

汉密尔顿·比米什转过身来，恼得有根有据。每回他立志要倾诉爱意，老天仿佛非得生出点变故来拦他一道。昨天是电话里那个可憎的查利，今天又换成了乔治·芬奇。乔治正立在门口，满面通红，像是一路急走而来。他直勾勾地盯着这位姑娘，那副神情教汉密尔顿·比米什大为不快。为了宣示这腔不快，他尖声咳嗽了一下。

乔治全没理会。他接着瞪眼瞧。

===Original===

“And how is Georgie? You have interrupted a most interesting story, George.”

“May!” George Finch placed a finger inside his collar, as if trying to loosen it. “May! I﻿—I’ve just been down to the station to meet you.”

“I came by car.”

“May?” exclaimed Hamilton Beamish, a horrid light breaking upon him.

===Chinese===

“乔吉一向可好？你把一桩顶有意思的故事给打断了，乔治。”

“梅！”乔治·芬奇把一根手指头塞进领口，仿佛想把它撑松些。“梅！我——我刚上车站接你去了。”

“我是坐汽车来的。”

“梅？”汉密尔顿·比米什失声叫道，一幕可怕的真相倏然照进他的心里。

===Original===

Madame Eulalie turned to him brightly.

“Yes, I’m the dowdy little thing.”

“But you’re not a dowdy little thing,” said Hamilton Beamish, finding thought difficult but concentrating on the one uncontrovertible fact.

“I was when George knew me.”

===Chinese===

尤拉莉夫人欢欢喜喜地转向他。

“不错，我就是那个寒碜的小可怜儿。”

“可你绝不是什么寒碜的小可怜儿，”汉密尔顿·比米什说。他只觉得思索艰难，却死死咬住这条无可辩驳的事实不放。

“乔治认识我那会儿，我是。”

===Original===

“And your name is Madame Eulalie.”

“My professional name. Didn’t we agree that anyone who had a name like May Stubbs would want to change it as quickly as possible?”

“You are really May Stubbs?”

“I am.”

===Chinese===

“而你的名字，是尤拉莉夫人。”

“那是营业用的名字。咱们方才不是一致同意：谁要是摊上梅·斯塔布斯这么个名字，都巴不得越快改掉越好吗？”

“你当真是梅·斯塔布斯？”

“我正是。”

===Original===

Hamilton Beamish bit his lip. He regarded his friend coldly.

“I congratulate you, George. You are engaged to two of the prettiest girls I have ever seen.”

“How very charming of you, Jimmy!” said Madame Eulalie.

George Finch’s face worked convulsively.

===Chinese===

汉密尔顿·比米什咬住了嘴唇。他冷冷地逼视着自己的朋友。

“恭喜你呀，乔治。我平生所见最标致的两位姑娘，你同她们二位全都订了婚。”

“你这话说得可真叫人受用，吉米！”尤拉莉夫人说。

乔治·芬奇的脸抽搐着，扭作一团。

===Original===

“But, May, honestly.﻿ ﻿… Have a heart!﻿ ﻿… You don’t really look on me as engaged to you?”

“Why not?”

“But﻿ ﻿… but﻿ ﻿… I thought you had forgotten all about me.”

“What, after all those beautiful letters you wrote!”

===Chinese===

“可是，梅，说句公道话……发发善心吧！……你该不会真把我看成同你订了婚的人吧？”

“为什么不？”

“可是……可是……我还当你早把我忘得一干二净了。”

“什么话？写了那么多情辞并茂的信，还能忘！”

===Original===

“Boy and girl affair,” babbled George.

“Was it, indeed!”

“But, May!﻿ ﻿…”

Hamilton Beamish had been listening to these exchanges with a rapidly rising temperature. His heart was pounding feverishly in his bosom. There is no one who becomes so primitive, when gripped by love, as the man who all his life has dwelt in the cool empyrean of the intellect. For twenty years and more, Hamilton Beamish had supposed that he was above the crude passions of the ordinary man, and when love had got him it got him good. And now, standing there and listening to these two, he was conscious of a jealousy so keen that he could no longer keep silent. Hamilton Beamish, the thinker, had ceased to be: and there stood in his place Hamilton Beamish, the descendant of ancestors who had conducted their love affairs with stout clubs and who, on seeing a rival, wasted no time in calm reflection but jumped on him like a ton of bricks and did their best to bite his head off. If you had given him a bearskin and taken away his spectacles, Hamilton Beamish at this moment would have been Prehistoric Man.

===Chinese===

“那不过是两小无猜的小孩子把戏，”乔治语无伦次地说。

“哦，是嘛！”

“可是，梅！……”

这一唱一和，汉密尔顿·比米什全听在耳里，只觉体温节节攀升。他的心在胸腔里狂热地擂鼓。天底下被爱情攫住时变得最原始的，莫过于一辈子住在理智清凉九天之上的人。二十多年以来，汉密尔顿·比米什一直自以为超然于凡夫俗子的粗鄙情欲之外；而爱情一旦攫住了他，就把他攫了个结结实实。此刻他站在那里，听这两个人你来我往，只觉一股妒意烧得尖利如锥，再也无法按捺着不出声了。思想家汉密尔顿·比米什已然寿终正寝：取而代之站在原地的，是另一位汉密尔顿·比米什——他的列祖列宗谈情说爱全凭一根粗大木棒，一见情敌，从无半刻从容思量的工夫，只管像一吨砖头压顶般扑将上去，再铆足了劲要把对方的脑袋咬将下来。这当口，你若给他披上一张熊皮，再摘走他的眼镜，汉密尔顿·比米什便活脱脱是一位史前人。

===Original===

“Hey!” said Hamilton Beamish.

“But, May, you know you don’t love me.﻿ ﻿…”

“Hey!” said Hamilton Beamish again in a nasty, snarling voice. And silence fell.

The caveman adjusted his spectacles, and glared at his erstwhile friend with venomous dislike. His fingers twitched, as if searching for a club.

===Chinese===

“喂！”汉密尔顿·比米什喝道。

“可是，梅，你明明白白并不爱我。……”

“喂！”汉密尔顿·比米什又喝了一声，恶声恶气，龇牙咧嘴。于是四下里鸦雀无声。

这位穴居人扶正了眼镜，恶毒而厌恶地瞪着昔日的朋友。他的手指抽动个不停，仿佛在摸索一根大棒。

===Original===

“Listen to me, you,” said Hamilton Beamish, “and get me right! See? That’ll be about all from you about this girl loving you, unless you want me to step across and bust you on the beezer. I love her, see? And she’s going to marry me, see? And nobody else, see? And anyone who says different had better notify his friends where he wants his body sent, see? Love you, indeed? A swell chance! I’m the little guy she’s going to marry, see? Me!”

And, folding his arms, the thinker paused for a reply.

It did not come immediately. George Finch, unused to primitive emotions from this particular quarter, remained completely dumb. It was left for Madame Eulalie to supply comment.

“Jimmy!” she said faintly.

===Chinese===

“你给我听着，”汉密尔顿·比米什说，“把话听真了！明白？这位姑娘爱你这种浑话，你给我就此打住，除非你想叫我跨过去，一拳砸烂你的蒜头鼻。我爱她，明白？她要嫁的是我，明白？不是旁的什么人，明白？谁要是说不一样的，最好赶紧知会他的亲友，交代清楚他的遗体打算送往何处，明白？还爱你呢？做你的春秋大梦！她要嫁的这个小子，是我，明白？是我！”

说罢，这位思想家抱起两臂，驻足静候回音。

回音没有即刻降临。乔治·芬奇素来不惯于从这么一位人物身上领教原始情绪，兀自木立，一言不发。接碴儿的差事，便落在了尤拉莉夫人肩上。

“吉米！”她有气无力地说。

===Original===

Hamilton Beamish caught her masterfully about the waist. He kissed her eleven times.

“So that’s that!” said Hamilton Beamish.

“Yes, Jimmy.”

“We’ll get married tomorrow.”

===Chinese===

汉密尔顿·比米什大手一揽，霸气十足地搂住她的腰。他吻了她十一回。

“就这么定了！”汉密尔顿·比米什说。

“是，吉米。”

“我们明天就结婚。”

===Original===

“Yes, Jimmy.”

“You are my mate!”

“Yes, Jimmy.”

“All right, then,” said Hamilton Beamish.

===Chinese===

“是，吉米。”

“你是我的女人！”

“是，吉米。”

“那就这样吧，”汉密尔顿·比米什说。

===Original===

George came to life like a clockwork toy.

“Hamilton, I congratulate you!”

“Thanks, thanks.”

Mr. Beamish spoke a little dazedly. He blinked. Already the ferment had begun to subside, and Beamish the caveman was fast giving place to Beamish of the Booklets. He was dimly conscious of having expressed himself a little too warmly and in language which in a calmer moment he would never have selected. Then he caught the girl’s eyes, fixed on him adoringly, and he had no regrets.

===Chinese===

乔治像上了发条的玩具似的活了过来。

“汉密尔顿，恭喜恭喜！”

“多谢，多谢。”

比米什先生说话已有些恍恍惚惚。他眨了眨眼。心头那股酵母般的翻腾已经开始平息，穴居人比米什正飞快地让位于小册子比米什。他隐约意识到，方才自己言辞未免过热，而且用的那些字眼，换了他心平气和的时候，是断然不会挑拣出口的。随后他触到姑娘的目光——那双眼睛正满含倾慕地凝定在他身上——于是他再无遗憾。

===Original===

“Thanks,” he said again.

“May is a splendid girl,” said George. “You will be very happy. I speak as one who knows her. How sympathetic you always were in the old days, May.”

“Was I?”

“You certainly were. Don’t you remember how I used to bring my troubles to you, and we would sit together on the sofa in front of your parlour fire?”

===Chinese===

“多谢，”他又说了一遍。

“梅是个顶好的姑娘，”乔治说，“你们会非常幸福的。这话出自知她甚深的人之口。梅，你从前总是那么善解人意。”

“是吗？”

“当然是。你不记得了？从前我总把烦心事拿去对你说，我们俩便挨着坐在你家客厅炉火前的沙发上。”

===Original===

“We were always afraid someone was listening at the door.”

“If they had been, the only thing they’d have found out would have been the lamp.”

“Hey!” said Hamilton Beamish abruptly.

“Those were happy days,” said Madame Eulalie.

===Chinese===

“那时候我们总提心吊胆，生怕有人在门外偷听。”

“就算真有，他们能偷听出来的，也无非是那盏灯的事。”

“喂！”汉密尔顿·比米什冷不丁又喝了一声。

“那时候的日子多快活，”尤拉莉夫人说。

===Original===

“And do you remember how your little brother used to call me April Showers?”

“He did, did he?” said Mr. Beamish, snorting a little. “Why?”

“Because I brought May flowers.”

“That’s quite enough,” said Hamilton Beamish, not without reason. “I should like to remind you, Finch, that this lady is engaged to me.”

===Chinese===

“你还记得不记得，你小弟弟从前总管我叫‘四月阵雨’（April Showers）？”

“他这么叫过，是吗？”比米什先生哼了哼鼻子。“为什么这么叫？”

“因为四月阵雨一落，梅花开呀。”

“够了，到此为止，”汉密尔顿·比米什说。他说这话，倒也不是没有缘故的。“芬奇，我愿意提醒你一句：这位姑娘已经同我订了婚。”

===Original===

“Oh, quite,” said George.

“Endeavour not to forget it,” said Hamilton Beamish curtly. “And, later on, should you ever come to share a meal at our little home, be sparing of your reminiscences of the dear old days. You get﻿—you take my meaning?”

“Oh, quite.”

“Then we will be getting along. May has to return to New York immediately, and I am going with her. You must look elsewhere for a best man at your wedding. You are very lucky to be having a wedding at all. Goodbye, George. Come, darling.”

===Chinese===

“哦，自然自然，”乔治说。

“务必别忘了这一点，”汉密尔顿·比米什没好气地说。“还有，往后你若是到我们这个小家来搭一顿饭，对你那些可怀恋的美好旧日，务必要省着点追忆。你明白——你懂我的意思吧？”

“哦，明白，明白。”

“那么，我们就此动身。梅必须立刻回纽约去，我陪她一道。你婚礼上的男傧相，另请高明吧。你这婚礼居然还办得起来，已经要算是天大的造化了。再见，乔治。走吧，亲爱的。”

---

===Original===

The two-seater was moving down the drive, when Hamilton Beamish clapped a hand to his forehead.

“I had quite forgotten,” he exclaimed.

“What have you forgotten, Jimmy dear?”

“Just something I wanted to say to George, sweetheart. Wait here for me.”

“George,” said Hamilton Beamish, returning to the hall. “I have just remembered something. Ring for Ferris and tell him to stay in the room with the wedding-presents and not leave it for a moment. They aren’t safe, lying loose like that. You should have had a detective.”

===Chinese===

双座小汽车正顺着车道驶下去，汉密尔顿·比米什忽地一巴掌拍上自己的脑门。

“我把这事忘了个一干二净！”他嚷道。

“你忘什么了，亲爱的吉米？”

“有句话我得对乔治交代一声，心肝儿。在这儿等着我。”

“乔治，”汉密尔顿·比米什回到门厅里说，“我方才想起一件事。拉铃把费里斯叫来，叫他守在放结婚礼物的那个房间里，一刻也不许离开。那些礼物就这么随意摊放着，可不保险。你们本该雇上一名侦探才是。”

===Original===

“We intended to, but Mr. Waddington insisted on it so strongly that Mrs. Waddington said the idea was absurd. I’ll go and tell Ferris immediately.”

“Do so,” said Hamilton Beamish.

He passed out on to the lawn: and reaching the rhododendron bushes, whistled softly.

“Now what?” said Fanny, pushing out an inquiring head.

===Chinese===

“我们本来是打算雇的，可是沃丁顿先生坚持这主意坚持得太过起劲，反倒弄得沃丁顿夫人说这个念头荒唐。我这就去告诉费里斯。”

“这就去办，”汉密尔顿·比米什说。

他踱到外头的草坪上，走到杜鹃花丛跟前，轻轻打了个呼哨。

“又有什么事？”芬妮探出一张探询的脑袋来。

===Original===

“Oh, there you are.”

“Yes, here I am. When does the show start?”

“It doesn’t,” said Hamilton Beamish. “Events have occurred which render our little ruse unnecessary. So you can return to your home and husband as soon as you please.”

“Oh?” said Fanny.

===Chinese===

“哦，原来你在这儿。”

“是啊，我在这儿。戏什么时候开场？”

“开不成啦，”汉密尔顿·比米什说，“事态有了变化，咱们那出小小的巧计已经用不着了。所以，你随时想走，就可以回家去找你的丈夫。”

“哦？”芬妮应了一声。

===Original===

She plucked a rhododendron leaf and crushed it reflectively.

“I don’t know as I’m in any hurry,” she said. “I kind of like it out here. The air and the sun and the birds and everything. I guess I’ll stick around for awhile.”

Hamilton Beamish regarded her with a quiet smile.

“Certainly, if you wish it,” he said. “I should mention, however, that if you were contemplating another attempt at those jewels, you would do well to abandon the idea. From now on a large butler will be stationed in the room, watching over it, and there might be unpleasantness.”

===Chinese===

她摘下一片杜鹃叶子，若有所思地把它捻碎了。

“我说不好自己急是不急，”她说，“外头这地方我倒挺中意。空气啦，太阳啦，鸟儿啦，样样都好。我寻思再待上一阵子。”

汉密尔顿·比米什含着一丝不动声色的微笑，打量着她。

“当然可以，只要你愿意，”他说，“不过有一点我得提上一句：假如你心里盘算的是再去打那些珠宝的主意，那你顶好趁早把这个念头撂下。从今往后，会有一名彪形大管家驻守在那个房间里，寸步不离地看守着，弄不好就要生出些不愉快来。”

===Original===

“Oh?” said Fanny meditatively.

“Yes.”

“You think of everything, don’t you?”

“I thank you for the compliment,” said Hamilton Beamish.

===Chinese===

“哦？”芬妮若有所思地应了一声。

“正是。”

“你什么都想得周周到到，是不是？”

“承蒙夸奖，”汉密尔顿·比米什说。

## XI / 第十一章

===Original===

George did not delay. Always sound, Hamilton Beamish’s advice appeared to him now even sounder than usual. He rang the bell for Ferris.

“Oh, Ferris,” said George, “Mr. Beamish thinks you had better stay in the room with the wedding-presents and keep an eye on them.”

“Very good, sir.”

“In case somebody tries to steal them, you know.”

“Just so, sir.”

===Chinese===

乔治没有耽搁。汉密尔顿·比米什的忠告一向稳妥，此刻在他听来，更是比平常还要稳妥几分。他摇铃唤来了费里斯。

“哦，费里斯，”乔治说，“比米什先生认为你顶好守在放结婚礼物的那个房间里，替它们留点儿神。”

“遵命，先生。”

“免得有人打它们的主意，你知道。”

“正是，先生。”

===Original===

Relief, as it always does, had given George a craving for conversation. He wanted to buttonhole some fellow-creature and babble. He would have preferred this fellow-creature to have been anyone but Ferris, for he had not forgotten the early passages of their acquaintanceship and seemed still to sense in the butler’s manner a lingering antipathy. But Ferris was there, so he babbled to him.

“Nice day, Ferris.”

“Yes, sir.”

“Nice weather.”

“Yes, sir.”

===Chinese===

如同往常一样，如释重负让乔治生出了一股攀谈的渴望。他想拽住随便哪个同类，唠叨个痛快。若容他挑拣，这位同类最好是费里斯以外的任何人，因为他没忘记两人初识时的那些过节，而且似乎至今仍能从这位男管家的做派里觉出一丝挥之不去的反感。可费里斯就在眼前，于是他便对着他唠叨开了。

“天气真好，费里斯。”

“是啊，先生。”

“天气很好。”

“是啊，先生。”

===Original===

“Nice country round here.”

“No, sir.”

George was somewhat taken aback.

“Did you say, No, sir?”

“Yes, sir.”

“Oh, Yes, sir? I thought you said No, sir.”

===Chinese===

“这一带的乡间真不坏。”

“不，先生。”

乔治着实吃了一惊。

“你是说，‘不，先生’？”

“是的，先生。”

“哦，是‘是的，先生’？我还当你说的是‘不，先生’。”

===Original===

“Yes, sir. No, sir.”

“You mean you don’t like the country round here?”

“No, sir.”

“Why not?”

“I disapprove of it, sir.”

===Chinese===

“是的，先生。不，先生。”

“你是说，这一带的乡间你不喜欢？”

“不，先生。”

“为什么不喜欢？”

“我不赞成它，先生。”

===Original===

“Why?”

“It is not the sort of country to which I have been accustomed, sir. It is not like the country round Little-Seeping-in-the-Wold.”

“Where’s that?”

“In England, sir.”

===Chinese===

“为什么？”

“这不是我素来惯见的那一类乡间，先生。它不像利特尔-西平-因-沃尔德左近的乡间。”

“那地方在哪儿？”

“在英国，先生。”

===Original===

“I suppose the English country’s nice?”

“I believe it gives uniform satisfaction, sir.”

George felt damped. In his mood of relief he had hoped that Ferris might have brought himself to sink the butler in the friend.

“What don’t you like about the country round here?”

“I disapprove of the mosquitoes, sir.”

===Chinese===

“想必英国的乡间很美吧？”

“我相信它普遍令人满意，先生。”

乔治觉得扫了兴。在如释重负的心境里，他原指望费里斯能勉强屈尊把男管家的架子沉下去，拿出点朋友的面目来。

“这一带的乡间，你究竟不喜欢它什么？”

“我不赞成那些蚊子，先生。”

===Original===

“But there are only a few.”

“I disapprove of even one mosquito, sir.”

George tried again.

“I suppose everybody downstairs is very excited about the wedding, Ferris?”

===Chinese===

“可蚊子统共也没几只啊。”

“哪怕只有一只蚊子，我也不赞成，先生。”

乔治又试了一回。

“想必楼底下的人都在为婚礼兴奋得不得了吧，费里斯？”

===Original===

“By ‘everybody downstairs’ you allude to﻿ ﻿… ?”

“The﻿—er﻿—the domestic staff.”

“I have not canvassed their opinions, sir. I mix very little with my colleagues.”

“I suppose you disapprove of them?” said George, nettled.

“Yes, sir.”

===Chinese===

“您说的‘楼底下的人’，指的是……？”

“就是——呃——那班家务仆役。”

“我未曾征询过他们的意见，先生。我很少与同僚们往来。”

“想必你也不赞成他们吧？”乔治说道，被顶得有点着恼。

“是的，先生。”

===Original===

“Why?”

The butler raised his eyebrows. He preferred the lower middle classes not to be inquisitive. However, he stooped to explain.

“Many of them are Swedes, sir, and the rest are Irish.”

“You disapprove of Swedes?”

“Yes, sir.”

“Why?”

===Chinese===

“为什么？”

男管家扬起了眉毛。他宁可中下层阶级不要打破砂锅问到底。不过，他到底还是屈尊作了解释。

“他们当中许多是瑞典人，先生，其余的是爱尔兰人。”

“你不赞成瑞典人？”

“是的，先生。”

“为什么？”

===Original===

“Their heads are too square, sir.”

“And you disapprove of the Irish?”

“Yes, sir.”

“Why?”

“Because they are Irish, sir.”

===Chinese===

“他们的脑袋太方了，先生。”

“那你不赞成爱尔兰人？”

“是的，先生。”

“为什么？”

“因为他们是爱尔兰人，先生。”

===Original===

George shifted his feet uncomfortably.

“I hope you don’t disapprove of weddings, Ferris?”

“Yes, sir.”

“Why?”

“They seem to me melancholy occasions, sir.”

===Chinese===

乔治不自在地挪了挪脚。

“但愿你不至于连婚礼也不赞成吧，费里斯？”

“是的，先生。”

“为什么？”

“在我看来，婚礼是些凄凄切切的场合，先生。”

===Original===

“Are you married, Ferris?”

“A widower, sir.”

“Well, weren’t you happy when you got married?”

“No, sir.”

“Was Mrs. Ferris?”

===Chinese===

“你结过婚吗，费里斯？”

“我是个鳏夫，先生。”

“那么，你成亲的时候不快活吗？”

“不快活，先生。”

“那费里斯太太快活吗？”

===Original===

“She appeared to take a certain girlish pleasure in the ceremony, sir, but it soon blew over.”

“How do you account for that?”

“I could not say, sir.”

“I’m sorry weddings depress you, Ferris. Surely when two people love each other and mean to go on loving each other.﻿ ﻿…”

“Marriage is not a process for prolonging the life of love. It merely mummifies its corpse.”

===Chinese===

“典礼上，她似乎还带着几分少女的欢喜，先生，可惜很快就烟消云散了。”

“这你怎么解释？”

“我说不上来，先生。”

“婚礼竟叫你这么消沉，真是遗憾，费里斯。两个人彼此相爱、又打算一直爱下去的时候，照理总该……”

“婚姻并非为爱情延寿的过程。它不过是把爱情的尸身制成木乃伊罢了。”

===Original===

“But, Ferris, if there were no marriages, what would become of posterity?”

“I see no necessity for posterity, sir.”

“You disapprove of it?”

“Yes, sir.”

George walked pensively out on to the drive in front of the house. He was conscious of a diminution of the exuberant happiness which had led him to engage the butler in conversation. He saw clearly now that, Ferris’s conversation being what it was, a bridegroom who engaged him in it on his wedding-day was making a blunder. A suitable, even an ideal, companion for a funeral, Ferris seemed out of harmony when the joy-bells were ringing.

===Chinese===

“可是，费里斯，倘若没有婚姻，子孙后代可怎么办呢？”

“我看不出子孙后代有什么必要存在，先生。”

“你连子孙后代也不赞成？”

“是的，先生。”

乔治心事重重地踱到宅前车道上。他觉察到，方才引得他找男管家攀谈的那股欢天喜地的兴致，已经消退了几分。他此刻看得分明：费里斯的谈吐既然是那么副模样，一个新郎官在自己大喜的日子里找他攀谈，实在是一步昏招。费里斯这种人，给葬礼做伴当是合适的、甚至是理想的，可待到喜庆的钟声敲响，他便显得格格不入了。

===Original===

He looked out upon the pleasant garden with sobered gaze: and, looking, was aware of Sigsbee H. Waddington approaching. Sigsbee’s manner was agitated. He conveyed the impression of having heard bad news or of having made a discovery which disconcerted him.

“Say, listen!” said Sigsbee H. “What’s that infernal butler doing in the room with the wedding-presents?”

“Keeping guard over them.”

“Who told him to?”

“I did.”

===Chinese===

他以冷静了几分的目光打量那座宜人的花园：望着望着，只见西格斯比·H·沃丁顿正朝这边走来。西格斯比神色慌乱，给人的印象是，不是听到了什么坏消息，就是发现了什么叫他心绪不宁的事情。

“喂，听着！”西格斯比·H说，“那个可恶透顶的男管家在放结婚礼物的屋里待着干什么？”

“替它们站岗放哨。”

“谁叫他去的？”

“是我。”

===Original===

“Hell’s bells!” said Sigsbee H.

He gave George a peculiar look and shimmered off. If George had been more in the frame of mind to analyse the looks of his future father-in-law, he might have seen in this one a sort of shuddering loathing. But he was not in the frame of mind. Besides, Sigsbee H. Waddington was not the kind of man whose looks one analysed. He was one of those negligible men whom one pushes out of sight and forgets about. George proceeded to forget about him almost immediately. He was still forgetting about him, when an automobile appeared round the bend of the drive and, stopping beside him, discharged Mrs. Waddington, Molly, and a man with a face like a horse whom, from his clerical costume, George took correctly to be the deputy from Flushing.

“Molly!” cried George.

“Here we are, angel,” said Molly.

“And mother!” said George, with less heartiness.

===Chinese===

“活见鬼！”西格斯比·H说。

他古怪地瞪了乔治一眼，随即闪身飘然而去。倘若乔治当时心境正适合去揣摩未来岳父的脸色，他兴许能从这一眼里看出某种毛骨悚然的憎恶。可他偏没有那份心思。再说，西格斯比·H·沃丁顿这种人，压根儿就不是让人费神揣摩脸色之辈。他属于那种无足轻重的男人，一眼推出视野，转头即忘。乔治几乎立刻就把他忘了个干净。他正忘得起劲，一辆汽车已绕过车道的弯道驶来，在他身旁停住，卸下了沃丁顿夫人、莫莉，还有一个马脸男人——从那身牧师行头看，乔治没有猜错，正是从法拉盛请来的替工牧师。

“莫莉！”乔治喊道。

“我们到啦，心肝儿，”莫莉说。

“还有妈妈！”乔治说，热络劲儿减了几分。

===Original===

“Mother!” said Mrs. Waddington, with still less heartiness than George.

“This is the Reverend Gideon Voules,” said Molly. “He is going to marry us.”

“This,” said Mrs. Waddington, turning to the clergyman and speaking in a voice which seemed to George’s sensitive ear to contain too strong a note of apology, “is the bridegroom.”

The Reverend Gideon Voules looked at George with a dull and poached-egg-like eye. He did not seem to the latter to be a frightfully cheery sort of person: but, after all, when you’re married, you’re married, no matter how like a poached egg the presiding minister may look.

“How do you do?” said the Rev. Gideon.

===Chinese===

“妈妈！”沃丁顿夫人说，热络劲儿比乔治又减了几分。

“这位是吉迪恩·沃尔斯牧师（the Reverend Gideon Voules），”莫莉说，“由他来为我们主持婚礼。”

“这位，”沃丁顿夫人转向那位牧师说道；在乔治灵敏的耳朵听来，她话音里的歉意浓得有点过分，“是新郎。”

吉迪恩·沃尔斯牧师用一只呆滞无神、活像水煮荷包蛋的眼睛打量着乔治。在后者看来，此人似乎算不上多么欢天喜地的路数：不过说到底，结婚就是结婚，主持婚礼的牧师长得多么像一枚水煮荷包蛋，也碍不了什么。

“你好？”吉迪恩牧师说。

===Original===

“I’m fine,” said George. “How are you?”

“I am in robust health, I thank you.”

“Splendid! Nothing wrong with the ankles, eh?”

The Rev. Gideon glanced down at them and seemed satisfied with this section of his lower limbs, even though they were draped in white socks.

“Nothing, thank you.”

===Chinese===

“我很好，”乔治说，“你呢？”

“多谢动问，我体魄康健。”

“好极了！脚脖子没什么毛病吧？”

吉迪恩牧师朝下瞄了一眼，似乎对下肢的这一部分颇为满意，尽管那里罩着一双白袜。

“没毛病，多谢。”

===Original===

“So many clergymen nowadays,” explained George, “are falling off chairs and spraining them.”

“I never fall off chairs.”

“Then you’re just the fellow I’ve been scouring the country for,” said George. “If all clergymen were like you.﻿ ﻿…”

Mrs. Waddington came to life.

“Would you care for a glass of milk?”

===Chinese===

“如今好些牧师，”乔治解释道，“动不动就从椅子上摔下来，把脚脖子扭了。”

“我从椅子上摔下来的时候从来没有过。”

“那你就正是我踏破铁鞋、搜遍全国要找的那一位了，”乔治说，“要是天下牧师都像你这样……”

沃丁顿夫人活了过来。

“想来一杯牛奶吗？”

===Original===

“No, thank you, mother,” said George.

“I was not addressing you,” said Mrs. Waddington. “I was speaking to Mr. Voules. He has had a long drive and no doubt requires refreshment.”

“Of course, of course,” said George. “What am I thinking about? Yes, you must certainly stoke up and preserve your strength. We don’t want you fainting halfway through the ceremony.”

“He would have every excuse,” said Mrs. Waddington.

She led the way into the dining-room, where light refreshments were laid out on a side-table﻿—a side-table brightly decorated by the presence of Sigsbee H. Waddington, who was sipping a small gin and tonic and watching with lowering gaze the massive imperturbability of Ferris, the butler. Ferris, though he obviously disapproved of wedding-presents, was keeping a loyal eye on them.

===Chinese===

“不了，谢谢你，妈妈，”乔治说。

“我没在跟你说话，”沃丁顿夫人说，“我是在同沃尔斯先生讲话。他坐了长途汽车而来，想必需要用点茶点。”

“当然，当然，”乔治说，“我这是想的哪儿去了！对，你一定得好好垫一垫，把力气攒足。婚礼进行到一半，我们可不想看你晕过去。”

“他要当真晕过去，那是十足情有可原的，”沃丁顿夫人说。

她头前带路进了餐室。茶点小食已在一张餐具柜上摆开——这张餐具柜因为有西格斯比·H·沃丁顿在场而蓬荜生辉：他正呷着一小杯金汤力（gin and tonic），阴沉沉地注视着男管家费里斯那庞大身躯的岿然不动。费里斯尽管分明不赞成结婚礼物，倒还是忠心耿耿地替它们看着。

===Original===

“What are you doing here, Ferris?” asked Mrs. Waddington.

The butler raised the loyal eye.

“Guarding the gifts, madam.”

“Who told you to?”

“Mr. Finch, madam.”

===Chinese===

“你在这儿干什么，费里斯？”沃丁顿夫人问。

男管家抬起了那只忠心耿耿的眼睛。

“看着礼物呢，夫人。”

“谁叫你看的？”

“芬奇先生，夫人。”

===Original===

Mrs. Waddington shot a look of disgust at George.

“There is no necessity whatever.”

“Very good, madam.”

“Only an imbecile would have suggested such a thing.”

“Precisely, madam.”

===Chinese===

沃丁顿夫人朝乔治射去一道嫌恶的目光。

“压根儿没有这个必要。”

“遵命，夫人。”

“只有傻子才会出这种主意。”

“正是，夫人。”

===Original===

The butler retired. Sigsbee Horatio, watching him go, sighed unhappily. What was the good of him going now? felt Sigsbee. From now on the room would be full. Already automobiles were beginning to arrive, and a swarm of wedding-guests had begun to settle upon the refreshments on the side-table.

The Rev. Gideon Voules, thoughtfully lowering a milk and ham-sandwich into the abyss, had drawn George into a corner and was endeavouring to make his better acquaintance.

“I always like to have a little chat with the bridegroom before the ceremony,” he said. “It is agreeable to be able to feel that he is, in a sense, a personal friend.”

“Very nice of you,” said George, touched.

“I married a young fellow in Flushing named Miglett the other day﻿—Claude R. Miglett. Perhaps you recall the name?”

===Chinese===

男管家退下了。西格斯比·霍雷肖目送他离去，怏怏地叹了口气。事到如今他还走，又有什么用呢？西格斯比暗想。打这以后，这屋里就要挤满人了。汽车已经陆续抵达，一大群贺喜的宾客也已经开始像蝇群似的朝餐具柜上的茶点落了脚。

吉迪恩·沃尔斯牧师一边若有所思地把一份牛奶和火腿三明治送进深渊，一边把乔治拽到一个角落里，竭力要跟他多结识亲近几分。

“我向来喜欢在仪式开始前同新郎聊几句小天，”他说，“能觉得新郎在某种意义上是一位私交好友，这是件惬意的事。”

“你真是太好了，”乔治说，颇为感动。

“前几天我在法拉盛给一个年轻人主持了婚礼，此人姓米格利特——克劳德·R·米格利特（Claude R. Miglett）。也许你还记得这个名字？”

===Original===

“No.”

“Ah! I thought you might have seen it in the papers. They were full of the affair. I always feel that, if I had not made a point of establishing personal relations with him before the ceremony, I should not have been in a position to comfort him as I did after the accident occurred.”

“Accident?”

“Yes. The bride was most unfortunately killed by a motor-lorry as they were leaving the church.”

“Good heavens!”

===Chinese===

“不记得。”

“啊！我还当兴许在报上见过呢。当年报纸上登满了这桩事。我一向觉得，倘若我不曾特意在仪式之前同他建立起私交，那么出了事故以后，我就不能像后来那样安慰他了。”

“事故？”

“是啊。他们正要离开教堂，新娘就不幸被一辆卡车轧死了。”

“天哪！”

===Original===

“I have always thought it singularly unfortunate. But then it almost seems as if there were some fatality about the weddings at which I officiate. Only a week before, I had married a charming young couple, and both were dead before the month was out. A girder fell on them as they were passing a building which was under construction. In the case of another pair whom I married earlier in the year, the bridegroom contracted some form of low fever. A very fine young fellow. He came out in pink spots. We were all most distressed about it.” He turned to Mrs. Waddington, whom an inrush of guests had driven into the corner. “I was telling our young friend here of a rather singular coincidence. In each of the last two weddings at which I officiated the bridegroom died within a few days of the ceremony.”

A wistful look came into Mrs. Waddington’s face. She seemed to be feeling that luck like that could not hold.

“I, personally,” she said, “have had a presentiment right from the beginning that this marriage would never take place.”

===Chinese===

“我一向觉得那件事倒霉得出奇。不过话说回来，凡我主持的婚礼，几乎总像带着点儿劫数难逃的味道。就在那之前一个礼拜，我刚为一对可爱的小夫妇证了婚，不出一个月，两人双双殒命。他们路过一栋在造的楼房时，一根大梁砸了下来，正落在他们身上。至于今年早些时候我证的另一对，新郎染上了某种低热。一个非常好的小伙子。浑身出了一层粉红色的斑点。我们大家都为此难过极了。”他转向沃丁顿夫人——一批客人蜂拥而入，把她挤到了角落里。“我正在给我们这位年轻朋友讲一桩相当蹊跷的巧合。最近两回经我主持的婚礼，新郎都在仪式过后没几天便死去了。”

沃丁顿夫人脸上浮起一副悠然神往的神色。她似乎在暗想，这样的好运道怕是难以长久。

“拿我本人来说，”她说，“打一开始就有一种预感，觉得这桩婚事根本办不成。”

===Original===

“Now, that is very curious,” said the Rev. Gideon. “I am a strong believer in presentiments.”

“So am I.”

“I think they are sent to warn us﻿—to help us to prepare ourselves for disaster.”

“In the present instance,” said Mrs. Waddington, “the word disaster is not the one I would have selected.”

===Chinese===

“哎，这就怪了，”吉迪恩牧师说，“我向来对预感深信不疑。”

“我也是。”

“我认为预感是上天差来警示我们的——好帮我们未雨绸缪，为灾祸早作准备。”

“就眼下这件事而论，”沃丁顿夫人说，“我挑的词儿可不是‘灾祸’。”

===Original===

George tottered away. Once more there was creeping over him that grey foreboding which had come to him earlier in the day. So reduced was his nervous system that he actually sought comfort in the society of Sigsbee Horatio. After all, he thought, whatever Sigsbee’s shortcomings as a man, he at least was a friend. A philosopher with the future of the race at heart might sigh as he looked upon Sigsbee H. Waddington, but in a bleak world George could not pick and choose his chums.

A moment later there was forced upon him the unpleasing discovery that in supposing that Mr. Waddington liked him he had been altogether too optimistic. The look which his future father-in-law bestowed upon him as he sidled up was not one of affection. It was the sort of look which, had he been sheriff of Gory Gulch, Arizona, the elder man might have bestowed upon a horse-thief.

“Darned officious!” rumbled Sigsbee H., in a querulous undertone. “Officious and meddling.”

“Eh?” said George.

“Telling that butler to come in here and watch the presents.”

===Chinese===

乔治踉踉跄跄地走开了。那股灰蒙蒙的不祥预感——今天早些时候曾来造访过他的——又一次悄悄爬上了心头。他的神经系统已经衰弱到这般地步，竟真的跑到西格斯比·霍雷肖身边寻求慰藉去了。他想，不管西格斯比作为一个人有多少短处，他至少是个朋友。一位心系人类前途的哲学家，见到西格斯比·H·沃丁顿或许不免要喟然长叹，可在这苍凉的尘世间，乔治交朋友已由不得自己挑拣了。

片刻之后，一桩败兴的发现便强塞给了他：他原以为沃丁顿先生喜欢自己，这实在是乐观得过了头。未来岳父侧身蹭到他跟前时投来的那副眼神，可算不上什么慈爱。那眼神，倘若这位长辈是亚利桑那州血腥峡谷（Gory Gulch）的警长，多半会赏给一个偷马贼。

“好家伙，真多管闲事！”西格斯比·H忿忿不平地低声嘟囔，“多管闲事，横插一杠子。”

“嗯？”乔治说。

“叫那个男管家进来守着礼物。”

===Original===

“But, good heavens, don’t you realise that, if I hadn’t told him, someone might have sneaked in and stolen something?”

Mr. Waddington’s expression was now that of a cowboy who, leaping into bed, discovers too late that a frolicsome friend has placed a cactus between the sheets: and George, at the lowest ebb, was about to pass on to the refreshment-table and see if a little potato-salad might not act as a restorative, when there stepped from the crowd gathered round the food a large and ornately dressed person chewing the remains of a slab of caviar on toast. George had a dim recollection of having seen him among the guests at that first dinner-party at Number 16, East Seventy-Ninth Street. His memory had not erred. The newcomer was no less a man than United Beef.

“Hello there, Waddington,” said United Beef.

“Ur,” said Sigsbee Horatio. He did not like the other, who had once refused to lend him money and﻿—what was more﻿—had gone to the mean length of quoting Shakespeare to support his refusal.

“Say, Waddington,” proceeded United Beef, “don’t I seem to remember you coming to me sometime ago and asking about that motion-picture company, the Finer and Better? You were thinking of putting some money in, if I recollect?”

===Chinese===

“可是，我的天哪，你难道没意识到，我要是不叫他，说不定就有人溜进来偷走点什么？”

沃丁顿先生此刻的表情，活像一个牛仔，纵身跳上床铺，却发觉得太迟——原来一个促狭的朋友在被单之间搁了株仙人掌；而乔治，情绪已跌到最低谷，正打算踱到茶点桌那边去，看看一点土豆沙拉能不能当作强心针，恰在此时，围在食品旁的人群里跨出来一个身躯魁伟、衣着花哨的人物，嘴里嚼着一片鱼子酱吐司的残部。乔治依稀记得，在东七十九街 16 号那第一场晚宴的宾客中间见过此人。他的记性没有出错。这位来客不是别人，正是联合牛肉。

“哟，沃丁顿，”联合牛肉说。

“呃，”西格斯比·霍雷肖说。他不喜欢对方。此公曾经拒绝借钱给他，而且——更有甚者——竟刻薄到引用莎士比亚来给自己的拒绝撑腰。

“我说，沃丁顿，”联合牛肉接着道，“我记得仿佛前些日子你来找过我，打听那家‘更精更美’电影公司？要是我没记错，你正琢磨着往里搭点儿钱？”

===Original===

An expression of acute alarm shot into Mr. Waddington’s face. He gulped painfully.

“Not me,” he said hastily. “Not me. Get it out of your nut that it was I who wanted to buy the stuff. I just thought that if the stock was any good my dear wife might be interested.”

“Same thing.”

“It is not at all the same thing.”

“Do you happen to know if your wife bought any?”

===Chinese===

一副惊恐万状的神色倏地爬上沃丁顿先生的脸。他艰难地咽了一口唾沫。

“不是我，”他慌忙说，“不是我。快把‘想买那玩意儿的是我’这个念头从你脑袋里扔出去。我只不过是寻思，那股票要是真值当，我亲爱的太太没准儿会有兴趣。”

“一码事。”

“根本不是一码事。”

“你可知道你太太买了没有？”

===Original===

“No, she didn’t. I heard later that the company was no good, so I did not mention it to her.”

“Too bad,” said United Beef. “Too bad.”

“What do you mean, too bad?”

“Well, a rather remarkable thing has happened. Quite a romance in its way. As a motion-picture company the thing was, as you say, no good. Couldn’t seem to do anything right. But yesterday, when a workman started to dig a hole on the lot to put up a ‘For Sale’ sign, I’m darned if he didn’t strike oil.”

The solid outlines of United Beef shimmered uncertainly before Mr. Waddington’s horrified eyes.

===Chinese===

“没有，她没买。我后来听说那家公司不怎么样，就没跟她提。”

“可惜，”联合牛肉说，“可惜。”

“你说可惜，是什么意思？”

“是这么回事，出了一件顶顶不寻常的事，就其本身而言也算得上一段传奇。作为一家电影公司，这家伙就像你说的，不成器，干什么都不顺当。可就在昨天，有个工人开工在那块地皮上挖坑，要竖一块‘待售’的牌子，你猜怎么着——他居然挖出了石油，真是活见鬼。”

联合牛肉那敦实的轮廓，在沃丁顿先生惊骇的眼前晃动得影影绰绰、不甚真切起来。

===Original===

“Oil?” he gurgled.

“Yes, sir. Oil. What looks like turning out the biggest gusher in the southwest.”

“But﻿—but﻿—do you mean to say, then, that the shares are﻿—are really worth something?”

“Only millions, that’s all. Merely millions. It’s a pity you didn’t buy some. This caviar,” said United Beef, champing meditatively, “is good. That’s what it is, Waddington﻿—good. I think I’ll have another slice.”

It is difficult to arrest the progress of a millionaire who is starting off in the direction of caviar, but Mr. Waddington, with a frenzied clutch at the other’s coat-sleeve, succeeded in doing so for a brief instant.

===Chinese===

“石油？”他咕哝出声。

“没错，先生。石油。瞧那架势，眼看要成为整个西南部最大的一口自喷井。”

“可——可——那么你的意思是说，那些股票——果真值钱了？”

“不过值上几百万罢了，仅此而已。区区几百万。可惜你当年没买上几股。这鱼子酱，”联合牛肉若有所思地嚼着，“好吃。就是这么回事，沃丁顿——好吃。我想再来一片。”

要拦阻一位正朝鱼子酱进发的百万富翁，谈何容易；但沃丁顿先生发狂似的一把攥住对方的衣袖，居然成功地拦停了那么一瞬。

===Original===

“When did you hear this?”

“Just as I was starting out this morning?”

“Do you think anybody else knows about it?”

“Everybody downtown, I should say.”

“But, listen,” said Mr. Waddington urgently. “Say, listen!” He clung to the caviar-maddened man’s sleeve with a desperate grip. “What I am getting at is, I know a guy﻿—nothing to do with business﻿—who has a block of that stock. Do you think there’s any chance of him not having heard about this?”

===Chinese===

“你什么时候听说的？”

“就在今天早上出门的时候？”

“你看还有别人知道这码事吗？”

“要我说，下城那边早就人人皆知了。”

“可是，听着，”沃丁顿先生急切地说，“喂，听着！”他死命攥住那位被鱼子酱迷得神魂颠倒的人的袖子。“我想问的是，我认识一个人——跟生意八竿子打不着——手里攥着一大笔那种股票。你说有没有可能，他还没听说这桩消息？”

===Original===

“Quite likely. But, if you’re thinking of getting it off him, you’d better hurry. The story is probably in the evening papers by now.”

The words acted on Sigsbee H. Waddington like an electric shock. He released the other’s sleeve, and United Beef shot off towards the refreshment-table like a homing pigeon. Mr. Waddington felt in his hip-pocket to make sure that he still possessed the three hundred dollars which he had hoped that day to hand over to Fanny Welch, and bounded out of the room, out of the house, and out of the front gate; and, after bounding along the broad highway to the station, leaped into a train which might have been meeting him there by appointment. Never in his life before had Sigsbee H. Waddington caught a train so expeditiously: and the fact seemed to him a happy omen. He looked forward with a cheery confidence to the interview with that policeman fellow to whom he had﻿—in a moment of mistaken generosity﻿—parted with his precious stock. The policeman had seemed a simple sort of soul, just the sort of man with whom it is so nice to do business. Mr. Waddington began to rehearse the opening speeches of the interview.

“Say, listen,” he would say. “Say, listen, my dear.﻿ ﻿…”

He sat up in his seat with a jerk. He had completely forgotten the policeman’s name.

===Chinese===

“大有可能。不过，你若是打算从他手里把股票弄回来，最好抓紧。这消息这会儿八成已经见报，上了晚报。”

这番话落在西格斯比·H·沃丁顿身上，不啻一道电击。他松开对方的衣袖，联合牛肉便如归巢的鸽子一般朝茶点桌飞奔而去。沃丁顿先生伸手往裤后袋摸了摸，确认那三百美元仍在——他原指望今天就把这笔钱交到芬妮·韦尔奇手上——随即蹦出了餐室，蹦出了宅子，又蹦出了大门；沿着通向车站的宽阔大道一路蹦去之后，跳进了一列车厢，那趟车简直像约好了在那儿恭候他似的。西格斯比·H·沃丁顿有生以来从没这样利索地赶上过一趟火车：这个事实在他看来是个吉兆。他满怀愉快的信心，盼着同那位警察老兄的会面——就是那个他一时错用了慷慨、把宝贝股票拱手让出去的家伙。那警察瞧着是个头脑简单的老实人，正是那种打起交道来顶舒服的主儿。沃丁顿先生开始排练起会面时的开场白来。

“喂，听着，”他会这么说，“喂，听着，我亲爱的……”

他猛地在座位上坐直了身子。那位警察的名字，他忘了个一干二净。

## XII / 第十二章

===Original===
Several hours later, when the stars had begun to peep out and the birds were rustling sleepily in the trees, a solitary figure might have been observed moving slowly up the drive towards the front door of the Waddington summer-residence at Hempstead, Long Island. It was Sigsbee H., returning from his travels.

He walked apprehensively, like a cat that expects a half-brick. Oh, sings the poet, to be home again, home again, home again: but Sigsbee H. Waddington could not bring himself to share that sunny viewpoint. With the opportunity for quiet reflection there had come to him the numbing realisation that beneath the roof before him trouble waited. On other occasions while serving his second sentence as a married man he had done things of which his wife had disapproved﻿—and of which she had expressed her disapproval in a manner that was frank and unrestrained: but never before had he committed such a domestic crime as the one beneath the burden of which he was staggering now. He had actually absented himself from the wedding of his only child after having been specifically instructed to give her away at the altar: and if on a theme like this his wife did not extend herself in a fashion calculated to stagger Humanity﻿—well, all Sigsbee H. could say was that past form meant nothing and could be ruled out as a guide completely.

He sighed drearily. He felt depressed and battered, in no mood to listen to home-truths about himself. All he wanted was to be alone on a sofa with his shoes off and something to drink at his elbow. For he had had a trying time in the great city.

===Chinese===
几个钟头之后，星星已开始探头探脑地露面，鸟儿在树丛里睡意惺忪地窸窣作响，这时兴许有人瞧见一个孤零零的身影，正沿着车道缓缓走向沃丁顿家坐落在长岛亨普斯特德的消夏宅邸正门。正是西格斯比·H，他游历归来了。

他走得提心吊胆，活像一只料到会有半块砖头当头飞来的猫。哦，回家啦，回家啦，回家啦——诗人如此吟唱；可西格斯比·H·沃丁顿怎么也打不起精神，去附和那般明媚的见解。静心沉思的机会一来，一个叫人浑身发麻的觉悟也跟着来了：眼前这片屋顶底下，正有麻烦恭候着他。以往在他第二次服婚姻刑期的当口，他也干过太太不赞成的事情——而太太表达不赞成的方式，向来是直截了当、毫无节制的：可他还从来没有犯下过眼下这样一桩家庭罪行——这桩罪的分量，正压得他步履蹒跚。他竟未出席独生女的婚礼，而他明明受过明确指示，要在圣坛前亲手把新娘交出去；而倘若这样一个题目还不足以让他太太倾力发挥、发挥到足以震垮全人类的地步——那么西格斯比·H只有一句话好说：前科说明不了任何问题，必须把它从参考依据里一笔勾销。

他凄然叹了口气。他只觉得意气消沉、遍体鳞伤，全无心情听人对他数落那些逆耳的家里真话。他一心想要的，不过是独自瘫在一张沙发上，脱掉鞋子，手肘边搁点喝的。因为这一趟在大都会里，他着实受了罪。

===Original===
Sigsbee H. Waddington, as has perhaps been sufficiently indicated in this narrative, was not a man who could think deeply without getting a headache: but even at the expense of an aching head he had been compelled to do some very deep thinking as he journeyed to New York in the train. From somewhere in the muddy depths of his subconsciousness it was imperative that he should bring to the surface the name of the policeman to whom he had sold that stock. He started the dredging operations immediately, and by the time the train had reached the Pennsylvania Station had succeeded in narrowing the search down to this extent﻿—that he felt sure the man was called either Mulcahy or Garrity.

Now, a man who goes about New York looking for a policeman named Mulcahy has quite an afternoon’s work in hand. So has the man who seeks a Garrity. For one who pursues both there is not a dull moment. Flitting hither and thither about the city and questioning the various officers he encountered, Sigsbee H. Waddington soon began to cover ground. The policeman on point duty in Times Square said that there was a Mulcahy up near Grant’s Tomb and a choice of Garritys at Columbus Circle and Irving Place. The Grant’s Tomb Mulcahy, expressing regret that he could not himself supply the happy ending, recommended the Hundred-and-Twenty-Fifth Street Mulcahy or﻿—alternately﻿—the one down on Third Avenue and Sixteenth. The Garrity at Columbus Circle spoke highly of a Garrity near the Battery, and the Garrity at Irving Place seemed to think his cousin up in the Bronx might fill the long-felt want. By this time the clocks were striking five, Mr. Waddington had come definitely to the decision that what the world wanted to make it a place fit for heroes to live in was fewer and better Mulcahys. At five-thirty, returning from the Bronx, he would have supported any amendment to the Constitution which Congress might have cared to introduce, totally prohibiting Garritys. At six sharp, he became suddenly convinced that the name of the man he sought was Murphy.

He was passing through Madison Square at the moment, having just flushed Fourteenth Street for another Mulcahy: and so deeply did this new idea affect him that he tottered to one of the benches and, sitting down, groaned heavily. It was the breaking-point. Mr. Waddington decided to give it up and go home. His head was aching, his feet were aching, and the small of his back was aching. The first fine careless rapture with which he had started his quest had ebbed away to nothing. In short, if there was one man in New York utterly incapable of going about the place looking for Murphys, that man was Sigsbee H. Waddington. He limped to the Pennsylvania Station and took the next train home, and here he was, approaching journey’s end.

===Chinese===
西格斯比·H·沃丁顿，正如本篇叙事大概已经交代得足够明白的那样，不是那种能深入思考而不闹头疼的人；可即便要以头疼为代价，他乘火车奔赴纽约的一路，也不得不做了些极为深入的思考。当务之急，是要从他潜意识那浑浊的深处，把那个买了他那只股票的警察的名字打捞到水面上来。疏浚作业随即破土动工；到火车抵达宾夕法尼亚车站（Pennsylvania Station）时，搜索范围已然缩小到了这个地步——他有十足把握，那人不是姓马尔卡希（Mulcahy），就是姓加里蒂（Garrity）。

要知道，一个在纽约满城奔走、要找一名姓马尔卡希的警察的人，手头可有足足一个下午的活儿要干。要找一位加里蒂的人，亦是如此。而两位一齐找的人，则一刻也不会闷。西格斯比·H·沃丁顿满城飞来飞去，逢警必问，不久便大有进展。时代广场（Times Square）站岗执勤的警察说，格兰特陵墓（Grant’s Tomb）附近住着一位马尔卡希，哥伦布圆环（Columbus Circle）和欧文街（Irving Place）则各有加里蒂可供挑选。格兰特陵墓那位马尔卡希深表遗憾，说是无法亲自成全这段圆满结局，随即推荐了一百二十五街的马尔卡希，再不然——二选一——第三大道和十六街口的那一位。哥伦布圆环的加里蒂对炮台公园（the Battery）附近的一位加里蒂推崇备至，而欧文街的加里蒂似乎觉得，他远在布朗克斯（the Bronx）的表亲或许能填补这个渴望已久的空缺。到这时，大钟敲响了五点，沃丁顿先生已断然拿定主意：这个世界要想成为适合英雄居住的地方，所缺的就是更少、更精的马尔卡希。五点半，从布朗克斯折返的路上，凡是国会乐意提出的全面禁绝加里蒂的宪法修正案，他一概愿意举手拥护。六点整，他忽然深信不疑：他要找的那个人，姓墨菲（Murphy）。

此刻他正穿过麦迪逊广场（Madison Square），刚把十四街从头到尾轰了一遍，指望再轰出一名马尔卡希；这个新念头对他打击太深，害他踉踉跄跄挪到一条长椅跟前，一屁股坐下，重重地呻吟起来。崩溃的临界点到了。沃丁顿先生决定就此罢手，回家去。他头疼，脚疼，后腰也疼。他踏上寻人征途时那第一阵无忧无虑的欢畅，已退潮退得干干净净。总而言之，倘若纽约城里有一个压根没本事满城奔走去找墨菲们的人，那个人就是西格斯比·H·沃丁顿。他一瘸一拐挪到宾夕法尼亚车站，搭上下一班车回家——于是便有了此刻的他，正一步步走近旅程的终点。

===Original===
The house, as he drew near, seemed very silent. And, of course, it had every right to be. Long since, the wedding must have taken place and the happy pair departed on their honeymoon. Long since, the last guest must have left. And now, beneath that quiet roof, there remained only Mrs. Waddington, no doubt trying out blistering phrases in the seclusion of her boudoir﻿—here, discarding an incandescent adjective in favour of a still zippier one that had just suggested itself; there, realising that the noun “worm” was too mild and searching in Roget’s Thesaurus for something more expressive. Mr. Waddington paused on the doorstep, half inclined to make for the solitude of the tool-shed.

Manlier counsels prevailed. In the tool-shed there would be nothing to drink, and, cost what it might, a drink was what his suffering soul demanded. He crossed the threshold, and leaped nimbly as a dark figure suddenly emerged from the telephone-booth.

“Oosh!” said Mr. Waddington.

“Sir?” said the figure.

===Chinese===
走近了，那座房子显得分外安静。当然，它完全有理由安静。早在许久之前，婚礼必定已经礼成，新婚燕尔的一对也必定已经动身度蜜月去了。早在许久之前，最后一位宾客也必定已经告辞。而此刻，那片静谧的屋顶底下，只剩下沃丁顿夫人一人，毫无疑问，正在闺房的清静独处里试炼火辣辣的词句——在这里，撇下一个白热化的形容词，换上脑子里刚冒出来的一个还要带劲的；在那里，发觉“虫豸”这个名词未免失之温和，正在《罗热同义词词典》（Roget’s Thesaurus）里翻找更有表现力的字眼。沃丁顿先生在门阶前停住脚，有半颗心已经打算奔工具棚的清净而去。

更有男子气概的考虑占了上风。工具棚里可没有酒，而不论付出什么代价，一杯下肚是他这受苦的灵魂此刻的迫切要求。他跨过门槛，身手敏捷地蹿了起来——一个黑影突然从电话亭里冒了出来。

“嗬唷！”沃丁顿先生叫道。

“先生？”那黑影说。

===Original===
Mr. Waddington felt relieved. It was not his wife. It was Ferris. And Ferris was the one person he particularly wanted at that moment to meet. For it was Ferris who could most expeditiously bring him something to drink.

“Sh!” whispered Sigsbee H. “Anyone about?”

“Sir?”

“Where is Mrs. Waddington?”

“In her boudoir, sir.”

Sigsbee H. had expected as much.

===Chinese===
沃丁顿先生放下心来。不是他太太，是费里斯。而费里斯正是他此刻最想见的人。因为能以最快速度给他弄点喝的来的，非费里斯莫属。

“嘘！”西格斯比·H压低了嗓门。“附近有人没有？”

“先生？”

“沃丁顿夫人在哪儿？”

“在她闺房里，先生。”

西格斯比·H早已料到如此。

===Original===
“Anyone in the library?”

“No, sir.”

“Then bring me a drink in there, Ferris. And don’t tell anybody you’ve seen me.”

“Very good, sir.”

Mr. Waddington shambled to the library and flung himself down on the chesterfield. Delicious, restful moments passed, and then a musical tinkling made itself heard without. Ferris entered with a tray.

===Chinese===
“书房里有人吗？”

“没有，先生。”

“那就给我送一杯喝的到那儿去，费里斯。还有，别告诉任何人你见过我。”

“遵命，先生。”

沃丁顿先生拖着脚步蹭进书房，一头栽进那张切斯特菲尔德沙发（chesterfield）里。美滋滋、懒洋洋的片刻时光过去了，随后门外传来一声悦耳的叮当。费里斯端着托盘进来了。

===Original===
“You omitted to give me definite instructions, sir,” said the butler, “so, acting on my own initiative, I have brought the whisky-decanter and some charged water.”

He spoke coldly, for he disapproved of Mr. Waddington. But the latter was in no frame of mind to analyse the verbal nuances of butlers. He clutched at the decanter, his eyes moist with gratitude.

“Splendid fellow, Ferris!”

“Thank you, sir.”

“You’re the sort of fellow who ought to be out West, where men are men.”

===Chinese===
“您没有给我明确的指示，先生，”管家说，“所以我自作主张，带来了威士忌细颈酒瓶和一些苏打水。”

他语气冷冰冰的，因为他看不惯沃丁顿先生。可后者此刻的心绪，已顾不上去剖析管家言辞间的微妙差异了。他一把抓住细颈酒瓶，两眼闪着感激的泪光。

“好样的，费里斯！”

“谢谢您，先生。”

“你这种家伙就该上西部去，在那儿男人才是男人。”

===Original===
The butler twitched a frosty eyebrow.

“Will that be all, sir?”

“Yes. But don’t go, Ferris. Tell me about everything.”

“On what particular point did you desire information, sir?”

===Chinese===
管家冷冰冰地抖了抖一边眉毛。

“没有别的吩咐了吗，先生？”

“没有了。不过别走，费里斯。把一切都讲给我听。”

“您想了解哪一方面的具体情况呢，先生？”

===Original===
“Tell me about the wedding. I wasn’t able to be present. I had most important business in New York, Ferris. So I wasn’t able to be present. Because I had most important business in New York.”

“Indeed, sir?”

“Most important business. Impossible to neglect it. Did the wedding go off all right?”

“Not altogether, sir.”

===Chinese===
“跟我说说婚礼。我没能出席。我在纽约有顶顶要紧的事，费里斯。所以我没能出席。因为我在纽约有顶顶要紧的事。”

“哦，是这样吗，先生？”

“顶顶要紧的事。万万耽误不得。婚礼办得还顺利吗？”

“不能说全然顺利，先生。”

===Original===
“What do you mean?”

“There has been no wedding, sir.”

Mr. Waddington sat up. The butler appeared to be babbling. And the one moment when a man does not want to mix with babbling butlers is immediately after he has returned home from a search through New York for a policeman named Mulcahy or Garrity.

===Chinese===
“你这是什么意思？”

“婚礼压根儿没有办成，先生。”

沃丁顿先生霍地坐直了身子。管家看来是在胡言乱语。而一个人刚刚为寻一名姓马尔卡希或加里蒂的警察搜遍纽约、踏上家门的那一刻，最不想与之打交道的，恰恰就是满嘴胡话的管家。

===Original===
“No wedding?”

“No, sir.”

“Why not?”

“At the last moment a hitch occurred, sir.”

“Don’t tell me the new clergyman sprained his ankle, too?”

===Chinese===
“没有婚礼？”

“没有，先生。”

“为什么没有？”

“临到最后一刻出了岔子，先生。”

“别告诉我是新请来的牧师又扭了脚脖子？”

===Original===
“No, sir. The presiding minister continues to enjoy good health in every respect. The hitch to which I allude was caused by a young woman who, claiming to be an old friend of the bridegroom, entered the room where the guests were assembled and created some little disturbance, sir.”

Mr. Waddington’s eyes bulged.

“Tell me about this,” he said.

===Chinese===
“不是，先生。主礼牧师方方面面依旧安康。我所说的那处岔子，起因于一位年轻女子。她自称是新郎的一位旧交，闯进了宾客齐聚的房间，闹出了点小小的乱子，先生。”

沃丁顿先生的眼睛鼓了出来。

“把这事讲给我听。”他说。

===Original===
The butler fixed a fathomless gaze on the wall beyond him.

“I was not actually present at the scene myself, sir. But one of the lower servants, who chanced to be glancing in at the door, has apprised me of the details of the occurrence. It appears that, just as the wedding-party was about to start off for the church, a young woman suddenly made her way through the French windows opening to the lawn, and, pausing in the entrance, observed ‘George! George! Why did you desert me? You don’t belong to that girl there. You belong to me﻿—the woman you have wronged!’ Addressing Mr. Finch, I gather.”

Mr. Waddington’s eyes were now protruding to such a dangerous extent that a sharp jerk would have caused them to drop off.

===Chinese===
管家把深不可测的目光钉在他身后那面墙上。

“我本人其实并未亲临现场，先生。不过一名下房仆役碰巧朝门里望了一眼，把事情的底细禀报给了我。看来是这样的：就在婚礼一行人正要动身去教堂的时候，一位年轻女子忽然从通向草坪的落地长窗里走了进来，在门口站定，朗声说道——‘乔治！乔治！你为什么抛弃我？你不属于那边那个姑娘。你属于我——属于被你亏负了的女人！’据我揣摩，是说给芬奇先生听的。”

此刻沃丁顿先生的眼睛已经凸到了危险的地步，只消猛地一颠，就会把它们整个儿颠出眼窝。

===Original===
“Sweet suffering soupspoons! What happened then?”

“There was considerable uproar and confusion, so my informant tells me. The bridegroom was noticeably taken aback, and protested with some urgency that it was all a mistake. To which Mrs. Waddington replied that it was just what she had foreseen all along. Miss Waddington, I gather, was visibly affected. And the guests experienced no little embarrassment.”

“I don’t blame them.”

“No, sir.”

===Chinese===
“受苦受难的甜心汤匙们哪！后来怎么样？”

“据报信人告诉我，当时闹得沸反盈天，乱作一团。新郎分明吓了一大跳，急赤白脸地声明这全是一场误会。对此，沃丁顿夫人回答说，这正是她自始至终预见到了的。沃丁顿小姐，据我揣摩，显然深受触动。宾客们则着实尴尬得不轻。”

“我不怪他们。”

“可不是，先生。”

===Original===
“And then?”

“The young woman was pressed for details, but appeared to be in an overwrought and highly emotional condition. She screamed, so my informant tells me, and wrung her hands. She staggered about the room and, collapsing on the table where the wedding-presents had been placed, seemed to swoon. Almost immediately afterwards, however, she appeared to recover herself: and, remarking ‘Air! Air! I want air!’ departed hastily through the French windows. I understand, sir, that nothing was seen of her after that.”

“And what happened then?”

“Mrs. Waddington refused to permit the wedding to take place. The guests returned to New York. Mr. Finch, after uttering certain protests which my informant could not hear distinctly but which appear to have been incoherent and unconvincing, also took his departure. Mrs. Waddington has for some little time past been closeted in the boudoir with Miss Waddington. A very unpleasant affair, sir, and one which could never have occurred at Brangmarley Hall.”

===Chinese===
“后来呢？”

“众人逼问那年轻女子细节，可她看上去神经紧张、情绪激动得厉害。她尖声喊叫，两手拧绞——据报信人告诉我——又在屋里跌跌撞撞地转悠，末了一头栽倒在摆着结婚贺礼的桌子上，像是晕了过去。然而几乎紧接着，她似乎又缓了过来，说了声——‘空气！空气！我要空气！’——便匆匆忙忙从落地长窗里出去了。据我所知，先生，打那以后就再没有人见过她。”

“那后来又怎么样？”

“沃丁顿夫人拒绝让婚礼继续举行。宾客们返回了纽约。芬奇先生发了几句抗议——报信人没能听真切，不过听上去语无伦次、毫无说服力——随后也走了。沃丁顿夫人这一阵子一直在闺房里同沃丁顿小姐闭门密谈。一桩很不愉快的事，先生。这种事，在布兰马利庄园是无论如何发生不了的。”

===Original===
One hates to have to record it, but it is a fact that the first emotion which came to Sigsbee H. Waddington after the waning of his initial amazement was relief. It was not the thought of this broken romance that occupied his mind, nor pity for the poor girl who had played the principal part in the tragedy. The aspect of the matter that touched him most nearly was the fact that he was not in for trouble, after all. His absence had probably escaped notice, and that wifely lecture to which he had been looking forward so apprehensively would never be delivered.

And then, cutting through relief, came a sudden thought that chilled his satisfaction.

“What sort of a girl was it that came in through the window?”

“My informant describes her as small, sir, and of a neat figure. She had a retroussé nose and expressive black eyes, sir.”

===Chinese===
写这一笔实在令人不快，然而事实终归是事实：最初的惊愕退潮之后，头一种涌上西格斯比·H·沃丁顿心头的情绪，竟是如释重负。盘踞在他心头的，不是这段良缘就此破碎的念头，也不是对那位在这场悲剧中担纲主角的可怜姑娘的怜悯。这件事最切中他痛处的一面，是他终究不必大祸临头。他的缺席多半没有引人注意，而他一路提心吊胆、只等领受的那场贤妻训话，看来永远不会开讲了。

可紧接着，一个猝然而至的念头划破了如释重负，把他的满足浇了个冰凉。

“打窗户里闯进来的那个姑娘，是个什么模样？”

“报信人形容她个子娇小，先生，身段匀称。她生着一个翘鼻子，还有一双会说话的黑眼睛，先生。”

===Original===
“Great Godfrey!” ejaculated Mr. Waddington.

He sprang from the sofa and, despite his aching feet, made good time along the hall. He ran into the dining-room and switched on the light. He darted across the room to the table where the wedding-presents lay. At first glance, they seemed to be all there, but a second look showed him that his suspicions had been well-founded.

The case containing the necklace was gone.

===Chinese===
“戈弗雷老天爷呀！”沃丁顿先生脱口叫道。

他从沙发上弹了起来，顾不得两脚酸痛，沿着门厅一路疾走。他冲进餐厅，啪地拧亮了灯。他一个箭步蹿过房间，直奔摆着结婚贺礼的那张桌子。乍一看去，贺礼似乎一件不少；可再定睛一瞧便向他证实，他的疑心绝非空穴来风。

盛着项链的那只盒子不见了。

## XIII / 第十三章

===Original===
One of the most sustaining gifts a man can possess is the ability to look upon the bright side of disaster. It was a gift which, until now, Sigsbee H. Waddington had lacked almost entirely: but at this moment, owing perhaps to the fact that he had just introduced into his interior a healing drink of quite exceptional strength, he suddenly found himself discerning with a limpid clearness the fact that the elimination of that near-pearl necklace from the scheme of things was, from his point of view, the very best thing that could have happened.

It had not been his intention to allow his young assistant to secure the necklace and convert it to her own uses: but, now that this had happened, what, he asked himself, had he to worry about? The main thing was that the necklace had disappeared. Coming right down to it, that was the consummation at which he had aimed all along.

What it amounted to was that, when all the tumult and the shouting had died, he was three hundred dollars in hand and consequently in a position, if he ever met that policeman again and the policeman had not happened to hear the news which United Beef had told him, to.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
一个人所能拥有的诸般禀赋里，最足以支撑人心的一种，便是从灾祸之中看出光明一面的本事。这份本事，西格斯比·H·沃丁顿在此之前几乎全然阙如；可就在此刻，也许是由于他方才往体内输送了一杯劲儿出奇地足的疗伤饮品，他忽然发现自己以澄澈无比的目力看清了一桩事实：把那串赝珍珠项链（near-pearl necklace）从世事格局中除掉，照他看来，实在是天底下顶顶美满的事。

听任他那位年轻的女助手把项链弄到手、挪作己用，本来并非他的本意；可事已至此，他扪心自问：还有什么可愁的？要紧的是项链已经不翼而飞。归根结底，这正是他自始至终矢志求取的圆满收场。

说穿了，这一来便是：等喧嚣与呐喊统统平息，他手头净落三百美元；因而，倘若他哪天再撞见那个警察，而那警察又碰巧没听到“联合牛肉”告诉他的那则消息，那么他就完全能够——……

===Original===
At this point in his meditations Mr. Waddington suddenly broke off and uttered a sharp exclamation. For before his eyes in letters of fire there seemed to be written the one word:

> Gallagher

===Chinese===
思绪行到这里，沃丁顿先生猛地打住，发出一声尖锐的惊呼。因为在他眼前，仿佛有一个词正以火焰般的大字书写出来：

> 加拉格尔（Gallagher）

===Original===
Sigsbee H. Waddington reeled in his tracks. Gallagher! That was the name. Not Mulcahy. Not Garrity. Not Murphy. Gallagher!

Like many another good man before him, Sigsbee Waddington chafed at the fatheaded imbecility with which Memory can behave. Why should Memory have presented to his notice futile Mulcahys and Garritys and Murphys when what he had been asking for was Gallagher? Wasting his time!

But it was not too late. If he went straight back to New York now and resumed his quest, all might yet be well. And Fortune had, he perceived, presented him with the most admirable excuse for going straight back to New York. In a crisis like this, with a valuable pearl necklace stolen it was imperative that a cool-headed, clear-thinking man of the world should take the next train up and place the facts in the possession of Police Headquarters.

“Good enough!” said Mr. Waddington to his immortal soul; and hobbled stiffly but light-heartedly to the boudoir.

===Chinese===
西格斯比·H·沃丁顿在原地踉跄了一下。加拉格尔！正是这个名字。不是马尔卡希。不是加里蒂。不是墨菲。是加拉格尔！

同他之前的许许多多好汉一样，西格斯比·沃丁顿对“记忆力”那副蠢头蠢脑的昏聩行径满腹愤懑。他明明点名要的是加拉格尔，记忆力凭什么端到他面前的尽是些不中用的马尔卡希、加里蒂和墨菲？纯然糟蹋他的光阴！

然而为时尚不为晚。倘若他此刻径直返回纽约，重续旧日的搜寻，局面或许仍有可为。而且他看出，命运已经递给了他一个返回纽约的再好不过的托辞。值此危局，一串贵重的珍珠项链失窃，当务之急便是必须有一位头脑冷静、思虑清晰、深谙世故的人物搭下一班火车进城，把案情呈交警察总局（Police Headquarters）。

“好得很！”沃丁顿先生对自己不朽的灵魂说道；随即拖着僵硬的双腿、心里却轻快无比地一瘸一拐朝闺房去了。

---

===Original===
Voices reached his ears as he opened the door. They ceased as he entered, and Mrs. Waddington looked up peevishly.

“Where have you been, I should like to know?” she said.

Sigsbee H. was ready for this one.

“I took a long country walk. A very long country walk. I was so shocked, horrified and surprised by that dreadful scene that the house seemed to stifle me. So I took a long country walk. I have just got back. What a very disturbing thing to happen! Ferris says it could never have occurred at Brangmarley Hall.”

===Chinese===
他推开门，人声传进耳朵。他一进门，人声便戛然而止，沃丁顿夫人气虎虎地抬眼望来。

“倒要请教，你上哪儿去了？”她说。

这一问西格斯比·H早有准备。

“我去乡间散了个长长的步。非常长的步。那可怕的一幕叫我又惊又骇又诧，这宅子简直闷得我喘不过气。于是我去乡间散了个长长的步。刚回来。出了这么搅得人心神不宁的事，还得了！费里斯就说，这种事在布兰马利庄园是压根儿出不了的。”

===Original===
Molly, somewhat red about the eyes and distinctly mutinous about the mouth, spoke for the first time.

“I’m sure there is some explanation.”

“Tchah!” said Mrs. Waddington.

“I know there is.”

===Chinese===
莫莉眼圈微微发红，嘴角却分明绷着一股不服，头一回开了口。

“我相信这里头一定有缘故。”

“呸！”沃丁顿夫人道。

“我知道一定有。”

===Original===
“Then why did not your precious Finch condescend to give it?”

“He was so taken aback.”

“I don’t wonder.”

“I’m sure there was some mistake.”

===Chinese===
“那你那位宝贝芬奇怎么不肯屈尊讲讲缘故？”

“他一下子惊呆了。”

“我一点也不奇怪。”

“我相信这里面是出了什么差错。”

===Original===
“There was,” said Mr. Waddington. He patted his daughter’s hand soothingly. “The whole thing was a put-up job.”

“Kindly talk sense, Sigsbee.”

“I am talking sense.”

“What you call sense, perhaps, but not what anyone outside the walls of an institution for the feebleminded would call sense.”

===Chinese===
“是出了差错，”沃丁顿先生说。他安抚地拍了拍女儿的手。“整件事是一出做好的局。”

“请你讲点正经话，西格斯比。”

“我讲的就是正经话。”

“你管它叫正经话，兴许吧；可低能者收容所高墙之外的世人，没有一个会管它叫正经话。”

===Original===
“Is zat so?” Mr. Waddington put his thumbs in the armholes of his waistcoat and felt rather conquering. “Well, let me tell you that that girl simply pretended to be what she wasn’t so as to fool you into thinking she wasn’t what she was.”

Mrs. Waddington sighed despairingly.

“Go away, Sigsbee,” she said.

“That’s all right about Go away, Sigsbee. I’m telling you that that girl was a crook. She couldn’t get in any other way, so she pulled that discarded stuff. She was after the wedding-presents.”

===Chinese===
“是嘛？”沃丁顿先生把两只大拇指往马甲袖孔里一插，颇觉自己威风八面。“那么容我告诉你：那个姑娘压根儿就是假扮成一副她并不是的模样，好哄得你当她并不是她实际上的那个。”

沃丁顿夫人绝望地叹了口气。

“走开，西格斯比，”她说。

“‘走开，西格斯比’这种话且由它去。我要告诉你的是，那个姑娘是个女骗子。她没有别的门路进得来，就耍出了那套‘惨遭抛弃’的把戏。她图的是结婚贺礼。”

===Original===
“Then why did she not take them?”

“She did. She took Molly’s pearl necklace.”

“What!”

“You heard. She took Molly’s pearl necklace.”

===Chinese===
“那她为什么不把它们拿走？”

“她拿了。她拿走的是莫莉的珍珠项链。”

“什么！”

“你听见了。她拿走的是莫莉的珍珠项链。”

===Original===
“Nonsense.”

“Well, it’s gone.”

Molly had risen with shining eyes.

“I thought as much. So my dear darling George is innocent after all.”

===Chinese===
“胡说。”

“反正项链是没了。”

莫莉早已站起身来，两眼放光。

“我就料到是这样。这么说，我那亲爱的宝贝乔治终究是清白的。”

===Original===
Very few people in this civilised world have ever seen a baffled tigress, but anybody who could have watched Mrs. Waddington’s face at this moment would have gained a very fair knowledge of how baffled tigresses look.

“I don’t believe it,” she said sullenly.

“Well, the necklace has gone, hasn’t it,” said Sigsbee H. “And you don’t suppose any of the guests took it, do you? Though I wouldn’t put it past that Lord Hunstanton guy. Of course that girl has got it. She fainted on the wedding-present table, didn’t she? She said she wanted air and rushed out, didn’t she? And nobody’s seen her since, have they? If it hadn’t been for going for my long country walk, I’d have got on to this hours ago.

“I’m going straight to New York to see George and tell him,” said Molly, breathing quickly.

===Chinese===
在这个文明世界里，很少有人亲眼见过计穷力竭的母老虎；可方才这一刻，谁要是能盯住沃丁顿夫人的脸瞧上一瞧，就能对计穷力竭的母老虎是副什么长相获得一番相当到位的认识。

“我不信，”她悻悻然说。

“可项链是没了，不是吗，”西格斯比·H说。“你总不见得以为是哪位客人拿的吧？不过那个叫亨斯坦顿勋爵的家伙嘛，我倒不敢保他干不出来。当然是那姑娘拿的。她在摆结婚贺礼的桌上晕了过去，是不是？她说要透透气，一冲就冲出去了，是不是？打那以后谁也没见过她，是不是？要不是我出去散那趟长长的乡间步，几个钟头以前我就该看穿这一层了。

“我要立刻上纽约去见乔治，把这事告诉他，”莫莉说，呼吸急促。

===Original===
“You will do nothing of the kind,” said Mrs. Waddington, rising.

“And I’m going to New York to see the police,” said Sigsbee.

“You are certainly not! I will go to New York, and I will inform the police. You and Molly will stay here.”

“But listen.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“这种事你一件也不许做，”沃丁顿夫人说着站起身来。

“我要上纽约去找警察，”西格斯比说。

“你当然不许去！上纽约去的是我，向警察报案的也是我。你和莫莉留在这里。”

“可是听我说……”

===Original===
“I want no further discussion.” Mrs. Waddington pressed the bell. “As for you,” she said, turning to Molly, “do you suppose I am going to allow you to pay nocturnal visits to the apartments of libertines like George Finch?”

“He is not a libertine.”

“Certainly not,” said Sigsbee H. “A very fine young fellow. Comes from Idaho.”

“You know perfectly well,” Molly went on, “that what father has told us absolutely clears George. Why, the girl might just as well have come in and said that father had deserted her.”

===Chinese===
“我不许再讨论下去。”沃丁顿夫人按了铃。“至于你，”她转身对莫莉说，“你以为我会容许你深更半夜跑到乔治·芬奇那路浪荡子的公寓里去夜访吗？”

“他不是浪荡子。”

“当然不是，”西格斯比·H说。“一个顶好的小伙子。爱达荷州人。”

“你心里再明白不过，”莫莉接着说，“父亲方才那番话，已经把乔治洗刷得清清白白。哼，那姑娘倒不如索性闯进来，说父亲把她给抛弃了呢。”

===Original===
“Here!” said Mr. Waddington. “Hi!”

“She only wanted an excuse for getting into the house.”

“It is possible,” said Mrs. Waddington, “that in this particular instance George Finch is not so blameworthy as I had at first supposed. But that does not alter the fact that he is a man whom any mother with her daughter’s happiness at heart must regard with the deepest suspicion. He is an artist. He has deliberately chosen to live in a quarter of New York which is notorious for its loose-thinking and Bohemian ways. And.﻿ ﻿…”

The door opened.

===Chinese===
“喂！”沃丁顿先生道，“嗨！”

“她不过是想找个由头混进宅子罢了。”

“很有可能，”沃丁顿夫人说，“就这一桩事而论，乔治·芬奇并不像我起初认定的那样罪责难逃。可这改变不了一个事实：凡是把女儿的幸福挂在心上的母亲，都必须以最深的戒心来看待这样一个人。他是个画家。他存心挑了纽约一个以思想轻浮、波西米亚习气出名的街区住下。而且……”

门开了。

===Original===
“You rang, madam?”

“Yes, Ferris. Tell Bassett to bring the car round immediately. I am going into New York.”

“Very good, madam.” The butler coughed. “I wonder, madam, if it is not taking a liberty, if I might be permitted to ride on the box-seat beside the chauffeur?”

“Why?”

===Chinese===
“您按铃了，夫人？”

“是的，费里斯。叫巴塞特（Bassett）立刻把车开过来。我要进纽约。”

“遵命，夫人。”男管家咳嗽了一声。“恕我放肆，夫人，不知可否容我坐在司机旁边的驭手座上一同进城？”

“为什么？”

===Original===
There are occasions in life when to give one’s true reasons for some particular course of action would be tedious. The actual explanation of the butler’s desire to visit the metropolis was that he wished to pay a call upon the editor of that bright and widely-read weekly paper, *Town Gossip*, in order to turn an honest penny by informing him of the sensational scene which had occurred that day in the highest circles. Almost immediately after the facts of this scandal in high life had been called to his attention, Ferris had started to telephone the *Town Gossip* offices in order to establish communication, only to be informed that the editor was out of town. At his last attempt, however, a cautious assistant, convinced at length that the butler had something of real interest up his sleeve and was not disposed to reveal it to underlings, had recommended him to call upon L. Lancelot Biffen, the editor-in-chief, at his private address on the ninth floor of the Sheridan Apartment House, near Washington Square. Mr. Biffen, the assistant thought, would be back after dinner.

All this the butler could, of course, have revealed to his employer, but, like all men of intellect, he disliked long explanations.

“I have just received a communication informing me that a near relative of mine is ill in the city, madam.”

===Chinese===
人生在世，有些时候，把自己采取某一行动的真实缘由和盘托出，未免啰嗦乏味。这位男管家想进大都会走一遭的实际缘由是：他打算去拜访那份内容活泼、销路极广的周刊《街谈巷议》（Town Gossip）的主笔，把当天在最上流的圈子里发生的那一幕耸动场景透露给他，好挣一笔干干净净的外快。几乎在上流生活的这桩丑闻一传进他的耳朵，费里斯便拨起了《街谈巷议》编辑部的电话，一心要接上头，却被告知主笔出城去了。不过在他最后一次尝试时，一位谨慎的助手终于相信这位男管家袖子里确乎藏着真材实料的猛料、又不肯向底下人泄露，便建议他径直去拜访主笔L·兰斯洛特·比芬（L. Lancelot Biffen）的私人住所——华盛顿广场附近谢里丹公寓楼的九层。据那位助手想来，比芬先生晚饭后就会回来的。

这一切，男管家当然尽可以向雇主和盘托出；然而，凡属才智之士，一概不喜欢冗长的解释。

“我刚接到一封信，说我的一位近亲在城里病了，夫人。”

===Original===
“Oh, very well.”

“Thank you, madam. I will inform Bassett at once.”

“Besides,” said Mrs. Waddington, as the door closed, going on where she had been interrupted, “for all we know, the girl’s story may have been perfectly true, and her theft of the pearls the result of a sudden temptation on the spur of the moment.”

“Mother!”

===Chinese===
“哦，那好吧。”

“谢谢夫人。我这就去通知巴塞特。”

“再说，”房门合上之际，沃丁顿夫人接着方才被打断的话头往下说，“谁知底细呢，那姑娘的故事也许句句属实，她偷珍珠不过是一时经不住诱惑、临时起了歹念的结果罢了。”

“妈妈！”

===Original===
“Well, why not? I suppose she was in need of money. No doubt your Finch callously omitted to provide for her in any way.”

“You’ve got it all wrong,” said Sigsbee H.

“What do you know about it?” said Mrs. Waddington.

“Nothing,” said Sigsbee H., prudently.

===Chinese===
“怎么，有什么不对？我想她手头缺钱。不用问，你那位芬奇铁石心肠，压根儿没想过以任何方式接济她。”

“你全弄拧了，”西格斯比·H说。

“你又知道些什么？”沃丁顿夫人说。

“什么也不知道，”西格斯比·H谨慎地说。

===Original===
“Then kindly refrain from talking nonsense.”

Mrs. Waddington left the room with ponderous dignity, and Sigsbee H., still prudent, closed the door.

“Say, listen, Molly,” he said, “I’ve got to get up to New York right away. I’ve just got to.”

“So have I. I certainly mean to see George tonight. I suppose he has gone back to his apartment.”

===Chinese===
“那就请你免开尊口，别再胡说八道。”

沃丁顿夫人迈着沉甸甸的威仪步子离了房；西格斯比·H依旧谨慎，随手把门关上了。

“喂，听着，莫莉，”他说，“我非得马上上纽约去不可。真的非去不可。”

“我也是。我今晚说什么也要见着乔治。我想他回自己的公寓去了。”

===Original===
“What’ll we do?”

“Directly the car has gone, I’ll run you up in my two-seater.”

“ ’At-a-baby!” said Mr. Waddington fervently. “That’s the way to talk.”

He kissed his daughter fondly.

===Chinese===
“那咱们怎么办？”

“那部车一动身，我就开我那部双座小汽车捎你上纽约。”

“好样的，闺女！”沃丁顿先生热切地说，“这才像句话。”

他深情地吻了吻女儿。

## XIV / 第十四章

### I / 一

===Original===
Mrs. Waddington found the authorities at Police Headquarters charming. It was some little time before they corrected their initial impression that she had come to give herself up to justice for committing a jewel-robbery: but, this done, they threw themselves heart and soul into her cause and became extraordinarily helpful. True, they were forced to admit that the description which she gave of the thief conveyed absolutely nothing to them: but if it had done, they assured her, she would have been amazed at the remorseless speed with which the machinery of the Law would have been set working.

If, for instance, the girl had been tall and thin with shingled auburn hair, they would have spread the net at once for “Chicago Kitty.” If, on the other hand, she had had a snub nose and two moles on her chin, then every precinct would have been warned by telephone to keep an eye out for “Cincinnati Sue.” While, if only she had limped slightly and spoken with a lisp, the arrest of “Indianapolis Edna” would have been a mere matter of hours. As it was, they were obliged to confess themselves completely baffled: and Mrs. Waddington came away with the feeling that, if she had not happened to possess large private means, she could have gone into the jewel-stealing business herself and cleaned up big without any fear of unpleasant consequences. It was wrong of her, of course, to call the chief detective a fat-faced goop, but by that time she had become a little annoyed.

She was still annoyed as she came out into the street, but the pleasant night air had a cooling effect. She was able now to perceive that the theft of the necklace was, after all, only a side-issue, and that there lay before her sterner work than the mere bringing to book of female criminals. The consummation to which she must devote all her faculties was the downfall of George Finch.

It was at this point that she decided that she needed an ally, a sympathetic coadjutor who would trot along by her side and do what he was told and generally supply aid and encouragement in the rather tricky operations on which she was about to embark. She went to a public telephone-office and invested five cents in a local call.

===Chinese===
沃丁顿夫人发现，警察总局的当权者们极可爱。过了好一阵子，他们才纠正了起初的印象——原以为她是来投案自首、交代一桩珠宝劫案的：不过一旦纠正过来，他们便全心全意扑到她的案子上，帮起忙来格外起劲。诚然，他们不得不承认，她对那贼人相貌的描述没给他们任何头绪：但他们向她担保，倘若有头绪，法律机器一经启动、那无情运转的惊人速度，准会叫她大吃一惊。

譬如说，倘若那姑娘是个瘦高条儿，剪着一头栗色碎短发，他们立刻就会为捉拿“芝加哥基蒂”（Chicago Kitty）撒下罗网。反过来，倘若她长了狮子鼻、下巴上有两颗痣，那每个分局都会接到电话警告，留意“辛辛那提苏”（Cincinnati Sue）的行踪。而倘若她只是走路微瘸、说话咬舌，那“印第安纳波利斯埃德娜”（Indianapolis Edna）落网不过是几个钟头的事。可实际情况是，他们只得承认自己一筹莫展：于是沃丁顿夫人离开时心里想，倘若她碰巧没有大笔私人进项，她尽可以亲自下海干珠宝盗窃这一行，大捞一票，绝无招惹不愉快后果之虞。当然，她骂侦缉队长是大脸盘蠢货，这话是不对的，可到那时候她已经有几分恼了。

她走到街上时火气仍未消，但宜人的夜气有清凉的功效。她如今能够看清：项链失窃终究只是一段插曲，等着她的，是比单纯把女罪犯绳之以法更为严峻的差事。她必须倾尽全力以求其成的终极目标，是乔治·芬奇的垮台。

正是在这个关头，她认定自己需要一名盟友，一位同心同德的帮手，能在她即将着手的那几手颇为棘手的行动中随侍在侧、唯命是从，总之供给援助与鼓舞。她走进一家公用电话间，投进五美分，打了个市内电话。

===Original===
“Lord Hunstanton?”

“Hullo?”

“This is Mrs. Waddington.”

“Oh, ah? Many happy returns.”

===Chinese===
“亨斯坦顿勋爵？”

“喂？”

“我是沃丁顿夫人。”

“哦，啊？祝你长命百岁。”

===Original===
“What are you doing just now?”

“I was thinking of popping out and having a bit to eat.”

“Meet me at the Ritz-Carlton in ten minutes.”

“Right-ho. Thanks, awfully. I will. Yes. Thanks. Right. Fine. Absolutely. Right-ho.”

===Chinese===
“你这会儿在干什么？”

“我正琢磨着溜达出去弄点儿吃的。”

“十分钟后到里茨-卡尔顿大饭店（the Ritz-Carlton）见我。”

“好嘞。多谢多谢。这就来。是。谢谢。好。没问题。没说的。好嘞。”

===Original===
So now we find Mrs. Waddington seated in the vestibule of the Ritz-Carlton Hotel, watching the door like a cat at a mouse-hole and tapping the carpet impatiently with an ample shoe. Like everybody else who has ever waited five minutes for anybody in a restaurant, she had the illusion of having been there for several hours. But at last her patience was rewarded. An elegant figure shimmered through the doorway and came towards her, beaming with happy anticipation. Lord Hunstanton was a man who combined a keen appetite with a rugged distaste for paying for his own meals, and the prospect of a dinner at the Ritz at somebody else’s expense enchanted him. He did not actually lick his lips, but as he looked brightly up the stairs to where benevolent waiters were plying contented diners with food, there flitted across his face a radiant smile.

“Hope I’m not late,” said Lord Hunstanton.

“Sit down,” said Mrs. Waddington. “I want to talk to you.” And proceeded to do so at some length.

Lord Hunstanton blinked pathetically.

“I’m awfully sorry,” he said, as his companion paused for breath. “I know it’s all frightfully interesting, but I don’t seem somehow to follow. How would it be if we slid into the dining-room and thrashed the whole thing out quietly over a thoughtful steak or something?”

===Chinese===
于是我们看到，沃丁顿夫人此刻坐在里茨-卡尔顿大饭店的门厅里，像守在鼠洞前的猫一样盯着门口，一只肥大的鞋不耐烦地叩着地毯。凡是曾在饭馆里等人等过五分钟的人，都懂得她那种错觉——仿佛自己已经坐了好几个钟头。但她的耐心终于得了报偿。一道优雅的身影闪过门口，朝她款款而来，满脸漾着幸福的期待。亨斯坦顿勋爵这人有两大特点并行不悖：胃口极旺，同时打骨子里厌恶自己掏饭钱；一听这场里茨的晚宴由别人破费，他顿时心花怒放。他倒没有真去舔嘴唇，可当他眉开眼笑地朝楼梯上望——那边慈眉善目的侍者们正给心满意足的食客殷勤添菜——一朵容光焕发的微笑掠过了他的脸。

“但愿我没迟到，”亨斯坦顿勋爵说。

“坐吧，”沃丁顿夫人说。“我有话跟你谈。”接着便当真滔滔不绝地谈了半天。

亨斯坦顿勋爵可怜巴巴地眨巴着眼睛。

“实在抱歉，”他趁同伴停下来换气的当口说。“我知道这一切都妙趣无穷，可不知怎么搞的，我好像没跟上。咱们不如溜进餐厅，一边安安静静受用一块善解人意的牛排什么的，一边把整件事掰扯清楚，你看如何？”

===Original===
Mrs. Waddington eyed him with a distaste that bordered on contempt.

“You surely do not imagine that I propose to waste time eating?”

“Eh?” His lordship’s jaw fell an inch. “Not eat?”

“Certainly not. I will repeat what I was saying, and please listen attentively this time.”

“But I say! No dinner?”

“No.”

===Chinese===
沃丁顿夫人用近乎鄙夷的嫌弃眼神打量着他。

“你总不至于以为，我是打算把时间浪费在吃东西上吧？”

“啊？”勋爵大人的下巴垂下了一英寸。“不吃？”

“当然不是。我把方才的话再说一遍，这一次请你用心听。”

“可是我说！连晚饭都不吃？”

“不吃。”

===Original===
“No soup?”

“No.”

“No fish? No nourishment of any description?”

“Certainly not. We have no time to lose. We must act promptly and swiftly.”

“How about a sand﻿—﻿ ﻿… ?”

===Chinese===
“汤也没有？”

“没有。”

“鱼也没有？任何形式的滋养都没有？”

“当然没有。我们没有时间可浪费。必须立刻行动，兵贵神速。”

“那么来份三明——？”

===Original===
“You were present at that appalling scene this afternoon,” said Mrs. Waddington, “so there is no need to describe it to you. You will not have forgotten how that girl came into the room and denounced George Finch. You recall all she said.”

“I do indeed. It was the real ginger.”

“But unfortunately untrue.”

“Eh?”

“It was a ruse. She was a thief. She did it in order to steal a pearl necklace belonging to my stepdaughter, which was among the wedding-presents.”

“No, really? I say! Fancy that!”

===Chinese===
“今天下午那骇人的一幕你在场，”沃丁顿夫人说，“所以无须再向你描述。那姑娘闯进屋里、当众声讨乔治·芬奇的情景，你不会忘记。她说过的每一句，你都记得。”

“记得一清二楚。那才叫真够味儿。”

“可惜不是实情。”

“啊？”

“那是个诡计。那姑娘是个贼。她那么干，是为了偷走我继女的一串珍珠项链，项链就摆在那些结婚礼物当中。”

“不会吧？真的？我说！竟有这等事！”

===Original===
“Unfortunately there seems to be no doubt of it. And so, instead of being appalled at George Finch’s moral turpitude, my stepdaughter looks upon him as a much-injured man and wishes the marriage to take place as arranged. Are you listening?”

Lord Hunstanton started. There had come frolicking towards him from the dining-room a lively young smell composed principally of tournedos and gravy, and his attention had wandered.

“Sorry,” he said. “Thinking of something else for the moment. You were saying that Miss Waddington was appalled at George Finch’s moral turpitude.”

“I was saying precisely the reverse. She is not appalled.”

“No? Very broad-minded, these modern girls,” said Lord Hunstanton, turning away and trying not to inhale.

===Chinese===
“可惜看来这是千真万确的了。于是呢，我继女非但没有为乔治·芬奇的道德败坏大为震骇，反倒把他看成了天大的受害人，一心要按原定安排把婚事办完。你在听吗？”

亨斯坦顿勋爵一个激灵。从餐厅那边蹦蹦跳跳地飘来一缕鲜活娇嫩的气味，主要成分是菲力牛排浇肉汁，他的注意力开了小差。

“抱歉，”他说。“刚才脑子溜了个号。你方才是在说，沃丁顿小姐为乔治·芬奇的道德败坏大为震骇。”

“我说的恰恰相反。她一点儿也不震骇。”

“是嘛？如今这些姑娘家，心胸可真开阔，”亨斯坦顿勋爵说着把脸扭开，拼命不去吸气。

===Original===
“But,” proceeded Mrs. Waddington, “I am convinced that, although in this particular matter this Finch may be blameless, his morals, if we only knew it, are as degraded as those of all other artists. I feel as certain as I am that I am sitting here that George Finch is a loose fish.”

“Fish!” moaned Lord Hunstanton.

“And I have made up my mind that there is only one thing to do if I am to expose the man in his true colours, and that is to go to the den which he maintains near Washington Square and question his manservant as to his private life. We will start at once.”

“But, I say, you don’t need me?”

“Certainly I need you. Do you imagine that I propose to call at this man’s lair alone?”

===Chinese===
“不过，”沃丁顿夫人接着说，“我深信，尽管在这桩事上这位芬奇或许无可指摘，可只要内情叫咱们摸透，他的道德准跟其他所有画家一样败坏。我就像自己坐在这儿一样确信：乔治·芬奇是条浪荡鱼。”

“鱼！”亨斯坦顿勋爵呻吟道。

“我已拿定主意：要想叫这家伙现出原形，只有一个办法——上他在华盛顿广场附近养的那个老巢去，向他的贴身男仆盘问他的私生活。咱们马上动身。”

“可是，我说，你用得着我吗？”

“自然用得着。你总不至于以为，我会孤身一人登门去这家伙的巢穴吧？”

===Original===
Across the landing at the top of the stairs there passed a waiter bearing a tray with a smoking dish upon it. Lord Hunstanton followed him with haggard eyes: and, having watched him enter the restaurant, wished he had not done so, for there by one of the tables stood another waiter carving for a party of four what looked like the roast chicken of a lifetime﻿—one of those roast chickens you tell your grandchildren about. His lordship uttered a faint, whinnying sound and clenched his hands.

“Come!” said Mrs. Waddington. “Let us go.”

The thought of defying this overpowering woman did not enter Lord Hunstanton’s mind. Nobody ever defied Mrs. Waddington. And so, some little time later, a cab drew up outside the Sheridan Apartment House and two figures proceeded to climb the stairs﻿—for it was one of the pleasing features of the Sheridan that the elevator was practically always out of order.

===Chinese===
楼梯顶端的过道那头，一名侍者托着盘子走过，盘上是一盘热气腾腾的菜。亨斯坦顿勋爵形容枯槁地目送他过去；眼看着他进了餐厅，又恨不得自己没看过——因为那边一张桌旁，另一名侍者正在给一桌四位切一只看似平生仅见的烤鸡——那种烤鸡，够你讲给孙儿孙女听一辈子的。勋爵大人发出一声微弱的、近乎马嘶的声音，双手攥紧了。

“来！”沃丁顿夫人说。“咱们走。”

亨斯坦顿勋爵的脑子里，压根没掠过违抗这位威凌逼人的女人的念头。从来没有谁违抗过沃丁顿夫人。于是，过了片刻，一辆出租车在谢里丹公寓楼前停下，两条人影开始拾级而上——因为谢里丹公寓楼有一桩令人愉快的特色：电梯几乎永远处于瘫痪状态。

===Original===
Arrived at the top floor, Lord Hunstanton rang the bell. The sound echoed faintly within.

“Seems to be out,” said his lordship, having tried again.

“We will wait.”

“What, here?”

“On the roof.”

“How long?”

===Chinese===
到了顶楼，亨斯坦顿勋爵按了门铃。铃声在屋里隐隐回荡。

“好像没人在，”勋爵大人又试了一遍说。

“我们等。”

“什么，就在这儿等？”

“在屋顶上。”

“等多久？”

===Original===
“Until this Finch’s manservant returns.”

“But he may be hours.”

“Then we will wait hours.”

Lord Hunstanton’s aching interior urged him to protest. “Be brave!” it gurgled. And, whilst still not sufficiently courageous to defy, he nerved himself to make a suggestion.

===Chinese===
“等到这位芬奇的贴身男仆回来。”

“可他也许要几个钟头。”

“那我们就等几个钟头。”

亨斯坦顿勋爵饥肠作痛的五脏六腑催促他抗议。“勇敢些！”肚子里咕噜咕噜地鼓劲。虽说还没胆量公然违抗，他总算给自己壮起一丝勇气，提了个建议。

===Original===
“How would it be,” he said, “if I just pushed round the corner somewhere and snatched a bite? I mean to say, you never know whether this manservant fellow won’t turn nasty. Sticking up for the young master, I mean to say. In which case, I should be twice the man with a bit of food inside me. With a dish of beans or something nicely poised within, I could do my bit.”

Mrs. Waddington regarded him scornfully.

“Very well. But kindly return as soon as possible.”

“Oh, I will, by Jove! Just want to pack away a hasty prune. I’ll be back before you know I’ve gone.”

“You will find me on the roof.”
