首先，正当加罗威警官的脑袋快要挣出桌布褶皱之际，朱塞佩怀着一种再怎么赞誉也不为过的对东家的忠心，抡起咖啡壶砸中了他的眼睛。这一下再度搅乱了警察的形势判断；等到他能够重新把事情想清楚，灯已经灭了。
与此同时，月亮本来自然就站在乔治这一边，巴不得竭尽所能帮点忙，这时也躲进一朵厚云彩里，赖着不肯出来。因此，没有任何凡人的眼睛，目睹这位年轻人夺命的一攀。
他听见在防火梯上的那两位，跟他一样，心里只装着一件事：火速撤离险区。而他们对乔治非但毫无敌意——倘若瞧见了他，两位还会催他快爬、祝他好运。因为其中一位是尤拉莉夫人，另一位更是 J·汉密尔顿·比米什本人。

===Original===
Hamilton Beamish, escorting his bride-to-be, had arrived at the Purple Chicken a few minutes after George, and, like George, had found the place crowded to its last table. But unlike George, he had not meekly accepted this situation as unalterable. Exerting the full force of his majestic personality, he had caused an extra table to appear, to be set, and to be placed in the fairway at the spot where the indoor restaurant joined the outdoor annex.
It was a position which at first had seemed to have drawbacks. The waiters who passed at frequent intervals were compelled to bump into Mr. Beamish’s chair, which is always unpleasant when one is trying to talk to the girl one loves. But the time was to arrive when its drawbacks were lost sight of in the contemplation of its strategic advantages. At the moment when the raid may be said to have formally opened, Hamilton Beamish was helping the girl of his heart to what the management had assured him was champagne. He was interrupted in this kindly action by a large hand placed heavily on his shoulder and a gruff voice which informed him that he was under arrest.
Whether Hamilton Beamish would have pursued George Finch’s spirited policy of enveloping the man in the tablecloth and thereafter plugging him in the eye, will never be known: for the necessity for such a procedure was removed by the sudden extinction of the lights: and it was at this point that the advantage of being in that particular spot became apparent.
From the table to the fire-escape was but a few steps: and Hamilton Beamish, seizing his fiancée by the hand, dragged her thither and, placing her foot on the lowest step, gave her an upward boost which left no room for misapprehension. A moment later, Madame Eulalie was hurrying roofwards, with Hamilton Beamish in close attendance.

===Chinese===
汉密尔顿·比米什护送着未婚妻，在乔治之后几分钟到达紫鸡餐厅，跟乔治一样，发现店里挤得连最后一张桌子都没有空。但与乔治不同，他并未温顺地把这局面当作不可更改的事实接受下来。他施展自己威严人格的全部威力，硬是变出一张加桌，摆上了台面，安在室内餐厅与露天副堂衔接处的那条通道上。
这个位置起初看着有毛病。不时来往穿行的侍者免不了撞上比米什先生的椅子；而当一个人正试着跟心爱的姑娘谈话时，这总是件败兴的事。然而时候一到，这些毛病便在战略优势的通盘考量中被忘得一干二净。可以说，突袭正式打响的那一刻，汉密尔顿·比米什正在给心爱的姑娘斟店掌柜拍胸脯担保是香槟的酒。这桩善举被一只重重按上他肩头的大手打断了，还有一个粗哑的嗓音通知他：他被捕了。
汉密尔顿·比米什究竟会不会采取乔治·芬奇那套豪迈的战术——先用桌布把人裹住、再往眼睛上招呼一下——将永远无人知晓：因为灯光猝然熄灭，这套程序已无必要；也正是在这一刻，坐在那个特定位置的好处显现了出来。
从桌子到防火梯不过几步路：汉密尔顿·比米什一把抓住未婚妻的手，把她拽到梯前，把她的脚放上最低一级，从底下向上猛地一送——这一送绝无误解的余地。片刻之后，尤拉莉夫人已经在朝屋顶疾奔，汉密尔顿·比米什紧随其后。

===Original===
They stood together at the end of their journey, looking down. The lights of the Purple Chicken were still out, and from the darkness there rose a confused noise indicative of certain persons unknown being rather rough with certain other persons unknown. It seemed to Madame Eulalie that she and her mate were well out of it, and she said so.
“I never realised before what a splendid man you were to have by one in an emergency, Jimmy dear,” she said. “Anything slicker than the way you scooped us out of that place I never saw. You must have had lots and lots of practice.”
Hamilton Beamish was passing a handkerchief over his dome-like forehead. The night was warm, and the going had been fast.
“I shall never forgive myself,” he said, “for exposing you to such an experience.”
“Oh, but I enjoyed it.”
“Well, all has ended well, thank goodness.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
两人并立在这段旅程的终点，向下俯望。紫鸡的灯还黑着，黑暗中升腾起一片乱糟糟的声响，听意思是不知什么人正对另一些不知什么人下狠手。在尤拉莉夫人看来，她和她的人脱身得正是时候，她照直说了。
“我以前真没想过，危急关头身边有你这么一位有多棒，吉米亲爱的，”她说，“你把我们从那个地方捞出来的那手利索劲儿，我从没见过第二回。你准是练过好多好多回。”
汉密尔顿·比米什正拿手帕擦拭他穹顶似的额头。夜里闷热，这一路又跑得飞快。
“让你经受这样的险境，”他说，“我永远不能原谅自己。”
“哦，可我高兴着呢。”
“不管怎样，结局总算圆满，谢天谢地……”

===Original===
“But has it?” interrupted Madame Eulalie.
“What do you mean?”
She pointed downwards.
“There’s somebody coming up!”
“You’re right.”
“What shall we do? Go out by the stairs?”

===Chinese===
“真的圆满了吗？”尤拉莉夫人打断道。
“你什么意思？”
她指向下方。
“有人上来了！”
“没错。”
“咱们怎么办？走楼梯出去？”

===Original===
Hamilton Beamish shook his head.
“In all probability they will be guarding the entrance.”
“Then what?”
It is at moments like these that the big brain really tells. An ordinary man might have been nonplussed. Certainly, he would have had to waste priceless moments in thought. Hamilton Beamish, with one flash of his giant mind, had the problem neatly solved in four and a quarter seconds.
He took his bride-to-be by the arm and turned her round.
“Look.”

===Chinese===
汉密尔顿·比米什摇了摇头。
“十有八九他们守着入口呢。”
“那怎么办？”
正是这样的时刻，大智慧才真正见分晓。换个凡夫俗子，多半要手足无措。至少，他得在苦思冥想中浪费掉千金难买的分分秒秒。汉密尔顿·比米什巨灵般的心智只一闪，四又四分之一秒钟，问题已经利利索索地解决了。
他挽住未婚妻的手臂，把她转过身来。
“瞧。”

===Original===
“Where?”
“There!”
“Which?”
“That.”
“What?” Bewilderment was limned upon the girl’s fair face. “I don’t understand. What do you want me to specially look at?”
“At what do you want me especially to look,” corrected Hamilton Beamish mechanically. He drew her across the roof. “You see that summerhouse thing? It is George Finch’s open-air sleeping-porch. Go in, shut the door, switch on the light.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“瞧哪儿？”
“那儿！”
“哪个？”
“那个。”
“什么？”困惑刻画在姑娘白皙的脸上，“我不明白。你要我特别注意看什么？”
“你要我特别地注意看什么。”汉密尔顿·比米什机械地纠正道。他拉着她穿过屋顶，“看见那个凉亭似的玩意儿没有？那是乔治·芬奇的户外睡廊。进去，关上门，打开灯……”

===Original===
“But.﻿ ﻿…”
“… and remove a portion of your clothes.”
“What!”
“And if anybody comes tell him that George Finch rented you the apartment and that you are dressing to go out to dinner. I, meanwhile, will go down to my apartment and will come up in a few minutes to see if you are ready to be taken out to dine.” Pardonable pride so overcame Hamilton Beamish that he discarded the English Pure and relapsed into the argot of the proletariat. “Is that a crackerjack?” he demanded with gleaming eyes. “Is that a wam? Am I the bozo with the big bean or am I not?”
The girl eyed him worshippingly. One of the consolations which we men of intellect have is that, when things come to a crisis, what captures the female heart is brains. Women may permit themselves in times of peace to stray after Sheiks and look languishingly at lizards whose only claim to admiration is that they can do the first three steps of the Charleston: but let matters go wrong; let some sudden peril threaten; and who then is the king pippin, who the main squeeze? The man with the eight and a quarter hat.
“Jimmy,” she cried, “it’s the goods!”

===Chinese===
“可是……”
“……再把身上的一部分衣裳脱掉。”
“什么！”
“要是有人来，就告诉他：公寓是乔治·芬奇租给你的，你正换衣服预备出去吃晚饭。我呢，这就下楼回自己的公寓，过几分钟再上来，看你收拾好没有，好带你去下馆子。”情不自禁的自豪压倒了汉密尔顿·比米什，他把《纯正英语》弃若敝屣，一头跌回无产者的黑话里。“这主意绝不绝？”他两眼放光地问，“妙不妙？我到底是不是那颗大脑袋瓜子灵光透顶的老大？”
姑娘用崇拜的眼光望着他。我们智力人士的种种安慰之中有一条便是：事情一到危急关头，俘虏女性芳心的终究是头脑。太平年月，女人们尽容许自己去追随情圣小生（sheik），去没精打采地凝望那些除会查尔斯顿舞（Charleston）的头三步之外一无可取的油头浪子；可一旦事情变糟，一旦什么灾祸突然临头，彼时谁是不折不扣的人中之王？谁又是黑话里顶顶要紧的“头号宝贝”？戴八叉四分之一号大礼帽的那一位。
“吉米，”她喊道，“这才是真家伙！”

===Original===
“Exactly.”
“It’s a lifesaver.”
“Precisely. Be quick, then. There is no time to waste.”
And so it came about that George Finch, nestling beneath the bed, received a shock which, inured though he should have been to shocks by now, seemed to him to turn every hair on his head instantaneously grey.

===Chinese===
“正是。”
“这是救命的法宝。”
“一点儿不错。那就快。没工夫可浪费了。”
于是事情就这样发生了：蜷在床底下的乔治·芬奇挨了一记晴天霹雳——按说他到这会儿早该对惊吓有了免疫力，可这一记落在他身上，竟像把满头头发霎时间全惊成了灰白。

### III / 三

===Original===
The first thing that impressed itself on George Finch’s consciousness, after his eyes had grown accustomed to the light, was an ankle. It was clad in a stocking of diaphanous silk, and was joined almost immediately by another ankle, similarly clad. For an appreciable time these ankles, though slender, bulked so large in George’s world that they may be said to have filled his whole horizon. Then they disappeared.
A moment before this happened, George, shrinking modestly against the wall, would have said that nothing could have pleased him better than to have these ankles disappear. Nevertheless, when they did so, it was all he could do to keep himself from uttering a stricken cry. For the reason they disappeared was that at this moment a dress of some filmy material fell over them, hiding them from view.
It was a dress that had the appearance of having been cut by fairy scissors out of moonbeams and stardust: and in a shopwindow George would have admired it. But seeing it in a shopwindow and seeing it bunched like a prismatic foam on the floor of this bedroom were two separate and distinct things: and so warmly did George Finch blush that he felt as if his face must be singeing the carpet. He shut his eyes and clenched his teeth. Was this, he asked himself, the end or but a beginning?
“Yes?” said a voice suddenly. And George’s head, jerking convulsively, seemed for an instant to have parted company with a loosely-attached neck.

===Chinese===
乔治·芬奇的眼睛适应了灯光之后，头一样印上他意识的东西，是一只脚踝。它裹在一双薄如蝉翼的丝袜里；几乎紧接着又来了另一只脚踝，装束相同。有好一小会儿，这两只脚踝虽然纤细，在乔治的世界里却体积庞大得可以说占满了他全部的地平线。随后它们不见了。
就在这情形发生前一刻，正谦谦地缩在墙边的乔治本来会赌咒说：天底下再没有比这两只脚踝消失更让他称心的事了。然而它们当真消失时，他却险些没能忍住一声惨叫。因为它们消失的缘由是：这当口，一条薄如轻纱的连衣裙从上面落下来，把它们遮没了。
那是一条仿佛出自仙姑剪刀、用月光和星尘裁成的裙子：摆进橱窗，乔治准会赞赏不已。可是在橱窗里看它，同看着它像一蓬虹彩泡沫堆在这间卧室的地板上，是两码事，是截然不同的两码事：乔治·芬奇的脸红得那样炽烈，他只觉得自己的脸快要把地毯烤糊了。他闭上眼睛，咬紧牙关。他问自己：这是终局呢，还是仅仅一个开头？
“什么事？”一个声音猝然说道。乔治的脑袋痉挛地一抽，那一瞬间仿佛同那条松脱相连的脖子分了家。

===Original===
The voice had spoken, he divined as soon as the power of thought returned to him, in response to a sharp and authoritative knock on the door, delivered by some hard instrument which sounded like a policeman’s nightstick: and there followed immediately upon this knock sharp and authoritative words.
“Open up there!”
The possessor of the ankles was plainly a girl of spirit.
“I won’t,” she said. “I’m dressing.”

===Chinese===
等思维能力回到身上，他便参悟出来：那声“什么事”是回应门上一记严厉而不容置疑的敲击的——敲门的什么硬家伙，听声音像警察的警棍；而这记敲击之后紧跟着的，是同样严厉而不容置疑的话语。
“开门！”
这双脚踝的主人分明是位有胆气的姑娘。
“我不开，”她说，“我在换衣服。”

===Original===
“Who are you?”
“Who are *you*?”
“Never mind who I am.”
“Well, never mind who *I* am, then!”
There was a pause. It seemed to George, judging the matter dispassionately, that the ankles had had slightly the better of the exchanges to date.

===Chinese===
“你是谁？”
“你又是*谁*？”
“你甭管我是谁。”
“好呀，那你也甭管*我*是谁！”
一阵冷场。平心而论，照乔治的判断，到目前为止，这番交锋里占上风的略偏于脚踝一方。

===Original===
“What are you doing in there?” asked the male duettist, approaching the thing from another angle.
“I’m dressing, I keep telling you.”
There was another pause. And then into this tense debate there entered a third party.
“What’s all this?” said the newcomer sharply.
George recognised the voice of his old friend Hamilton Beamish.

===Chinese===
“你在这里头干什么？”男声二重唱的另一半换了个路数发问。
“我在换衣服，跟你说多少遍了。”
又一阵冷场。随后，这场剑拔弩张的辩论里杀进了第三方。
“这是怎么回事？”新来的人声色俱厉。
乔治听出那是他的老朋友汉密尔顿·比米什的声音。

===Original===
“Garroway,” said Hamilton Beamish, with an annoyed severity, “what the devil are you doing, hanging about outside this lady’s door? Upon my soul,” proceeded Mr. Beamish warmly, “I’m beginning to wonder what the duties of the New York constabulary are. Their life seems to consist of an endless leisure, which they employ in roaming about and annoying women. Are you aware that the lady inside there is my fiancée and that she is dressing in order to dine with me at a restaurant?”
Officer Garroway, as always, cringed before the superior intelligence.
“I am extremely sorry, Mr. Beamish.”
“So you ought to be. What are you doing here, anyway?”

===Chinese===
“加罗威，”汉密尔顿·比米什带着气恼的严峻说，“你到底在搞什么鬼，在这位女士的门外转来转去？说句实在话，”比米什先生愤然接着说，“我都开始纳闷纽约警察的职责究竟是什么了。他们的生涯似乎就是无穷无尽的清闲，而这些清闲全都用来四处游荡、骚扰妇女。你可知道，里头那位女士是我的未婚妻，她正在换衣服，好同我上馆子吃饭？”
加罗威警官照例在更高等的智慧面前矮了半截。
“我实在非常抱歉，比米什先生。”
“你也该抱歉。说到底，你跑这儿来干什么？”

===Original===
“There has been some little trouble down below on the premises of the Purple Chicken, and I was violently assaulted by Mr. Finch. I followed him up here on the fire-escape.﻿ ﻿…”
“Mr. Finch? You are drivelling, Garroway. Mr. Finch is on his wedding-trip. He very kindly lent this lady his apartment during his absence.”
“But, Mr. Beamish, I was talking to him only just now. We sat at the same table.”
“Absurd!”
The dress had disappeared from George’s range of vision now, and he heard the door open.

===Chinese===
“底下紫鸡餐厅的地界上出了点小小的乱子，我遭到了芬奇先生的暴力袭击。我顺着防火梯追他上来的……”
“芬奇先生？你在说胡话，加罗威。芬奇先生旅行结婚去了。他人不在，好意把公寓借给了这位女士。”
“可是，比米什先生，我方才还在同他说话呢。我们同桌坐来着。”
“荒唐！”
这会儿裙子已经从乔治的视野里消失了，他听见门开了。

===Original===
“What does this man want, Jimmy?”
“A doctor, apparently,” said Hamilton Beamish. “He says he met George Finch just now.”
“But George is miles away.”
“Precisely. Are you ready, darling? Then we will go off and have some dinner. What you need, Garroway, is a bromo-seltzer. Come down to my apartment and I will mix you one. Having taken it, I would recommend you to lie down quietly on the sofa and rest awhile. I think you must have been over-exercising your brain, writing that poem of yours. Who blacked your eye?”
“I wish I knew,” said Officer Garroway wistfully. “I received the injury during the fracas at the Purple Chicken. There was a tablecloth over my head at the moment, and I was unable to ascertain the identity of my assailant. If, and when, I find him I shall soak him so hard it’ll jar his grandchildren.”

===Chinese===
“这个人要干什么，吉米？”
“显然是要个大夫，”汉密尔顿·比米什说，“他说他方才还见到乔治·芬奇来着。”
“可乔治远在天边呢。”
“一点儿不错。准备好了吗，亲爱的？那咱们就去吃晚饭。加罗威，你需要的是一份布罗莫-塞尔策泡腾盐（bromo-seltzer）。下楼到我公寓来，我给你调一杯。喝完，我建议你在沙发上安安静静躺一会儿，歇一歇。我想你准是写那首诗把脑子使过头了。你那只眼睛是谁打的？”
“我要是知道就好了，”加罗威警官不胜神往地说，“这伤是在紫鸡餐厅的混战里落下的。当时我头上罩着桌布，没法查明袭击我的是何方神圣。等到找出他来——只要能找出来——我要狠狠揍他一家伙，揍得他孙子都跟着震。”

===Original===
“A tablecloth?”
“Yes, Mr. Beamish. And while I was endeavouring to extricate myself from its folds, somebody hit me in the eye with a coffeepot.”
“How do you know it was a coffeepot?”
“I found it lying beside me when I emerged.”
“Ah! Well,” said Hamilton Beamish, summing up, “I hope that this will be a lesson to you not to go into places like the Purple Chicken. You are lucky to have escaped so lightly. You might have had to eat their cheese. Well, come along, Garroway, and we will see what we can do for you.”

===Chinese===
“桌布？”
“是，比米什先生。我正奋力从桌布的褶皱里往外挣呢，有人抡起咖啡壶砸中了我的眼睛。”
“你怎么知道那是把咖啡壶？”
“我钻出来的时候，发现它就躺在我旁边。”
“哦！好啦，”汉密尔顿·比米什总结道，“我希望这回能给你个教训：别再进紫鸡餐厅那类地方。你能这么轻伤脱身已属万幸。本来你多半还得吃他们的奶酪呢。好了，走吧，加罗威，咱们看看能为你做点什么。”

### IV / 四

===Original===
George stayed where he was. If he had known of a better ’ole, he would have gone to it: but he did not. He would have been the last person to pretend that it was comfortable lying underneath this bed with fluff tickling his nose and a draught playing about his left ear: but there seemed in the circumstances nothing else to do. To a man unable to fly there were only two modes of exit from this roof﻿—he could climb down the fire-escape, probably into the very arms of the constabulary, or he could try to sneak down the stairs, and most likely run straight into the vengeful Garroway. True, Hamilton Beamish had recommended the policeman after drinking his bromo-seltzer to lie down on the sofa, but who knew if he would follow the advice? Possibly he was even now patrolling the staircase: and George, recalling the man’s physique and remembering the bitterness with which he had spoken of his late assailant, decided that the risk was too great to be taken. Numerous as were the defects of his little niche beneath the bed, considered as a spot to spend a happy evening, it was a good place to be for a man in his delicate position. So he dug himself in and tried to while away the time by thinking.
He thought of many things. He thought of his youth in East Gilead, of his manhood in New York. He thought of Molly and how much he loved her; of Mrs. Waddington and what a blot she was on the great scheme of things; of Hamilton Beamish and his offhand way of dealing with policemen. He thought of Officer Garroway and his nightstick; of Guiseppe and his coffeepot; of the Reverend Gideon Voules and his white socks. He even thought of Sigsbee H. Waddington.
Now, when a man is so hard put to it for mental occupation that he has to fall back on Sigsbee H. Waddington as a topic of thought, he is nearing the end of his resources: and it was possibly with a kindly appreciation of this fact that Fate now supplied something else to occupy George’s mind. Musing idly on Sigsbee H. and wondering how he got that way, George became suddenly aware of approaching footsteps.

===Chinese===
乔治待在原地没动。他要是知道一处更好的“窑洞”，早就投奔去了：可惜他不知道。要他昧着良心说躺在床底下很舒服——绒毛挠着鼻子，穿堂风在左耳旁边打转——他打死也不会：可情势如此，似乎也没有别的路可走。对一个不会飞的人来说，离开这片屋顶只有两条道——顺着防火梯爬下去，多半正落进警察的怀抱；或是想法从楼梯溜下去，十有八九迎头撞上怀恨在心的加罗威。诚然，汉密尔顿·比米什嘱咐过这位警察，喝完布罗莫-塞尔策泡腾盐要在沙发上躺一躺，可谁知道他听不听劝？说不定这会儿正在楼梯上巡逻呢：乔治想起此人的体格，又记起他谈起方才那位袭击者时的切齿之恨，认定这个风险冒不得。他床底下这处小小的窝，当作欢度良宵的去处毛病固然数不胜数，可对一个身处境地微妙的人来说，这里是块宝地。于是他把自己往里埋了埋，打算用想心思来消磨光阴。
他想了很多事。他想在东吉利亚德的少年时代，想他在纽约的成年岁月。他想莫莉，想自己多么爱她；想沃丁顿夫人，想她是这森罗万象的世界上一块多么刺眼的污渍；想汉密尔顿·比米什和他打发警察时的那种信手一挥。他想加罗威警官和他的警棍；想朱塞佩和他的咖啡壶；想吉迪恩·沃尔斯牧师和他的白袜子。他甚至想到了西格斯比·H·沃丁顿。
话说回来，当一个人穷乏到不得不拿西格斯比·H·沃丁顿充当思考的题材时，他的精神储备也就行将见底了：命运女神或许正是体察到这一点，才好心地另备了些东西来占据乔治的头脑。他正漫无边际地想着西格斯比·H，纳闷他怎么会是那么个人，忽然惊觉脚步声在逼近。

===Original===
He curled himself up into a ball, and his ears stood straight up like a greyhound’s. Yes, footsteps. And, what was more, they seemed to be making straight for the sleeping-porch.
A wave of self-pity flooded over George Finch. Why should he be so ill-used? He asked so little of Life﻿—merely to be allowed to lie quietly under a bed and inhale fluff: and what happened? Nothing but interruptions. Nothing but boots, boots, boots, boots, marching up and down again, as Kipling has so well put it. Ever since he had found his present hiding-place, the world had seemed to become one grey inferno of footsteps. It was wrong and unjust.
The only thing that could possibly be said in extenuation of the present footsteps was that they sounded too light to be those of any New York policeman. They had approached now to the very door. Indeed, they seemed to him to have stopped actually inside the room.
He was right in this conjecture. The switch clicked. Light jumped at him like a living thing. And when he opened his eyes he found himself looking at a pair of ankles clad in stockings of diaphanous silk.
The door closed. And Mrs. Waddington, who had just reached the top of the fire-escape, charged across the roof and, putting her ear to the keyhole, stood listening intently. Things, felt Mrs. Waddington, were beginning to move.

===Chinese===
他把自己蜷成一团，耳朵像灵缇犬似的直竖起来。没错，脚步声。而且，更要命的是，它们好像是笔直朝睡廊来的。
一阵自怜的浪潮淹没了乔治·芬奇。他凭什么要受这样的罪？他对人生所求几希——不过容他安安静静躺在床底下、吸一吸绒毛罢了：可结果呢？除了打断还是打断。除了靴子还是靴子，靴子、靴子、靴子，上上下下走个不停，正如吉卜林（Kipling）写得那样传神。自打他寻得眼下这处藏身之所，世界似乎已化作一片灰蒙蒙的足音地狱。这不公道，简直没天理。
眼下这串脚步唯一可以替它开脱的地方，是那声音太轻，轻得不像任何一个纽约警察。它们已经来到门前。说真的，在他听来，简直像是已经停在了房间里面。
他猜得一点不错。开关咔哒一响。灯光像活物一样朝他扑过来。他睁开眼睛，发现自己正对着一双裹在薄如蝉翼的丝袜里的脚踝。
门关上了。而刚爬上防火梯顶端的沃丁顿夫人，飞奔着冲过屋顶，把耳朵贴上钥匙孔，聚精会神地偷听起来。在沃丁顿夫人的感觉里，事情开始有眉目了。

### V / 五

===Original===
For a moment, all that George Finch felt as he glared out at this latest visitation was a weak resentment at the oafishness of Fate in using the same method for his tormenting that it had used so short a while before. Fate, he considered, was behaving childishly, and ought to change its act. This ankle business might have been funny enough once: but, overdone, it became tedious.
Then to indignation there succeeded relief. The remarks of Hamilton Beamish in his conversation with the policeman had made it clear that the possessor of the ankles had been his old friend May Stubbs of East Gilead, Idaho: and, seeing ankles once again, George naturally assumed that they were attached, as before, to Miss Stubbs, and that the reason for her return was that she had come back to fetch something﻿—some powder-puff, for example, or a lipstick﻿—which in the excitement of the recent altercation she had forgotten to take along with her.
This, of course, altered the whole position of affairs. What it amounted to was that, instead of a new enemy he had found an ally. A broad-minded girl like May would understand at once the motives which had led him to hide under the bed and would sympathise with them. He could employ her, it occurred to him, as a scout, to see if the staircase was not clear. In short, this latest interruption of his reverie, so far from being a disaster, was the very best thing that could have happened.

===Chinese===
有那么一瞬，瞪着这最新一场侵扰的乔治·芬奇，心里只剩下一种有气无力的愤懑：命运女神真是笨拙得可以，隔了这么短的时间，竟还用同一套把戏来折腾他。他认为，命运这做派孩子气十足，该换换戏码了。脚踝这出戏，演头一回兴许还够滑稽：可一来二去演过了头，就变得乏味了。
接着，愤懑让位给了宽心。汉密尔顿·比米什同警察的那番对话已经说得明明白白：这双脚踝的主人，正是他从前的老朋友、爱达荷州东吉利亚德的梅·斯塔布斯。如今再见到脚踝，乔治自然认定它们照旧长在斯塔布斯小姐身上，而她去而复返，是为了取回什么东西——譬如一只粉扑，或者一支口红——方才那场争执闹得心慌意乱，忘在屋里没带走了。
这么一来，整个局面自然全盘改观。说穿了，他找到的不是新敌人，而是同盟军。像梅这样心胸开阔的姑娘，立刻就会明白他躲到床底下来是何缘故，并且跟他感同身受。他灵机一动：还可以雇她当个侦察兵，去探一探楼梯上放没放哨。总而言之，这场对他沉思冥想最新的搅扰，非但不是灾祸，反而是天上掉下来的最好的事。

===Original===
Sneezing heartily, for he had got a piece of fluff up his nose, George rolled out from under the bed: and, scrambling to his feet with a jolly laugh, found himself gazing into the bulging eyes of a complete stranger.
That, at least, was how the girl impressed him in the first instant of their meeting. But gradually, as he stared at her, there crept into his mind the belief that somewhere and at some time he had seen her before. But where? And when?
The girl continued to gape at him. She was small and pretty, with vivid black eyes and a mouth which, if it had not been hanging open at the moment like that of a fish, would have been remarkably attractive. Silence reigned in the sleeping-porch: and Mrs. Waddington, straining her ears outside, was beginning to think that George could not be in this lair and that a further vigil was before her, when suddenly voices began to speak. What they were saying, she was unable to hear, for the door was stoutly built: but beyond a doubt one of them was George’s. Mrs. Waddington crept away, well content. Her suspicions had been confirmed, and now it remained only to decide what it was best to do about it. She moved into the shadow of the water-tank, and there remained for a space in deep thought.

===Chinese===
乔治打了个响亮的喷嚏——一撮绒毛钻进了他的鼻子——从床底下滚了出来：他笑呵呵地一骨碌爬起身，发现自己面对的，是一双暴突的眼睛，而眼睛的主人是个彻头彻尾的陌生人。
至少，两人照面的头一瞬间，这姑娘给他的印象就是如此。然而渐渐地，他凝视着她，心头悄悄爬上一种确信：从前在某个地方、某个时候，他见过她。可是在哪儿？又是什么时候？
姑娘继续呆呆地张着嘴瞪他。她个子小巧，眉眼可人，一双黑眼睛亮得有神，一张嘴若不是这会儿像鱼嘴似的张着，本该是千娇百媚的。睡廊里一片死寂：外头竖起耳朵的沃丁顿夫人开始觉得，乔治断不会藏在这处窝里，自己还得再熬一轮放哨的苦役——就在此时，人声忽然响了起来。说的是什么，她听不见，因为那门造得扎实：但其中一个人的声音，千真万确是乔治的。沃丁顿夫人蹑手蹑脚地退开了，心满意足。她的疑心得了证实，如今只剩下一件事：拿定主意该怎么办才最好。她挪进水塔的阴影里，在那儿深沉地思索了片刻。

---

===Original===
Inside the sleeping-porch, the girl, her eyes fixed on George, had begun to shrink back. At about the third shrink she bumped into the wall, and the shock seemed to restore her power of speech.
“What are you doing in my bedroom?” she cried.
The question had the effect of substituting for the embarrassment which had been gripping George a sudden bubbling fury. This, he felt, was too much. Circumstances had conspired that night to turn this sleeping-porch into a sort of meeting-place of the nations, but he was darned if he was going to have his visitors looking on the room as their own.
“What do you mean, your bedroom?” he demanded hotly. “Who are you?”
“I’m Mrs. Mullett.”

===Chinese===
睡廊里面，姑娘两眼盯住乔治，开始向后退缩。大约缩到第三缩，她背撞上了墙，这一撞仿佛把她的说话能力撞了回来。
“你在我卧室里干什么？”她喊道。
这句话像一根火柴，把攥着乔治的窘迫点着，换上了一股腾腾直冒的怒火。他觉着，这未免欺人太甚。那一晚的种种情势合谋，硬把这座睡廊变成了一个万国会议场所，可要让他这帮客人把这屋子当成自己家，他死也不干。
“你倒说说，什么叫你的卧室？”他火冒三丈地质问，“你是谁？”
“我是马利特太太。”

===Original===
“Who?”
“Mrs. Frederick Mullett.”
Mrs. Waddington had formed her plan of action. What she needed, she perceived, was a witness to come with her to this den of evil and add his testimony in support of hers. If only Lord Hunstanton had been present, as he should have been, she would have needed to look no further. But Lord Hunstanton was somewhere out in the great city, filling his ignoble tummy with food. Whom, then, could she enroll as a deputy? The question answered itself. Ferris was the man. He was ready to hand and could be fetched without delay.
Mrs. Waddington made for the stairs.
“Mrs. Mullett?” said George. “What do you mean? Mullett’s not married.”

===Chinese===
“谁？”
“弗雷德里克·马利特太太。”
沃丁顿夫人已定好了行动方案。她看明白了：她需要一位证人，同她一道去那罪恶的巢穴，添上他的证词，为她的证词作保。倘若亨斯坦顿勋爵能在场——他本该在场——她便无须另觅旁人。可亨斯坦顿勋爵正在大都会的某个角落，用吃食填塞他那不高贵的肚皮。那么，她能招募谁来充当副手呢？答案不问自明。费里斯就是那个人。他召之即来，毫不耽搁。
沃丁顿夫人朝楼梯奔去。
“马利特太太？”乔治说，“你什么意思？马利特没结婚。”

===Original===
“Yes, he is. We were married this morning.”
“Where is he?”
“I left him down below, finishing a cigar. He said we’d be all alone up here, nesting like two little birds in a tree top.”
George laughed a brassy, sardonic laugh.
“If Mullett thought anyone could ever be alone for five minutes up here, he’s an optimist. And what right has Mullett to go nesting like a little bird in my apartment?”

===Chinese===
“结了。今天早上我们结的婚。”
“他在哪儿？”
“我把他留在楼下了，抽完一支雪茄就来。他说待会儿咱俩就清净了，上头就我们两个，像两只小鸟儿在树梢上筑巢。”
乔治发出一阵刺耳的冷笑。
“要是马利特以为这楼上能有谁清净得上五分钟，那他可真够乐观的。再说，马利特凭什么学小鸟儿似的跑到我公寓里来筑巢？”

===Original===
“Is this your apartment?”
“Yes, it is.”
“Oh! Oh!”
“Stop it! Don’t make that noise. There are policemen about.”

===Chinese===
“这是你的公寓？”
“正是。”
“哎呀！哎呀！”
“别叫了！别这么嚷嚷。附近有警察。”

===Original===
“Policemen!”
“Yes.”
Tears suddenly filled the eyes that looked into his. Two small hands clasped themselves in a passionate gesture of appeal.
“Don’t turn me over to the bulls, mister! I only did it for ma’s sake. If you was out of work and starvin’ and you had to sit and watch your poor old ma bendin’ over the washtub.﻿ ﻿…”
“I haven’t got a poor old ma,” said George curtly. “And what on earth do you think you’re talking about?”

===Chinese===
“警察！”
“是。”
那双望着他的眼睛里，泪水陡然盈眶。两只小手激情满怀地合拢，做出一副哀告的姿态。
“别把我交给条子，先生！我干那都是为了俺妈。要是你没活儿干、饿着肚子，还得干坐着看你那可怜的老妈弯腰洗衣裳……”
“我没有什么可怜的老妈，”乔治冷冷地说，“你到底知道自己这番话在说些什么吗？”

===Original===
He stopped suddenly, speech wiped from his lips by a stunning discovery. The girl had unclasped her hands, and now she flung them out before her: and the gesture was all that George’s memory needed to spur it to the highest efficiency. For unconsciously Fanny Mullett had assumed the exact attitude which had lent such dramatic force to her entrance into the dining-room of Mrs. Waddington’s house at Hempstead earlier in the day. The moment he saw those outstretched arms, George remembered where he had met this girl before: and, forgetting everything else, forgetting that he was trapped on a roof with a justly exasperated policeman guarding the only convenient exit, he uttered a short, sharp bark of exultation.
“You!” he cried. “Give me that necklace.”
“What necklace?”
“The one you stole at Hempstead this afternoon.”
The girl drew herself up haughtily.

===Chinese===
他猛地顿住，一个惊雷般的发现把话从唇边抹掉了。姑娘已松开双手，此刻向前一伸：而正是这个手势，把乔治的记忆鞭策到了最高的效率。因为芬妮·马利特无意之间摆出的，恰是当日早些时候她闯进沃丁顿夫人亨普斯特德宅邸餐厅时那个气派十足的身段。一见到那两条张开的手臂，乔治立刻想起了从前在什么地方见过这姑娘：他顿时把一切都抛诸脑后——忘了自己被困在屋顶上、唯一便当的出口还有一位怒气冲冲得情有可原的警察把守着——短促而尖利地狂呼了一声。
“是你！”他喊道，“把那条项链给我。”
“什么项链？”
“你今天下午在亨普斯特德偷的那条。”
姑娘倨傲地把身子一挺。

===Original===
“Do you dare to say I stole a necklace?”
“Yes, I do.”
“Oh? And do you know what I’ll do if you bring a charge like that against me! I’ll.﻿ ﻿…”
She broke off. A discreet tap had sounded on the door.
“Honey!”

===Chinese===
“你敢说我偷了项链？”
“我敢。”
“哦？你可知道，你要是敢这么诬赖我，我会怎么着！我就……”
她话说到一半，收住了。门上响起一声克制的轻叩。
“小甜心！”

===Original===
Fanny looked at George. George looked at Fanny.
“My husband!” whispered Fanny.
George was in no mood to be intimidated by a mere Mullett. He strode to the door.
“Honey!”
George flung the door open.
“Honey!”

===Chinese===
芬妮看看乔治。乔治看看芬妮。
“我丈夫！”芬妮悄声说。
乔治可没心情被一个区区马利特吓住。他大步走向门口。
“小甜心！”
乔治把门猛地拉开。
“小甜心！”

===Original===
“Well, Mullett?”
The valet fell back a pace, his eyes widening. He passed the tip of his tongue over his lips.
“A wasp in the beehive!” cried Mullett.
“Don’t be an idiot,” said George.
Mullett was gazing at him in the manner of one stricken to the core.
“Isn’t your own bridal-trip enough for you, Mr. Finch,” he said reproachfully, “that you’ve got to come butting in on mine?”

===Chinese===
“怎么，马利特？”
贴身男仆倒退了一步，双眼越睁越大。他伸出舌尖舔了舔嘴唇。
“蜂巢里飞进了马蜂！”马利特喊道。
“别犯傻，”乔治说。
马利特凝望着他，一副被创到心窝深处的神情。
“您自个儿的新婚之旅还不够受用吗，芬奇先生，”他嗔怪道，“非得跑来搅和我这趟？”

===Original===
“Don’t be a fool. My wedding was temporarily postponed.”
“I see. And misery loves company, so you start in breaking up my home.”
“Nothing of the kind.”
“If I had known that you were on the premises, Mr. Finch,” said Mullett with dignity, “I would not have taken the liberty of making use of your domicile. Come, Fanny, we will go to a hotel.”
“Will you?” said George unpleasantly. “Let me tell you there’s a little matter to be settled before you start going to any hotel. Perhaps you are not aware that your wife is in possession of a valuable necklace belonging to the lady who, if it hadn’t been for her, would now be Mrs. George Finch?”

===Chinese===
“别犯糊涂。我的婚礼只是暂时延期。”
“原来如此。同病要相怜，所以您就来拆我的家。”
“根本没那回事。”
“我要是知道您在这片地界，芬奇先生，”马利特端着架子说，“就断不敢冒昧借用您的宅子了。走吧，芬妮，我们上旅馆去。”
“上旅馆？”乔治不客气地说，“我告诉你，你上任何旅馆之前，先有一桩小事要了结。你或许还不知道吧，你老婆手里有一串值钱的项链，那本是属于一位太太的——要不是她横插一杠子，如今那位早就是乔治·芬奇太太了？”

===Original===
Mullett clapped a hand to his forehead.
“A necklace!”
“It’s a lie,” cried his bride.
Mullett shook his head sadly. He was putting two and two together.
“When did this occur, Mr. Finch?”
“This afternoon, down at Hempstead.”

===Chinese===
马利特一巴掌拍在脑门上。
“项链！”
“撒谎，”他的新娘喊道。
马利特悲伤地摇了摇头。他正忙着把二加二凑成四。
“这是什么时候的事，芬奇先生？”
“今天下午，在亨普斯特德。”

===Original===
“Don’t you listen to him, Freddy. He’s dippy.”
“What precisely happened, Mr. Finch?”
“This woman suddenly burst into the room where everybody was and pretended that I had made love to her and deserted her. Then she fell on the table where the wedding-presents were and pretended to faint. And then she dashed out, and some time afterwards it was discovered that the necklace was gone. And don’t,” he added, turning to the accused, “say that you only did it for your poor old ma’s sake, because I’ve had a lot to put up with today, and that will be just too much.”
Mr. Mullett clicked his tongue with a sort of sorrowful pride. Girls will be girls, Frederick Mullett seemed to say, but how few girls could be as clever as his little wife.

===Chinese===
“你别听他的，弗雷迪。他脑子有毛病。”
“事情究竟是怎么发生的，芬奇先生？”
“这个女人突然闯进众人所在的房间，一口咬定我跟她谈过情又把她甩了。接着她扑到摆着结婚贺礼的桌子上，装晕倒。然后她冲出门去，过了一阵子，大家发现项链不见了。还有，”他转身冲着被告补充道，“别说你干这个是为了你那可怜的老妈，我今天忍下的已经够多了，再来这么一出可就实在受不了了。”
马利特先生啧啧咂舌，带着一种哀伤的骄傲。女孩子到底是女孩子呀，弗雷德里克·马利特似乎在这么说；可天底下有几个女孩子，能精得像他的小媳妇这样。

===Original===
“Give Mr. Finch his necklace, pettie,” he said mildly.
“I haven’t got any necklace.”
“Give it to him, dearie, just like Freddie says, or there’ll only be unpleasantness.”
“Unpleasantness,” said George, breathing hard, “is right!”
“It was a beautiful bit of work, honey, and there isn’t another girl in New York that could have thought it out, let alone gone and got away with it. Even Mr. Finch will admit it was a beautiful bit of work.”

===Chinese===
“把项链还给芬奇先生吧，小心肝，”他温和地说。
“我根本没有什么项链。”
“给他吧，亲爱的，照弗雷迪说的办，不然可就要伤和气了。”
“伤和气，”乔治喘着粗气说，“可不就是伤和气！”
“干得真漂亮，小甜心，整个纽约再找不出第二个姑娘能想出这条计来，更别说干成了还能全身而退。就连芬奇先生也得承认，这是一手漂亮的活儿。”

===Original===
“If you want Mr. Finch’s opinion﻿ ﻿…” began George heatedly.
“But we’ve done with all that sort of thing now, haven’t we, pettie? Give him his necklace, honey.”
Mrs. Mullett’s black eyes snapped. She twisted her pretty fingers irresolutely.
“Take your old necklace,” she said.

===Chinese===
“你要是想听听芬奇先生的意见……”乔治火冒三丈地开了口。
“可如今咱们跟那路营生一刀两断了，对不对，小心肝？把项链给他吧，小甜心。”
马利特太太的黑眼睛闪了闪。她漂亮的指头迟疑地绞在一起。
“拿去你那条破项链，”她说。

===Original===
George caught it as it fell.
“Thanks,” he said, and put it in his pocket.
“And now, Mr. Finch,” said Mullett suavely, “I think we will say good night. My little girl here has had a tiring day and ought to be turning in.”

===Chinese===
乔治一把接住落下来的项链。
“多谢，”他说着，把项链塞进口袋。
“那么，芬奇先生，”马利特和颜悦色地说，“我看咱们就道晚安了。我们家小丫头今天累了一整天，该歇息了。”

---

===Original===
George hurried across the roof to his apartment. Whatever the risk of leaving the safety of the sleeping-porch, it must be ignored. It was imperative that he telephone to Molly and inform her of what had happened.
He was pulling the French window open when he heard his name called: and perceived Mullett hurrying towards him from the door that led to the stairs.
“Just one moment, Mr. Finch.”
“What is it? I have a most important telephone-call to make.”
“I thought you would be glad to have this, sir.”

===Chinese===
乔治匆匆穿过屋顶，奔向自己的公寓。无论离开睡廊这个安全地带有多大风险，也顾不上了。刻不容缓，他必须给莫莉挂个电话，把发生的事告诉她。
他正拉开落地窗，忽听得有人叫他的名字：只见马利特从通楼梯的那扇门朝他快步赶来。
“稍等片刻，芬奇先生。”
“什么事？我要打个顶要紧的电话。”
“我想您会乐意收下这个的，先生。”

===Original===
With something of the air of a conjurer who, to amuse the children, produces two rabbits and the grand old flag from inside a borrowed top-hat, Mullett unclasped his fingers.
“Your necklace, sir.”
George’s hand flew to his pocket and came away empty.
“Good heavens! How﻿ ﻿… ?”
“My little girl,” explained Mullett with a proud and tender look in his eyes. “She snitched it off you, sir, as we were going out. I was able, however, to persuade her to give it up again. I reminded her that we had put all that sort of thing behind us now. I asked her how she could expect to be happy on our duck-farm if she had a thing like that on her mind, and she saw it almost at once. She’s a very reasonable girl, sir, when tactfully approached by the voice of love.”

===Chinese===
马利特松开手指，那份气派，活像一位魔术师为了逗孩子们开心，从借来的高顶礼帽里变出两只兔子和一面星条大旗。
“您的项链，先生。”
乔治的手闪电般伸向口袋——抽出来时空空如也。
“我的老天爷！怎么……？”
“我们家小丫头，”马利特解释道，眼里带着骄傲而温柔的光，“咱们出门的时候，她从您身上顺走的，先生。不过我说服她把东西还了回来。我提醒她，如今咱们跟那路营生已经一刀两断。我问她，心里揣着这么个物件，往后在咱们的养鸭场上还怎么盼着快活，她几乎立刻就想通了。只要用爱情的声音婉转相劝，先生，她是个非常通情达理的姑娘。”

===Original===
George drew a deep breath. He replaced the necklace in his inside breast-pocket, buttoned his coat and drew away a step or two.
“Are you going to let that woman loose on a duck-farm, Mullett?”
“Yes, sir. We are taking a little place in the neighbourhood of Speonk.”

===Chinese===
乔治深深吸了一口气。他把项链重新放进西装内胸袋，扣好外套，退开一两步。
“你打算放这么个女人上养鸭场去撒欢，马利特？”
“是，先生。我们在斯皮昂克（Speonk）左近盘了个小地方。”

===Original===
“She’ll have the tail-feathers off every bird on the premises before the end of the first week.”
Mullett bowed his appreciation of the compliment.
“And they wouldn’t know they’d lost them, sir,” he agreed. “There’s never been anyone in the profession fit to be reckoned in the same class with my little girl. But all that sort of thing is over now, sir. She is definitely retiring from business﻿—except for an occasional visit to the department stores during bargain-sales. A girl must have her bit of finery. Good night, sir.”
“Good night,” said George.

===Chinese===
“不出头一个礼拜，场子里每一只鸭子的尾毛都得叫她薅光。”
马利特鞠了一躬，答谢这句恭维。
“而且它们根本不会发觉自己丢了毛，先生，”他附和道，“这一行里还从来没有谁，够得上同我们家小丫头相提并论的份儿。不过那一类营生，如今都翻篇了，先生。她正式金盆洗手——只是偶尔趁大减价光顾一下百货公司。女孩子家总得有那么几件俏衣裳。晚安，先生。”
“晚安，”乔治说。

## XVII / 第十七章

### I / 一

===Original===
Mrs. Waddington had once read a story in which a series of emotions including fear, horror, amazement, consternation and a sickly dismay were described as “chasing one another” across the face of a dastardly person at the moment of realisation that his villainy had been discovered past concealment: and it was with the expectation of watching a similar parade on the moon-like countenance of Ferris, the butler, that she pressed the bell outside the door of the apartment of Mr. Lancelot Biffen on the ninth floor.
She was disappointed. Ferris, as he appeared in the doorway in answer to her ring, lacked a little of his customary portentous dignity, but that was only because we authors, after a gruelling bout at the desk, are always apt to look a shade frazzled. The butler’s hair was disordered where he had plucked at it in the agony of composition, and there was more ink on the tip of his nose than would have been there on a more formal occasion: but otherwise he was in pretty good shape, and he did not even start on perceiving the identity of his visitor.
“Mr. Biffen is not at home, madam,” said Ferris equably.

===Chinese===
沃丁顿夫人从前读到过一个故事：一个奸恶之徒眼看恶行败露、再难遮掩的那一刻，恐惧、惊骇、诧异、错愕，外加一阵病恹恹的沮丧，一连串情绪在他脸上“你追我赶”。此刻她按响兰斯洛特·比芬先生九楼公寓门外的铃铛，指望着在男管家费里斯那张月亮似的面孔上，一睹一场大同小异的游行。
她失望了。费里斯应铃现身门口，比起平素那副煞有介事的威严稍稍欠缺了一点，但那也只是因为我们这些执笔为生的人，在书桌前鏖战一场之后，难免要显出几分憔悴。男管家的头发乱糟糟，那是构思到苦处自己揪的；鼻尖上的墨水，也比正式场合该有的多了那么一些：不过除此之外他状态相当好，甚至认出来客是谁时，眼皮都没有跳一下。
“比芬先生不在家，夫人，”费里斯不慌不忙地说。

===Original===
“I do not wish to see Mr. Biffen.” Mrs. Waddington swelled with justifiable wrath. “Ferris,” she said, “I know all!”
“Indeed, madam?”
“You have no sick relative,” proceeded Mrs. Waddington, though her tone suggested the opinion that anyone related to him had good reason to be sick. “You are here because you are writing a scurrilous report of what happened this afternoon at my house for a gutter rag called *Town Gossip*.”

===Chinese===
“我并不想见比芬先生。”沃丁顿夫人义愤填膺，浑身都涨大了一圈。“费里斯，”她说，“我全知道了！”
“哦？是吗，夫人？”
“你根本没有什么生病的亲戚，”沃丁顿夫人继续说道，尽管她那口吻分明表示，凡跟他沾亲带故的人都有充分的理由生病。“你来这里，是替一份叫做《街谈巷议》的地摊小报，撰写一篇诽谤今天下午我宅中所出之事的下流报道。”

===Original===
“With which is incorporated ‘Broadway Whispers’ and ‘Times Square Tattle,’ ” murmured the butler, absently.
“You ought to be ashamed of yourself!”
Ferris raised his eyebrows.

===Chinese===
“另附刊《百老汇耳语》（Broadway Whispers）与《时代广场碎语》（Times Square Tattle），”男管家心不在焉地咕哝道。
“你也不怕丢人！”
费里斯扬了扬眉毛。

===Original===
“I venture to take issue with you, madam. The profession of journalism is an honourable one. Many very estimable men have written for the Press. Horace Greeley,” said Ferris, specifying. “Delane.﻿ ﻿…”
“Bah!”
“Madam?”

===Chinese===
“恕我斗胆提出异议，夫人。新闻这一行是个高尚的行业。许多极可敬的人物都曾为报界执笔。霍勒斯·格里利（Horace Greeley），”费里斯举例道，“还有德莱恩（Delane）……”
“呸！”
“夫人？”

===Original===
“But we will go into that later.”
“Very good, madam.”
“Meanwhile, I wish you to accompany me to the roof.﻿ ﻿…”
“I fear I must respectfully decline, madam. I have not climbed since I was a small lad.”

===Chinese===
“不过这笔账咱们回头再算。”
“遵命，夫人。”
“现在，我要你陪我上屋顶去。……”
“恕我不得不恭敬回绝，夫人。打从我很小的时候起，我就没再爬过高了。”

===Original===
“You can walk up a flight of stairs, can’t you?”
“Oh, stairs? Decidedly, madam. I will be at your disposal in a few moments.”
“I wish you to accompany me now.”
The butler shook his head.

===Chinese===
“走一段楼梯你总会吧？”
“哦，楼梯？那自然，夫人。稍候几分钟，我便听候您的差遣。”
“我要你现在就陪我走。”
男管家摇了摇头。

===Original===
“If I might excuse myself, madam. I am engaged on the concluding passages of the article to which you alluded just now, and I am anxious to complete it before Mr. Biffen’s return.”
Mrs. Waddington caused the eye before which Sigsbee H. had so often curled up and crackled like a burnt feather to blaze imperiously upon the butler. He met it with the easy aplomb of one who in his time has looked at dukes and made them feel that their trousers were bagging at the knees.
“Would you care to step inside and wait, madam?”

===Chinese===
“恕我容我告个便，夫人。我正在赶写您方才提到的那篇文章的收尾几段，一心想在比芬先生回来之前完稿。”
沃丁顿夫人把那双眼睛点燃了——西格斯比·H 在那双眼睛面前可不止一回缩成一团，像片烧焦的羽毛似的蜷曲作响——此刻，那双眼睛威风凛凛地直射男管家。费里斯迎着这道目光，神色自若；这份从容属于那种一辈子打量过公爵、还能叫公爵觉得自己裤子膝头松松垮垮的人物。
“您可愿意进屋里等，夫人？”

===Original===
Mrs. Waddington was reluctantly obliged to realise that she was quelled. She had shot her bolt. A cyclone might shake this man, but not the human eye.
“I will not step inside.”
“Very good, madam. For what reason do you desire me to accompany you to the roof?”

===Chinese===
沃丁顿夫人不情不愿地不得不承认：自己被镇住了。她的箭已经射完了。龙卷风或许能撼动此人，人的眼睛却不能。
“我不进去。”
“遵命，夫人。您要我陪您上屋顶，所为何事？”

===Original===
“I want you to﻿—to look at something.”
“If it is the view, madam, I should mention that I have already visited the top of the Woolworth Building.”
“It is not the view. I wish you to look at a man who is living in open sin.”
“Very good, madam.” There was no surprise in Ferris’s manner, only a courteous suggestion that he was always glad to look at men living in open sin. “I will be at your disposal in a few minutes.”

===Chinese===
“我要你——去看一样东西。”
“倘若看的是景致，夫人，那么容我禀告：伍尔沃思大楼的楼顶，在下已经游览过了。”
“不是景致。我要你去看一个公然姘居的男人。”
“遵命，夫人。”费里斯的举止里没有半点诧异，只有一种彬彬有礼的意味，表示他向来乐于观看公然姘居的男人。“几分钟之后，我便听候您的差遣。”

===Original===
He closed the door gently, and Mrs. Waddington, full of the coward rage which dares to burn but does not dare to blaze, abandoned her intention of kicking in a panel and stood on the landing, heaving gently. And presently there was borne up to her from the lower levels a cheerful sound of whistling.
Lord Hunstanton came into view.
“Hullo-ullo-ullo!” said Lord Hunstanton exuberantly. “Here I am, here I am, here I am!”﻿—meaning, of course, that there he was.
A striking change had taken place in the man’s appearance since Mrs. Waddington had last seen him. He now wore the carefree and debonair expression of one who has dined and dined well. The sparkle in his eye spoke of clear soup, the smile on his lips was eloquent of roast duck and green peas. To Mrs. Waddington, who had not broken bread since lunchtime, he seemed the most repellant object on which she had ever gazed.

===Chinese===
他轻轻掩上了门。沃丁顿夫人满腔是那种敢燃不敢燎的懦夫之怒，只得放弃一脚踹破门板的打算，站在楼梯平台上，轻轻地起伏喘气。不多时，楼下有什么人的口哨声欢快地飘了上来。
亨斯坦顿勋爵露面了。
“好嗬——嗬——嗬！”亨斯坦顿勋爵兴高采烈地嚷道。“我在这儿，我在这儿，我在这儿！”——意思当然是说，他在那儿。
自沃丁顿夫人上回见他以来，此人的外貌发生了惊人的变化。他如今一脸悠然自得、风流蕴藉的神情，那是吃饱了饭、而且吃得极美的人才有的。他眼里的光彩讲的是清汤，他唇边的笑意则雄辩地诉说着烤鸭配青豆。而对从午饭之后粒米未进的沃丁顿夫人来说，他简直是她平生所见最可憎的东西。

===Original===
“I trust you have had a good dinner,” she said icily.
His lordship’s sunny smile broadened, and a dreamy look came into his eyes.
“Absolutely!” he replied. “I started with a spruce spoonful of Julienne and passed on by way of a breezy half-lobster on the shell to about as upstanding a young Long Island duckling as I have ever bitten.”
“Be quiet!” said Mrs. Waddington, shaken to the core. The man’s conversation seemed to her utterly revolting.

===Chinese===
“想必您这顿晚饭享用得十分美满。”她冷冰冰地说。
勋爵脸上阳光般的笑容愈发灿烂，眼睛里泛起一片梦也似的神色。
“美极了！”他答道。“开场是清清爽爽的一匙朱利恩菜丝清汤（Julienne），接着顺风顺水地啃了半只带壳龙虾，末了落到一只挺拔精神的长岛嫩鸭身上——我这辈子咬过的鸭子里，数它出挑。”
“闭嘴！”沃丁顿夫人说，一颗心震得七零八落。这人的谈话在她听来简直令人作呕。

===Original===
“Finishing up with.﻿ ﻿…”
“Will you be quiet! I have no desire to hear the details of your repast.”
“Oh, sorry! I thought you had.”
“You have been away long enough to have eaten half-a-dozen dinners. However, as it happens, you are not too late. I have something to show you.”

===Chinese===
“最后压轴的还有……”
“叫你闭嘴！你那顿饭的细节，我一个字也不想听。”
“哦，抱歉！我还当您想听呢。”
“你走开的工夫，足够吃上六顿饭了。不过，说来也巧，你回来得还不算太晚。我有样东西给你看。”

===Original===
“That’s good. Moral turpitude pretty strong on the wing, eh?”
“A few moments ago,” said Mrs. Waddington, leading the way to the roof, “I observed a young woman enter what appears to be some kind of outdoor sleeping-porch attached to George Finch’s apartment, and immediately afterwards I heard her voice in conversation with George Finch within.”
“Turpy,” said his lordship, shaking his head reprovingly. “Very turpy.”
“I came down to fetch Ferris, my butler, as a witness, but fortunately you have returned in time. Though why you were not back half an hour ago I cannot understand.”

===Chinese===
“那敢情好。伤风败俗这野味，翅膀挺硬吧，嗯？”
“几分钟之前，”沃丁顿夫人说着，在前引路往屋顶而去，“我亲眼看见一个年轻女人，走进了像是乔治·芬奇公寓附带的那种户外睡廊；紧接着，我就听见屋里有她说话的声音，正在跟乔治·芬奇交谈。”
“败俗，”勋爵责备地摇着头说。“非常败俗。”
“我下楼是去叫我的男管家费里斯来做见证的，好在你们回来得正是时候。不过你半个钟头前怎么不回来，我实在想不通。”

===Original===
“But I was telling you. I dallied with a mouthful of Julienne.﻿ ﻿…”
“Be quiet!”
Lord Hunstanton followed her, puzzled. He could not understand what seemed to him a morbid distaste on his companion’s part to touch on the topic of food. They came out on the roof, and Mrs. Waddington, raising a silent and beckoning finger, moved on tiptoe towards the sleeping-porch.

===Chinese===
“我不是正跟您说嘛。我陪一小口朱利恩清汤磨了半天工夫……”
“闭嘴！”
亨斯坦顿勋爵满腹狐疑地跟在后面。在他看来，这位同伴一沾吃的话题便露出病态的嫌恶，实在叫人费解。两人来到屋顶，沃丁顿夫人竖起一根手指，无声地招了招，蹑手蹑脚朝睡廊走去。

===Original===
“Now what?” inquired his lordship, as they paused before the door.
Mrs. Waddington rapped upon the panel.
“George Finch!”
Complete silence followed the words.

===Chinese===
“现在怎么着？”两人在那扇门前停住时，勋爵问道。
沃丁顿夫人笃笃敲响门板。
“乔治·芬奇！”
话音落地，一片死寂。

===Original===
“George Finch!”
“George Finch!” echoed his lordship, conscious of his responsibilities as a chorus.
“Finch!” said Mrs. Waddington.
“George!” cried Lord Hunstanton.
Mrs. Waddington flung open the door. All was darkness within. She switched on the light. The room was empty.

===Chinese===
“乔治·芬奇！”
“乔治·芬奇！”勋爵跟着喊，深深意识到自己身为和声的职责。
“芬奇！”沃丁顿夫人喊。
“乔治！”亨斯坦顿勋爵喊。
沃丁顿夫人猛地把门推开。屋里一片漆黑。她啪地拧亮电灯。房间空空如也。

===Original===
“Well!” said Mrs. Waddington.
“Perhaps they’re under the bed.”
“Go and look.”
“But suppose he bites at me.”
Nothing is truer than that the secret of all successful operations consists in the overlooking of no eventuality, but it was plain that Mrs. Waddington considered that in this instance her ally was carrying caution too far. She turned on him with a snort of annoyance: and, having turned, remained staring frozenly at something that had suddenly manifested itself in his lordship’s rear.

===Chinese===
“好啊！”沃丁顿夫人说。
“没准儿他们在床底下。”
“去，看看去。”
“可万一他咬我呢。”
一切成功的行动，其秘诀端在于不放过任何一种不测，此言千真万确；可明摆着，沃丁顿夫人认为她的盟友这一回把谨慎做过头了。她气呼呼地哼了一声，冲他转过身去；这一转身，整个人却僵住了，死死盯住勋爵背后突然显形的一件东西。

===Original===
This something was a long, stringy policeman: and, though Mrs. Waddington had met this policeman only once in her life, the circumstances of that meeting had been such that the memory of him had lingered. She recognised him immediately: and, strong woman though she was, wilted like a snail that has just received a handful of salt between the eyes.
“What’s up?” inquired Lord Hunstanton. He, too, turned. “Oh, what ho! the constabulary!”
Officer Garroway was gazing at Mrs. Waddington with an eye from which one of New York’s Bohemian evenings had wiped every trace of its customary mildness. So intense, indeed, was the malevolence of its gleam that, if there had been two such eyes boring into hers, it is probable that Mrs. Waddington would have swooned. Fortunately, the other was covered with a piece of raw steak and a bandage, and so was out of action.
“Ah!” said Officer Garroway.

===Chinese===
这件东西是一个又瘦又长的警察；沃丁顿夫人平生虽只跟这位警察打过一回照面，可那回的遭遇非同小可，此人的音容从此赖在她记忆里不走。她一眼便认出了他；尽管是条响当当的女汉子，却也像一只眉心刚被撒了一大撮盐的蜗牛，唰地蔫了下去。
“怎么回事？”亨斯坦顿勋爵问。他也转过身去。“哦嗬！官府的人来啦！”
加罗威警官正拿一只眼睛死死瞪着沃丁顿夫人——纽约某个波西米亚之夜，早已把那只眼睛惯常的温和擦拭得干干净净。那目光里的凶光实在炽烈，倘若这样的眼睛有两只、一齐朝她钻过来，沃丁顿夫人多半要当场昏厥。所幸另一只蒙着一块生牛排和绷带，暂时无法上阵。
“啊！”加罗威警官说。

===Original===
There is little in the word “Ah!” when you write it down and take a look at it to suggest that under certain conditions it can be one of the most sinister words in the language. But hear it spoken by a policeman in whose face you have recently thrown pepper, and you will be surprised. To Mrs. Waddington, as she shrank back into the sleeping-porch, it seemed a sort of combination of an Indian war-whoop, the Last Trump, and the howl of a pursuing wolf-pack. Her knees weakened beneath her, and she collapsed on the bed.
“Copped you, have I?” proceeded the policeman.
The question was plainly a rhetorical one, for he did not pause for a reply. He adjusted the bandage that held the steak, and continued his remarks.
“You’re pinched!”

===Chinese===
“啊！”这个词，你写下来端详端详，实在瞧不出它有什么凶险；可在特定条件下，它却是英语里最不吉利的字眼之一。然而，倘若你刚朝一位警察脸上撒过胡椒面，再听他亲口说出来，你准会吃上一惊。在沃丁顿夫人听来——正当她往睡廊里退缩时——这一声简直就是印第安人的战嚎、末日审判的号角、追猎的狼群的嗥叫三样合一。她双膝一软，瘫倒在床上。
“这回逮着你了，啊？”警察接着说。
这问题分明是修辞性的反问，因为他压根没停下来等回答。他紧了紧绑牛排的绷带，继续发表高论。
“你被捕了！”

===Original===
It seemed to Lord Hunstanton that all this was very odd and irregular.
“I say, look here, you know, what I mean to say is.﻿ ﻿…”
“So are you,” said Officer Garroway. “You seem to be in it, too. You’re both pinched. And start any funny business,” concluded the constable, swinging his nightstick in a ham-like fist, “and I’ll bend this over your nut. Get me?”
There followed one of those pauses which so often punctuate the conversation of comparative strangers. Officer Garroway seemed to have said his say. Mrs. Waddington had no observations to make. And, though Lord Hunstanton would have liked to put a question or two, the spectacle of that oscillating nightstick had the effect of driving the words out of his head. It was the sort of nightstick that gave one a throbbing feeling about the temples merely to look at it. He swallowed feebly, but made no remark.
And then from somewhere below there sounded the voice of one who cried “Beamish! Hey, Beamish!” It was the voice of Sigsbee H. Waddington.

===Chinese===
在亨斯坦顿勋爵看来，这一整套把戏稀奇古怪，全不合章程。
“我说，你听着，你知道，我的意思是说……”
“你也一样，”加罗威警官说。“你瞧着也是一伙的。你们俩一起被捕了。谁敢耍花样，”这名警察抡着火腿般大拳里攥的警棍，收尾道，“我就拿这家伙敲你天灵盖。听明白了没有？”
接下来是一阵冷场——素昧平生之人交谈之间，常有这种冷场来点缀。加罗威警官似乎话已说尽。沃丁顿夫人无话可说。亨斯坦顿勋爵虽有一两个问题想提，可那根来回晃悠的警棍实在扎眼，把他满脑子的话晃了个精光。那是一种你只要看上一眼、太阳穴便突突直跳的警棍。他有气无力地咽了口唾沫，没敢吱声。
就在这时，楼下什么地方传来一个人的喊声：“比米什！喂，比米什！”那是西格斯比·H·沃丁顿的声音。

### II / 二

===Original===
Nothing is more annoying to the reader of a chronicle like this than to have somebody suddenly popping up in some given spot and to find that the historian does not propose to offer any explanation as to how he got there. A conscientious recorder should explain the exits and the entrances of even so insignificant a specimen of the race as Sigsbee H. Waddington: and the present scribe must now take time off in order to do so.
Sigsbee H., it may be remembered, had started out to search through New York for a policeman named Gallagher: and New York had given him of its abundance. It had provided for Mr. Waddington’s inspection a perfect wealth of Gallaghers: but, owing to the fact that what he really wished to meet was not a Gallagher but a Garroway, nothing in the nature of solid success had rewarded his efforts. He had seen tall Gallaghers and small Gallaghers, thin Gallaghers and stout Gallaghers, a cross-eyed Gallagher, a pimpled Gallagher, a Gallagher with red hair, a Gallagher with a broken nose, two Gallaghers who looked like bad dreams, and a final supreme Gallagher who looked like nothing on earth. But he had not found the man to whom he had sold the stock of the Finer and Better Motion Picture Company of Hollywood, Cal.
Many men in such a position would have given up the struggle. Sigsbee H. Waddington did. The last Gallagher had been on duty in the neighbourhood of Bleeker Street, and Mr. Waddington, turning into Washington Square, tottered to a bench and sagged down on it.

===Chinese===
读这样一部编年史，最叫读者恼火的莫过于：某个人物冷不防在某个指定地点冒将出来，而史家却不打算解释一句他是怎么到的那儿。哪怕像西格斯比·H·沃丁顿这般无足轻重的人类样品，一位尽职的记事者也该把他的出场与退场交代明白；眼下这个写书的，只好暂且搁下正题，来办这件事了。
读者或许还记得，西格斯比·H 出门是要把纽约城筛上一遍，找一个姓加拉格尔的警察；而纽约倾其所有，慷慨相赠。供沃丁顿先生过目检阅的加拉格尔，存货丰盈：怎奈他真正想见的并非哪位加拉格尔，而是加罗威，因此他的辛苦始终没换来一点实打实的成果。高个儿的加拉格尔他见过，矮个儿的加拉格尔他见过，瘦的加拉格尔、胖的加拉格尔，一个斗鸡眼的加拉格尔，一个满脸粉刺的加拉格尔，一个红头发的加拉格尔，一个塌鼻梁的加拉格尔，两位瞧着活像噩梦的加拉格尔，末了还有一位登峰造极、瞧着简直不像人世间之物的加拉格尔。可是他当年把加利福尼亚州好莱坞“更精更美电影公司”的股票卖与的那位主顾，他没能找到。
处在他这个境地，许多人早就洗手不干了。西格斯比·H·沃丁顿正是不干了的那个。最后一位加拉格尔在布利克街（Bleeker Street）左近当值；沃丁顿先生一拐进华盛顿广场，便跌跌撞撞挪到一条长椅前，整个人瘫了上去。

===Original===
For some moments, the ecstatic relief of resting his feet occupied his mind to the exclusion of everything else. Then there occurred to him a thought which, had it arrived earlier in the day, would have saved him a considerable output of energy. He suddenly recollected that he had met the missing policeman at the apartment of Hamilton Beamish: and, pursuing this train of thought to its logical conclusion, decided that Hamilton Beamish was the one person who would be able to give him information as to the man’s whereabouts.
No tonic, however popular and widely-advertised, could have had so instantly revivifying an effect. The difference between Mr. Waddington before taking and after taking this inspiration was almost magical. An instant before, he had been lying back on the bench in a used-up attitude which would have convinced any observer that the only thing to do with a man in such a stage of exhausted dejection was to notify the City authorities and have him swept up and deposited in the incinerator with the rest of the local garbage. But now, casting off despair like a cloak, he sprang from his seat and was across the Square and heading for the Sheridan before such an observer would have had time to say “What ho!”
Not even the fact that the elevator was not running could check his exhilarated progress. He skimmed up the stairs to Hamilton Beamish’s door like a squirrel.
“Beamish!” he cried. “Hey, Beamish!”

===Chinese===
有那么一阵子，双脚得到休息的狂喜占据了他的身心，把其余一切都排遣出去。接着，一个念头冒了上来——这念头若在今天早些时候驾到，本可替他省下一大笔气力。他猛然想起：那个遍寻不着的警察，他是在汉密尔顿·比米什的公寓里遇见的。顺着这条思路推到合乎逻辑的结论便是：普天之下，能告诉他那人下落的，唯有汉密尔顿·比米什一人。
任何补药，无论多么风行、广告做得多么铺天盖地，都产生不了这般立竿见影的还魂之效。沃丁顿先生服此灵药之前与之后的判若两人，简直近乎魔法。前一瞬间，他还仰躺在长椅上，一副油尽灯枯的姿态，任哪个旁观者看了都会确信：对落到这般枯竭颓丧地步的人，唯一的办法就是通知市政当局，把他跟本地的其余垃圾一道扫走，送进垃圾焚化炉。可这会儿，他像甩斗篷似的甩掉了绝望，从椅子上一跃而起——那位旁观者还没来得及喊出一声“好家伙”，他已穿过整个广场，直奔谢里丹公寓而去了。
连电梯停开也没能拦住他兴冲冲的脚步。他像只松鼠似的，一路蹿上楼梯，来到汉密尔顿·比米什的门前。
“比米什！”他嚷道。“喂，比米什！”


---

===Original===
Up on the roof, Officer Garroway started as a warhorse at the sound of the bugle. He knew that voice. And, if it should seem remarkable that he should have remembered it after so many days, having been in conversation with it but once, the explanation is that Mr. Waddington’s voice had certain tonal qualities that rendered it individual and distinctive. You might mistake it for a squeaking file, but you could not mistake it for the voice of anybody but Sigsbee H. Waddington.
“Gosh!” said Officer Garroway, shaking like an aspen.
The voice had had its effect also on Mrs. Waddington. She started up as if the bed on which she sat had become suddenly incandescent.
“Siddown!” said Officer Garroway.

===Chinese===
屋顶上，加罗威警官闻声一震，恰似战马听到了号角。他认得这个嗓音。隔了这么多天，拢共只跟它谈过一回话，居然还记得，听着也许稀奇；解释只有一个：沃丁顿先生的嗓音别具某些音质，独特而鲜明。你或许会把它错听成锉刀的吱吱声，可你绝不会把它错当成西格斯比·H·沃丁顿以外任何人的嗓音。
“我的妈呀！”加罗威警官说，抖得像片山杨树叶。
这个嗓音对沃丁顿夫人也立竿见影。她腾地跳了起来，仿佛坐着的床突然烧得白炽。
“坐下！”加罗威警官说。

===Original===
Mrs. Waddington sat down.
“My dear old constable,” began Lord Hunstanton.
“Shut up!” said Officer Garroway.
Lord Hunstanton shut up.

===Chinese===
沃丁顿夫人坐下了。
“我亲爱的老差官，”亨斯坦顿勋爵开了腔。
“闭嘴！”加罗威警官说。
亨斯坦顿勋爵闭了嘴。

===Original===
“Gosh!” said Officer Garroway once more.
He eyed his prisoners in an agony of indecision. He was in the unfortunate position of wanting to be in two places at once. To rush down the stairs and accost the man who had sold him that stock would mean that he would have to leave these two birds, with the result that they would undoubtedly escape. And that they should escape was the last thing in the world that Officer Garroway desired. These two represented the most important capture he had made since he had joined the Force. The female bird was a detected burglar and assaulter of the police, and he rather fancied that, when he took him to headquarters and looked him up in the Rogues Gallery, the male bird would prove to be Willie the Dude, wanted in Syracuse for slipping the snide. To land them in the coop meant promotion.
On the other hand, to go down and get his fingers nicely placed about the throat of the man downstairs meant that he would get his three hundred dollars back.

===Chinese===
“我的妈呀！”加罗威警官又说了一遍。
他眼巴巴地打量着两名阶下囚，拿不定主意，煎熬万分。他不幸处在一心二用的位置上：冲下楼去，揪住那个把股票卖给他的人，就意味着得丢下这两只鸟儿，而这两只鸟儿无疑会趁机逃之夭夭。他们一旦逃了，那可是加罗威警官天底下最不乐意的事。这两位代表他自穿上警服以来最重要的一票捕获。雌鸟是个窃案现行犯兼袭警犯；至于雄鸟，他颇有几分把握——把他押到总局，往罪犯照片馆里一查，八成就是锡拉丘兹（Syracuse）正在通缉的使用假钞犯“花花公子”威利（Willie the Dude）。把这双鸟儿送进局子，就意味着升职。
可从另一方面说，下楼去把手指头稳稳当当掐住楼下那位的喉咙，就意味着他那三百美元能够物归原主。

===Original===
What to do? What to do?
“Oh, gosh! Oh, Gee!” sighed Officer Garroway.
A measured footstep made itself heard. There came into his range of vision an ambassadorial-looking man with a swelling waistcoat and a spot of ink on his nose. And, seeing him, the policeman uttered a cry of elation.

===Chinese===
怎么办？怎么办？
“哎呀妈呀！哎呀老天！”加罗威警官长叹道。
一阵不紧不慢的脚步声传了过来。一位气派宛如大使的人物进入他的视野：马甲绷得鼓鼓的，鼻尖上沾着一个墨点。一看见他，警察发出一声欢呼。

### III / 三

===Original===
“Hey!” said Officer Garroway.
“Sir?” said the newcomer.
“You’re a deputy.”
“No, sir. I am a butler.”
“Say, Be-eeeee-mish!” bleated the voice below.

===Chinese===
“喂！”加罗威警官说。
“先生？”来人应道。
“你当副手了。”
“不是，先生。我是男管家。”
“喂——比——米——什！”楼下那嗓音拉长了调子，咩咩地哀鸣起来。

===Original===
It roused the policeman to a frenzy of direct action. In a calmer moment he might have been quelled by the protruding green-grey eyes that were looking at him with such quiet austerity: but now they had no terrors for him.
“You’re a deputy,” he repeated. “You know what that means, don’t you, dumbbell? I’m an officer of the Law and I appoint you my deputy.”
“I have no desire to be a deputy,” said the other with the cold sub-tinkle in his voice which had once made the younger son of a marquess resign from his clubs and go to Uganda.

===Chinese===
这一声激得警察奋起直动，如疯似狂。换一个心平气和的时刻，那双凸出的灰绿眼睛正带着不动声色的凛然端详着他，兴许能把他镇住：可这会儿，那双眼睛在他身上全无威慑之力。
“你当副手了，”他重复道。“这话什么意思，你懂吧，蠢货？我是执法的警官，我任命你做我的副手。”
“我无意当什么副手，”对方说，嗓音里带着那种冷冰冰的铮鸣——它曾叫一位侯爵家的幼子退出名下所有俱乐部，远走乌干达。

===Original===
It was wasted on Officer Garroway. The man was berserk.
“That’s all right what you desire and don’t desire. I’ve made you a deputy, and you’ll be one or go up the river for resisting an officer of the Law, besides getting a dot over the bean with this stick that’ll make you wish you hadn’t. Now then?”
“The position being such as you have outlined,” said the butler with dignity, “I have no alternative but to comply with your wishes.”
“What’s your name?”
“Rupert Anthony Ferris.”

===Chinese===
这番功夫落在加罗威警官身上全然白费。此人已然疯魔。
“你想不想，不碍事。副手我已经任命了，你当也得当；不当就是抗拒执法警官、发配大狱，外加挨这根棍子在你脑门上敲出一个疙瘩，叫你后悔都来不及。怎么样？”
“既然形势如你所勾画的那般，”男管家庄重地说，“除了从命，我别无选择。”
“你叫什么名字？”
“鲁珀特·安东尼·费里斯（Rupert Anthony Ferris）。”

===Original===
“Where do you live?”
“I am in the employment of Mrs. Sigsbee H. Waddington, at present residing at Hempstead, Long Island.”
“Well, I’ve got two birds in here that are wanted at headquarters, see? I’m locking them in.” Officer Garroway slammed the door and turned the key. “Now, all you have to do is to stand on guard till I come back. Not much to ask, is it?”
“The task appears to be well within the scope of my powers, and I shall endeavour to fulfil it faithfully.”

===Chinese===
“住在哪里？”
“我受雇于西格斯比·H·沃丁顿夫人，夫人眼下暂居长岛亨普斯特德。”
“这么着，我这屋里关着两只鸟儿，总局正通缉着呢，明白吗？我把他们锁在里头。”加罗威警官砰地摔门，拧上钥匙。“眼下，你要做的就是站岗，等我回来。不过分吧？”
“这项任务看来完全在我能力范围之内，我必当恪尽职守。”

===Original===
“Then go to it,” said Officer Garroway.
Ferris stood with his back to the sleeping-porch, looking at the moon with a touch of wistfulness. Moonlight nights always made him a little homesick, for Brangmarley Hall had been at its best on such occasions. How often had he, then a careless, lighthearted footman, watched the moonbeams reflected on the waters of the moat and, with all the little sounds of the English country whispering in his ear, pondered idly on what would win the two o’clock race at Ally Pally next day. Happy days! Happy days!
The sound of someone murmuring his name brought his wandering thoughts back to the workaday world. He looked about him with interest, which deepened as he saw that he had apparently got the roof to himself.

===Chinese===
“那就开工吧。”加罗威警官说。
费里斯背朝睡廊站着，望着月亮，心头微觉怅惘。月明之夜总叫他有一点想家，因为布兰马利庄园最美的时节，恰是这种天气。那时他还是个无忧无虑、心高气爽的跟班，多少个这样的夜晚，他望着月光映上护城河的水面，英伦乡间种种细碎的声响在耳畔低语，一边出神地琢磨：明天阿利帕利（Ally Pally）两点钟那场赛马，哪匹马能赢。好日子啊！好日子！
有人低声念他名字的声音，把游散的思绪拉回了眼前的尘世。他兴致盎然地环顾四周；待看清整片屋顶显然只归他一人时，这兴致愈发深了。

===Original===
“Ferris!”
The butler was a man who never permitted himself to be surprised, but he was conscious now of something not unlike that emotion. Disembodied voices which whispered his name were new in his experience. It could hardly be one of the two birds in the sleeping-porch that was speaking, for they were behind concrete walls and a solid door, and would have had to raise their voices far louder to make themselves heard.
“Ferris!”

===Chinese===
“费里斯！”
男管家向来不容许自己吃惊，可这会儿他分明觉出一种跟吃惊相差无几的情绪。脱了人身的嗓音低唤他的名字，这在他的阅历里还是头一遭。说话的总不至于会是睡廊里的两只鸟儿之一：他们隔着水泥墙和一扇实心门，要叫人听见，非得把嗓门提得老高不可。
“费里斯！”

===Original===
Possibly an angel, thought the butler: and was turning his mind to other things when he perceived that in the wall by which he stood there was a small window high up in the concrete. So it was one of the birds, after all. Scarcely had he made the discovery of the window when the voice spoke again, and so distinctly this time that he was able to recognise it as that of his employer, Mrs. Sigsbee H. Waddington.
“Ferris!”
“Madam?” said Ferris.
“It is I, Ferris﻿—Mrs. Waddington.”

===Chinese===
兴许是位天使吧，男管家想。他正要把心思转去别处，忽然发觉自己倚着的那堵墙上，高处的水泥里嵌着一扇小窗。原来还是两只鸟儿之一。他刚发现那扇窗，那声音又说话了；这一回字字分明，他听出正是他的东家、西格斯比·H·沃丁顿夫人。
“费里斯！”
“夫人？”费里斯应道。
“是我，费里斯——沃丁顿夫人。”

===Original===
“Very good, madam.”
“What did you say? Come closer. I can’t hear you.”
The butler, though not a man who did this sort of thing as a general rule, indulgently stretched a point and stood on tiptoe. He advanced his mouth towards the hole in the wall and repeated his remark.
“I said ‘Very good, madam,’ ” explained this modern Pyramus.

===Chinese===
“遵命，夫人。”
“你说什么？靠近点。我听不见。”
男管家虽不是惯干这种事的人，这回也破格通融，踮起了脚尖。他把嘴凑向墙上的洞口，把方才的话又说了一遍。
“我说的是：‘遵命，夫人。’”这位现代皮拉摩斯（Pyramus）解释道。

===Original===
“Oh? Well, be quick, Ferris.”
“Quick, madam?”
“Be quick and let us out.”
“You wish me to release you, madam?”

===Chinese===
“哦？那好，快着点，费里斯。”
“快，夫人？”
“赶快放我们出去。”
“您是要我放您出去，夫人？”

===Original===
“Yes.”
“H’m!”
“What did you say?”
The butler, who had found the strain of standing on tiptoe a little hard on his fallen arches, reared himself up once more.

===Chinese===
“正是。”
“哼！”
“你说什么？”
男管家只觉踮着脚尖这差事对一双扁平足未免苛酷，便又重新直起身子。

===Original===
“I said ‘H’m!’ madam.”
“What did he say?” asked the voice of Lord Hunstanton.
“He said ‘H’m!’ ” replied the voice of Mrs. Waddington.
“Why?”

===Chinese===
“我说的是：‘哼！’夫人。”
“他说什么？”亨斯坦顿勋爵的声音问。
“他说：‘哼！’”沃丁顿夫人的声音答。
“为什么？”

===Original===
“How should I know? I believe the man has been drinking.”
“Let *me* talk to the fellow,” said Lord Hunstanton.
There was a pause. Then a male understudy took up Thisbe’s portion of the performance.
“Hi!”

===Chinese===
“我哪儿知道？我看这人是喝多了。”
“让*我*来跟这家伙讲，”亨斯坦顿勋爵说。
停顿了片刻。随后，一位男演员替角接过了提斯柏（Thisbe）的那部分戏码。
“喂！”

===Original===
“Sir?” said Ferris.
“You out there, what’s your name.﻿ ﻿…”
“My name is﻿—and has always been﻿—Ferris, sir.”
“Well, then, Ferris, listen to me and understand that I’m not the sort of man to stand any dashed nonsense or anything like that of any description whatsoever. Why, when this dear, good lady told you to let us out, did you reply ‘H’m!’? Answer me that﻿—yes or no.”

===Chinese===
“先生？”费里斯应道。
“外头那位，你叫什么名字。……”
“我名叫——一向都叫——费里斯，先生。”
“那么，费里斯，你听好，给我弄明白：我可不是那种容得下任何混账胡话、不管它什么名堂的人。我问你，这位可亲的好太太叫你放我们出去，你凭什么答一句‘哼’？回答我——是，还是不是。”

===Original===
The butler raised himself on tiptoe again.
“The ejaculation was intended to convey doubt, your lordship.”
“Doubt? What about?”
“As to whether I could see my way to letting you out, your lordship.”

===Chinese===
男管家又踮起了脚尖。
“那声感叹意在表达怀疑，勋爵。”
“怀疑？怀疑什么？”
“怀疑我自己能否想出放您二位出去的门道，勋爵。”

===Original===
“Don’t be a silly idiot. It’s not so dark as all that.”
“I was alluding to the difficulties confronting me as the result of the peculiar position in which I find myself situated, your lordship.”
“What did he say?” asked the voice of Mrs. Waddington.
“Something about his peculiar position.”

===Chinese===
“别犯傻充愣。天还没黑到那个地步。”
“我指的是我自己所处奇特境地给我造成的种种困难，勋爵。”
“他说什么？”沃丁顿夫人的声音问。
“说他什么奇特的境地。”

===Original===
“Why is he in a peculiar position?”
“Ah! There you have me.”
“Let *me* talk to the man.”
There was a scuffling noise, followed by a heavy fall and a plaintive cry from a female in distress.

===Chinese===
“他怎么会有奇特的境地？”
“啊！这一下可把我问住了。”
“让*我*来跟这人讲。”
一阵推推搡搡的乱响，接着是一声沉重的跌落，外加一位落难女性的哀叫。

===Original===
“I knew that chair would break if you stood on it,” said Lord Hunstanton. “I wish I could have had a small bet on that chair breaking if you stood on it.”
“Wheel the bed under the window,” replied the indomitable woman beside him. She had lost an inch of skin from her right ankle, but her hat was still in the ring.
A grating noise proclaimed the shifting of the bed. There was a creak of springs beneath a heavy weight. The window, in its capacity of loud speaker, announced Mrs. Waddington calling.

===Chinese===
“我早知道你一站上去，那把椅子非塌不可，”亨斯坦顿勋爵说。“早知道该就‘你一站上去那把椅子就塌’这件事押上一小注的。”
“把床推到窗户底下，”他身旁那位百折不挠的女人答道。她右脚踝上剐掉了一寸皮，可她的帽子还留在场子当中。
一阵刺耳的摩擦声宣告床铺挪动到位。沉重的分量压得床簧吱嘎作响。那扇小窗以扬声器的身份，播出了沃丁顿夫人的呼叫。

===Original===
“Ferris!”
“Madam?”
“What do you mean? Why is your position peculiar?”

===Chinese===
“费里斯！”
“夫人？”
“你什么意思？你的处境怎么个奇特法？”

===Original===
“Because I am a deputy, madam.”
“What does that matter?”
“I represent the Law, madam.”
“The what?” asked Lord Hunstanton.

===Chinese===
“因为我当上了副手，夫人。”
“那有什么相干？”
“我代表着法律，夫人。”
“代表什么？”亨斯坦顿勋爵问。

===Original===
“The Law,” said Mrs. Waddington. “He says he represents the Law.”
“Let *me* speak to the blighter!”
There was another interval, which the butler employed in massaging his aching insteps.

===Chinese===
“法律，”沃丁顿夫人说。“他说他代表着法律。”
“让*我*跟那个家伙讲！”
又停了一阵，男管家趁机揉了揉酸痛的脚背。

===Original===
“Hi!”
“Your lordship?”
“What’s all this rot about your representing the Law?”
“I was placed in a position of trust by the officer who has recently left us. He instructed me to guard your lordship and Mrs. Waddington and to see that you did not effect your escape.”

===Chinese===
“喂！”
“勋爵？”
“你代表法律，这是什么浑话？”
“方才离开我们的那位警官，把一份须守信义的差事交给了我。他指示我看守勋爵您和沃丁顿夫人，务使二位无法脱逃。”

===Original===
“But, Ferris, try not to be more of an ass than you can help. Pull yourself together and use your intelligence. You surely don’t suppose that Mrs. Waddington and I have done anything wrong?”
“It is not my place to speculate on the point which you have raised, your lordship.”
“Listen, Ferris. Let’s get down to the stern, practical side of this business. If the old feudal spirit hadn’t died out completely, you’d do a little thing like letting us out of this place for the pure love of service, if you know what I mean. But, seeing that we live in a commercial age, what’s the figure?”
“Are you suggesting that I should accept a bribe, your lordship? Am I to understand that you propose that, in return for money, I should betray my trust?”

===Chinese===
“可是，费里斯，能不那么蠢，就别那么蠢。打起精神，动动你的脑子。你总不至于真以为沃丁顿夫人跟我干了什么不法的事吧？”
“您提出的这一点，不归我妄加揣测，勋爵。”
“听着，费里斯。咱们来谈谈这件事冷酷而实际的一面。倘若古老的封建精神还没死绝，像放我们出这个地方这样的小忙，你会出于纯粹的忠仆之心顺手就办了——你懂我的意思。可如今既然是个商业时代，开个价吧？”
“您这是在建议我收受贿赂吗，勋爵？我是否可以这样理解：您提议我为了金钱，背弃自己所受之托？”

===Original===
“Yes. How much?”
“How much has your lordship got?”
“What did he say?” asked Mrs. Waddington.
“He asked how much we’d got.”

===Chinese===
“正是。多少钱？”
“勋爵身上带了多少？”
“他说什么？”沃丁顿夫人问。
“他问我们带了多少钱。”

===Original===
“How much what?”
“Money.”
“He wishes to extort money from us?”
“That’s what it sounded like.”

===Chinese===
“多少什么？”
“钱。”
“他想敲我们的竹杠？”
“听着就是这个意思。”

===Original===
“Let *me* speak to the man.”
Mrs. Waddington came to the window.
“Ferris.”
“Madam?”

===Chinese===
“让*我*来跟这人讲。”
沃丁顿夫人来到窗前。
“费里斯。”
“夫人？”

===Original===
“You ought to be ashamed of yourself.”
“Yes, madam.”
“Your behaviour surprises and revolts me.”
“Very good, madam.”

===Chinese===
“你也不怕丢人。”
“是，夫人。”
“你的行径让我震惊，让我恶心。”
“遵命，夫人。”

===Original===
“You cease from this moment to be in my employment.”
“Just as you desire, madam.”
Mrs. Waddington retired for a brief consultation with her companion.

===Chinese===
“从这一刻起，你不再受雇于我。”
“悉听尊便，夫人。”
沃丁顿夫人退开去，同她的同伴紧急磋商了片刻。

===Original===
“Ferris,” she said, returning to the window.
“Madam?”
“Here is all the money we have﻿—two hundred and fifteen dollars.”

===Chinese===
“费里斯，”她回到窗前说。
“夫人？”
“这儿是我们全部的钱——二百一十五美元。”

===Original===
“It will be ample, madam.”
“Then kindly make haste and unlock this door.”
“Very good, madam.”
Mrs. Waddington waited, chafing. The moments passed.

===Chinese===
“足够了，夫人。”
“那就劳驾赶紧把这扇门打开。”
“遵命，夫人。”
沃丁顿夫人等着，愈等愈焦躁。一刻又一刻过去了。

===Original===
“Madam.”
“Well, what is it now?”
“I regret to have to inform you, madam,” said Ferris respectfully, “that, when the policeman went away, he took the key with him.”

===Chinese===
“夫人。”
“怎么，又怎么了？”
“我很遗憾地不得不禀告您，夫人，”费里斯恭恭敬敬地说，“警察走的时候，把钥匙也带走了。”

## XVIII / 第十八章

===Original===
It had taken George some considerable time to establish connection with the Waddington home at Hempstead; but he had done it at last, only to be informed that Molly did not appear to be on the premises. She had driven up in her two-seater, a Swedish voice gave him to understand, but after remaining in the house a short while had driven off again.
“Fine!” said George, as his informant was beginning to relapse into her native tongue.
A yeasty feeling of pleasure and goodwill towards his species filled him as he hung up the receiver. If Molly had started back to New York, he might expect to see her at any moment now. His heart swelled: and the fact that he was in the unfortunate position of being a fugitive from justice and the additional fact that the bloodhound of the Law most interested in his movements was probably somewhere very close at hand entirely escaped him. Abandoning the caution which should have been the first thought of one situated as he was, he burst into jovial song.

===Chinese===
乔治费了好大的工夫，才同亨普斯特德的沃丁顿家宅接通了电话；可是等到接通，却只落得被告知：莫莉似乎不在宅子里。
“好极了！”乔治说。这时，他的报告人眼看又要退回她的母语去了。
挂上听筒，一股发酵般翻腾的喜悦，外加一股善待世人的温情，充满了他全身。既然莫莉已经动身回纽约，他眼下随时都可能见到她。他的心胸为之鼓胀；至于自己正不幸沦为一名在逃之犯，以及那条对他的行踪最感兴趣的法律猎犬多半就在咫尺之间，这两桩事实，他全然忘在了脑后。处在他的境地，谨慎本该是头一件要紧事，他却把它抛到了九霄云外，纵声欢唱起来。

===Original===
“Hey, Pinch!”
George, who had been climbing towards a high note, came back to earth again, chilled and apprehensive. His first impulse was to dash for his bedroom and hide under the bed﻿—a thing which he knew himself to be good at. Then his intelligence asserted itself and panic waned. Only one man of his acquaintance could have addressed him as “Hey, Pinch!”
“Is that Mr. Waddington?” he murmured, opening the door of the sitting-room and peering in.
“Sure it’s Mr. Waddington.” The reek of a lively young cigar assailed George’s nostrils. “Don’t you have any lights in this joint?”

===Chinese===
“喂，平奇！”
乔治正朝着一个高音奋力攀登，闻声摔回地面，浑身冰凉，惶惶不安。他的头一个念头是冲进卧室、钻到床底下去——这路活儿他知道自己拿手。随后神智回来了，恐慌退了潮。他相识的人里，会喊他“喂，平奇！”的只有一位。
“是沃丁顿先生吗？”他喃喃地问，一面推开起居室的门往里瞧。
“正是沃丁顿先生。”一支生龙活虎的年轻雪茄的烟气直冲乔治的鼻孔。“你们这地方连个灯都不开吗？”

===Original===
“Are there any policemen about?” asked George in a conspiratorial undertone.
“There’s one policeman down in young Beamish’s apartment,” replied Mr. Waddington with a fruity chuckle. “He’s just sold me all his holdings in the Finer and Better Motion Picture Company of Hollywood, Cal., for three hundred smackers: and I’ve come here to celebrate. Set up the drinks,” said Mr. Waddington, who was plainly in as festive a mood as a man can be without actually breaking up the furniture.
George switched on the light. If the enemy was in as distant a spot as Hamilton Beamish’s apartment, prudence might be relaxed.
“ ’At’s right,” said Mr. Waddington, welcoming the illumination. He was leaning against a bookshelf with his hat on the back of his head and a cigar between his lips. His eyes were sparkling with an almost human intelligence. “I’ve got a smart business head, Pinch,” he said, shooting the cigar from due east to due west with a single movement of his upper lip. “I’m the guy with the big brain.”

===Chinese===
“外头有没有警察？”乔治压低嗓子，用串通机密的耳语问道。
“有一个警察，这会儿正在楼下小比米什的公寓里，”沃丁顿先生圆润地呵呵一笑，答道。“他刚把他手里全部的更精更美电影公司——加利福尼亚州好莱坞那家——的股份卖给了我，整整三百块大票子：我就是上这儿庆祝来的。把酒摆上。”沃丁顿先生说。他分明处在一个人不真动手砸家具所能达到的喜庆顶点。
乔治啪地拧亮了灯。既然敌人远在汉密尔顿·比米什的公寓里，谨慎不妨稍稍松一松。
“这就对啦，”沃丁顿先生说，对灯光的到来表示欢迎。他斜倚着书架，帽子推到后脑勺，嘴里叼着雪茄，两眼闪闪发亮，闪耀着一种近乎人类的智慧。“平奇，我长着一颗精明的生意脑袋，”他说，上唇一抖，那支雪茄便从正东被发射到了正西。“我就是那个有大头脑的家伙。”

===Original===
Although all the data which he had been able to accumulate in the course of their acquaintanceship went directly to prove the opposite, George was not inclined to combat the statement. He had weightier matters to occupy him than an academic discussion of the mentality of this poor fish.
“I found that girl,” he said.
“What girl?”
“The girl who stole the necklace. And I’ve got the necklace.”

===Chinese===
虽然凭两人相识以来所能积累的全部资料，得出的结论无一不指向反面，乔治却无意反驳这句话。他有更要紧的事压在心头，犯不着就这条可怜虫的心智问题展开学术讨论。
“我找到那个姑娘了，”他说。
“哪个姑娘？”
“偷项链的那个姑娘。项链我也到手了。”

===Original===
He had selected a subject that gripped. Mr. Waddington ceased to contemplate the smartness of his business head and became interested. His eyes widened, and he blew out a puff of poison-gas.
“You don’t say!”
“Here it is.”
“Gimme!” said Mr. Waddington.

===Chinese===
他这一题选中了要害。沃丁顿先生不再端详自己生意脑袋的精明，来了兴致。他两眼睁大，喷出一口毒瓦斯。
“真的假的！”
“喏，就是它。”
“拿来！”沃丁顿先生说。

===Original===
George dangled the necklace undecidedly.
“I think I ought to hand it over to Molly.”
“You’ll hand it over to me,” said Mr. Waddington with decision. “I’m the head of the family, and from now on I act as such. Too long, Pinch, have I allowed myself to be trampled beneath the iron heel and generally kicked in the face with spiked shoes, if you get my meaning. I now assert myself. Starting from today and onward through the years till my friends and relatives gather about my bier and whisper, ‘Doesn’t he look peaceful,’ what I say goes. Give me that necklace. I intend to have it reset or something. Either that, or I shall sell it and give Molly the proceeds. In any case, and be that as it may, gimme that necklace!”
George gave it to him. There was a strange new atmosphere of authority about Sigsbee H. tonight that made one give him things when he asked for them. He had the air of a man whom somebody has been feeding meat.

===Chinese===
乔治把项链拎在手里晃来晃去，一时拿不定主意。
“我看我该把它交给莫莉。”
“你得交给我，”沃丁顿先生斩钉截铁地说。“我是一家之主，从今往后，我就要照一家之主的样子行事。平奇，太久太久，我任凭自己被铁蹄践踏，还让人穿着钉鞋照着脸上猛踢——你若明白我的意思的话。如今我要挺直腰杆了。打今天起，往后岁岁年年，直到我的亲朋好友围着我的灵床悄声念叨‘瞧他睡得多安详’，凡事都得听我的。把那条项链给我。我打算把它重新镶一镶再说。不然的话，我就把它卖掉，钱给莫莉。无论如何，总而言之，把项链给我！”
乔治给了他。今晚的西格斯比·H 周身笼罩着一种陌生而崭新的权威气氛，叫人他要什么就给什么。他浑身透着一股刚让人喂过肉的气派。

===Original===
“Pinch,” said Mr. Waddington.
“Finch,” said George.
“George,” said a voice at the window, speaking with a startling abruptness which caused Mr. Waddington to jerk his cigar into his eye.
A wave of emotion poured over George.

===Chinese===
“平奇，”沃丁顿先生说。
“芬奇，”乔治说。
“乔治。”窗口一个声音说，来得如此突兀惊人，害得沃丁顿先生一哆嗦，把雪茄戳进了自己眼睛。
一股情感的浪潮劈头浇遍乔治全身。

===Original===
“Molly! Is that you?”
“Yes, darling. Here I am.”
“How quick you’ve been.”
“I hurried.”
“Though it seems hours since you went away.”
“Does it really, precious?”

===Chinese===
“莫莉！是你吗？”
“是我，亲爱的。我在这儿哪。”
“你回来得真快。”
“我一路紧赶。”
“可你走了这一小会儿，倒像隔了好几个钟头似的。”
“是吗，宝贝儿？”

===Original===
Mr. Waddington was still shaken.
“If I had been told that any daughter of mine would come and bark at me from behind like that,” he said querulously, “I would not have believed it.”
“Oh, father! There you are. I didn’t see you.”
“There,” said Mr. Waddington, “is right. You nearly scared the top of my head off.”
“I’m sorry.”
“Too late to be sorry now,” said Mr. Waddington moodily. “You’ve gone and spoiled the best ten-cent cigar in Hempstead.”

===Chinese===
沃丁顿先生还没缓过神来。
“要是早先有人对我说，我哪个女儿会这样从背后扑上来朝我狺狺狂吠，我是万万不会信的。”他委屈巴巴地说。
“哦，爸爸！你在这儿哪。我没瞧见你。”
“没瞧见才对，”沃丁顿先生说。“你差点儿把我天灵盖都吓掉了。”
“对不起。”
“现在说对不起，晚啦，”沃丁顿先生怏怏地说。“你可倒好，把亨普斯特德顶好的一支十美分雪茄给毁了。”

===Original===
He eyed the remains sadly: and, throwing them away, selected another from his upper waistcoat-pocket and bit the end off.
“Molly, my angel,” said George vibrantly, “fancy you really being with me once more!”
“Yes, Georgie, darling. And what I wanted to say was, I believe there’s somebody in your sleeping-porch.”
“What!”
“I’m sure I heard voices.”

===Chinese===
他哀伤地端详着那截残骸，随即扔掉，从马甲上兜里又摸出一支，咬掉了头。
“莫莉，我的天使，”乔治声音发颤地说，“想不到你真的又来到我身边了！”
“是啊，乔吉，亲爱的。我想说的是，我疑心你的睡廊里有人。”
“什么！”
“我敢肯定听见了人声。”

===Original===
Come right down to it, and there is no instinct so deeply rooted in the nature of Man as the respect for property﻿—his own property, that is to say. And just as the mildest dog will tackle bloodhounds in defence of its own backyard, so will the veriest of human worms turn if attacked in his capacity of householder. The news that there was somebody in his sleeping-porch caused George to seethe with pique and indignation. It seemed to him that the entire population of New York had come to look on his sleeping-porch as a public resort. No sooner had he ejected one batch of visitors than another took their place.
With a wordless exclamation he rushed out upon the roof, closely followed by Molly and her father. Molly was afraid he would get hurt. Sigsbee H. was afraid he would not. It had been a big night for Sigsbee H. Waddington, and he did not want it to end tamely.
“Have your gun ready,” advised Sigsbee H., keeping well in the rear, “and don’t fire till you see the whites of their eyes.”

===Chinese===
说到底，人的天性里再没有哪一种本能，比对财产的敬重埋得更深了——当然，说的是他自己的财产。正如顶温驯的狗也会为保卫自家后院同猎犬拼死一搏，人这条虫豸，只要以房主的身份受到进犯，也断然会翻身反咬。听说睡廊里有人，乔治登时气得心头翻腾，愤懑难平。在他看来，全纽约的人都把他那座睡廊当成了公共游乐场。前一批客人才撵出门去，后一批转眼就顶了班。
乔治一声无言的呐喊，冲上屋顶，莫莉和她父亲紧随其后。莫莉怕他吃亏。西格斯比·H 怕他吃不着亏。今晚对西格斯比·H·沃丁顿来说，是场热闹的大戏，他可不愿让它虎头蛇尾地收场。
“把枪预备好，”西格斯比·H 远远缩在后头支招，“等瞧见了他们的眼白再开火。”

===Original===
George reached the door of the sleeping-porch, and smote it a lusty blow.
“Hi!” he cried. He twisted the handle. “Good heavens, it’s locked.”
From the upper window, softened by distance, came a pleading voice.
“I say! I say! I say!” To Lord Hunstanton the beating on the door had sounded like the first guns of a relieving army. He felt like the girl who heard the pipers skirling as they marched on beleaguered Lucknow. “I say, whoever you are, dear old soul, let us out, would you mind.”

===Chinese===
乔治奔到睡廊门前，抡圆了擂上一记。
“喂！”他喊道，一面拧动门把。“天哪，锁上了。”
楼上窗口飘下来一个哀告的声音，隔着距离，显得柔柔和和。
“我说！我说！我说呀！”对亨斯坦顿勋爵来说，这擂门声听来不啻解围大军的第一阵炮响。他的感受，就像当年那位姑娘，听见风笛手一路高奏战曲，杀向被围困的勒克瑙（Lucknow）。“我说，不管您是哪一位，好心的老哥，放我们出去，劳您大驾。”

===Original===
George ground his teeth.
“What do you mean﻿—whoever you are? I’m George Finch, and that sleeping-porch belongs to me.”
“Good old George! Hunstanton speaking. Let us out, George, old top, like the sportsman you are!”
“What are you doing in there?”
“A policeman locked us in. And a blighter of a butler, after promising to undo the door, told some thin story about not being able to find the key and legged it with all our available assets. So play the man, dear old George, and blessings will reward you. Also and moreover, by acting promptly you will save the life of my dear good friend and hostess here, who has been hiccuping for some little time and is, I rather fancy, on the point of hysterics.”

===Chinese===
乔治咬紧了牙关。
“您这是什么话——‘不管您是哪一位’？我是乔治·芬奇，那睡廊是我的产业。”
“好样的乔治！亨斯坦顿通话。放我们出去吧，乔治，老伙计，拿出你那运动家的气派来！”
“你们在里头干什么？”
“一个警察把我们锁在了里头。还有个混账男管家，先前满口答应给我们开门，过后却编了个找不着钥匙的蹩脚瞎话，卷上我们全部随身的家当，一溜烟跑了。所以拿出男子汉的样子来吧，亲爱的老乔治，福报自会犒赏你。再者说了，你行事麻利些，还能救下我这位亲爱的好友兼女主人一条性命——她打嗝已经打了好一阵子，依我看来，眼看就要歇斯底里发作了。”

===Original===
“What are you talking about?”
“Mrs. Waddington.”
“Is Mrs. Waddington in there with you?”
“Is she not, laddie!”
George drew his breath sharply.
“Mother,” he said reproachfully in through the keyhole, “I had not expected this.”

===Chinese===
“你在胡说些什么呀？”
“沃丁顿夫人。”
“沃丁顿夫人在里头跟你在一起？”
“岂止在呢，小伙子！”
乔治猛地倒抽一口冷气。
“妈妈，”他隔着钥匙孔语带责备地朝里说，“我可没想到您会这样。”

===Original===
Sigsbee H. Waddington uttered a fearful cry.
“My wife! In there! With a man with a toothbrush moustache! Let *me* talk to them!”
“Who was that?” asked Lord Hunstanton.
“Mr. Waddington,” replied George. “Who was that?” he said, as a scream rent the air.
“Mrs. Waddington. I say, George, old man,” queried his lordship anxiously, “what do you do when a woman starts turning blue and making little bubbling noises?”

===Chinese===
西格斯比·H·沃丁顿发出一声惊天动地的叫喊。
“我的老婆！在里头！跟一个蓄牙刷胡子的男人！让*我*来同他们讲！”
“刚才是谁？”亨斯坦顿勋爵问。
“沃丁顿先生，”乔治答道。“那又是谁？”他问，恰在此时，一声尖叫划破了夜空。
“沃丁顿夫人。我说，乔治，老伙计，”勋爵忧心忡忡地问，“一个女人开始脸色发青、嘴里咕嘟咕嘟冒小泡，该怎么办来着？”

===Original===
Sigsbee H., finding that a man of his stature could not hope to speak to any advantage through the window unless he stood on something, had darted across the roof and was now returning with one of the potted shrubs in his arms. The wildness of his eyes and the fact that even in this supreme moment he had gone on puffing at his cigar gave him a striking resemblance to a fire-eating dragon. He bumped the tub down and, like a man who rises on stepping-stones of his dead self to higher things, elevated himself upon it.
This brought him nicely within range of the window and enabled him to push Lord Hunstanton in the face﻿—which was all to the good. His lordship staggered back, leaving the way clear for the injured man to gaze upon his erring wife.
“Ha!” said Sigsbee H. Waddington.
“I can explain everything, Sigsbee!”

===Chinese===
西格斯比·H 发觉，凭他那副身量，除非脚下垫点什么，休想隔着窗口占到言语上的便宜，便一溜烟奔过屋顶，此刻正抱着一盆盆栽灌木往回走。他两眼瞪得滚圆，何况即便在这千钧一发的当口，嘴里那支雪茄也没停过喷云吐雾，通身的气派活像一头喷火的恶龙。他把花桶咚地墩下，随即像个踏着自己死去旧我垫脚石攀向高处的人，稳稳登了上去。
这一来，他不偏不倚正好够着窗口，得以一掌推在亨斯坦顿勋爵脸上——这可是再好不过。勋爵踉跄退开，给这位蒙冤的男人腾出了地方，好教他凝望自己那位失足的妻子。
“哈！”西格斯比·H·沃丁顿说。
“西格斯比，一切都可以解释！”

===Original===
Mr. Waddington snorted.
“Nerve,” he said, “in its proper place and when there’s not too much of it, I admire. But when a woman has the crust to disparage the morals of one of the finest young fellows who ever came out of the golden West and then I happen to pop into New York on important business and find her closeted with a man with a toothbrush moustache and she has the audacity to say she can explain everything.﻿ ﻿…”
Here Mr. Waddington paused to take in breath.
“Sigsbee!”
“It’s living in this soul-destroying East that does it,” proceeded Mr. Waddington, having refilled his thoracic cavities. “If I’ve said it once, I’ve said it a hundred times, that.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
沃丁顿先生从鼻子里哼了一声。
