“胆气这东西，搁对了地方、又不掺太多，我是佩服的。可要是一个女人竟敢厚着脸皮，败坏一位有史以来打黄金西部走出来的顶优秀小伙子的名誉，随后我恰巧因要紧买卖上纽约来，撞见她同一个蓄牙刷胡子的男人关起门来密谈，她居然还有脸说一切都能解释得清……”
说到这里，沃丁顿先生停下来喘了口气。
“西格斯比！”
“都是住在这个摧残灵魂的东部住出来的，”沃丁顿先生重新灌满两片胸腔，接着说道。“这话我说过一回，就等于说过一百遍，那就是……”

===Original===
“But, Sigsbee, I couldn’t help it. It’s quite true what Lord Hunstanton was saying. A policeman locked us in.”
“What were you doing up here, anyway?”
There was a brief silence within.
“I came to see what that Finch was doing. And I heard him in here, talking to an abandoned creature.”

===Chinese===
“可是，西格斯比，我实在没法子呀。亨斯坦顿勋爵说的全是实情。是个警察把我们锁在里头的。”
“那你到底跑上这屋顶来，是打算干什么？”
里面静了一小会儿。
“我来看看那个芬奇究竟在干什么。我听见他在里头，跟一个自甘堕落的坏女人谈话。”

===Original===
Mr. Waddington directed a questioning gaze at George.
“Have you been talking to any abandoned creatures tonight?”
“Of course he hasn’t,” cried Molly indignantly.
“I have spoken with no one of the opposite sex,” said George with dignity, “except the girl who stole the necklace. And that was a purely business discussion which would not have brought a blush to the cheek of the sternest critic. I said ‘Hand over that necklace!’ and she handed it over, and then her husband came and took her away.”

===Chinese===
沃丁顿先生朝乔治投去询问的一瞥。
“你今晚同哪个堕落女人说过话没有？”
“他当然没有，”莫莉愤然嚷道。
“异性我可是谁也没搭过话，”乔治仪态庄重地说，“只除了偷项链的那位姑娘。况且那是一场纯属公务的谈话，再严苛的批评家听了也不会脸红。我说‘把项链交出来！’她就交了出来，随后她丈夫来了，把她领走了。”

===Original===
“You hear?” said Mr. Waddington.
“No, I don’t,” said Mrs. Waddington.
“Well, take it from me that this splendid young man from the West is as pure as driven snow. So now let’s hear from you once more. Why did the policeman lock you in?”

===Chinese===
“听见没有？”沃丁顿先生说。
“没听见，”沃丁顿夫人说。
“那就请听我一句：这位打西部来的杰出青年，纯洁得像才落的白雪。好，现在再听听你这边的说法。警察为什么把你们锁起来？”

===Original===
“We had a misunderstanding.”
“How?”
“Well, I﻿—er﻿—happened to throw a little pepper in his face.”
“Sweet artichokes of Jerusalem! Why?”
“He found me in Mr. Finch’s apartment and wanted to arrest me.”

===Chinese===
“我们之间闹了点误会。”
“怎么闹的？”
“这个嘛，我——呃——一时失手，往他脸上撒了点胡椒粉。”
“耶路撒冷的甜洋蓟！为什么？”
“他撞见我在芬奇先生的公寓里，要逮捕我。”

===Original===
Mr. Waddington’s voice grew cold and grim.
“Indeed?” he said. “Well, this finishes it! If you can’t live in the East without spending your time throwing pepper at policemen, you’ll come straight out with me to the West before you start attacking them with hatchets. That is my final and unalterable decision. Come West, woman, where hearts are pure, and there try to start a new life.”
“I will, Sigsbee, I will.”
“You bet your permanent henna hair-wash you will!”
“I’ll buy the transportation tomorrow.”

===Chinese===
沃丁顿先生的声音冷了下来，也狠了起来。
“是吗？好，这下子全完了！你要是在东部过日子，成天不干别的，光拿胡椒面喂警察，那就趁早跟我直奔西部去，省得你哪天抄起斧头去劈他们。这是我最后的、不容更改的决定。回西部来吧，女人，那儿人心干净，就在那儿重新做人。”
“我会的，西格斯比，我会的。”
“拿你那瓶永久定型的指甲草染发水打赌，你准去！”
“明天我去买车票。”

===Original===
“No, sir!” Mr. Waddington with a grand gesture nearly overbalanced the tub on which he stood. “I will buy the transportation tomorrow. You will be interested to learn that, owing to commercial transactions resulting from the possession of a smart business head, I am now once more an exceedingly wealthy man and able to buy all the transportation this family requires and to run this family as it should be run. I’m the big noise now. Yes, me﻿—Sigsbee Horatio.﻿ ﻿…” The tub tilted sideways, and the speaker staggered into the arms of Officer Garroway, who had come up to the roof to see how his prisoners were getting on and was surprised to find himself plunged into the middle of what appeared to be a debating society.
“… Waddington,” concluded Sigsbee H.
The policeman eyed him coldly. The fever of dislike which he had felt towards this man had passed, but he could never look on him as a friend. Moreover, Mr. Waddington, descending from the tub, had stamped heavily on his right foot, almost the only portion of his anatomy which had up till then come unscathed through the adventures of the night.

===Chinese===
“不行，女士！”沃丁顿先生大手一挥，差点从脚下的花桶上栽过去。“明天买票的是我。有一件事你听了准感兴趣：凭着精明的生意脑袋做成几笔买卖，我如今再一次成了顶顶有钱的人，这个家需要的车票，我全买得起；这个家，也该照它该当的样子由我来当家了。如今的大人物是我。没错，就是我——西格斯比·霍雷肖……”花桶朝一边歪去，演说家一个趔趄，栽进了加罗威警官的怀里。警官方才上屋顶来，想看看他的两名犯人情况如何，不想一头扎进了这场面——瞧着俨然一场辩论会。
“……沃丁顿。”西格斯比·H 把话收了尾。
警察冷冷地打量他。他对这人曾有过的那股深恶痛绝的高烧已经退了，但他永远没法把此人当朋友看待。何况，沃丁顿先生打花桶上下来的时候，一脚重重踩在他的右脚上——那是他全身上下历经今夜种种劫难、到彼时为止几乎唯一完好无损的部位。

===Original===
“What’s all this?” inquired Officer Garroway.
His eye fell upon George: and he uttered that low, sinister growl which is heard only from the throats of leopards seeking their prey, tigers about to give battle, and New York policemen who come unexpectedly upon men who have thrown tablecloths over them and hit them in the eye.
“So there you are!” said Officer Garroway.
He poised his nightstick in his hand, and moved softly forward. Molly flung herself in his path with a cry.

===Chinese===
“这是唱的哪一出？”加罗威警官问。
他的目光落在乔治身上，随即发出一声低沉、凶险的咆哮——那种咆哮，只有觅食的豹子、临战的猛虎，再有就是猝然撞见那个曾拿桌布罩过自己、又一拳打中自己眼窝的家伙的纽约警察，才从喉咙里发得出来。
“好哇，原来你在这儿！”加罗威警官说。
他把警棍在手里掂了掂，蹑手蹑脚地逼上前来。莫莉一声惊呼，扑身挡在他道上。

===Original===
“Stop!”
“Miss,” said the policeman, courteously as was his wont in the presence of the sex. “Oblige me by getting to hell out of here.”
“Garroway!”
The policeman wheeled sharply. Only one man in the world would have been able to check his dreadful designs at that moment, and that man had now joined the group. Clad in a sweater and a pair of running-shorts, Hamilton Beamish made a strangely dignified and picturesque figure as he stood there with the moonlight glinting on his horn-rimmed spectacles. He wore soft shoes with rubber soles, and he was carrying a pair of dumbbells. Hamilton Beamish was a man who lived by schedule: and not all that he had passed through that day could blur his mind to the fact that this was the hour at which he did his before-retiring dumbbell exercises.

===Chinese===
“站住！”
“小姐，”警察说，他在女性面前照例彬彬有礼。“劳您大驾，赶紧滚蛋。”
“加罗威！”
警察霍地转过身来。放眼天下，此刻能遏止他那恶毒图谋的只有一个人，而那人此刻已经来到人群之中。汉密尔顿·比米什一身毛衣，配一条跑步短裤，站在那里，月光在他角质框眼镜上熠熠闪烁，那形象庄严得出奇，也入画出奇。他脚上是一双软底胶鞋，手里拎着一对哑铃。汉密尔顿·比米什是个按作息表过日子的人：这一天纵然历经千般风波，也没能模糊他心里的一件事——此刻正是他做睡前哑铃操的钟点。

===Original===
“What is the trouble, Garroway?”
“Well, Mr. Beamish.﻿ ﻿…”
Confused voices interrupted him.
“He was trying to murder George.”

===Chinese===
“出什么事了，加罗威？”
“是这样的，比米什先生……”
七嘴八舌打断了他。
“他要谋杀乔治。”

===Original===
“He’s got my wife locked up in this room.”
“The brute!”
“Darned fresh guy.”
“George didn’t do a thing to him.”
“My wife only threw a little pepper in his face.”

===Chinese===
“他把我老婆锁在这间屋里。”
“这个畜生！”
“好一个放肆的小子。”
“乔治压根儿没招他惹他。”
“我老婆不过往他脸上撒了一小撮胡椒粉。”

===Original===
Hamilton Beamish raised a compelling dumbbell.
“Please, please! Garroway, state your case.”
He listened attentively.
“Unlock that door,” he said, when all was told.

===Chinese===
汉密尔顿·比米什举起一只哑铃，不怒自威。
“诸位，诸位！加罗威，陈述你的案情。”
他凝神细听。
“把那扇门打开，”等一切听完，他说。

===Original===
The policeman unlocked the door. Mrs. Waddington, followed by Lord Hunstanton, emerged. Lord Hunstanton eyed Mr. Waddington warily, and sidled with an air of carelessness towards the stairway. Accelerating his progress as he neared the door, he vanished abruptly. Lord Hunstanton was a well-bred man who hated a fuss: and every instinct told him that this was one. He was better elsewhere, he decided.
“Stop that man!” ejaculated Officer Garroway. He turned back, baffled, with a darkening brow. “Now he’s gone!” he said soberly. “And he was wanted up in Syracuse.”
Sigsbee H. Waddington shook his head. He was not fond of that town, but he had a fair mind.
“Even in Syracuse,” he said, “they wouldn’t want a man like that.”

===Chinese===
警察开了锁。沃丁顿夫人走了出来，亨斯坦顿勋爵跟在后面。亨斯坦顿勋爵警惕地瞟了沃丁顿先生一眼，随即摆出一副漫不经心的架势，朝楼梯口溜边儿蹭去。一近门口，脚下越来越快，倏忽间不见了踪影。亨斯坦顿勋爵是个有教养的人，最怕的就是是非热闹：本能告诉他，眼前这一场正是不折不扣的是非热闹。他想，还是别处为妙。
“拦住那个人！”加罗威警官脱口喊道。他转回身来，一无所获，眉头越拧越黑。“人可跑了！”他板着脸说。“锡拉丘兹那边还通缉着他呢。”
西格斯比·H·沃丁顿摇了摇头。那座城他并不喜欢，但他为人公道。
“就算在锡拉丘兹，”他说，“也不会要那样的人。”

===Original===
“It was Willie the Dude, and I was going to take him to the station-house.”
“You are mistaken, Garroway,” said Hamilton Beamish. “That was Lord Hunstanton, a personal acquaintance of mine.”
“You know him, Mr. Beamish?”
“Quite.”
“Do you know her?” asked the policeman, pointing to Mrs. Waddington.
“Intimately.”

===Chinese===
“那是‘花花公子’威利，我正要押他去分局呢。”
“你认错了，加罗威，”汉密尔顿·比米什说。“那是亨斯坦顿勋爵，我的私交。”
“您认识他，比米什先生？”
“认识。”
“那她呢？”警察指着沃丁顿夫人问。
“熟极了。”

===Original===
“And him?” said Officer Garroway, indicating George.
“He is one of my best friends.”
The policeman heaved a dreary sigh. He relapsed into silence, baffled.
“The whole affair,” said Hamilton Beamish, “appears to have been due to a foolish misunderstanding. This lady, Garroway, is the stepmother of this young lady here, to whom Mr. Finch should have been married today. There was some little trouble, I understand from Mr. Waddington, and she was left with the impression that Mr. Finch’s morals were not all they should have been. Later, facts which came to light convinced her of her error, and she hastened to New York to seek Mr. Finch out and tell him that all was well and that the marriage would proceed with her full approval. That is correct, Mrs. Waddington?”

===Chinese===
“那他呢？”加罗威警官朝乔治一指。
“他是我顶要好的朋友之一。”
警察凄然长叹一声，重归沉默，一筹莫展。
“整件事情，看来都起于一场愚蠢的误会。这位夫人，加罗威，是这位小姐的继母；而芬奇先生原定今日就与这位小姐完婚。据我从沃丁顿先生处了解，其间出过一点小小的岔子，害她误以为芬奇先生的品行有亏。后来，真相大白，她认识到了自己的错误，便赶忙来到纽约寻访芬奇先生，要把话带给他：一切都过去了，婚事将在她完全赞同之下照常举行。我说得不错吧，沃丁顿夫人？”

===Original===
Mrs. Waddington gulped. For a moment her eye seemed about to resume its well-known expression of a belligerent fish. But her spirit was broken. She was not the woman she had been. She had lost the old form.
“Yes.﻿ ﻿… Yes.﻿ ﻿… That is to say﻿ ﻿… I mean, yes,” she replied huskily.
“You called at Mr. Finch’s apartment with no other motive than to tell him this?”
“None.﻿ ﻿… Or, rather.﻿ ﻿… No, none.”

===Chinese===
沃丁顿夫人喉咙里咕地一声咽了口唾沫。有那么一瞬，她那只眼睛眼看又要摆出那副人人熟知的好斗鱼表情。但她的斗志已然崩溃。她不再是昔日的她。旧日的功架，尽失。
“是……是……也就是说……我是说，是。”她哑着嗓子回答。
“您登门芬奇先生的公寓，除了把这话告诉他，别无任何别的动机？”
“没有……或者说……不，没有。”

===Original===
“In fact, to put the thing in a nutshell, you wished to find your future son-in-law and fold him in a mother-in-law’s embrace. Am I right?”
This time the pause before Mrs. Waddington found herself able to reply was so marked and the look she directed at George so full of meaning that the latter, always sensitive, could not but wonder whether in refraining from punching her on the nose he was not neglecting his duty as a man and a citizen. She gazed at him long and lingeringly. Then she spoke.
“Quite right,” she said huskily.
“Excellent,” said Hamilton Beamish. “So you see, Garroway, that Mrs. Waddington’s reason for being in the apartment where you found her was wholly admirable. That clears up that point.”

===Chinese===
“一句话概括，您是想找到您未来的女婿，把他搂进一位岳母的怀抱。我说得对吗？”
这一回，沃丁顿夫人到能开口回答之间那段停顿，意味深长到了极点；她射向乔治的目光，含义又丰满到了溢出来的地步，以致一向敏感的后者不由暗自嘀咕：自己方才克制住没朝她鼻梁上揍那一拳，莫非反倒失了职守，既亏了身为男人的本分，又亏了身为公民的义务？她久久地、恋恋地凝望着他。然后开了口。
“一点不错，”她哑声说。
“妙极，”汉密尔顿·比米什说。“那么你明白了，加罗威：沃丁顿夫人之所以待在你发现她的那间公寓里，动机是无可指摘的。这一点算是水落石出了。”

===Original===
“It doesn’t clear up why she threw pepper in my face.”
Hamilton Beamish nodded.
“There, Garroway,” he said, “you have put your finger on the one aspect of Mrs. Waddington’s behaviour which was not completely unexceptionable. As regards the pepper, you have, it seems to me, legitimate cause for pique and, indeed, solid grounds for an action for assault and battery. But Mrs. Waddington is a reasonable woman, and will, no doubt, be willing to settle this little matter in a way acceptable to all parties.”
“I will pay him whatever he wants,” cried the reasonable woman. “Anything, anything!”

===Chinese===
“她干吗往我脸上撒胡椒面，这一点可还没水落石出。”
汉密尔顿·比米什点点头。
“说到这儿，加罗威，你总算一针见血，点中了沃丁顿夫人种种行止里唯一并非全无可议的一桩。关于胡椒面，依我看，你意气难平，名正言顺；说实在的，你握有提起一桩殴打人身侵害之诉的坚实理据。不过沃丁顿夫人是位通情达理的女人，想必愿意以一种各方都能接受的方式，了结这桩小小的公案。”
“他要多少我赔多少，”这位通情达理的女人喊道。“什么都行，什么都行！”

===Original===
“Hey!”
It was the voice of Sigsbee H. He stood there, forceful and dominant. His cigar had gone out, and he was chewing the dry remains aggressively.
“Say, listen!” said Sigsbee H. Waddington. “If there’s any bribing of the police to be done, it’s my place to do it, as the head of the family. Look me up at my little place at Hempstead tomorrow, Gallagher, and we’ll have a talk. You will find me a generous man. Openhanded. Western.”
“Capital,” said Hamilton Beamish. “So everything is happily settled.”

===Chinese===
“喂！”
这是西格斯比·H 的声音。他站在那里，强悍而威风。他的雪茄灭了，他正气势汹汹地嚼着那截干巴巴的残骸。
“喂，听着！”西格斯比·H·沃丁顿说。“要贿赂警察的话，那是我分内的事，一家之主是我。加拉格尔，明天到我亨普斯特德的小宅子来找我，咱们谈谈。你会发现我是个慷慨的人。出手大方。西部做派。”
“好极了，”汉密尔顿·比米什说。“如此说来，一切圆满了结。”

===Original===
There was not much of Officer Garroway’s face that was not concealed by the bandage and the steak, but on the small residuum there appeared a look of doubt and dissatisfaction.
“And what about this bird here?” he asked, indicating George.
“This individual before me,” corrected Hamilton Beamish. “What about him, Garroway?”
“He soaked me in the eye.”
“No doubt in a spirit of wholesome fun. Where did this happen?”

===Chinese===
加罗威警官的脸上，没被绷带和牛排遮住的地方已经所剩无几；可就在那一小块残余上，浮起了疑虑与不满的神色。
“那这小子呢？”他问，朝乔治一指。
“是站在我面前的这一位，”汉密尔顿·比米什纠正道。“他怎么了，加罗威？”
“他一拳砸在我眼睛上。”
“想必是出于健康有益的玩笑精神。这事发生在哪儿？”

===Original===
“Down there in the Purple Chicken.”
“Ah! Well, if you knew that restaurant better, you would understand that that sort of thing is the merest commonplace of everyday life at the Purple Chicken. You must overlook it, Garroway.”
“Can’t I push his head down his throat?”
“Certainly not. I cannot have you annoying Mr. Finch. He is to be married tomorrow, and he is a friend of mine.”
“But.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
“就在楼下那家紫鸡餐厅。”
“哦！这个嘛，你若对那家馆子了解更多些，就会明白，那一类事情在紫鸡餐厅的日常生活里，不过是稀松平常的家常便饭。你务必高抬贵手，加罗威。”
“那我把他的脑袋按进他嗓子眼里，总可以吧？”
“当然不可以。我不容许你纠缠芬奇先生。他明天就要成婚，而且他是我的朋友。”
“可是……”

===Original===
“Garroway,” said Hamilton Beamish, in a quiet, compelling voice, “Mr. Finch is a friend of mine.”
“Very well, Mr. Beamish,” said the policeman resignedly.
Mrs. Waddington was plucking at her husband’s sleeve.
“Sigsbee.”
“Hello?”

===Chinese===
“加罗威，”汉密尔顿·比米什说，声音平静，却不容抗辩，“芬奇先生是我的朋友。”
“好吧，比米什先生。”警察认了命。
沃丁顿夫人在扯她丈夫的袖子。
“西格斯比。”
“啊？”

===Original===
“Sigsbee, dear, I’m starving. I have had nothing to eat since lunch. There is some wonderful soup in there.”
“Let’s go,” said Sigsbee H. “You coming?” he said to George.
“I thought of taking Molly off somewhere.”
“Oh no, do come with us, George,” said Mrs. Waddington winningly. She drew closer to him. “George, is it really true that you hit that policeman in the eye?”
“Yes.”

===Chinese===
“西格斯比，亲爱的，我饿坏了。打午饭起，我粒米没进。里头有妙极了的汤。”
“走吧，”西格斯比·H 说。“你一起来吗？”他对乔治说。
“我本想带莫莉上哪儿去转转。”
“哦，别呀，跟我们一起去吧，乔治，”沃丁顿夫人说得婉转动人。她朝他又挨近了些。“乔治，你一拳打中那警察的眼睛，当真是真的？”
“是的。”

===Original===
“Tell me about it.”
“Well, he was trying to arrest me, so I threw a tablecloth over his head and then plugged him a couple of rather juicy ones which made him leave go.”
Mrs. Waddington’s eyes glistened. She put her arm through his.
“George,” she said, “I have misjudged you. I could wish Molly no better husband.”

===Chinese===
“讲给我听听。”
“是这样，他要逮捕我，我就拿桌布罩住他的脑袋，然后结结实实喂了他两三记拳头，叫他撒了手。”
沃丁顿夫人两眼放光。她挽住了他的胳膊。
“乔治，”她说，“是我错看了你。莫莉能嫁的丈夫，再没有比你更好的了。”

---

===Original===
Hamilton Beamish stood in the moonlight, swinging his dumbbells. Having done this for awhile, he embarked on a few simple setting-up exercises. He stood with his feet some six inches apart, his toes turned slightly out: then, placing his hands on his hips, thumbs back, bent slightly forward from the shoulders﻿—not from the hips. He retracted the lower abdomen, and, holding it retracted, leaned well over to the left side, contracting the muscles of the left side forcibly. He kept his legs straight all the time, his knees stiff. He reversed to right side, and repeated twenty times﻿—ten right, ten left. This exercise was done slowly and steadily, without jerking.
“Ah!” said Hamilton Beamish, relaxing. “Splendid for the transversalis muscle, that, converting it into a living belt which girds the loins. Have you ever given considered thought to the loins, Garroway?”
The policeman shook his head.
“Not that I know of,” he said indifferently. “I’ve seen ’em in the Bronx Zoo.”

===Chinese===
汉密尔顿·比米什站在月光里，一左一右地挥着哑铃。挥了一阵，他又着手做了几节简便的健身操。他两脚分开约六英寸站定，脚尖稍稍朝外；随后两手叉腰，拇指朝后，上身打肩部——不是打胯部——微微前倾。他收紧小腹，保持收紧，向左侧深深弯去，用力收缩左侧肌肉。全程两腿绷得笔直，膝盖不打弯。再换到右侧，如此往复二十次——右边十次，左边十次。这套动作做得缓慢平稳，绝不猛拉硬拽。
“啊！”汉密尔顿·比米什松下一口气来说。“这一节对腹横肌大有裨益，能把它练成一条束住两腰的活腰带。加罗威，你可曾认真琢磨过腰？”
警察摇了摇头。
“没琢磨过，”他淡淡地说，“鹞子嘛，布朗克斯动物园里倒是见过。”

===Original===
Hamilton Beamish eyed him with concern.
“Garroway,” he said, “you seem distrait.”
“If that’s how a feller is when he’s been hit and punched, and stepped on and had pepper thrown at him and tablecloths put over his head I’ve got a swell license to seem distrait,” replied the policeman bitterly. “And on top of all that, when I thought I had made a cop.﻿ ﻿…”
“Brought about an arrest.”
“… brought about an arrest which would have got me promotion, I find they’re all friends of yours and have to be allowed to make a clean getaway. That’s what jars me, Mr. Beamish.”

===Chinese===
汉密尔顿·比米什关切地望着他。
“加罗威，”他说，“你瞧着一副心不在焉的模样。”
“一个人挨了打、吃了拳、叫人踩了脚、撒了一脸胡椒面、脑袋上还罩过桌布，要是这之后就是这副模样，那我可有天大的资格显得心不在焉，”警察酸溜溜地答道。“这还不算完，我满以为办成了一桩拘捕……”
“促成了一桩逮捕。”
“……促成了一桩本来能叫我升官的逮捕案，结果发现他们个个都是你的朋友，还非得放人家全身而退不可。就是这件事硌着我呢，比米什先生。”

===Original===
Hamilton Beamish patted him on the shoulder.
“Every poet, Garroway, has to learn in suffering before he can teach in song. Look at Keats! Look at Chatterton! One of these days you will be thankful that all this has happened. It will be the making of you. Besides, think of the money you are going to get from Mr. Waddington tomorrow.”
“I’d give it all for one long, cool drink now.”
“Mr. Garroway.”

===Chinese===
汉密尔顿·比米什拍了拍他的肩膀。
“加罗威，每一个诗人都得先在苦难中学习，然后才能在歌吟中施教。瞧瞧济慈（Keats）！瞧瞧查特顿（Chatterton）！总有一天，你会为今夜这一切感念不已。它们会成全你。再说，想想明天你就要从沃丁顿先生那儿领到的那笔钱吧。”
“眼下只要能来上一大杯冰凉的酒，那些钱我全都舍得换。”
“加罗威先生。”

===Original===
The policeman looked up. Molly was standing in the window.
“Mr. Garroway,” said Molly, “a most mysterious thing has happened. Mr. Finch has found two large bottles of champagne in his cupboard. He can’t think how they got there, but he says would you care to come in and examine them and see whether they are good or not.”
The cloud which had hung about the policeman’s face passed from it as if beneath some magic spell. His tongue came slowly out of his mouth and moved lovingly over his arid lips. His one visible eye gleamed with the light which never was on land or sea.
“Are you with me, Mr. Beamish?” he asked.
“I precede you, Mr. Garroway,” said Hamilton Beamish.

===Chinese===
警察抬起头来。莫莉正站在窗口。
“加罗威先生，”莫莉说，“出了一件顶顶神秘的事。芬奇先生在他的食橱里发现了两大瓶香槟。他自己也想不出它们是打哪儿来的；不过他说，请您可否进来验一验，看看那酒究竟好不好。”
笼罩在警察脸上的阴云，像叫什么魔咒一挥而散。他的舌头缓缓探出嘴来，爱怜地舔过干渴的嘴唇。他那只露在外面的眼睛里，闪动着一种陆地上海上都从来不曾有过的光。
“你跟我一道来吗，比米什先生？”他问。
“我在您前头先走，加罗威先生，”汉密尔顿·比米什说。
